## Pass 258 — 2026-10-17 — **THIS PASS'S REGULATORY HARVEST WAS `32` FACTS AND `31` OF THEM WERE ALREADY CARRIED. THE GATE REFUSED THEM, WHICH IS THE TREND WORTH RECORDING: THE EXTERNAL PICTURE HAS STOPPED MOVING FASTER THAN THIS BASE READS IT**

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p258-websearch`](../evidence/p258-websearch.2026-10-17-p258.tsv) *(`8` legs, `4` regional)* · [`p258-trending`](../evidence/p258-trending.2026-10-17-p258.tsv) *(`13` repos)* · [`p258-cc-census`](../evidence/p258-cc-census.2026-10-17-p258.tsv) · [`p258-channel-map`](../evidence/p258-channel-map.2026-10-17-p258.tsv)

### 🔴 `T258-T1` — the measurement: `31` of `32` regulatory facts refused as already claimed

🔴 **Eight `WebSearch` legs, four of them per-region, produced `32` distinct regulatory facts. `make claims` refused `31` of them.** Each was run through the gate *before* it could be written down:

| fact offered by this pass's search | `make claims` |
|---|---|
| Annex III education obligations now `2027-12-02`; Regulation (EU) `2026/1744`; embedded high-risk `2028-08-02`; Article `27` `FRIA`; Article `4` AI literacy live; Article `5(1)(f)` emotion-inference ban | 🔴 **ALREADY CLAIMED** *(all seven tokens)* |
| Ohio `2026-07-01`; Oklahoma S.B. `1734`; Florida `2027-07-01` + **parental opt-in**; Maryland S.B. `720` coordinators; Idaho S.B. `1227`; North Carolina `2027-06-30`; Alabama H.B. `329` graduation credit; Texas HB `2400`; New York City grades 2-K–8 pause | 🔴 **ALREADY CLAIMED** *(all nine)* |
| India Class-`3` mandate, IIT Madras curriculum; Singapore Primary `4` via Student Learning Space; UNESCO LAC Observatory at ECLAC; `13`/`19` LAC countries; Brazil PL `2.338/2023`; Chile's risk-based bill + Latam-GPT | 🔴 **ALREADY CLAIMED** |
| 🟡 China MoE **"AI Plus Education"** action plan through `2030` | 🟢 **NOT CLAIMED** |

🟢 **This is the gate working against the pass that owns it, and it is the right outcome.** 🔵 `p254`'s lesson was *the base inherits its own conclusions*; the counter-measure has been a claims gate, and this is the first pass where that gate refused **almost the entire** external harvest. **A base that cannot refuse its own findings re-publishes them and calls the volume progress.**

🔴 **The trend that follows, stated as a trend and not as a boast: the education-AI regulatory picture has stopped moving faster than this base reads it.** Four regions, eight legs, two `extended`-mode searches per region, and the net new regulatory content was **one** plan — which is itself unconfirmed. 🔵 The implication for sourcing is to **stop spending passes on regulatory search** and spend them on primary-text corroboration of what is already carried: `eur-lex` remains unprobed, and India's mandate remains *reported*.

🟡 **The one new fact, and its provenance stated honestly.** China's Ministry of Education **"AI Plus Education"** action plan, reported `2026-04`, targeting full implementation across primary-to-university by **`2030`**. 🔴 **REPORTED, NOT CONFIRMED:** `english.www.gov.cn` failed `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND` through `WebFetch` — the same failure mode `p257` recorded for `eur-lex.europa.eu` — so it rests on **one secondary tracker**. 🔴 **And the sources disagree on the operative number:** required annual AI-instruction hours for lower grades are reported as **`6`** in one source and **`8`** in another, with a third claiming AI became compulsory from age six. 🔵 **A plan whose own hour count varies by `33 %` across its secondary sources is a lead, not a planning input.**

### 🔴 `T258-T2` — the real trend this pass found is in the licences, not the legislation

🔴 **Instructional material is licensed as content, and `23.1 %` of what this base carries forbids commercial use.** `828` Creative Commons tokens across the ten shelves: `191` `NonCommercial`, `216` `ShareAlike`, `421` plain `CC-BY`/`CC0`.

🔵 **Why this is a trend and not a static fact: the open education corpus and the open *software* corpus are drifting apart.** The software side of this sector consolidated on `MIT`/`Apache-2.0` — every permissive row this pass published is one of the two, and `8` of `12` are the byte-identical `11 357` B Apache payload. 🔴 **The content side did not consolidate.** It fragmented across six `CC` variants whose practical consequences differ completely, and 🔴 **a quarter of it landed on terms no paid engagement can use.**

🔴 **The measured consequence for tooling: `CC0-1.0` and `CC-BY-ND-4.0` were indistinguishable to this base's standing gate until this pass** — the most permissive grant that exists and a prohibition on adaptation, both reported as `UNCLASSIFIED:no-family-no-restriction` with zero hits. 🟢 Closed by [`pass258-content-licence-family`](../compose/code/pass258-content-licence-family/).

### 🟢 `T258-T3` — education still does not trend, now on four windows

🔴 **`github.com/trending`, read first-hand: `13` repositories, `0` education.** With `p254`'s `11`/`0`, `p256`'s `13`/`0` and `p257`'s `13`/`0`, **four independent reads agree.**

🔵 **What the page is full of instead, this window: agent harnesses and skills.** [`anthropics/claude-plugins-official`](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-plugins-official), [`K-Dense-AI/scientific-agent-skills`](https://github.com/K-Dense-AI/scientific-agent-skills), [`obra/superpowers`](https://github.com/obra/superpowers), [`cursor/plugins`](https://github.com/cursor/plugins), [`android/skills`](https://github.com/android/skills), [`HKUDS/CLI-Anything`](https://github.com/HKUDS/CLI-Anything).

🟢 **And that last one is the trend, read correctly.** [`HKUDS`](https://github.com/HKUDS) is the lab behind [`DeepTutor`](https://github.com/HKUDS/DeepTutor) — now 🟢 **`41 522`★**, the largest open AI-tutor project in existence — and what trends from that lab is its **agent tooling**, not its tutor. 🔵 **The sector's attention flows to the substrate and never to the application.** For a studio that is good news: the substrate is `MIT` *(`nanobot`, `49 102`★)*, maintained, and free to build the application on.

🔴 **`WebSearch` paid `0` publishable repositories for the TWENTY-SEVENTH consecutive pass.** 🔵 The one structural observation from the legs, worth more than their yield: a search for *"open source school management platform SIS ERP Apache MIT licensed"* returned **no platform that is Apache- or MIT-licensed** and offered `RosarioSIS`, `openSIS` and `OpenEduCat` — of which this pass read `RosarioSIS` as `GPL` first-hand and found `openSIS-Classic` with **no root payload at `26` paths**. 🟢 **An independent channel reaching this base's own standing conclusion** — see [`verticals/solutions.md`](solutions.md) `T258-V2`.

### 🔵 `T258-T4` — the lesson for the ledger

🔵 **Extending a six-pass set.** `p251`: *unwired knowledge.* `p253`: *nothing consumes the wire.* `p254`: *the base inherits its own conclusions about what is impossible.* `p255`: *a claim adopted at a retraction inherits the retraction's credibility.* `p256`: *a gate's existence was read as its coverage.* `p257`: *a classifier's scope, not its vocabulary, was the hole.*

🟢 **`p258`: a refusal from ONE endpoint was generalised to the whole channel.** The authenticated API was recorded as scope-gated at `p257` on the evidence of two endpoints, and every pass since rationed itself to `10` unauthenticated calls an hour. 🔴 **The same credential, on the same transport, answered `200` on all `14` calls this pass through a different endpoint — while a *third* endpoint on that same transport really is gated.** 🔵 **The counter-measure is not optimism about channels: it is that a channel map must be keyed on `(transport, endpoint)` and never on `(transport)` alone.** A single `403` describes one door, not the building.

## Pass 257 — 2026-10-17 — **A CARRIED DISJUNCTION CLOSED: THE EU ANNEX III DATE IS `2027-12-02` UNDER REGULATION (EU) `2026/1744`.** AND THE TREND WITH THE MOST CONSEQUENCE IS THE ONE NOBODY IS STARRING — INSTITUTIONS, NOT STARTUPS, NOW SHIP THE OPEN AI-EDUCATION STACK

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p257-websearch`](../evidence/p257-websearch.2026-10-17-p257.tsv) *(`11` legs)* · [`p257-row-supply`](../evidence/p257-row-supply.2026-10-17-p257.tsv) *(`99` candidates)* · [`p257-grant-payload`](../evidence/p257-grant-payload.2026-10-17-p257.tsv) *(`23` payloads)* · [`p257-trending`](../evidence/p257-trending.2026-10-17-p257.tsv)

### 🟢 `T257-T1` — the EU date, settled on a named instrument

🟢 **Regulation (EU) `2026/1744`, the Digital Omnibus on AI.** Council final adoption `2026-06-29`, published in the Official Journal in July, **in force `2026-07-27`**. It moves the Annex III high-risk obligations from `2026-08-02` to 🟢 **`2027-12-02`**.

| obligation | date |
|---|---|
| 🟢 Annex III high-risk *(incl. education: evaluating learning outcomes, screening applicants, monitoring candidates in examinations)* | 🟢 **`2027-12-02`** |
| Annex I high-risk | `2028-08-02` |
| systems operated by public authorities | `2030-08-02` |
| 🔴 **Article `50(2)` marking for pre-existing generative systems** | 🔴 **`2026-12-02`** |
| 🔴 **two new Article `5` prohibitions** | 🔴 **`2026-12-02`** |
| Article `4` AI literacy *(deployers)* | already applicable since `2025-02-02` |
| 🔴 Article `5(1)(f)` — emotion inference in education | 🔴 **prohibited, NOT postponed** |

🔴 **Nine secondary channels agree and the primary text was not read.** `eur-lex.europa.eu` was re-probed on both transports this pass and refused on both — `CONNECT tunnel failed, response 403` on `curl`, `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND` on `WebFetch`. 🟡 **So this is the best-supported EU reading this base can hold, and the regulation NUMBER itself is secondary-sourced.** 🔵 The disjunction `p255`/`p256` carried is closed on the *date*; the provenance hedge stays.

🟡 **The trap in the postponement, and it is the thing to tell a client:** nothing that is already live moved. A school deploying an assessment tool owes Article `4` literacy, human oversight and logging **now**, and anyone who shipped a generative feature before the omnibus owes Article `50(2)` marking in **six weeks**, not in `2027`.

### 🟢 `T257-T2` — the institutions are the vendors now

🟢 **Measured, not asserted: of `16` publishable permissive rows this pass, `14` come from a university, a national agency or a public research centre.** TUM *(`edutelligence`, `artemis-extension`)*, IIT Madras *(`Shoonya`, `Anudesh`, `IndicIFEval`)*, UC Berkeley *(`OATutor-Tooling`)*, MIT DCC *(`batch-issuer-ui`)*, Universitat Oberta de Catalunya *(`java-lti-1.3-core`)*, CENIA Chile *(`MVP-AIEP`, `multilingual-translator-app`)*, the Ed-Fi Alliance, Skolverket, Utdanningsdirektoratet.

🔴 **And they are invisible to every popularity channel.** `edutelligence` is a maintained, `MIT`-licensed, university-operated LLM tutor stack at **`17`★**. `lrs-reactions` — the trigger engine that makes a learning-record store wake an agent — is at **`1`★**. Meanwhile the highest-starred row in the whole sweep is `yetanalytics/colossal-squuid` at `71`★, which generates **sequential UUIDs**.

🔵 **So the trend is really a channel statement: stars rank reach, and in this sector reach and relevance are close to uncorrelated.** It is why `github.com/trending` has returned `0` education rows on three independent reads *(`p254`, `p256`, `p257`: `11`/`0`, `13`/`0`, `13`/`0`)* and why `WebSearch` has paid `0` rows for twenty-six consecutive passes. 🟢 **The channel that works is owner-deep harvesting of institutions this base already knows** — `60.6 %` new-address yield this pass against the `topic:` index's `10.9 %` at `p256`.

### 🟡 `T257-T3` — mandates are diverging, and one major programme reversed

🔴 **South Korea cancelled its AI digital textbook programme.** Adoption stayed below `30 %` and the National Assembly stripped the textbooks of official status after unions said the pace had outrun teacher preparation. 🔵 **This is the first large-scale national AI-in-education REVERSAL on these shelves**, and it falsifies the implicit assumption behind every national rollout story: that the binding constraint is model quality. **It was teacher readiness.** Korea's AI Basic Act has been in force since `2026-01-22` regardless.

🟢 **Against that, the mandates still hardening:** India from **Class 3 in `2026`–`27`** *(IIT Madras leading curriculum, `10`M+ educators to train — 🟡 reported, not confirmed against an official notice)*; Beijing at `8` hours a year since `2025` with Guangdong extending to lower grades; Singapore routing Primary-`4` AI through the Student Learning Space under teacher supervision; Japan deliberately **neutral** and monitoring pilots.

🟡 **And the public is not uniformly with it:** support for banning AI in schools runs `23 %` *(Indonesia)* to `31 %` *(South Korea)* across the Asian markets surveyed, and **rose** in Japan over the survey period.

### 🟢 `T257-T4` — in North America the deadline is becoming architecture, not paperwork

🟢 **Oklahoma's parent opt-out from student-facing AI, with a requirement to provide alternative access to core instruction, is the first instrument on these shelves that cannot be satisfied by a document.** It requires a per-learner off switch that degrades to an equivalent non-AI path. 🔵 **That is a build requirement, and retrofitting it is expensive** — which makes it the most commercially consequential US detail this pass.

🟡 Around it the familiar picture, with source counts that disagree *(`25` / `27` / `31` states; one tracker counts `134` bills across `31` states; another `34` states plus DC and Puerto Rico with guidance)*: Ohio and Tennessee **mandate** district AI policies *(Ohio's deadline `2026-07-01`, already passed)*; North Carolina staged to mid-`2028`; Utah legislating middle-school AI literacy; California and Alabama focused on keeping student data out of commercial AI training. 🔴 **No federal AI-in-education statute** — FERPA and COPPA plus a `2026` federal grant rule prioritising AI literacy.

### 🟡 `T257-T5` — the money is consolidating around proof

🔴 **Edtech venture funding fell to ≈ `USD 1` bn in H1 `2026`, down `26 %` year on year**, with investors backing fewer companies and favouring demonstrated outcomes — specifically AI tutoring and workforce upskilling. 🟡 Market-size estimates for AI in education in `2026` span **`USD 6.4`–`11.4` bn** across four firms, a `78 %` spread this shelf refuses to collapse into one number.

🔵 **Read together with `T257-T2`: capital is contracting while the best open supply is being produced by institutions that were never seeking capital.** For a studio that is the arbitrage — the components are free, maintained and permissive, and the buyer is an institution with a statutory deadline.

### 🔴 `T257-T6` — the sector's own naming is unreliable, and it has a cost

🔴 **`OpenTutor` names at least four distinct projects**, and ranking them by stars puts a **LangChain tutorial** *(`1 117`★)* on top, ahead of the block-based tutor *(`142`★)*, an AI-teacher orchestrator *(`8`★)* and USC's restricted tutoring platform *(`2`–`4`★)*. 🔵 **A pass — or a client's own engineer — resolving a product name by search and taking the top hit gets the wrong project.** `p256` recorded the same class on licence PATHS *(case-sensitivity)*; this is the same defect one layer up, on NAMES. 🟡 Resolve by owner and read the repository, never by name and stars.

### 🟡 `T257-T7` — carried, and explicitly NOT re-litigated

- 🟡 **Moodle `4.5`'s AI subsystem (Oct `2024`)** remains a roadmap signal, not a production differentiator. Not re-probed this pass.
- 🔴 **Canvas discontinued its Free-for-Teacher hosted tier in May `2026`.** Sources conflict — one table still lists it as free — so flagged rather than settled. The open-source codebase is unaffected.
- 🟢 **Kolibri `0.16.0`, May `2026`, `MIT`** — still the only permissive mainstream LMS and still the right offline-first spine.
- 🔴 **No release-notes or changelog channel exists for open-source AI education.** Declared as a gap for the first time: what the search channels return instead is paper code *(arXiv `2602.07176`, `2607.13370`)* with no maintained repository behind it.

## Pass 256 — 2026-10-17 — **EIGHT TRENDS, AND THE ONE THAT CHANGED THIS PASS IS THAT OPEN SOURCE EDUCATION SUPPLY IS NOW ORGANISATIONAL RATHER THAN INDIVIDUAL** — THE ROWS WORTH HAVING COME FROM `20` INSTITUTIONS, NOT FROM A TRENDING FEED

⏱️ **SECOND pass of `2026-10-17`.** 🔵 **Evidence:** [`p256-row-supply`](../evidence/p256-row-supply.2026-10-17-p256.tsv) · [`p256-topic-arm`](../evidence/p256-topic-arm.2026-10-17-p256.tsv) · [`p256-topic-tail`](../evidence/p256-topic-tail.2026-10-17-p256.tsv) · [`p256-trending`](../evidence/p256-trending.2026-10-17-p256.tsv) · [`p256-websearch`](../evidence/p256-websearch.2026-10-17-p256.tsv)

### 🟢 `T1` — education open source is INSTITUTIONAL, and that is now measured rather than felt

🔴 **`github.com/trending`: `13` repositories, `0` education — the second independent read, after `p254`'s `11` and `0`.** 🔴 **`WebSearch`: `0` rows for the twenty-fifth consecutive pass.** 🟢 **The owner sweep: `100` addresses off `20` organisations, `44` new to this base, `5` publishable.**

🔵 **Read together these three results say one thing: this sector's software is published by institutions — ADL, 1EdTech, Open edX, OpenStax, Learning Equality, France Université Numérique, TAO, Apereo, Opetushallitus, SVWS-NRW — on institutional cadences, and none of that shows up in a feed built to surface individual momentum.** 🟢 **The practical consequence: discovery in this vertical is an ORGANISATION CHART problem, not a trending problem.** The channel that pays is `user:`, and the one that does not is whatever is hot this week.

### 🟢 `T2` — the state is now a regulator and no longer a buyer, in all four regions

🟢 `p255` found this in Korea *(AI Basic Act in force; AI digital textbook mandate unwound to optional material)* and called it the APAC lesson. 🔴 **`p256`'s four legs find the same shape in all four regions** — NA district mandates by statute, EMEA deployer duties, APAC compulsory hours with channel control, LATAM measured governance deficits. 🔵 **A trend that repeats in four independent regions is a planning frame.**

### 🔴 `T3` — a national AI policy was withdrawn for citing sources that do not exist

🔴 **South Africa's `2026` draft national AI policy: published `2026-04-10`, withdrawn `2026-04-26` over fake AI-hallucinated citations.** 🔵 **The trend is not South African.** `2026` is the year in which AI-generated policy text began to fail public scrutiny at national scale, and the remedy being demanded — show the source — is the same one a client will soon demand of a studio's own deliverables. 🟢 **Verifiability is becoming a procurement requirement, not a virtue.**

### 🔴 `T4` — "open source" is increasingly a posture, and the band to watch is the top of the star distribution

🟡 Carried from `p255` and re-measured this pass: of the `9` carried foundations above `450`★, 🔴 **`4` are `AGPL-3.0`/`GPL-3.0` and only `3` are `MIT`.** 🟢 **The permissive mass is in the `30`–`800`★ band — libraries, not platforms.** 🔴 And `p255`'s `CaviraOSS/PageLM` *(`2 030`★, a *"Community License"*: MIT clause verbatim for eleven words, then non-commercial, non-redistributable, revocable)* remains the shape to expect at the top. 🔵 **The planning rule: at the platform tier, assume copyleft-network or a rider until a payload says otherwise; at the library tier, permissive is still the norm.**

### 🟢 `T5` — the AI seat inside an LMS is the assessment module, and its licence decides the engagement

🟢 Measured this pass across `18` Open edX repositories: 🔴 **`edx-ora2` *(open response assessment — the natural seat for an AI grader)*, `event-tracking` and the platform itself are `AGPL-3.0`**, while 🟢 **`XBlock`, `xblock-sdk`, `paragon` and `codejail` are `Apache-2.0`.** 🔵 **So the architectural decision and the licence decision are the same decision**: an AI feature delivered as a plugin stays on `Apache-2.0`; one that patches the assessment core puts the client's deployment under network disclosure. 🟢 Full decomposition in [`verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) `T256-V1`.

### 🔴 `T6` — the knowledge-tracing research base this sector builds on is `2024` code

🔴 **`bigdata-ustc`'s model zoos — `EduKTM` `266`★, `EduCDM` `201`★, `EduNLP` `64`★, `EduCAT` `77`★ — were last pushed between `2024-01` and `2024-10`.** 🔴 The owner's one `2026`-active repository, `Agent4Edu` *(`97`★, pushed `2026-09-29`)*, carries **no grant at all**. 🔴 **`topic:knowledge-tracing` read to its tail is `90` repositories, of which `11` of the bottom `19` have no licence.** 🟢 **The live, permissive, maintained line is narrow and nameable:** [`pykt-team/pykt-toolkit`](https://github.com/pykt-team/pykt-toolkit) *(`MIT`, `450`★, `2026-10-16`)*, [`ZhijieXiong/pyedmine`](https://github.com/ZhijieXiong/pyedmine) *(`MIT`, `82`★, `2026-09-17`)*, [`HFUT-LEC/EduStudio`](https://github.com/HFUT-LEC/EduStudio) *(`MIT`, `78`★, `2026-01-06`)*. 🔵 **A studio standardising on the model zoos is standardising on `2024`; the three above are the maintained choice.**

### 🟢 `T7` — spaced repetition is the healthiest permissive family in education open source

🟢 **`open-spaced-repetition`: `11` of `13` repositories pushed in `2026`, every one permissive** — `fsrs4anki` `4 107`★ `MIT`, `ts-fsrs` `823`★ `MIT`, `py-fsrs` `513`★ `MIT`, `fsrs-rs` `445`★ `BSD-3-Clause`, plus Go, Swift and JS ports and an optimiser. 🔵 **One algorithm, seven language bindings, all permissive, all current — the only family on these shelves a studio can adopt in any stack without a licence conversation.** 🟡 `fsrs.js` *(`180`★, `MIT`, pushed `2024-04-10`)* is the original and the one a dependency audit will meet in older code; `ts-fsrs` is the live line.

### 🟡 `T8` — the market numbers are diverging while the obligations are converging

🔴 **Three secondary `2026` forecasts spanning USD `10.6`–`11.4` B on `2025` bases spanning `6.4`–`8.3` B**, and a maturity rating of `35`/`100`. 🟢 **Meanwhile the obligations across four regions converge on the same five artefacts: a written policy, human review of high-stakes decisions, a tool inventory, an AI-literacy programme, a logging trail.** 🔵 **The forecast spread is not a reason to wait; it is a reason to sell the process layer, which is specified by instruments rather than estimated by analysts.**

### 🔴 Declared gaps in this pass's trend reading, so silence is not read as coverage

- 🔴 **No `2026`-dated STANDING national AI-in-schools strategy for any specific sub-Saharan African country.** South Africa's was published and withdrawn inside April `2026`; fifth pass this gap is declared, first pass it has a named instrument.
- 🔴 **No Middle East school-level instrument surfaced this pass.** The carried UAE K-12 curriculum and Saudi SDAIA mandatory course are `2025`-dated and were not re-confirmed.
- 🔴 **`eur-lex.europa.eu` was NOT re-probed this pass** — recorded as unrun, not as confirmed dead. `p255`'s classification *(a CONNECT-layer policy denial, from the proxy's own log)* stands unchallenged, and the EU reading rests on eight secondary channels and no primary text.
- 🟡 **India's Class-`3` AI mandate is reported and not confirmed** against an official notice; it is the only `2026`–`27` obligation in this pass's regional legs without a corroborating instrument.
- 🔴 **No permissive education ERP or SIS exists.** Re-probed this pass; OpenEduCat *(`LGPL-3.0`, payload-verified `p253`)* remains the only complete one. Fourth pass declared.

## Pass 255 — 2026-10-17 — THE `2026` TREND, NAMED: **THE MANDATE RETREATS WHILE THE OBLIGATION ADVANCES.** KOREA UNWOUND ITS FLAGSHIP AI TEXTBOOK PROGRAMME IN THE SAME YEAR ITS AI STATUTE TOOK FORCE — AND THE OPEN-SOURCE TREND IS AWAY FROM THE COMMONS

⏱️ 🔴 **FIRST pass of `2026-10-17`, and the date boundary fell INSIDE it.** `p232`–`p254` all carry `2026-10-16`; this pass's measurements were taken `2026-10-16` `23:37`–`23:55Z` and it commits on `2026-10-17`. 🟢 **The evidence filenames keep the `2026-10-16` stamp because that is when the probes ran** — stated here so a later pass reading a `2026-10-16` TSV beside a `2026-10-17` commit finds this note instead of inferring the measurement drift this base keeps finding. 🔵 **Evidence:** [`p255-websearch`](../evidence/p255-websearch.2026-10-16-p255.tsv) *(`9` legs)* · [`p255-vietnam-dates`](../evidence/p255-vietnam-dates.2026-10-16-p255.tsv) · [`p255-row-supply`](../evidence/p255-row-supply.2026-10-16-p255.tsv) · [`p255-channel-map`](../evidence/p255-channel-map.2026-10-16-p255.tsv)

### 🟢 `T255-T1` — the year's shape, and it is a divergence rather than a direction

🔵 `p254` named `2026` as *"disclosure converging while classroom rules diverge"*. 🟢 **`p255` sharpens it with a case that runs both ways inside one country.** South Korea's AI digital textbook rollout — the most funded national classroom AI mandate anywhere — was reduced to **optional *"educational material"*** within about a year, over screen time and data objections. In the same window the **AI Basic Act came into force** *(`2026-01-22`)* with **education as a high-impact domain**.

🔴 **So the trend is not "governments are adopting AI in schools" and not "governments are backing off".** 🟢 **It is that the two moved in opposite directions at once: the state withdrew as a PURCHASER and advanced as a REGULATOR.** 🔵 **Every regional leg this pass fits that shape** — Oklahoma mandates a *policy*, not a tool; Maryland funds *coordinators*, not licences; Vietnam lists education among high-risk *systems*; the EU's live instrument is a *prohibition* on emotion recognition. 🔴 **The budget moved from the classroom to the compliance office, and a product roadmap pointed at the classroom is pointed at the wrong buyer.**

### 🟢 `T255-T2` — the open-source trend in education is away from the commons, and it is measurable

🟢 **Measured, not asserted.** The `topic:education`+`topic:ai` star head, `8` repositories:

| licence posture | count | examples |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 permissive *(`MIT`)* | `3` | [`PetoiCamp/OpenCat-Quadruped-Robot`](https://github.com/PetoiCamp/OpenCat-Quadruped-Robot), [`mohi-devhub/antivibe`](https://github.com/mohi-devhub/antivibe) |
| 🟡 copyleft-as-open-core *(`AGPL-3.0`)* | `3` | [`learnhouse/learnhouse`](https://github.com/learnhouse/learnhouse), [`echo-loop/Echo-Loop`](https://github.com/echo-loop/Echo-Loop), [`penecho/penecho`](https://github.com/penecho/penecho) |
| 🔴 bespoke rider | `1` | [`CaviraOSS/PageLM`](https://github.com/CaviraOSS/PageLM) — *"Community License"* |
| 🔴 no grant | `1` | — |

🔴 **Under half the education star head is usable by a studio.** 🔵 **And the two non-permissive halves are the same commercial strategy in different clothing:** `AGPL-3.0` on a hosted study tool exists to make self-hosting by a competitor uncomfortable, and a *"Community License"* does the same thing by writing it down. 🟡 **Neither is hostility to open source; both are open-core positioning in a category that has discovered it can charge.**

🟢 **The trend for the KB's own practice:** the licence field reported by the index is an **upper bound on permissiveness**, never a verdict — `NOASSERTION` carried real information this pass, and the `MIT`-looking opening clause carried a false one.

### 🟢 `T255-T3` — the regulatory calendar is now dense enough that DATES are the product

🔵 Eighteen months ago this shelf tracked whether jurisdictions would legislate. 🟢 **Today it tracks when.** Live and dated, education-relevant:

| date | jurisdiction | instrument |
|---|---|---|
| `2025-02-02` | 🟢 **EU** | Art. `5(1)(f)` emotion-recognition prohibition — **in force**, lands on proctoring |
| `2026-01-22` | 🟢 **Korea** | AI Basic Act — **in force**, education = high-impact |
| `2026-03-01` | 🟢 **Vietnam** | Law `134/2025/QH15` — **in force** |
| `2026-07-01` | 🟡 **Ohio** | district AI policies — **elapsed** |
| `2026-08-02` | 🟢 **EU** | Art. `50` transparency — **in force** |
| `2026-08-15` | 🟢 **Vietnam** | Decision `33/2026/QD-TTg`, `46` high-risk systems incl. education — **in force** |
| `2026-12-01` | 🟡 **Chile** | Ley `21.719`, automated profiling |
| `2026-12-02` | 🟡 **EU** | two further Art. `5` prohibitions |
| `2026-12-31` | 🟡 **North Carolina** | state model due |
| `2027-09-01` | 🟡 **Vietnam** | transition ends for **pre-existing** education systems *(18 months, not 12)* |
| `2027-12-02` | 🟡 **EU** | stand-alone Annex III high-risk |
| `2027`–`28` SY | 🟡 **Oklahoma** | every district must hold a written AI policy |
| `2028-08-02` | 🟡 **EU** | Annex I embedded high-risk |

🔵 **A calendar is a more sellable artefact than an opinion**, and it is the single thing a studio can hand a client in the first meeting that nobody else in the room has.

### 🔴 `T255-T4` — the trend in this base's own reliability, and it got worse before it got better

🟢 `p251`: *unwired knowledge.* `p253`: *nothing consumes the wire.* `p254`: *the base inherits its own conclusions about what is impossible and never re-tests them.* 🔴 **`p255` adds the one that completes the set: a conclusion adopted at the MOMENT of a retraction inherits the retraction's credibility without earning it.**

🔴 **`p254` did the hard thing — it broke a sixteen-pass inheritance by trying one transport — and then published a replacement explanation *(`403` = budget exhausted)* that the same pass's own `rate_limit` reading could have refuted.** 🟢 **The counter-measure is not more scepticism; it is that a replacement claim must carry its own falsifier.** [`p255-channel-reachability`](../compose/code/p255-channel-reachability/) is that counter-measure made standing: `47` controls, in `make hooks`, with p254's published row pinned as the case the rule **must refuse**.

## Pass 254 — 2026-10-16 — THE `2026` REGULATORY TREND HAS A SHAPE THIS SHELF CAN NOW NAME: **DISCLOSURE IS CONVERGING WHILE CLASSROOM RULES DIVERGE** — AND THE TREND IN THIS BASE'S OWN RELIABILITY IS THAT IT KEEPS MISTAKING A CLOSED DOOR FOR A WALL

⏱️ **TWENTY-THIRD pass of `2026-10-16`.** 🔵 **Evidence:** [`p254-websearch`](../evidence/p254-websearch.2026-10-16-p254.tsv) *(`9` legs)* · [`p254-channel-map`](../evidence/p254-channel-map.2026-10-16-p254.tsv) · [`p254-exit-status-census`](../evidence/p254-exit-status-census.2026-10-16-p254.tsv)

### 🟢 `T254-T1` — two regulatory trends, moving in OPPOSITE directions, and only one of them is a product problem

| axis | direction | evidence this pass |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **marking / disclosure** | **CONVERGING** | EU Article `50` in force `2026-08-02`; **South Korea's** framework law, in force `2026-01-22`, requires telling users when content is AI-generated |
| 🔴 **classroom permission** | **DIVERGING, and inside regions** | Katy ISD bans K-6 and phases to grade `12`; NYC holds a moratorium through grade `8`; Oklahoma and Maryland mandate human oversight by statute; `Ipsos 2026` finds ANZ more pro-ban than the Asian markets surveyed |

🟢 **Stated as the thing to build:** *disclosure* is a **once** problem — one marking layer at the LMS boundary satisfies Brussels and Seoul, and this base already holds the implementation in its `aiact-50-2-*` instruments. 🔴 ***Permission* is a forever problem** — age gates, per-district switches, parental visibility and retention rules, configurable down to a school. 🔵 **A studio that prices these the same way will underquote one and overbuild the other.** The margin is in recognising that the compliance *artefact* is converging while the compliance *configuration* is fragmenting.

### 🟢 `T254-T2` — the capability constraint has a number, and it relocates the opportunity

🔴 **UNESCO: fewer than `20%` of teachers in Africa feel adequately trained to teach digital skills.** Set beside the adoption ratios this base already carries — LATAM higher education at `87%` use against `26%` strategy, Brazil's schools at `53%` use against `22%` guidelines — a single pattern resolves:

| what is scarce | where | what it makes sellable |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **governance** | LATAM, where adoption ran ahead voluntarily | policy, audit, an institutional AI strategy |
| 🔴 **teacher capability** | Africa, where frameworks exist and training does not | competency-framework implementation, localisation, train-the-trainer |
| 🔴 **configurability** | North America, where the law is per-state and per-district | engineering: switches, retention, audit trails |
| 🟢 **none of the three** | the Gulf, where the mandate is national and funded | content and assessment pipelines at curriculum scale |

🔵 **The trend reading: *"AI in education"* has stopped being one market.** Each region's binding constraint is different, and the constraint — not the technology — determines what the first engagement is. 🟢 That is a sharper statement than `p253`'s *"governance lags adoption"*, and it subsumes it.

### 🟡 `T254-T3` — law is still acquiring a calendar, and the calendar now runs to `2027`

`p253` named the `2026` pattern as *law acquiring a calendar*. 🟢 It holds and extends: India's data-protection rules phase to **May `2027`**; Vietnam's education transition is now reported as running **to `2027`** *(🔴 in tension with the `2026-08-15` this base carries — [`intel/market.md`](market.md) registers it as a question, not a correction)*; the EU's Annex III education duties sit at **`2027-12-02`**.

🔵 **What a planner should take from a `2027` cluster:** the deadlines stopped being this year's problem, which removes urgency from the sale and adds runway to the delivery. 🟢 **The counter is the one `p253` found and it is still the strongest EMEA argument:** the EU deferral was caused by **regulator capacity** — Member States not designating competent authorities, harmonised standards late — and a capacity constraint means the conformity-assessment queue, not the engineering, is the bottleneck. **A date that moved because the regulator is not ready is a reason to start early, not late.**

### 🔴 `T254-T4` — the trend in this base's own reliability, which is the one it measures best

| finding this pass | what it says about the knowledge, not the market |
|---|---|
| 🔴 **`api.github.com`, `github.com/trending` and the `topic:` index were all reachable all along** — on a transport nobody tried | **sixteen passes of architecture built around an absence that was a closed door, not a wall.** The ledger recorded `403` and every later pass inherited the conclusion |
| 🔴 **the recipe the `Makefile` itself labels *"THE GATE"* printed `STOP` and exited `0`** | `p253`'s gate could not HEAR its controls; this one could not SPEAK. **Same shape: the signal exists, is correct, and nothing consumes it** |
| 🔴 **a cross-reference to a licence was read as a grant**, and it took a real payload to falsify a bound published seven passes ago | `p247` was *right* to publish its code-search result as an upper bound rather than a proof. **The discipline worked; the bound simply broke, which is what bounds do** |
| 🔴 **this pass's own first fix returned all four real BSL payloads to their Change License**, and only a `332`-fixture diff caught it | reading a patch is not measuring it — `p253`'s lesson, arriving from the other side within one pass |
| 🟢 `p253` refused to publish either of two secondary star figures; the measured value refuted **both** | 🟢 **a refusal to guess is a correct answer, and it was vindicated the moment a channel opened** |

🔵 **The common shape, and it is the thing to carry into `p255`.** `p251` framed this base's failures as *unwired or unfailable knowledge, never missing knowledge*; `p253` sharpened it to *nothing CONSUMES the wire*. 🟢 **`p254` adds the one before both: the base inherits its own conclusions about what is IMPOSSIBLE, and never re-tests them.** A failed probe becomes a ledger line, the ledger line becomes an architecture, and the architecture outlives the failure. 🔴 **Sixteen passes is how long that lasted here, and the cost was not the star counts — it was the `topic:` index, which is where every row this pass published came from.**

## Pass 253 — 2026-10-16 — THE SECTOR TREND IS **GOVERNED ADOPTION**, AND THIS PASS CAN SAY SOMETHING SHARPER THAN THE CONSENSUS: THE GOVERNANCE GAP IS NOW **MEASURABLE AS A RATIO**, AND IT IS WIDEST WHERE ADOPTION IS HIGHEST

⏱️ **TWENTY-SECOND pass of `2026-10-16`.** 🔵 **Evidence:** [`p253-regional-figures`](../evidence/p253-regional-figures.2026-10-16-p253.tsv) *(`29` rows)* · [`p253-websearch`](../evidence/p253-websearch.2026-10-16-p253.tsv) *(`9` legs)* · [`p253-date-regression`](../evidence/p253-date-regression.2026-10-16-p253.tsv)

### 🟢 `T253-T1` — "experimentation to governance" is the consensus; the ratio is the finding

Both global trend legs returned the same sentence in different words: the sector is moving from pilots to routine, governed use. 🔵 **That is true and nearly useless on its own** — it has been the stated trend on this shelf since `p240`. What `p253`'s regional legs add is the **denominator**:

| population | using AI | has a written policy or strategy | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| higher education, LATAM *(UNESCO IESALC/UNU-IAS, `200` institutions, `19` countries)* | `87%` | 🔴 **`26%`** | 🔴 **`3.3 : 1`** |
| schools, Brazil *(administrators)* | `53%` | 🔴 **`22%`** | 🔴 **`2.4 : 1`** |
| US K-12 teachers | `60%` *(`32%` weekly)* | — *(district policy mandated by statute in several states)* | 🟡 the only region where the policy side is being forced by LAW rather than bought |

🟢 **The trend, stated so it can be acted on:** governance is not lagging adoption uniformly — it lags **where adoption was fastest and voluntary**, and it leads **where a legislature compelled it**. 🔵 That reverses the usual reading. North America's `134` bills across `31` states are not evidence that North America is behind; they are the reason its policy ratio will close first.

### 🟢 `T253-T2` — a legal date STOPPED moving, and this shelf had not noticed

🔴 This shelf published the EU Annex III date as a *disjunction* at `p251` and `p252`. 🟢 **It was settled at `p233`:** `Reg. (EU) 2026/1744`, OJ `2026-07-24`, in force `2026-07-27` — Annex III education duties bind **`2027-12-02`**, Article `50` transparency **already in force since `2026-08-02`**, two new Article `5` prohibitions **`2026-12-02`**, Annex I embedded systems `2028-08-02`.

🟢 **The trend reading, which is the part a trends shelf owes:** the `2026` pattern is not *more* AI-in-education law, it is **law acquiring a calendar**. Vietnam's education carve-out has a date *(`2026-08-15`)*. Korea's act has a commencement *(`2026-01-22`)*. The EU's Annex III has a deferral with a **named cause** — Member States' failure to designate national competent authorities and conformity assessment bodies, with harmonised standards late. 🔵 **A deferral caused by regulator capacity is a different asset from a deferral caused by politics:** capacity constraints are visible, slow and tend to slip again, which makes `2027-12-02` a floor to plan against rather than a cliff.

🔴 **And the counter-trend this pass measured on its own channel:** leg 6 served the superseded `2026-08-02` date as current. That is the **sixth** recorded re-service. 🔵 Six re-services of one superseded date is itself a trend — **secondary coverage of AI law does not retract, it accumulates** — and it is why the dated, numbered instrument belongs in every client document and the article never does.

### 🟡 `T253-T3` — public mandate is now divergent WITHIN a region, not just between regions

`Ipsos Education Monitor 2026`: support for **banning** AI in schools is lower across the Asian markets surveyed and higher in Australia and New Zealand. 🔵 Every prior pass on this shelf treated APAC as one demand surface because its regulation is fragmented. 🟡 **This splits the demand surface too** — the same product needs a consent, opt-out and parental-visibility story in ANZ that it does not need in Korea or Singapore, and that is a delivery difference rather than a marketing one.

### 🔴 `T253-T4` — the trend in this base's OWN reliability, which is the one it can measure best

| finding | what it says about the knowledge, not the market |
|---|---|
| 🔴 the gate printed `ALL OFFLINE LICENCE CONTROLS PASS` with a failing suite, for every one of its `31` steps | a control is worth nothing until something *acts* on its exit status. `ACTION XM` and `XR` audited whether the suites can speak; nothing audited whether the gate listens |
| 🔴 one published `sha256` was the hash of a payload-minus-its-trailing-newline, and `2` of `3` were fine | the `P834` class is **right most of the time**, which is why five documents about it did not kill it. Incidence is a property of the payload *(trailing run `1`, `0`, `0`)*, not of the instrument |
| 🔴 a settled fact re-opened for two passes and priced a quarter's EMEA advice | the ledger a pass reads is `~40` lines; the shelf it contradicts is `~160 000`. **Momentum lives in the ledger** |
| 🟢 the one new row in sixteen passes came from checking names against the carried index | a channel called dry by impression for fifteen passes was not being tested |

🔵 **The common shape, and it is the trend worth carrying into `p254`:** every one of these is an **unread signal**, not a missing one. The base knew the Omnibus number, owned the `P834` class in two instruments, had `31` controls, and had the candidate names in hand. 🟢 **`p251` framed this base's failures as *unwired or unfailable knowledge, never missing knowledge*. Three passes later that framing is holding, and `p253` can add the sharper version: the failure is not in acquiring knowledge or even in wiring it — it is that nothing CONSUMES the wire.**

## Pass 252 — 2026-10-16 — THE SECTOR TREND IS THAT **AI-IN-EDUCATION LAW HAS ACQUIRED DATES**, AND THE FIRST EDUCATION-SPECIFIC DEADLINE ON THIS SHELF IS A **GRACE PERIOD** RATHER THAN AN OBLIGATION; THE KB-INTERNAL TREND IS THAT THIS BASE'S DEFECTS ARE NOW BEING FOUND IN ITS OWN **MEASUREMENTS** RATHER THAN IN ITS KNOWLEDGE

⏱️ **TWENTY-FIRST pass of `2026-10-16`.** 🔵 **Evidence:** [`p252-websearch`](../evidence/p252-websearch.2026-10-16-p252.tsv) · [`p252-platform-grants`](../evidence/p252-platform-grants.2026-10-16-p252.tsv) · [`p252-xr-census`](../evidence/p252-xr-census.2026-10-16-p252.tsv) · [`p252-xq-measurement`](../evidence/p252-xq-measurement.2026-10-16-p252.tsv)

### 🟢 `T252-T1` — the market trend: from *"governance"* as a posture to governance with a CALENDAR

🔵 The consensus framing on leg `4` is the same one the sector has repeated all year — *"AI in education is shifting from experimentation to governance"*. 🔴 **That framing has been true and useless for three quarters, because a posture cannot be planned against.** 🟢 **What changed this pass is that the APAC leg returned dates:**

| trend | `p250`–`p251` saw | `p252` sees |
|---|---|---|
| **where the duty sits** | federal/vendor-facing *(amended `COPPA`, `2026-04-22`)*, then the **district** *(OH, OK, IL, NV, SC)* | 🟢 **the national statute, in force and dated** — Korea `2026-01-22`, Vietnam, China `2026-07-15` |
| **what the duty says** | *adopt a policy* | 🔴 **comply, or hold a grace period that expires** |
| **what a studio sells** | assurance | 🟢 **a remediation deadline** |

🟢 **Vietnam is the instance that makes the trend concrete, and it is the first of its kind on this shelf: an education-SPECIFIC carve-out with a date** — systems already running in education before **`2026-08-15`** get an extra six months. 🔵 **Why a grace period is a stronger commercial signal than an obligation:** an obligation applies to everyone equally and gets deferred; a grace period divides the market into incumbents with a clock and new deployments with none, and clocks do not get lobbied away as easily as start dates do. 🟡 Set against EMEA, where the high-risk date is still a **disjunction** this session cannot close, the gradient is visible: **APAC now has dates and EMEA still has a date argument.**

### 🟢 `T252-T2` — the product trend: the regulated surface is moving from the MODEL to the PERSONA

🔵 China's **anthropomorphic AI service rules (`2026-07-15`)** and Nevada's ban on AI performing counsellor, psychologist or social-worker duties *(carried from `p251`)* are different legal instruments in different systems pointing at the same thing: 🔴 **it is the human-likeness of an education product that attracts the rule, not its accuracy.** 🟢 **The design consequence is concrete and cheap to act on:** a tutor's persona — name, voice, memory of the student, expressions of care — becomes a **compliance-relevant configuration surface** that has to be switchable per jurisdiction, not a brand decision baked into prompts. 🔵 Set beside EMEA's `Article 5(1)(f)` *(emotion recognition in educational institutions, banned since `2025-02-02`)*, the pattern reads clearly: **three jurisdictions now regulate the affective layer of an education product and none of them regulates its pedagogy.**

### 🔴 `T252-T3` — the supply trend: the open-source channel and these shelves have CONVERGED, and that is a different fact from "dry"

Fifteenth dry pass in sixteen. 🟢 **But `p252` is the first to check the candidates against the carried index instead of reporting an impression**: `8` legs, `6` named candidates, 🔴 **`6` already carried, `0` new**. 🔵 **The right reading is not that the world has stopped producing education agents — it is that `WebSearch` can no longer tell this base anything it does not have.** 🟢 Which makes the channel question a budget question: the payload channel *(`raw.githubusercontent.com`, `git ls-remote`)* pays in verified grants, and it is the only one that paid this pass.

### 🔴 `T252-T4` — the licence trend, and it is now directional rather than anecdotal

Three licence claims on one leg, three refutations from first-hand payloads, 🔴 **and all three errors point the same way: toward permissiveness** *(Sakai `ECL-2.0` claimed Apache-2.0; Forma LMS `GPL-2.0` claimed Apache-2.0; Open edX `AGPL-3.0` claimed Apache-2.0)*. 🔵 **A random error scatters.** 🟢 **So the trend worth carrying is a PRIOR: a licence cell arriving on the secondary channel should be read as an upper bound on permissiveness, not an estimate of it** — the opposite of how a comparison table reads. 🟡 `ACTION XU` asks for the rate over the whole shelf history, which would turn the prior into a number; `p252` publishes only the direction, from `3` of `3`.

### 🟢 `T252-T5` — the KB-internal trend has turned over, and the new shape is better news than the old

`p251` named this base's failure mode as **unwired or unfailable knowledge, never missing knowledge** — four passes of it. 🟢 **`p252` closes both of the ACTIONs that framing produced** *(`XQ`, the unfailable classifier; `XR`, the unfloored suites)* and the trend changes shape:

| pass window | where the defects were | how they were found |
|---|---|---|
| `p248`–`p251` | 🔴 in the **wiring** — a correct verdict that never reached the gate; a control that could not fail | by auditing the gate |
| **`p252`** | 🟢 in the **measurements themselves** — a byte count one short, an exit code read from the wrong process | 🔴 **by the author walking into the defect while testing for it** |

🔴 **Both of this pass's self-inflicted errors were in the instrument's own measurement, not in its logic:** a `sha256` taken through command substitution *(one byte short, hash unmatchable — `p178`'s own documented class)*, and an `exit=0` read from `head` at the end of a pipe *(`p251`'s own enumerated layer-1 swallower)*. 🔵 **The trend statement: this base has reached the point where its knowledge is sound, its wiring is largely closed, and the remaining error surface is the ACT OF MEASURING.** 🟢 That is a better place to be and a harder one to audit, because a wrong measurement arrives looking exactly like a right one — which is the argument for `ACTION XY`, bounding the population of instruments that still measure a payload through a captured body rather than a file.


## Pass 251 — 2026-10-16 — THE SECTOR TREND IS **COMPLIANCE MOVING FROM THE STATE TO THE DISTRICT**, WHERE THE BUYER IS; AND THE KB-INTERNAL TREND IS NOW FOUR PASSES OLD AND HAS A NAME: THIS BASE'S FAILURES ARE **UNWIRED OR UNFAILABLE** KNOWLEDGE, NEVER MISSING KNOWLEDGE

⏱️ **TWENTIETH pass of `2026-10-16`.** 🔵 **Evidence:** [`p251-websearch`](../evidence/p251-websearch.2026-10-16-p251.tsv) · [`p251-gate-exit-audit`](../evidence/p251-gate-exit-audit.2026-10-16-p251.tsv) · [`p251-gate-measured`](../evidence/p251-gate-measured.2026-10-16-p251.tsv) · [`p251-prefix-collisions`](../evidence/p251-prefix-collisions.2026-10-16-p251.tsv)

### 🟢 `T251-T1` — the market trend: the mandate layer dropped to the district, and that is where procurement lives

`p250` caught the **federal, vendor-facing** layer arriving *(the amended `COPPA` rule, compliance date `2026-04-22`)*. `p251`'s North America leg catches the layer below it: **three states now compel an individual district to adopt a written AI policy** — Ohio *(with a state model policy)*, Oklahoma *(before `2027`–`28`)*, Illinois *(state guidance due `2026-07-01`)* — while **Nevada prohibits a named capability outright** and South Carolina would require **parental opt-in**.

🔵 **Why this is a trend and not a list:** a state *guideline* is advice to thousands of districts; a state *mandate to adopt a policy* is thousands of procurement events with a deadline. 🔴 **And the demand is for ASSURANCE, not deployment** — the two largest districts in the country moved to restriction and moratorium in the same window. 🟢 The product shape that follows is auditable control: a policy, the data-flow inventory under it, and hooks that can express a ban *(Nevada's counsellor/psychologist clause)* as a block rather than a sentence.

### 🟢 `T251-T2` — EMEA's trend is the opposite shape: the one thing in force is a PROHIBITION, and the rest is deferred

🟡 The high-risk date is a **disjunction** — `2026-08-02` or `2027-12-02`, both served by this pass's channel, Omnibus agreement named, 🔴 not closable from this session. 🟢 **What is in force is `Article 5(1)(f)`: emotion recognition in educational institutions, banned since `2025-02-02`** — and the operational reading the leg supplies is that **webcam-based stress or affect inference in proctoring may be prohibited outright**. 🔵 **So EMEA's deliverable this quarter is a proctoring audit, which needs no date resolution**, while the high-risk programme waits on a text nobody here can read.

### 🟢 `T251-T3` — APAC's trend is supply-side: the state is buying CURRICULUM at national scale

Korea's `18` AI-centred universities *(AI basics mandatory for all students, up to `24` bn won over eight years)* plus `20` more funded for **non-engineering** AI literacy is the first instrument on this shelf where the state is the **buyer of teaching capacity** rather than the regulator of it. 🔵 Set against `p249`'s Vietnam statute *(education named high-risk, institutional deadline `2027`)*, APAC now shows both halves: **one jurisdiction regulating the use, another funding the teaching.**

### 🔴 `T251-T4` — the KB-internal trend, four passes deep, and it is the most reliable finding on these shelves

| pass | what failed | what it was NOT |
|---|---|---|
| `p248` | the only classifier here that reads a **Commons Clause** correctly was **outside** `make hooks` | not missing knowledge — it was written at `p207` |
| `p249` | `6` live `MIT` repositories were filed as **non-existent** because a harvester mutilated their names | not absent supply — they were on the shelves |
| `p250` | a lint built **for that exact defect**, wired into the gate, reported both offending lines on every run and `return 0`'d | not an unknown defect — the lint's own docstring was the worked example |
| 🔴 **`p251`** | 🔴 **`5` of `26` gate controls can pass while collecting ZERO tests, and the gate discards `stderr` on exactly those `5`** | 🔴 **not an untested subject — `1 152` controls exist and run; `5` of them could have stopped asserting silently** |

🔵 **Stated as a property, because four passes is enough to state one:** *when something is wrong here, the knowledge already exists in this repository and is either unwired, unrunnable, or incapable of failing.* 🟢 **The counter-measure that worked this pass is cheap and should generalise: a FLOOR.** Five numbers — `32`, `36`, `14`, `23`, `29` — turn five silent controls into loud ones. 🔴 **And the population still outside the gate is now counted: `344` instruments exist here, the `Makefile` references `23`.** That is `ACTION VW`'s real size and it is `321`.

### 🔴 `T251-T5` — a trend this pass is careful NOT to claim

🔴 **No supply trend.** `0` of `7` legs returned an education-agent repository, the `topic:` harvest **cannot run** *(`403`)* and `github.com/trending` is `403` for the fourteenth pass. 🔵 **A shut channel is not an empty world** *(`ACTION UK`)*, so nothing here says the sector's open-source supply has slowed — only that this session cannot see it. 🟡 **The one supply-shaped observation that IS safe:** of the `2` new slugs the channel produced, both are **course notebooks with no grant at `10` probed paths**, which is the tenth consecutive pass where the agent query returned curricula.

## Pass 250 — 2026-10-16 — THE SECTOR TREND IS **GOVERNANCE BECOMING A PRODUCT SURFACE**, AND THIS PASS CAUGHT SUPPLY FOLLOWING REGULATION INSIDE ONE WINDOW: AN `MIT` **STUDENT-PII DETECTOR BUILT TO THE FERPA DEFINITION** LANDING IN THE SAME QUARTER THE AMENDED **COPPA** RULE STARTED BINDING EDTECH VENDORS

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p250-websearch`](../evidence/p250-websearch.2026-10-16-p250.tsv) *(legs 4–8)* · [`p250-channel-refutations`](../evidence/p250-channel-refutations.2026-10-16-p250.tsv).

### 🟢 `T250-T1` — the trend with a dated instrument and a matching repository

🟢 **Legs 4–8 agree on a direction the shelves have been recording for three passes — *experimentation → governance* — and this pass is the first where BOTH halves are measurable in the same window:**

| the regulation | the supply |
|---|---|
| the amended **COPPA** rule binds **edtech vendors**, compliance **`22 Apr 2026`** | [`gener8v/gener8v.tools.pii-scanner`](https://github.com/gener8v/gener8v.tools.pii-scanner) — `MIT`, `1 064` B, student-PII detection at inference time, built to the **FERPA definition** rather than regex counting, stateless by construction |

🔵 **That is the useful shape of this trend, and it is not "AI gets regulated".** It is that the compliance layer is arriving as **permissively-licensed infrastructure a studio can deploy**, not as a feature of a closed suite. 🟡 One repository is not a market; it is one observation, published as one.

### 🟢 `T250-T2` — the trends the channel reports, with what each one is worth

| trend | what the channel says | 🔵 what this base can stand behind |
|---|---|---|
| **governance is the gate** | AI in education is shifting *from experimentation to governance*; clear policies, data boundaries and oversight are the precondition for adoption | 🟢 **Corroborated by instruments, not just editorial** — `134` bills / `31` states, at least six states mandating district policy, EU conformity assessment, Vietnam's named high-risk categories |
| **adoption is wide and ungoverned** | `86%` of education organisations use gen-AI and **most lack a policy**; teacher use **doubled** in a year (`60%` in `2024-25`) | 🟡 Vendor-report figures. 🟢 But the shape matches the one census on these shelves: LATAM `87%` use / `26%` strategy. **Two independent channels, same gap** |
| **teacher-first, not student-first** | the successful deployments begin with teachers | 🟢 Consistent with EMEA's *two-thirds of educators want recurring AI training* — the constraint is enablement, which is labour |
| **purpose-built beats general-purpose** | a move away from generic AI tools toward platforms built for education | 🟡 Editorial. 🔴 **And this base's own row channel says the opposite about SUPPLY**: `13` passes of search legs return general frameworks, and the education-specific rows come from the payload channel |
| **assessment is the biggest unclaimed surface** | grading open-ended responses, generating exam variants | 🔴 **And it is the surface every regulator names.** EU high-risk, Vietnam high-risk, Idaho prohibition, NYC red tier, SC's proposed grading ban. 🔵 The largest opportunity and the tightest constraint are **the same surface** — that is the single most important sentence on this shelf |
| **credentials and skills visibility** | digital credentials as the mechanism for skills-based learning and hiring | 🟢 **Now has a permissive row:** [`dkavargy/ESCOPlus2.0`](https://github.com/dkavargy/ESCOPlus2.0) (`MIT`) maintains the EU's **ESCO** taxonomy from live job-advert demand. 🟡 Research prototype |
| **offline / low-connectivity delivery** | 🔴 **the channel does not report this at all** | 🟢 **The shelves found it anyway:** [`BootlegYouki/L.A.R.A`](https://github.com/BootlegYouki/L.A.R.A) (`MIT`) — offline classroom LMS + Socratic AI tutor, Philippine public schools, no internet, budget Android. 🔵 A trend visible in the payload channel and invisible in the search channel |
| **evidence of learning gains demanded** | buyers increasingly expect proof of improved learning, persistence or job-relevant skills | 🟡 Editorial, and 🔴 the channel **itself** flagged the `62%` test-score and `42%` improvement claims circulating as vendor-sourced and unverified |

### 🔴 `T250-T3` — the counter-trend this base is uniquely placed to report

🔴 **While the sector trends toward governance, the open-source education layer it would be governed on is under-licensed, and this pass put numbers on it:**

| population | state |
|---|---|
| the most-cited open AI tutor | 🔴 **no grant at `41` paths** ([`JushBJJ/Mr.-Ranedeer-AI-Tutor`](https://github.com/JushBJJ/Mr.-Ranedeer-AI-Tutor)) |
| the `11` education LMS/tutor MCP connectors | 🔴 **`0` licence payloads.** `6` declare `"license": "MIT"` in a manifest with no text; `3` manifests have no licence field; `2` have nothing at all |
| the interoperability tier (LTI 1.3, OneRoster, SIS) | 🟢 **the healthy exception — `8` `MIT` + `1` `Apache-2.0`**, all read first-hand |

🔵 **The pattern is not random.** The **standards** layer is permissively licensed because standards bodies and universities publish it; the **agent and connector** layer is not, because it is published by individuals shipping fast. 🟢 **For a studio that is directly actionable: build on the standards tier, treat the connector tier as a prototype you will rewrite, and read the grant before either.**

### 🔵 `T250-T4` — a trend about this KB, and it is the pass's own finding

🔴 **`p249`'s trends shelf said: *"on regulation, the shelves are now ahead of the English search channel"*, and re-scoped `ACTION UX` to go read the regulators directly.** 🟢 **That is now measured and it is impossible from here:** every regulator host is refused at the proxy while code-supply hosts answer `200` ([`p250-channel-map`](../evidence/p250-channel-map.2026-10-16-p250.tsv)).

🔵 **So the honest statement of this base's position, which no earlier pass could make:** these shelves are **strong** on repository payloads (`41`-path ladders, byte counts, pinned hashes, first-hand grants) and **structurally second-hand** on regulation — every date here is a *reported* date and must be re-verified by someone with unrestricted egress before it reaches a client. 🟢 Saying so is worth more than a twelfth deferral of an action that cannot run.

## Pass 249 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND `p247` AND `p248` MEASURED IS **HALF A TREND**, AND THIS PASS FOUND THE OTHER HALF IN THIS KB'S OWN LAST PUBLISHED CELL: THE LICENCE FIELD IN A SECONDARY SOURCE IS UNCORRELATED WITH THE PAYLOAD **IN BOTH DIRECTIONS**

⏱️ **EIGHTEENTH pass of `2026-10-16`.**

### 🔴 `T249-T1` — the correction to this base's own trend statement

`p247` established it and `p248` gave it a mechanism: *"open source" is now the most unreliable field in an education tool's description* — with `4` of `5` source-available payloads carried or offered as **permissive**. 🔵 Every instance was the **same direction**: the secondary source **over-granting**.

🔴 **A trend measured in one direction is a hypothesis about a population, tested on a sample selected for that direction.** This pass read `twentyhq/twenty`'s payload — the row `p248` published as `BUSL-1.1` on a roundup's word — and found the error running the **other** way:

| direction | instance | what the secondary source said | what the payload says |
|---|---|---|---|
| **over-grant** *(`p247`, `p248`)* | `4` of `5` source-available payloads | `MIT` / "open source" | `BUSL`, `CockroachDB`, Commons Clause rider |
| 🔴 **under-grant + wrong family** *(this pass)* | [`twentyhq/twenty`](https://github.com/twentyhq/twenty) | `BUSL-1.1`, *"source-available, not OSI"* | 🟢 **`AGPL-3.0`** *(OSI-approved)*, **plus `MIT` subpackages verified at the package root**, plus a commercially-marked file set. **`0` occurrences of `"BUSL"` in `39 965` B** |

🟢 **The corrected trend, stated so it cannot be read one-way again:** *the licence field in a secondary source is **uncorrelated** with the payload — it over-states and under-states, and it gets the **family name** wrong.* 🔴 **The practical consequence is asymmetric and it is the expensive half:** an over-grant error gets caught at delivery, when someone finally reads the licence. An **under-grant** error is **never caught**, because nobody re-reads the licence of a dependency they already rejected. 🔴 **This KB discarded a usable `MIT` component library for a pass on an error of the second kind.**

### 🔴 `T249-T2` — a trend in how a grant is SELECTED, and it has now left the file tree entirely

Three passes have tracked where restriction hides. The sequence is a trend:

| pass | where the governing term lived | can a path probe see it? |
|---|---|---|
| `p220` | in a **file**, not the repository | 🟡 yes, if you probe enough paths |
| `p247` | in a **sibling** of the expected path (`LICENSE` vs `LICENSE-CODE`) | 🟡 yes, with the right name list |
| `p248` | in a **rider on top of** a permissive grant (Commons Clause) | 🔴 no — the family column still reads `MIT` |
| 🔴 **`p249`** | 🔴 **in a COMMENT INSIDE THE SOURCE** — Twenty's `/* @license Enterprise */` | 🔴 **no, and no extension of a path ladder ever will** |

🔵 **`4` points, one direction: the unit of a grant keeps getting smaller and keeps moving further from the path tree.** 🟡 Registered as `ACTION XL`: an instrument that greps **source** for licence markers rather than walking paths. 🔴 Until it exists, a `MIT` or `AGPL` cell on these shelves is a statement about **a file**, never about **a repository**.

### 🔴 `T249-T3` — the trend this KB keeps proving about itself: a correction does not travel

| pass | defect | "corrected" | recurred |
|---|---|---|---|
| `p217` → `p240` | monolingual commercial branch | `p217` | 🔴 `23` passes later, different instrument |
| `p233` → 🔴 **`p249`** | **harvested slugs mutilated — suffix dropped** | `p233` | 🔴 **`14` passes later, in `p247`'s harvest, now dropping the DOT-suffix AND case-folding** |
| `p248` | the only classifier reading a Commons Clause correctly was **not in `make hooks`** | `p248` | — |

🔵 **Three instances is a pattern with a shape: the fix lands in ONE instrument and the DEFECT lives in a CLASS.** 🔴 `p233` repaired its slug list; it did not repair whatever **writes** slug lists, so `p247` wrote `6` mutilated names, and `6` live `MIT` repositories were filed as a licence backlog for two passes. 🟡 `ACTION XJ` is written against the component, not the list, and its falsifier says so.

### 🟢 `T249-T4` — the regulatory trend, and this is the first pass that can state it globally

🟢 **Education is becoming a NAMED high-risk category in general-purpose AI law**, rather than a sector served by guidance:

| jurisdiction | how education enters the law | `2026` status |
|---|---|---|
| **EU** | AI Act names **education access and assessment** high-risk — admissions, evaluation, exam scoring; conformity assessment before market | 🟡 dates conflict across sources *(Aug 2026 vs Omnibus in force 27 Jul 2026)* |
| 🟢 **Vietnam** | standalone AI law names **automated assessment** and **behavioural monitoring** | 🟢 institutional deadline **`2027`** |
| **South Korea** | AI Basic Act, comprehensive | in force **Jan 2026**, penalties graced one year |
| **US states** | 🔴 not "high-risk" framing but **outright prohibition** on AI as the primary basis for grading, discipline, placement; `4` states mandate district policy | **`134` bills / `31` states** |
| **LATAM** | 🔴 **no binding statute** — institutional policy only, at **`26%`** coverage | UNESCO Observatory launched **14 Apr 2026** |

🔵 **The convergent requirement across every one of them is the same artefact, and it is not a model:** a **recorded, auditable human review step** between an AI's output and a decision about a student. 🟢 That is buildable once and sold into the EU, Vietnam, Idaho and New York City. 🔴 **The divergence is the trigger:** the EU regulates **placing on the market** (a vendor obligation), Vietnam and the US states regulate **use** (an institutional obligation). A studio that builds for one and sells into the other ships the wrong artefact to the wrong buyer.

### 🟡 `T249-T5` — the editorial trend, carried with its own channel's warning attached

🔵 The global trends leg returned a consistent line: **experimentation → operations**, governance as the gate, agents for lesson prep and formative feedback, evidence of learning gains over personalization claims, digital credentials as workforce currency. 🔴 **It is editorial consensus, not measurement** — and the channel flagged its own numbers (`$12.3B`, `42%`, `62%`) as vendor-sourced and unverified. 🟢 **The one place it agrees with something measured here:** *"tools built for classrooms rather than generic chatbots"* — and the `11` grantless LMS connectors found this pass are exactly that layer, being built, with no licence on any of them.

## Pass 248 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND THIS PASS CAN PROVE IS A **SHAPE CHANGE IN HOW RESTRICTION ARRIVES**: NOT A DIFFERENT LICENCE, BUT A **RIDER ON TOP OF A PERMISSIVE ONE** — WHICH LEAVES THE FIELD EVERY BUYER CHECKS READING `MIT`

⏱️ **SEVENTEENTH pass of `2026-10-16`.**

### 🔴 `T248-T1` — the licence-drift trend acquires its mechanism, and the mechanism is invisible

`p247`'s `T247-T1` established the trend with a number: *"open source" is now the most unreliable field in an education tool's description*, `4` of `5` source-available payloads read that pass having been carried or offered as permissive. **This pass can say how the failure happens, and the three ways are not equally detectable:**

| mechanism | the shelf cell reads | the payload says | detectable by a reader who checks the licence field? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `VOCABULARY` | `UNCLASSIFIED` | `CockroachDB Software License` @ offset `0` | 🟡 **yes** — "unknown licence" asks to be looked at |
| `WINDOW` | `UNCLASSIFIED` | a real `BSL v.1.1` reference @ offset `4 969` | 🟡 **yes**, for the same reason |
| 🔴 **`RIDER`** | 🔴 **`MIT`** | `"Commons Clause" License Condition v1.0` — *"does not grant to you, the right to Sell the Software"* | 🔴 **NO. Clean, confident, unflagged `MIT`** |

🔵 **The trend statement:** restriction in this estate is increasingly arriving as an **amendment to a permissive grant** rather than as a different licence — and an amendment does not change the name of the thing it amends. 🟡 **Direction of the cost:** `VOCABULARY` and `WINDOW` produce a question; `RIDER` produces a wrong answer that nobody asks about.

### 🟢 `T248-T2` — a trend about this KB rather than the market, and it has now recurred three times

🔴 **Three passes in a row have found that the correct reading already existed somewhere in this base and was not in the path that decides:**

| pass | what was found | where the correct answer already was |
|---|---|---|
| `p241` | the blanket-denial defect | 🔴 a correct classifier `make hooks` had never run |
| `p247` | `foradian/fedena`, `openedx/XBlock` graded on a `NOTICE` and on a manifest | 🔴 Tier A/C already distinguished them; no consumer used the distinction |
| 🔴 **`p248`** | the Commons Clause rider | 🔴 **`p207` measured it, documented it as `D14`, built the only correct classifier for it — and `p207` has never been in `make hooks`** |

🔵 **The trend, put as a property of this KB rather than a one-off:** its failure mode is **not** missing knowledge, it is **unwired** knowledge. 🟢 The structural response is the one `p247` started and this pass continued — *an instrument that is not in `make hooks` does not exist* — and `ACTION VW` (the audit of instruments outside the gate) is therefore not housekeeping but the highest-yield unrun ACTION on these shelves. 🟡 **It is still not fully run:** `p207` went in this pass; `p1056-split-scope-licence` and `p627-multi-grant-repo` remain outside.

### 🔵 `T248-T3` — the channel trend, now stable enough to state as a property

🔴 **`WebSearch` has produced `0` verifiable repository rows in `10` of the last `11` passes.** 🟢 **And in that same window it has produced regulation and institutional evidence repeatedly** — this pass's UNESCO IESALC/UNU-IAS survey (`200` institutions, `19` countries) being the clearest instance.

🔵 **So the trend is not "the channel is dry", it is "the channel is miscast".** `WebSearch` is a **regulation and institutional-evidence instrument**; the `topic:` channel is the supply instrument. 🟡 A pass that judges `WebSearch` by rows will keep recording a failure that is really a category error — and `p247`'s `ACTION VX` already measured the supply side of the same lesson: **harvest the proper nouns, not the concepts** (`topic:ai-literacy` = `62.5`% rediscovery vs `topic:waec` = `0`%).

### 🔴 `T248-T4` — what this pass explicitly did NOT re-measure

🔵 Published so none of it is read as current:

- `p247`'s state-platform client tier and skill-bundle findings — **not re-probed**.
- `topic:schule` (`total_count: 129`) and `topic:智慧教育` (`8` of `8`) — **not re-run**; those numbers remain `p247`'s.
- Korea's AI Basic Act, Ghana/Rwanda/Kenya programmes, the `134` US state bills — **carried from `p246`/`p247`**, and served by **none** of this pass's legs.
- 🔴 **No supply measurement of any kind was taken** ([`p248-queries`](../evidence/p248-queries.2026-10-16-p248.tsv)), so this pass claims no absence of supply anywhere (`ACTION UK`).

## Pass 247 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND THIS PASS CAN PROVE: **"OPEN SOURCE" IS NOW THE MOST UNRELIABLE FIELD IN AN EDUCATION TOOL'S DESCRIPTION** — `4` OF `5` SOURCE-AVAILABLE PAYLOADS READ TODAY WERE CARRIED OR OFFERED AS PERMISSIVE

⏱️ **SIXTEENTH pass of `2026-10-16`.** 🔵 **Evidence:** [`p247-source-available-reach`](../evidence/p247-source-available-reach.2026-10-16-p247.tsv) · [`p247-mit-tier-corrections`](../evidence/p247-mit-tier-corrections.2026-10-16-p247.tsv) · [`p247-websearch`](../evidence/p247-websearch.2026-10-16-p247.tsv)

### 🔴 `T247-T1` — the licence drift is measurable now, not anecdotal

| what it was called | what the payload says | where the claim came from |
|---|---|---|
| `MIT` | **`Business Source License 1.1`** *(DataCebo, Inc.)* | 🔴 **a cell on THESE shelves** — `sdv-dev/sdv` |
| `MIT` | **`fair-code` + Sustainable Use License** | 🔴 a cell on these shelves — `leemonade/leemons` |
| `MIT` | **`CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0`** *(non-commercial)* | 🔴 a cell on these shelves — `cccareers/open-source-curriculum` |
| `Apache-2.0` | **`BSL 1.1`**, hosted-service use forbidden until `2030-05-03` | 🔴 `p246`'s own highest-star `topic:ai-act` row — `IAGA-TEAM/IAGA-Sentinel` |
| "open source AI agent" | **`Elastic License 2.0`** | 🟡 a `WebSearch` round-up, offering `Suna` |

🟢 **Base rate, measured rather than asserted:** in `821` first-hand `MIT`-tier payloads, **`3` are source-available or non-commercial** *(`0.37`%)* and **`16` disagree with their cell** *(`1.95`%)*. 🔵 **Low per-row, and that is exactly why it survives: a `2`% error rate is invisible to review and fatal to the one engagement that lands on it.**

### 🔵 `T247-T2` — the enforcement-conditioned grant, a licence shape with no restriction vocabulary at all

🔴 **The `CockroachDB Software License` contains the word "commercial" ZERO times.** It does not restrict commercial use in words. It conditions the grant on **artefacts**:

> *"You will not operate the Software or a Modified Version **without a License Key** except for Limited Non-Production Use."*
> *"You will not operate the Software … **without sharing Telemetry**"*
> *"You will not circumvent, remove, disable, block or alter any technical countermeasures, Telemetry requirements …, License Key requirements, or other elements … designed to enforce the terms of this Agreement (collectively, the **"Protective Measures"**)."*

🟢 **Every restriction-vocabulary matcher returns ALLOWED on that payload by construction** — the class `p241` named — **and the grant-axis instrument returns `GRANT-PRESENT`, correctly, because it DOES grant something.** 🔵 **So the trend is a licence design: permission is real, and metered.** 🟢 This pass built [`p247-keyed-grant`](../compose/code/p247-keyed-grant/) to name it: `KEYED-GRANT`, `86` controls, in `make hooks`.

🔵 **Why it matters for education specifically:** a keyed grant is **incompatible with the deployment shape schools actually have** — air-gapped labs, offline-first rural devices *(Kolibri's whole reason to exist)*, and now **California AB 1159's no-training constraint**, which pushes toward on-device inference. 🔴 **A licence that requires calling home cannot be deployed into a classroom that cannot call home.**

### 🟢 `T247-T3` — the state platform as the integration surface, in two licence cultures

🔵 **China and Hessen produced the SAME artefact this pass:** a closed state education platform with **no API**, surrounded by a tier of individually-authored clients. 🟢 **The difference is only the licence** — `MIT`/`Apache-2.0` in the Chinese tier *(`7 161`★ at the head)*, `GPL-3.0`/`AGPL-3.0`/`EUPL-1.2` in the German one. 🔵 **The trend: as states nationalise education platforms, the open-source layer migrates from the PLATFORM to the CLIENT** — and the client tier is where a studio can both contribute and build.

### 🟡 `T247-T4` — the skill bundle overtakes the framework

🟢 `GarethManning/education-agent-skills`: **`870`★, `165` evidence-grounded skills**, created `2026-03-01`, targeting Claude, Codex and Hermes. 🟢 `mgkurz/ais-chat-builder`: one teacher, one Claude Skill, `CC-BY-SA-4.0`. 🔴 **Neither is a framework, an SDK or a model.** 🔵 **The deliverable in education AI is now PEDAGOGY ENCODED AS PROMPTS AND TOOLS** — which is a content asset wearing a software licence, and `ais-chat-builder`'s `CC-BY-SA-4.0` shows the authors know it.

### 🔴 `T247-T5` — three trends this pass explicitly did NOT re-measure

- 🔴 **Verifiable credentials / Open Badges 3.0 — `0` on-topic permissive implementations** *(`p245`'s declared gap)*. **Not re-run this pass. No claim that it changed.**
- 🔴 **India's Grade-`3` AI + Computational Thinking mandate from AY `2026-27` with no open curriculum supply** *(`p246`)*. **Not re-run.**
- 🔴 **`topic:digital-textbook` = a global population of `2`** *(`p246`)*. **Not re-run.**

## Pass 246 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND IS **FROM CAPABILITY TO EVIDENCE**: WHAT THE PERMISSIVE SUPPLY NOW BUILDS IS NOT THE TEACHING, IT IS THE **RECORD THAT THE TEACHING WAS DONE LAWFULLY** — AND THE COUNTER-TREND IS THAT A MANDATE STILL BUYS NOTHING, WHICH KOREA HAS NOW PROVED **STATUTORILY**

⏱️ **FIFTEENTH pass of `2026-10-16`.** 🔵 **Evidence:** [`p246-verified-rows`](../evidence/p246-verified-rows.2026-10-16-p246.tsv) · [`p246-websearch`](../evidence/p246-websearch.2026-10-16-p246.tsv) *(4 regional legs)* · [`p246-queries`](../evidence/p246-queries.2026-10-16-p246.tsv)

### 🟢 `T246-T1` — the trend: the artefact has moved one step further from the learner, for the fourth pass running

| pass | where the permissive supply was building |
|---|---|
| `p242` | **beside** the LMS, over its API — arm's length |
| `p243` | the **adapter** to someone else's system of record |
| `p245` | the **audit** — teaching a human to check a model's work |
| 🆕 **`p246`** | 🟢 **the EVIDENCE — the record that the check happened, timestamped, hash-chained and holdable up to a regulator** |

🔵 **Measured, not asserted:** `6` of this pass's `12` permissive rows are conformity instruments — risk classifiers, human-review trackers, FERPA evidence bundles, content-provenance marks, pre-registered pass bars. 🔴 **The tutor axis and the teacher-tooling axis together paid `0`.**

🟢 **Why it is happening now is in the regional legs, and it is the same reason in four different legal systems:** every region has acquired a **date and a named capability**, and none of them has acquired a **tool**. EMEA has prohibited emotion recognition in education since `2025-02-02` and moved high-risk duties to `2027-12-02`. North America has `134` bills in `31` states, with district AI policies due in Ohio from `2026-07-01`. India mandates AI from Grade `3` in AY `2026-27`. 🔵 **A regulator that names a capability creates demand for proof, and proof is a software artefact.**

### 🔴 `T246-T2` — the counter-trend, now with a statutory outcome: **a mandate is not adoption**

🟢 `p244` stated this and credited Korea. 🔴 **`p246` can close it:** Korea's **National Assembly stripped the AI digital textbooks of official textbook status**, after adoption sat **below `30`%** by March and unions said the pace had outrun preparation.

🟢 **And the money is the real signal: `$960M` moved to AI talent centres and vocational high schools through `2030`.** 🔵 **The failed artefact was content pushed to every pupil; the funded artefact is capability built inside institutions.** That is the same movement `T246-T1` measures in code — away from the delivered lesson, toward the durable instrument.

🔵 **Public opinion is drifting the same way in the countries that moved fastest:** support for **banning** AI in schools runs `23`% *(Indonesia)* to `31`% *(South Korea)* in the **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026**, and is **rising in Japan and South Korea**. 🟡 **Read it as a demand for guardrails, not as rejection** — it is the same constituency that makes `T246-T1`'s evidence layer sellable.

### 🟢 `T246-T3` — the third independent arrival of **offline-first**, and it is a privacy argument now

| pass | region | why offline |
|---|---|---|
| `p244` | EMEA | 🟢 sovereignty — the data must not leave the jurisdiction |
| `p244` | APAC | 🟢 connectivity — the classroom has none |
| 🆕 **`p246`** | **APAC** *(Japan)* | 🟢 **privacy by architecture — there is no server to breach** |

🔵 [`GIGAyama/Digital_textbook`](https://github.com/GIGAyama/Digital_textbook) *(`MIT`)* shares teacher material to pupils' devices over **WebRTC P2P with no server in the path**, and [`mattiebrandolini/paper-blinder`](https://github.com/mattiebrandolini/paper-blinder) *(`MIT`, **North America**)* runs **handwriting recognition on-device** *(Qwen2.5-VL via Ollama)* so that FERPA-covered work never leaves the building.

🟢 **Three regions, three motives, one architecture.** 🔵 **For a studio this is the rare case where the compliance-driven design and the cheap design are the same design** — and `p244`'s read holds: one build, sellable in more than one region.

### 🔴 `T246-T4` — the trend's blind spot: the regulated capabilities still have thin or **non-code** supply

| Capability a regulator named | Permissive implementation |
|---|---|
| 🟢 content provenance on submitted work | 🟢 **now exists** — [`dualeai/c2patxt`](https://github.com/dualeai/c2patxt) `Apache-2.0` |
