| 🟢 pre-registered assessment criteria | 🟢 **now exists** — [`studio-11-co/falsify`](https://github.com/studio-11-co/falsify) `MIT` |
| 🔴 **district AI policy** *(demanded by `134` US bills)* | 🔴 exists as **`CC-BY-4.0` content** — [`trevor050/honorlyai-resources`](https://github.com/trevor050/honorlyai-resources). **Not a code grant** |
| 🔴 **AI literacy curriculum, Grade `3`+** *(India, AY `2026-27`)* | 🔴 **none.** `topic:cbse` = `24` student projects, its one AI row **archived**; `topic:computational-thinking` holds **zero Indian repositories** |
| 🔴 **digital textbook** *(Korea, Japan `2030`, China)* | 🔴 **a population of TWO on all of GitHub**, both `0`★, one ungranted |
| 🔴 verifiable credentials / Open Badges 3.0 | 🔴 **still `0`** — `p245`'s declared gap, **not re-run this pass** and not claimed changed |

🟢 **This is the trend stated as a buy-list:** the capabilities with permissive supply are the ones a studio should compose; the four red rows are the ones a studio must build, and three of them have a statute with a date attached.

### 🔴 `T246-T5` — the trend in licensing: **source-available is now common enough to be a diligence line item**

🔵 `p245` found the best teacher-agent repository on its axis licensed as `CC-BY-SA` content. 🔴 **`p246` found four distinct ways a licence cell can be wrong, in one pass, across `321` payloads read first-hand:**

1. 🔴 **`BUSL-1.1` reading as `Apache-2.0`** — `196`★ [`IAGA-TEAM/IAGA-Sentinel`](https://github.com/IAGA-TEAM/IAGA-Sentinel), because BSL names Apache as its *future* licence *(`Change Date 2030-05-03`)*.
2. 🔴 **`GPL-3.0` published as `Apache-2.0`** — a carried row, `MuriloCalegari/Ifes-Academico`, found only by resolving its real default branch `QAcad`.
3. 🔴 **`MIT` over a `CC-BY-SA` text half** — `2 839`★ `mitmath/computational-thinking`, where for a *course* repo the text is the product.
4. 🔴 **An unresolved merge conflict inside a `LICENSE`** — `0xnavarro/IA-PARA-TODOS`, with two competing copyright lines and no way to tell whom to attribute.

🟢 **Base rate from the population: `2.3`% of carried `Apache-2.0` rows are not Apache at all.** 🔵 **Low enough to be invisible to sampling, high enough that one of them lands in a client deliverable eventually.** 🟢 **The trend to act on: budget the first-hand licence read into every engagement, and run it at the branch `ls-remote` reports, not at `main`.**

## Pass 245 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND IS THAT **THE REGULATOR NOW ARRIVES BEFORE THE PLATFORM**, AND THE COUNTER-TREND IS THAT THE TWO CAPABILITIES BEING REGULATED — **AUTOMATED ASSESSMENT** AND **CREDENTIALS** — HAVE **NO PERMISSIVE IMPLEMENTATION AT ALL**

⏱️ **FOURTEENTH pass of `2026-10-16`.**

🔵 Evidence: [`p245-websearch`](../evidence/p245-websearch.2026-10-16-p245.tsv) *(8 legs)* · [`p245-queries`](../evidence/p245-queries.2026-10-16-p245.tsv) *(11 queries, with the two that returned `total_count: 0` from well-formed shapes)* · [`p245-verified-rows`](../evidence/p245-verified-rows.2026-10-16-p245.tsv) · [`p245-apache-variant`](../evidence/p245-apache-variant.2026-10-16-p245.tsv)

### 🔴 `T1` — the inversion: regulation precedes deployment, for the first time in this KB's record

🔵 Through cycle 3 these shelves recorded adoption racing ahead of every rule. **This pass that ordering reverses, and it reverses with dates:** the EU has been **enforcing** since `2 Aug 2026` with education as a **high-risk** category; Korea's Framework Act has been in force since `22 Jan 2026`; **Vietnam's Law on AI `134/2025/QH15`** carries an education transition to **`1 Sep 2027`** and **Decision `33/2026/QD-TTg`** names education among six high-risk sectors.

🟢 **The consequence for a studio is a change in what the first deliverable is.** A pilot that cannot produce a conformity assessment is not deployable in the EU at all, and the assessment is **pre-deployment** — it is not a phase-two concern. 🔵 **Governance moves from the end of the engagement to the front of it.**

### 🔴 `T2` — the counter-trend, and it is the sharpest finding of the pass: the regulated capabilities are the unbuilt ones

| What regulators name | What the permissive code axis holds |
|---|---|
| **Automated assessment** *(EU high-risk; Vietnam Decision 33)* | 🔴 `topic:grading automated assessment AI stars:>10` → **`total_count: 0`** |
| **Learner evaluation, access to qualifications** *(EU high-risk)* | 🔴 `lesson plan grading rubric teacher agent LLM … stars:>20 pushed:>2026-08-01` → **`total_count: 0`** |
| **Digital credentials** *("workforce currency", per the 2026 trends leg)* | 🔴 `37` results, `25` read, **`0` on-topic permissive rows** |

🟢 **All three queries were well-formed** — `p244` established that a malformed query reports an axis empty, so shape is now checked before emptiness is claimed. 🔴 **These emptinesses are real.**

🔵 **So the sector's most-regulated functions are the ones a studio cannot start from an existing open-source base.** That is simultaneously the largest build opportunity on this shelf and the reason an engagement in those functions will be quoted as construction, not integration.

### 🟢 `T3` — AI literacy outgrew the tutor: the axis now pays **audit skills**, not explanation

🔵 `topic:ai-literacy` carries **`257`** repositories and was this pass's most productive axis — `7` of `13` new rows. 🔴 **And six of the seven teach a human to check a model's work rather than to learn from one**: [`UnVibe`](https://github.com/Omnikon-Org/UnVibe) has the model write the hardest solution so the learner must decode and defend it; [`ai-literacy-guardian`](https://github.com/AidanandMe/ai-literacy-guardian) ships **LLM-as-a-Judge evaluation** inside a literacy curriculum; [`knowledge-base-modrn-mind`](https://github.com/jarmolkowicz/knowledge-base-modrn-mind) and [`TamingTechnology`](https://github.com/Magnussmari/TamingTechnology) are both explicitly about **not outsourcing judgement**.

🟢 **This lines up with what the institutions are measuring.** `87`% of LATAM institutions use AI somewhere and `26`% have a strategy; `53`% of Brazilian administrators use AI and `22`% have guidelines; `10`% of European institutions have formal guidelines. 🔵 **The missing artefact in every one of those gaps is a workforce that can evaluate model output** — which is what this axis builds, and it is now permissively licensed in Chinese, Icelandic and English.

### 🟢 `T4` — the skills lab: the first form on these shelves where completion is not the outcome

🔵 A new foundation class, four `Apache-2.0` rows, `241`–`1 407` stars: [`k8sgames`](https://github.com/rohitg00/k8sgames), [`codeworld`](https://github.com/google/codeworld), [`tank-royale`](https://github.com/robocode-dev/tank-royale), [`kubekosh`](https://github.com/zeborg/kubekosh). 🟢 **The learner's work is running the real tool in a disposable environment, and the system observes the outcome.**

🔵 **Why it is a trend and not a toy:** it is the only form here that produces **demonstrated capability** instead of a completion record — and it appears in the same window in which credentials are being described as *workforce currency* while the credentials code axis is empty. 🟢 **A lab that can be observed is the evidence a credential is supposed to stand for.** 🔴 **The limit is honest:** all four teach technology skills; none teaches a school subject.

### 🟢 `T5` — edge inference held, and this pass placed the architecture rather than the wish

🔵 `p244` identified edge inference in the classroom from two regions and opposite constraints. 🟢 **It holds, and the evidence moved from intent to deployment shape:** [`project-nomad`](https://github.com/Crosstalk-Solutions/project-nomad) is at **`40 351` stars** — Wikipedia, books, courses, maps and **optional local AI** on owned hardware with no internet — and [`dbeley/cairn`](https://github.com/dbeley/cairn) now expresses that same server as a **declarative NixOS configuration**, which turns one installation into a fleet with rollback.

🔵 **Two independent demand signals this pass:** [`see-ai`](https://github.com/dreamnight16/see-ai) ships an **offline** prompt playground for Chinese undergraduates, and [`zhangl1001/civil-ai`](https://github.com/zhangl1001/civil-ai) *(carried)* is **local-first** adaptive tutoring on iOS. 🟢 **And the market numbers say why**: Argentina runs `75`% AI use in high-SES households against `42`% in low-SES ones. **The constraint is the device and the link, not the model.**

### 🟡 `T6` — a mandate is not adoption, and consent is regional

🔵 `p244` recorded this from Korea; this pass adds the sentiment measurement underneath it. **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026:** support for *banning* AI in schools is **lower** across the Asian markets examined and **higher in Australia and New Zealand** — two jurisdictions inside one region taking opposite public positions. 🔴 **And in North America the restrictions are already concrete:** New York City holds a one-year moratorium on student-facing AI **through grade 8**; **Katy ISD** bars generative-AI chatbots for **K-6** from `2026-27`.

🟢 **So "APAC permissive, West restrictive" is not a usable generalisation** — Australia and New Zealand sit on the restrictive side of their own region, and the binding constraint in NA is **district-level and age-gated**. 🔵 **Age-gating and opt-out are product features, not deployment settings.**

### 🔴 `T7` — the licence text is drifting away from the licence, and it looks like paraphrase

🔵 Measured, not inferred: **`3` of `15`** `Apache-2.0` payloads read first-hand this pass are **not** the canonical `apache.org` text, and **two of the three are byte-identical to each other** under different copyright holders *(`project-nomad`, `40 351`★, and `k8sgames`, `1 407`★)*. The deviations — an inserted article, `those`→`any` inside **§4(d)'s operative NOTICE condition** — are **paraphrase-shaped**, which is what reproduction **from recall** looks like.

🟢 **The family is still `Apache-2.0` and the repositories are still usable.** 🔴 **The cost lands at the OSS-review gate**, where exact-text and hash matchers answer `AND-modified` or `UNKNOWN` rather than passing. 🔵 **If licence texts are increasingly generated rather than copied, family classification stops being sufficient diligence** — so this pass shipped [`p245-apache-canonical`](../compose/code/p245-apache-canonical/) *(25 controls, in `make hooks`)* instead of a note.

### 🟡 `T8` — the consolidation nobody is announcing: the best teacher-agent work is licensed as **content**

🔵 The highest-star row the literacy axis produced — **`870` stars**, `165` evidence-grounded skills for teachers and school leaders — is [`GarethManning/education-agent-skills`](https://github.com/GarethManning/education-agent-skills) under **`CC-BY-SA-4.0`**. A second substantial row is **`CC-BY-NC-ND-4.0`** *(non-commercial **and** no-derivatives)*; a third, a Nordic training repository that ships Python, is **`CC-BY-4.0`**.

🔴 **This pass's withheld budget went mostly to rows that are deliberately licensed, just not as software** — where `p243` and `p244` spent theirs on repositories with no licence at all. 🟢 **That is a better problem**: a missing `LICENSE` usually has no counterparty, while a share-alike content licence has a named author and a plausible yes. 🔵 **But it predicts the pattern**: as educators rather than engineers publish the best pedagogical agent work, `CC` licences arrive with it, and **a studio's default assumption that "open" means "buildable" will be wrong more often.**

## Pass 244 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND IS **EDGE INFERENCE IN THE CLASSROOM**, ARRIVED AT INDEPENDENTLY IN TWO REGIONS FROM OPPOSITE CONSTRAINTS — AND THE COUNTER-TREND IS THAT A **MANDATE IS NOT ADOPTION**, WHICH KOREA JUST PROVED

⏱️ **THIRTEENTH pass of `2026-10-16`.**

🔵 Evidence: [`p244-verified-rows`](../evidence/p244-verified-rows.2026-10-16-p244.tsv) · [`p244-websearch`](../evidence/p244-websearch.2026-10-16-p244.tsv) · [`p244-estate-census`](../evidence/p244-estate-census.2026-10-16-p244.tsv) · [`p244-queries`](../evidence/p244-queries.2026-10-16-p244.tsv).

### 🟢 `T1` — Edge inference is the classroom default now, and two regions got there separately

🔵 **Measured from `9` of this pass's rows, in their own words, not inferred from a trend report:**

| row | region | what it says | the constraint behind it |
|---|---|---|---|
| `appbuildersph/modes` | APAC | *"Gemma 4 E2B runs on a laptop, **not the cloud**"* | 🟢 cost, connectivity |
| `BootlegYouki/L.A.R.A` | APAC | *"**Offline** LAN-Based Classroom"*, `edge-ai` | 🟢 cost, connectivity |
| `JuLiusGI/Elementary-Learning-Hub` | APAC | *"**Offline-capable** … PWA sync"* | 🟢 cost, connectivity |
| `straussbastian/ai_flashcards_for_school` | EMEA | *"no sign-in, **no stored results**"* | 🟢 data protection |
| `foerbsnavi/Notenberechner` | EMEA | *"Alles im Browser, **ohne Anmeldung**"* | 🟢 data protection |
| `Obi-Wahn/zeitstrahl-werkstatt` | EMEA | *"**ohne Cloud, ohne Konten**, ohne weitere Pakete"* | 🟢 data protection |
| `Lukas1120987/Schul-System` | EMEA | *"**lokal laufende** Schulverwaltungssoftware"* | 🟢 data protection |
| `mr89adloff/taschenrechner-fx87de` | EMEA | *"rechnet komplett **im Browser**"* | 🟢 data protection |
| `MRL-creator/attendance-workspace` *(p243)* | Global | *"Students are records, **not login accounts**"* | 🟢 data minimisation |

🟢 **The engineering is identical and the pitch is not.** 🔵 **For a studio this is the pass's most commercially useful line: the local-inference work, the no-account session model and the local-store-then-sync path are one build, sellable to an EMEA buyer as compliance and to an APAC buyer as economics.** 🟡 **And it is the exact inverse of the vendor-platform rows**, which are cloud-first SSO systems throughout — so the two halves of this market are diverging architecturally, not converging.

### 🔴 `T2` — The counter-trend, and it is the fact worth carrying into every pitch: **a mandate is not adoption**

🔴 **Korea planned mandatory AI textbooks for 2025. Adoption sat under `30%` by March. In August 2026 the National Assembly STRIPPED them of official status** after unions said the pace had outrun preparation. A **`$960M`** plan for AI talent centres and vocational high schools through 2030 replaced them.

🟢 **Set that beside the mandates currently being quoted as market certainty:**

| jurisdiction | the mandate | 🟡 what Korea says about it |
|---|---|---|
| **India** | AI + Computational Thinking **mandatory from Class 3**, AY 2026-27; IIT Madras curriculum; `₹500 crore` CoE | 🟡 the largest mandate in the world by pupil count, and teacher readiness is the same unpriced variable |
| **China** | Beijing `8` hours/year minimum from Sep 2025; national rollout intended | 🔴 sources conflict on whether the national scope exists yet |
| **EU** | high-risk education obligations **`2 Dec 2027`**, deferred from `2 Aug 2026` | 🔵 **the deferral is itself this pattern** — a timetable met reality and moved |

🟢 **The pattern across all four: the instrument moves faster than the teaching workforce, and the instrument then yields.** 🔵 A plan that assumes a mandate converts to deployment on schedule has one unpriced dependency, and it is teacher preparation.

### 🟢 `T3` — Teacher-side tools are where the permissive licences are

🔵 **Measured on `topic:schule topic:education`: `7` permissive rows, and all seven are built for the TEACHER, not for the institution.** Marking scanned work, computing grade keys, generating exam rubrics, preparing lessons, building seating plans, timelines, flashcards.

🟢 **And the licence split maps onto that boundary almost exactly:** the single-purpose teacher tool is `MIT`; the moment a row becomes an institutional system it becomes `AGPL-3.0` *(`Erwartungshorizont-Studio`, `sitzplan-studio`, `koakademy`, `chalk`)* or `GPL` *(`openSIS`, `CleverNC`, `tidal-ptc`)*. 🟡 **So the permissive supply and the institutional demand are not in the same place**, and an engagement that needs an institutional system is a deployment-and-configuration engagement, not a fork.

### 🟢 `T4` — MCP has moved from integration plumbing to the **authoring** surface

🔵 `p242` recorded MCP-over-LMS as a category. 🟢 **This pass finds MCP on the production side of the classroom, which is a different thing:**

- [`straussbastian/ai_flashcards_for_school`](https://github.com/straussbastian/ai_flashcards_for_school) *(`MIT`)* — **the teacher authors a learning bundle by talking to an MCP agent**, and the class consumes it through a three-word link with no sign-in and nothing stored.
- [`bncc-dev/bncc-pacotes`](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-pacotes) — **an MCP server over Brazil's national curriculum**, so an agent can ask what a grade is supposed to learn. 🔴 `CC-BY-4.0`, so the server is not the part to depend on.
- [`iblai/standard`](https://github.com/iblai/standard) *(`MIT`, `11`★)* — **the `.iblai` agent file format**: a portable Markdown spec for *defining* an agent. 🔵 A format rather than a product, which is the only part of a vendor estate a studio can adopt without adopting the vendor.
- [`iblai/k-12-agents`](https://github.com/iblai/k-12-agents) *(`MIT`, `9`★)* — a `powerschool/` tool skill that **reads and writes** student records, enrolment, attendance and grades.

🟡 **The write path is the thing to watch.** `p243` found `che-ntucool-mcp` gating grade posting behind a two-layer human confirmation. `p244` finds `k-12-agents` declaring SIS **write** access as a tool skill with **no equivalent gate stated in the row**. 🔴 **Write access to a gradebook is the highest-consequence capability in this entire KB, and the ecosystem is not consistent about guarding it.**

### 🟢 `T5` — Curriculum-grounded evaluation exists, in Portuguese, and this KB kept declaring it did not

🔴 Earlier cycles of this KB declared a standing gap: *"no dominant OSS agent eval/observability"*. 🟢 **[`bncc-dev/bncc-benchmark`](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-benchmark) is an education-specific eval harness and it is public:** LLM **hallucination** measured against Brazil's **BNCC** national curriculum — `15 300` answers from `17` models, methodology and raw data published, **held-out set kept private against contamination**.

🟢 **The held-out design is the part that makes it credible**, and it is the part most education "benchmarks" omit. 🔴 **`CC-BY-4.0`, so the harness's licence is wrong for code** — but the *method* is reusable and the gap statement needs retiring. 🟡 **Registered: the gap claim should be re-read against `make claims` before any pass repeats it.**

### 🔴 `T6` — Licence architecture is now a recurring *defect* class, not an occasional surprise

🔵 **Four distinct licence-architecture defects in `42` rows this pass, each a different shape:**

| shape | row | what is wrong |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **the inversion** | `bncc-dev` | **data under a software licence, software under a data licence** — both ways round, same owner, three repos |
| 🔴 **the truncation** | `belentani7/aprende-brasil` | `MIT` with the **liability paragraph missing**; `814` B clears every byte-floor and classifies clean |
| 🔴 **the carve-out that grants to nobody** | `Lukas1120987/Schul-System` | `/icons` excluded from the author's copyright and assigned to *"Chat-GPT von OpenAI"* — **an entity that cannot hold it and does not claim it** |
| 🔴 **the third party's grant** | `iblai/botbuilder-python` | `MIT` whose holder is **Microsoft Corporation**, inside a vendor estate, with `fork: false` |

🟢 **Plus the `0`-byte `LICENSE` that started the pass, and `10` of `35` estate repositories with no grant at all.** 🔵 **The generalisation: as AI-assisted authoring spreads, licence files are increasingly generated or pasted rather than chosen** — and the four shapes above are all what that looks like from the outside. 🟢 **For a studio doing due diligence, "what does the LICENSE say" has stopped being a one-line check.**

### 🟡 `T7` — Adoption numbers are moving faster than anyone's ability to verify them

🔴 **The WebSearch leg produced `0` usable rows for the sixth pass in seven, and the market figures it did return do not reconcile**: `$10.6B`–`$11.4B` for the *current* year, three incompatible horizons, a `66%`→`92%` student-adoption jump on a single secondary citation, and a maturity score a vendor describes as its own opinion.

🟢 **What the legs DID pay was regulatory and institutional fact** — dates, bill numbers, a stripped mandate, a Commission finding that **two-thirds of EU education systems** now have an AI strategy. 🔵 **That asymmetry is itself the trend to record: the policy record is becoming citable while the market record is not.** 🟢 **So quote the dates, range the dollars.** 🔵 Per-leg source quality: [`p244-websearch`](../evidence/p244-websearch.2026-10-16-p244.tsv).

### 🔴 `T8` — And the methodological trend this pass cares most about: **absence is being over-reported**

🔵 Three channels, measured in one pass, each turning *"I could not read it"* into *"it is not there"*:

```
PAYLOAD   200 + 0 bytes          -> recorded as "no licence file"        (the file exists)
ADDRESS   rc=0 + 0 refs          -> recorded as "no such repository"     (the repo exists, empty)
QUERY     void shape -> count 0  -> recorded as "the axis is exhausted"  (124 rows behind it)
```

🟢 **`p241` named the dual of this on the licence side — a matcher treating the absence of its own vocabulary as permission. `p244` names it for probes: a probe treats the absence of its own vocabulary as the absence of the thing.** 🔵 **For a knowledge base whose value is partly its declared gaps, this is the error that matters most**, because an over-reported absence is indistinguishable from coverage and nobody ever goes back to it. 🟢 Gated in [`p244-absence-states`](../compose/code/p244-absence-states/), `61` controls, wired into `make hooks`.

## Pass 243 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND THIS PASS MEASURED IS **WHO IS BUILDING**: NOT VENDORS, NOT SCHOOLS — **TEACHERS, PARENTS AND PUPILS**, ONE INSTITUTION EACH, AGAINST A PLATFORM THEY DO NOT CONTROL

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p243-verified-rows`](../evidence/p243-verified-rows.2026-10-16-p243.tsv) *(41 rows)* · [`p243-withheld`](../evidence/p243-withheld.2026-10-16-p243.tsv) *(31 rows)* · [`p243-queries`](../evidence/p243-queries.2026-10-16-p243.tsv) · [`p243-region-saturation`](../evidence/p243-region-saturation.2026-10-16-p243.tsv) · [`p243-channels`](../evidence/p243-channels.2026-10-16-p243.tsv)

### 🟢 Trend 1 — **the unit of software in this sector is ONE school, and that is a trend, not noise**

Read across `102` rows, the modal education repository of this pass is **built by one person for one institution and published anyway**: [`Vallaury/*`](https://github.com/Vallaury/vallaury-desktop) *(I.I.S. Vallauri, Fossano)* · [`stasolejnik/elektron`](https://github.com/stasolejnik/elektron) *(one Bydgoszcz school)* · [`JohnMartinRB/CE-IPET424`](https://github.com/JohnMartinRB/CE-IPET424) *(IPET N° 424, Córdoba)* · [`rhmatzeka/AppAbsensiSDN1Bintaro`](https://github.com/rhmatzeka/AppAbsensiSDN1Bintaro) *(one Indonesian primary school)* · [`AkshobyaRaoSWE/studentvue-glowup`](https://github.com/AkshobyaRaoSWE/studentvue-glowup) *(Indian Prairie SD 204)* · [`AlekseiLopatin/school-website`](https://github.com/AlekseiLopatin/school-website) *("the school where I teach in Thailand")*.

🔵 **The consequence for discovery is structural, and it explains the star distribution:** `35` of this pass's `41` permissive rows have **`3`★ or fewer**. 🟡 **A one-school tool has no reason to accumulate stars, and a KB that filters this sector by popularity will see almost none of its actual supply.** 🟢 **It also explains the licence chaos:** nobody building for one school is thinking about downstream reuse, which is why `13` of `31` refusals are a **missing** licence rather than a restrictive one.

### 🟢 Trend 2 — **the integration target is drifting away from the institution**

`p242` measured the MCP-server-over-an-incumbent-LMS category. `p243` measured where those clients **land**, and in EMEA the answer is not a school system:

| landing surface | rows this pass |
|---|---|
| 🔵 **Home Assistant / ioBroker** *(a home-automation hub)* | **`6`** |
| an MCP client *(Claude and others)* | `4` |
| the browser, as an extension or a static page | `4` |
| **Obsidian**, **Emacs org-mode**, **Google Calendar**, a **Siri shortcut** | `4` |

🟡 **Sixteen of this pass's rows deliver school data into a surface the family or the teacher already runs, and none of them asks the school for anything.** 🔵 Read beside `p242`'s finding that the permissive supply *"has stopped trying to be the LMS and started sitting beside it"* — `p243`'s refinement is that it has now stopped sitting beside the LMS too, and moved into the kitchen.

### 🔴 Trend 3 — **the function word has displaced the category word, and the gap is a factor of forty-five**

| what the word is | the word | repositories |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 the **procurement** word | `topic:sis` + `topic:school` | 🔴 **`4`** |
| 🟢 the **function** words | `gradebook` · `attendance` · `timetable` | 🟢 **`180`** |

🟡 **Generalised: this sector's builders tag the VERB, and its buyers name the NOUN.** Anyone searching the way the market talks about itself finds four repositories. 🔵 `p242` ranked product vocabulary above statute vocabulary; `p243` finds the same effect one level further down, between a product category and the task it performs.

### 🔴 Trend 4 — **the non-OSI licence is now the best-resourced licence on this axis**

| licence | rows | the biggest row under it |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 `MIT` | **35** | [`iblai/os`](https://github.com/iblai/os) `821`★ |
| 🟢 `Apache-2.0` | 4 | [`artyom7774/Schedule`](https://github.com/artyom7774/Schedule) `9`★ |
| 🟢 `ECL-2.0` | 1 | [`sakaicontrib/attendance`](https://github.com/sakaicontrib/attendance) `14`★ |
| 🟢 `ISC` | 1 | [`alejooroncoy/campus-cli`](https://github.com/alejooroncoy/campus-cli) `11`★ |
| 🔴 `GPL-3.0` / `AGPL-3.0` | 8 | [`EdupageAPI/edupage-api`](https://github.com/EdupageAPI/edupage-api) **`102`★** |
| 🔴 `SSPL-1.0` · `PolyForm-NC` · `GSANCL-1.0` · source-available · `CC-BY-NC-SA` | 6 | [`databayt/hogwarts`](https://github.com/databayt/hogwarts) **`18`★** *(the biggest SIS)* |

🔵 **Set aside the one vendor estate and the pattern is stark: in the system-of-record category, the row with the most stars, the row with the most forks and the row with the most open issues are all three UNUSABLE for a proprietary deliverable.** 🟡 **That is a market reading, not a licence complaint: the projects that attract contributors in this category are deliberately choosing licences that stop a vendor taking them, and they are choosing non-OSI licences to do it** — `SSPL`, `PolyForm`, and in one case a licence the author wrote and named themselves.

### 🔴 Trend 5 — **the licence file is a worse signal than these shelves have been treating it as**, and this pass has the census

Of `72` payload probes this pass:

| the state the declared licence path was actually in | rows |
|---|---|
| 🟢 a canonical, unmodified licence body | `44` |
| 🟡 a canonical body **plus** something *(scope note, exclusion note, upstream attribution, long holder)* | `5` |
| 🔴 **no licence payload at `15` paths on the real default branch** | **`13`** |
| 🔴 a bespoke document that grants nothing | `6` |
| 🔴 a canonical grant sentence with a **restriction qualifying it** | `1` |
| 🟡 a grant payload that is **defective** *(holderless, truncated mid-clause)* | `1` |
| 🔴 **a `0`-byte file at the path the README names** | **`1`** |

🔴 **One row in four has something wrong with its grant that a metadata field cannot express.** 🔵 **GitHub's own `license.spdx_id` returns `NOASSERTION` for the `0`-byte case — technically correct, and it gives the same answer it would for a bespoke proprietary licence, a dual grant and a typo'd filename.** 🟡 **The trend is not that licensing is getting worse; it is that this sector's supply is built by people for whom the licence is the last file they touch, so the only reliable channel is reading the payload.**

### 🟢 Trend 6 — **regulation is converging on a SHAPE, not on a rulebook**

Four jurisdictions, four unrelated instruments, one requirement:

- 🔴 **China** *(MOE 2025 guidance)* — teachers should not use AI-generated content to evaluate pupils; no personal data into AI tools.
- 🔴 **India** *(CBSE position)* — board scripts are marked by **human evaluators who make the final call**.
- 🔴 **Idaho `SB 1227`** *(carried, `p242`)* — prohibits AI **replacing** teachers.
- 🔴 **Colorado `SB 26-189`** *(`T243-L`)* — the replacement category is **"automated decision-making technology"**, and a gradebook that flags at-risk pupils sits inside it.

🟢 **None of these bans the technology. All four place the same fence in the same place: the human keeps the judgement.** 🔵 **The technical expression of that fence already exists, permissively, and it came from Taiwan this pass** — [`PsychQuant/che-ntucool-mcp`](https://github.com/PsychQuant/che-ntucool-mcp) reads Canvas freely and requires **two independent human confirmations** on every write. 🟡 **A trend worth stating as a design rule: in education, make the write path harder than the read path, and the regulatory question answers itself in every region at once.**

### 🔴 Trend 7 — **the data is opening faster than the code**

🔵 Three measurements from this pass, in the same direction:

- 🟢 **South Korea's NEIS** is a national Ministry of Education **open API**, and one `MIT` repository uses it to cover **every school in the country**.
- 🟡 **Thailand's Ministry of Education school registry** is published on **`data.go.th` under Open Data Common** — and it reaches these shelves inside a repository whose **code is all rights reserved**. 🔴 **The dataset is usable and the row is not.**
- 🟢 **`OksigeniaSL/oga-veve`** builds Paraguayan terrain from **public-domain NASADEM** and declares its OpenStreetMap-derived aerodrome database **`ODbL 1.0`**, separately from its `Apache-2.0` code.

🟡 **So a sector whose software is `GPL`-locked and licence-chaotic has a data layer that is increasingly government-published and openly licensed.** 🔵 **For a studio that is the cheaper entry point: the registry, the curriculum, the calendar and the meal plan are often already open, while the application that reads them is not.**

## Pass 242 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND THIS PASS MEASURED IS **ARM'S LENGTH**: THE PERMISSIVE SUPPLY HAS STOPPED TRYING TO BE THE LMS AND STARTED SITTING BESIDE IT, OVER AN API — AND THAT IS ALSO HOW IT ESCAPES THE COPYLEFT ESTATE

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p242-verified-rows`](../evidence/p242-verified-rows.2026-10-16-p242.tsv) · [`p242-withheld`](../evidence/p242-withheld.2026-10-16-p242.tsv) · [`p242-websearch`](../evidence/p242-websearch.2026-10-16-p242.tsv) · [`p242-delta-sweep`](../evidence/p242-delta-sweep.2026-10-16-p242.tsv) · [`p242-axis-rediscovery`](../evidence/p242-axis-rediscovery.2026-10-16-p242.tsv)

### 🟢 `1` — Arm's length, measured across `8` rows

🔵 **The `topic:lms` harvest returned a cluster: `MIT` agent surfaces over incumbent LMS platforms.** `7` of `8` are permissive; **every host is `AGPL-3.0`, `GPL-3.0` or proprietary**; and the single copyleft exception, [`jeanlucio/moodle-local_aihub`](https://github.com/jeanlucio/moodle-local_aihub), is the one that is a **plugin** — it runs inside Moodle and links its code.

🟢 **So the mechanism is visible in the data, not inferred:** a plugin inherits the host's licence, an API client does not. 🔵 **Read beside `p240`** — which measured the richest European and Latin American education supply as `16`/`16` `GPL-3.0` Moodle plugins, i.e. structurally unavailable — **this is the supply side finding its way out of that trap.** Full table in [`verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) *(`T242-E`)*.

🟡 **And it is where the stars are:** `59`★, `13`★, `10`★, `8`★, `7`★ on thin MCP wrappers against `0`–`1`★ on most full tutors in the same harvest. 🔴 **One harvest, and `55`% of this axis was already carried, so this is a shape with momentum — not yet a trend line.**

### 🟢 `2` — The reusable layer has moved DOWN again, to the system of record

🔵 **Three passes, three different answers to "what is actually reusable", and they are consistent rather than contradictory:**

| pass | where the permissive supply was | measured by |
|---|---|---|
| `p240` | 🔴 nowhere usable in EMEA/LATAM — `16`/`16` `GPL-3.0` | the Moodle plugin estate |
| `p241` | 🟢 **compliance and records** — privacy trackers, audit streams, RAG pre-filters | `topic:ferpa`, `topic:ed-fi` |
| 🟢 **`p242`** | 🟢 **the system of record itself** — attendance, gradebook, transcript | **`topic:gradebook`** |

🟡 **The trend across the three is one direction: AWAY from the learning product and DOWN the stack.** 🔵 Nobody is shipping a permissive tutor that wins; people are shipping the plumbing underneath one.

### 🟢 `3` — A gradebook cannot hide its jurisdiction, and a tutor can

🟢 **This is the pass's most useful structural observation, and it fell out of the axis measurement rather than being looked for.**

`topic:gradebook` returned `30`/`30` on-topic and produced **every regional row of this pass**. The reason: **a gradebook has to encode a national marking scheme**, so it names its jurisdiction whether the author intends to or not — `trimestre` and `recuperação substitutiva` *(Brazil)*, `Librus Synergia` *(Poland)*, `小学到高中` *(China)*, `모의고사` *(Korea)*, `ParentVUE`/`MCPS` *(US)*, the `3e`/`CM2`/`lycée` ladder *(France)*.

🔴 **Whereas `8` of this pass's `21` rows — `38`% — carry NO regional evidence at all, and they are overwhelmingly the tutors and study apps.** 🔵 **A tutor is region-free because teaching a topic does not require a jurisdiction. Record-keeping does.**

🟡 **Consequence for this KB, registered as `ACTION WA`:** the system-of-record axes *(`topic:gradebook`, `topic:sis`, `topic:attendance`, `topic:timetable`)* are where regional coverage comes from. 🔴 **Three passes of statute vocabulary produced no EMEA or LATAM row; one product axis produced six.**

### 🔴 `4` — "Education AI" is a vocabulary collision, and it has cost four passes

🔴 **The WebSearch legs have produced `0` new rows for FOUR consecutive passes, and the cause is now diagnosable.** The trending leg returned `microsoft/generative-ai-for-beginners` *(`112.6`k★)*, `mlabonne/llm-course` *(`79.9`k★)* and `rohitg00/ai-engineering-from-scratch` — **AI pedagogy, every one. Teaching material ABOUT AI, not software FOR education.**

🔵 **The same collision appears inside GitHub's own tags:** `edmozley/freeitsm` *(`107`★, an ITSM tool)* ranks on `topic:lms` via `scorm`, and `gdmka/openai-compat-endpoint` is tagged `lms` because it means **LM Studio**.

🟡 **And the language axis is the same failure in another costume:** `educacion OR educativa OR educação in:readme topic:ai` returned **`10` of `30`** on topic — the worst rate these shelves have measured — because a language word selects for the **author's language**, not the row's **sector**. 🟢 **`ACTION WD`: never run a bare language axis again; pair the language token with a PRODUCT token.**

### 🔴 `5` — Three for three: every body-grepping licence classifier eventually reads the licence's own text as foreign matter

🔵 **This pass makes the pattern undeniable, because the instrument built to stop it committed it.**

| # | pass | the defect |
|---|---|---|
| `1` | `P171` | GPL-3.0's section 13 is TITLED *"Use with the GNU Affero GPL"*, so a body-grep labelled every GPL-3.0 payload `AGPL-3.0` |
| `2` | `p241` / `ACTION VU` | The standard AGPL-3.0 text NAMES GPL-3.0 and carries the FSF's 2007 dates, so *"two licences and a date"* fires on `4`/`4` ordinary copyleft payloads and finds `0` relicensings |
| `3` | 🔴 **`p242`, on its own first sweep** | `p1054`'s vocabulary contains `noncommercial`, and **standard GPL-3.0 section 6 uses that word** — so `delta_of` returned `NARROWING` on all `8` GPL/AGPL payloads |

🟢 **The structural answer, now implemented: a payload that IS a canonical licence text has no delta to read.** It is reported `STANDARD` and handed to `lib/license_family.sh`. 🔵 **A control asserts the offending word is still present and still not read as an addition**, so removing the guard fails the suite rather than silently regressing.

🟡 **The generalisable rule, written down for the next instrument author:** *a token inside a standard licence body is that licence's own term. Classify the payload's IDENTITY first, and only then read what has been ADDED to it.*

### 🟢 `6` — And the opposite error is just as expensive: the gate was withholding a usable row

🔵 **`p241` established that the absence of restriction vocabulary is never permission. `p242` establishes the converse limit: the presence of ADDED TEXT is not a restriction either.**

[`AnonymousOnyx22/brightspace-d2l-mcp`](https://github.com/AnonymousOnyx22/brightspace-d2l-mcp) carried `50` bytes above `p1054`'s MIT ceiling and was marked **not usable**. Those bytes are an **upstream attribution** naming `RohanMuppa/brightspace-mcp-server` *(read first-hand, `MIT`, `1 068` B)*. 🟢 **A disclosure. The row grants exactly MIT and tells you more than a bare MIT file does.**

🟡 **Both halves now have a name:** `DISCLOSURE` *(grants the same, says more)*, `NARROWING` *(grants less)*, `TRANSITION` *(grants differently over time)*, `STANDARD` *(nothing added)*, and an addition the instrument cannot read stays **UNRESOLVED and uncleared**. 🔴 **`ACTION VY`: these shelves have not been swept for OTHER rows withheld on an `MIT-BODY-PLUS` that was only ever a disclosure. This pass found one. It did not look for the rest.**

### 🟢 `7` — "Never give the answer" shows up twice, independently

🔵 Two rows in one harvest, two jurisdictions, two licences, same pedagogical refusal:

- [`alexxb2mg-svg/jules`](https://github.com/alexxb2mg-svg/jules) — `MIT`, **France**: *"il guide pas à pas et ne donne jamais la réponse"*.
- 🔴 [`danielcregg/moodle-local_aitutor`](https://github.com/danielcregg/moodle-local_aitutor) — `GPL-3.0`, **Ireland**: Socratic escalating hints over Moodle STACK, *"never the answer"*. **Withheld on copyleft.**

🟡 **Two instances is not a trend and this is logged as a watch.** 🔵 **But it is a design constraint worth carrying into a proposal**, because it is the opposite of what a generic LLM tutor does by default, and it is the form that survives an academic-integrity review. 🟢 **Both also run local-first** *(Ollama; server-side institutional AI)*, so the constraint travels with a privacy posture rather than against it.

### 🔴 `8` — A regulatory deadline moved, and a trend built on the old one would now be wrong

🔴 **The EU Digital Omnibus moves Annex III high-risk duties — the education tier — to `2027-12-02`** *(embedded-product high-risk to `2028-08-02`)*, Council sign-off reported `2026-06-29`. 🟡 **Reported with its uncertainty: sources disagree on firmness and this pass did not read the Official Journal** *(`ACTION WF`)*.

🟢 **What did NOT move:** the **emotion-recognition prohibition in schools** *(in force since `2025-02-02`)* and the **Article 4 AI-literacy duty**. 🔵 **So the trend is not "Europe relaxed" — it is that the ARTEFACT deadline slipped while the PROHIBITIONS stayed.** A proctoring or engagement-detection feature is still a today problem; the conformity file is a 2027 one.

🔵 **Corroborated from the supply side**: an Italian serious-game on the AI Act surfaced in this pass's own harvest describing itself as *"aggiornato al Digital Omnibus"*. 🟡 Withheld on `GPL-3.0`, but a second independent signal of what the European market is reading.

### 🔴 `9` — APAC enthusiasm and APAC intent have decoupled

🔴 **APAC faculty are the MOST optimistic of any region — `57`% excited — and their intent to use AI FELL `9` points, `76`% (2025) → `67`% (2026), the LOWEST future-adoption intent of any region.**

🟡 **These point in opposite directions and this KB does not reconcile them.** 🔵 It is the shape pilot fatigue makes — enthusiasm intact, commitment down — but one survey is not a cause. 🔴 **Logged as a number to watch.** 🔴 **And the regulatory context for the region's two largest systems is a DECLARED GAP: no India or China source on these shelves** *(`ACTION WB`)*, which is uncomfortable given that `4` of this pass's `21` rows are Chinese or Indian.

## Pass 241 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND THIS PASS MEASURED IS **ENCLOSURE**: AN OPEN K-12 TUTOR RELICENSED TO PROPRIETARY, AND THE PERMISSIVE SUPPLY THAT REMAINS HAS MOVED **UNDER** THE LEARNING PRODUCT INTO COMPLIANCE AND RECORDS

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p241-verified-rows`](../evidence/p241-verified-rows.2026-10-16-p241.tsv) · [`p241-withheld`](../evidence/p241-withheld.2026-10-16-p241.tsv) · [`p241-websearch`](../evidence/p241-websearch.2026-10-16-p241.tsv) · [`p241-cross-instrument-agreement`](../evidence/p241-cross-instrument-agreement.2026-10-16-p241.tsv)

### 🔴 `1` — Enclosure, with a date on it

🔴 **[`snflwr-ai/snflwr.ai`](https://github.com/snflwr-ai/snflwr.ai) relicensed `AGPL-3.0` → proprietary on `2026-09-18`**, read first-hand from its own `LICENSE` *(`2 833` B · `7feea47e4759`)*. An offline, parental-oversight K-12 tutor — the exact shape the US district market procures — and its author concluded the open grant was not how to capture that.

🟡 **One event is not a trend, and this is logged as a watch rather than a claim.** 🟡 **`ACTION VU`: sweep the K-12 tutor rows for further relicensing, keying on a `LICENSE` payload that names TWO licences and a DATE** — a shape this pass can now detect and which no instrument here looks for.

### 🟢 `2` — The permissive supply has moved a layer down

🔵 **Not one of this pass's `6` North American permissive rows is a tutor or an LMS.** They are: a 50-state privacy-regime tracker, a student-records audit stream, an identity-scoped RAG pre-filter, a records de-duplication pipeline, a learning-analytics methods release, a hackathon session kit.

🟡 **Read beside `p240`'s GPL finding, the two halves agree from opposite directions.** `p240` measured that the richest European and Latin American education supply is **structurally unavailable** to a commercial engagement (`16`/`16` `GPL-3.0` Moodle plugins). `p241` measures that the available North American supply **is not the learning product at all**. 🔵 **Both point a studio at the same place: the integration and compliance layer, sold as service work.**

### 🟢 `3` — The cross-jurisdiction compliance ADAPTER as an emerging category

🔵 **Four of this pass's rows are one-person MIT projects at `0`–`2`★ that map several regulatory regimes onto one enforcement surface** — `enterprise-rag-patterns`, `regulated-ai-governance`, `voice-ai-governance`, `kinetic-gain-protocol-suite`. 🟢 **Two do it as ADAPTERS for existing agent frameworks** *(CrewAI, AutoGen, LangChain, Semantic Kernel, Haystack)* **rather than as frameworks of their own**, which is the architecturally interesting choice: it concedes the orchestration layer and competes on the policy layer.

🟡 **At `0`–`2`★ none of these has won the category, which is precisely when a studio can still shape it.** 🔴 **Logged as a shape observed across four rows in one harvest, not as a market forecast.**

### 🟢 `4` — Automated assessment is where education AI meets its regulator, in two jurisdictions at once

🔵 **[`HoangNam2464/ai-teacher-copilot`](https://github.com/HoangNam2464/ai-teacher-copilot) generates Bloom-taxonomy quizzes from teacher documents.** That is automated assessment, which is:

- 🔴 named in **Vietnam's Decision `33/2026/QĐ-TTg`** education high-risk list *(carried since `p138`; `2027-03-01`, and `2027-09-01` for education systems already in operation)*, and
- 🔴 named in the **EU AI Act Annex III point `3`** *(applies `2027-12-02` under `Reg. (EU) 2026/1744`)*.

🟡 **The same product feature triggers high-risk obligations in two unrelated jurisdictions on timelines six months apart.** 🟢 **And its citation traceability is the mitigation both regimes ask for, built in before either applies** — which is the shape of the sale.

### 🟢 `5` — "Privacy-first" is becoming an architecture, not a claim

🔵 Measured across this pass's rows rather than asserted: `record-bridge` runs **entirely on synthetic data with zero PII**; `slack-behavioral-segmentation` ships **no data by design**, naming human-subjects consent and FERPA as the reason; `educore` and `snflwr.ai` are **offline-first**; `district-brain` *(carried)* is local-first. 🟡 **For a demo, "runs on synthetic data" is worth more than a compliance page — it is demonstrable in a pitch without a DPA.**

### 🔴 `6` — The English WebSearch channel is now a re-verification channel, measured three passes running

🔴 **`0` new rows and `0` corrections for a third pass**, with every offered finding checked through `make claims`. 🔴 **Two defects reproduced verbatim**: the superseded `2026-08-02` Annex III date *(≥5th re-service)* and the UNESCO IESALC `2025` working paper offered as `2026` data *(3rd consecutive)*.

🟢 **The EMEA search is the instructive one: it returned the CORRECT Omnibus deferral and the SUPERSEDED enforcement date in the same response.** 🟡 **So the channel's failure is not ignorance, it is non-determinism — which is why a claims gate in front of it is the only thing that makes it safe to read.**

### 🔵 `7` — And the trend inside this KB: a correct instrument that is not in the gate is not a control

🔴 **`p1054-mit-plus-clause` classified the carve-out case correctly from pass `188` and `make hooks` never ran it** — because its suite died under the invocation its own docstring prescribed. `53` passes later the shared classifier in the gate published a bare `MIT` on a payload `p1054` refuses. 🟢 **Fixed and wired this pass.** 🟡 **`ACTION VW` generalises it to every instrument in `compose/code/`.**

## Pass 240 — 2026-10-16 — `ACTION VK` IS DISCHARGED AND THE THRESHOLDS IT WAS REGISTERED TO CALIBRATE ARE **FALSIFIED**: A QUOTED PHRASE RESTRICTED TO `name,description` RETURNS `≥96.7`% AT EVERY COUNT FROM `264` TO `1 368`, AND THE `48`-POINT COLLAPSE IS **FIELD SCOPE**, NOT BREADTH

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p240-query-shape`](../evidence/p240-query-shape.2026-10-16-p240.tsv) · [`p240-queries`](../evidence/p240-queries.2026-10-16-p240.tsv)

### 🔴 `T240-B` — the measurement `ACTION VK` asked for, and it refutes the instrument that asked

`p239` set `CAUTION_MAX = 253` *(the last count measured to work)* and `REFUSE_MIN = 2326` *(the first measured to fail)* and registered **`ACTION VK`: the `(253, 2326]` interval wants one harvest at `~800` and one at `~1 500` to close it.** Four interior harvests, same sort key, same day:

| `total_count` | sort | field scope | on-topic | published rows |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `264` | `updated` | `name,description` | 🟢 **`100.0`%** | **3** |
| `775` | `updated` | `name,description` | 🟢 **`96.7`%** | 1 |
| `1 368` | `updated` | `name,description` | 🟢 **`100.0`%** | 🔴 **0** |
| `2 091` | `updated` | 🔴 **`name,description,readme`** | 🔴 **`40.0`%** | 0 |

🔴 **The interval is not a degradation zone at all.** `264` sits *above* `CAUTION_MAX` and returns the highest rate this KB has measured; `1 368` sits mid-interval at `100`%. **A count threshold cannot be the predicate.**

### 🔵 The controlled pair, which is why this is an attribution and not a correlation

Rows one and four are **the same phrase** — `"intelligent tutoring"` — with one parameter changed:

| field scope | `total_count` | on-topic |
|---|---|---|
| `in:name,description` | `264` | 🟢 **`100.0`%** |
| `in:name,description,readme` | 🔴 `2 091` | 🔴 **`40.0`%** |

🔵 **`in:readme` matches a phrase anywhere in a long document, so page one fills with profile READMEs, arXiv trackers and awesome-lists that merely MENTION the topic.** The count rose `7.9`× *because* the field scope widened — **breadth is the symptom, field scope is the cause**, and `p239` read the symptom. 🟡 **`p239`'s own evidence agrees in hindsight: every one of its four `SLICE` harvests carried `in:...,readme` or bare unquoted terms; neither of its two high scorers did.**

🟢 **The remedy is one parameter and it is not the sort key:** *quote the phrase and keep it out of the README*. 🟡 **`ACTION VS`: `p239-query-shape` must take `field_scope` and `term_shape` as inputs; `total_count` alone cannot classify a harvest.** 🔵 `p239`'s **`SCOPED`-has-no-threshold** rule survives intact and gained a point at `1.5`× `REFUSE_MIN`: `topic:edtech` at `total_count` `3 581` still returned `4` of `5` on-topic.

### 🔴 `T240-B2` — and on-topic rate is not yield

🔴 **The `1 368` harvest scored `100`% on-topic and produced `0` publishable rows.** Thirty genuine student-information systems, nearly all first-semester coursework. 🔵 **On-topic measures whether the QUERY found the subject; it says nothing about whether the SUBJECT is reusable.** 🟡 **`ACTION VT`: every query-evidence table records published rows beside on-topic rate** — this pass's does, and the two columns disagree on three of eight harvests.

### 🟡 `T240-G` — a channel fact worth one line

🔴 `topic:kaoyan OR topic:gaokao OR topic:jiaoyu` → **HTTP 422**, *"The search contains only logical operators (AND / OR / NOT) without any search terms."* **Qualifiers cannot be OR-ed on this channel.** 🔵 A multi-topic regional sweep is therefore **one harvest per topic**, which is a cost that has to be budgeted rather than discovered mid-pass.

### 🔴 `T240-F` — the regulatory and market trend leg: `0` new, `0` corrected, for a further pass

Four global searches and one per region produced **no row and no correction**. The two concrete recurrences are recorded in [`agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md): UNESCO IESALC's `87`%/`26`% offered again as *2026* data against fieldwork these shelves date to **August–October 2025**, and Vietnam's Decision `33/2026/QĐ-TTg` offered as new when `p138` carried it with its full compliance split.

🔵 **The trend that matters is the one about this KB rather than the sector: on regulation, the shelves are now ahead of the English search channel.** 🟡 **`ACTION UX` is re-scoped rather than re-registered: stop asking the English channel for EMEA and APAC primary sources, and spend the same budget on the regulators' own publications** — which is the same lesson `T240-A` teaches about payloads, one level up.

## Pass 239 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND IS THAT **EDUCATION AI IS ARRIVING FILED UNDER DATA INTEROPERABILITY**, NOT UNDER EDUCATION AI — AND THE COUNTER-TREND IS THAT THIS KB'S OWN RETRIEVAL HAS BEEN MEASURING **PUSH FREQUENCY** AND CALLING IT RELEVANCE

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p239-query-shape`](../evidence/p239-query-shape.2026-10-16-p239.tsv) · [`p239-sortkey-reattribution`](../evidence/p239-sortkey-reattribution.2026-10-16-p239.tsv) · [`p239-lure-farm-scope`](../evidence/p239-lure-farm-scope.2026-10-16-p239.tsv) · [`p239-edfi-grant-lengths`](../evidence/p239-edfi-grant-lengths.2026-10-16-p239.tsv)

### 🟢 `T239-G` — the real trend: the agents are under the STANDARD, not under "education AI"

`ACTION UC` — *"find top open-source education AI agents"* — has failed **nine consecutive passes** on the English web-search channel. 🟢 **This pass found two production education agents, and neither would ever have answered that query:**

| agent | filed under | what it actually is |
|---|---|---|
| [`Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Fiona`](https://github.com/Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Fiona) | a **data-interoperability standards body** | a deployed Slack assistant over education standards and APIs, `Apache-2.0`, human-approval gate on its only write |
| [`Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/AI-Tools-for-Ed-Fi-SDLC`](https://github.com/Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/AI-Tools-for-Ed-Fi-SDLC) | the same org's **SDLC tooling** | prompts, skills, hooks and agent definitions, `Apache-2.0` |

🔵 **The institutions actually shipping education AI are the ones that already own the data plumbing, and they file their agents beside their pipelines.** 🟡 **So `ACTION UC` is very likely a vocabulary failure rather than an absence — which is a different finding from nine passes of "no supply", and a more useful one.** 🔴 **Kept unreworded regardless, so its failure stays on the record.**

### 🔴 `T239-A` — the counter-trend, and it is about this KB

🔴 **`0` of `20` of this KB's query-evidence tables record a sort key.** Measured this pass over six harvests:

| `total_count` | sort | on-topic page one |
|---|---|---|
| `24` *(`org:`-scoped)* | `updated` | 🟢 **`91.7`%** |
| `253` | `updated` | 🟢 `63.3`% |
| `7 576` | **`stars`** | 🟡 `48.0`% |
| `2 326` | `updated` | 🔴 **`2.5`%** |
| `8 618` | `updated` | 🔴 **`0.0`%** |
| `81 905` | `updated` | 🔴 **`0.0`%** |

🔵 **A recency sort over a broad result set ranks by LAST PUSH, so it selects for push FREQUENCY.** `8 618` at `0`% against `7 576` at `48`% is comparable breadth and a **`48`-point swing from the sort key alone**. 🟡 **The practical shape of the trend: what pushes most often on GitHub is bot-maintained — job boards refreshed nightly, trending-trackers, VPN subscription lists, and freshly minted lure repositories. A recency-sorted broad harvest is a census of automation, not of a field.**

### 🔴 `T239-B/C` — the impersonation trend, rescaled

`p238` read its `13`-repository cohort as an education-targeted operation timed to the GitHub Classroom shutdown. 🔴 **A census says otherwise: `≥5 525` repositories in a SIX-DAY window, `100` distinct owners per `100` results, and `1` of `100` page-one names is an education brand.**

🟢 **What IS a trend, and it is sharper than the education reading:**

- 🔴 **Brand impersonation at industrial scale with a VALID permissive payload.** Six members read first-hand — BioShock, Hulu Plus, Cyberduck, EverQuest II, GeoGebra, JClic — every one serving `LICENSE` at `HTTP 200`, every one funnelling to the single host `softyne.com`.
- 🔵 **The attacked signal is the one the open-source world treats as ground truth.** `p228`–`p238` spent eleven passes establishing that a grant must be read from the payload. 🔴 **A deliberately labelled repository passes that read.** 🟡 **A grant is a permission; it was never a provenance claim** — and that generalises well beyond education.
- 🟡 **It is now reaching public-sector education brands in EMEA** *(`JClic`, Generalitat de Catalunya)*, where `p238`'s set was `Moodle`, `Google Classroom` and `PRONOTE`.

### 🟡 `T239-F` — a quieter finding, and the pass's own overreach caught before publication

🔴 **A census of all `24` `Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS` repositories, every grant read first-hand: `13` are the PRISTINE `11 357` B Apache-2.0, `9` are `10 173`, exactly `1` is `10 172`, and `1` has no grant file at all.** 🔴 **`10 172` is the only length these shelves carried — generalised across `10` files from a sample of ONE repository.**

🟡 **This pass first explained the split as VINTAGE and that explanation is falsified by its own census:** the two groups' push dates overlap almost entirely, the org's oldest-pushed repository *(`EdFi.Installer.AppCommon`, `2026-03-19`)* is **pristine** and a recent one *(`Ed-Fi-Data-Standard`, `2026-10-09`)* is **trimmed**. 🟢 **The predicate that survives is LINEAGE: all four `LICENSE.txt` files sit in the short group and all are the ODS product line.**

🔵 **The trend worth carrying is about fingerprints, not licences: a byte length identifies a TEMPLATE, never a provenance and never an org.** 🟡 **`ACTION VN`: pair length with the `APPENDIX` / `END OF TERMS` predicates, which are what actually distinguish the shapes, and never generalise a length from one repository to its org.**

### 🔴 `T239-E` — and the newest licence-risk shape is SOCIAL, not textual

🔴 **[`edanalytics/edu_wh`](https://github.com/edanalytics/edu_wh) `(19★)` and [`edanalytics/edu_edfi_source`](https://github.com/edanalytics/edu_edfi_source) `(10★)` are `PolyForm Noncommercial License 1.0.0`** — while the same owner's [`DPS-Ed-Fi-ODS`](https://github.com/edanalytics/DPS-Ed-Fi-ODS) is carried on these shelves as `Apache-2.0` and cited as *"evidence the grant is actually exercised by a third party"*.

🔵 **The trend worth naming: as permissive-by-default erodes, owners become MIXED rather than switching.** Source stays `Apache-2.0`; the **analytics and data-product layers** go source-available or noncommercial — which is precisely the layer a client engagement wants. 🟡 **`ACTION VM`: no row inherits a grant from a sibling repository, however well the owner is established here.**

### 🔴 What this pass did NOT establish

- 🔴 **No regional trend for EMEA, APAC or LATAM was measured.** The English news channel returned secondary vendor press for EMEA and enterprise IT press for APAC; the LATAM supply harvest was refused as a `SLICE`. 🔵 **Three of four regions are unmeasured and said so — `p238`'s own rule, that silence looks exactly like coverage.**
- 🔴 **`ACTION VA` stands: still no education-agent eval harness.** Both new agent rows are `0`★ and neither ships a benchmark, while the adjacent compliance category has had a published four-tool comparative eval since `p238`.
- 🔴 **`ACTION VB` unrun**: no compliance instrument has been executed over a carried education repository. 🟡 It now has a natural target in `Ed-Fi-Extensions`.

## Pass 238 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND IS THAT **COMPLIANCE HAS BECOME A SOFTWARE CATEGORY**, AND THE COUNTER-TREND IS THAT THE OPEN-SOURCE SUPPLY CHAIN HAS LEARNED TO **FORGE THE ONE SIGNAL THIS KB TRUSTS**

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p238-verified-rows`](../evidence/p238-verified-rows.2026-10-16-p238.tsv) · [`p238-licence-filter-lure-farm`](../evidence/p238-licence-filter-lure-farm.2026-10-16-p238.tsv) · [`p238-queries`](../evidence/p238-queries.2026-10-16-p238.tsv)

### 🟢 Trend `1` — EU AI Act compliance is now a populated open-source category, not a consultancy service

🟢 **`13` permissive instruments located in one pass, `11` of them clean, `0` of those previously carried.** The category has a production member *(`airblackbox`, `v1.17.0`, on PyPI and npm)*, **a published four-tool comparative benchmark over `144` hand-labelled programs**, four distinct architectural shapes — **scanner** *(`eu-ai-act-scanner`, `mcp-eu-ai-act`)*, **logger** *(`aiact-audit-log`, `epi-recorder`, `provena`, `lar`)*, **gateway** *(`sphragis`, `preloop`)*, **CI gate** *(`precommiteu`)* — and a dated obligation to sell against.

🔵 **The structural read: a regulation with a `2027-12-02` date produced a software category inside `~15` months of entering into force, and the category organised itself around ARTICLE NUMBERS.** `frai` targets Article `50`. `aiact-audit-log` names Article `12`. `eu-ai-act-scanner` cites `Regulation 2024/1689` in its own description. 🟡 **That is a procurement-ready vocabulary, and it is the vocabulary a studio should be quoting.**

🔴 **The counter-measurement, on this KB's own text:** `C2PA` appears **`171`** times across these shelves; **`Article 12` appears `2`** and `tamper-evident` **`0`**. 🔵 **This KB tracked the obligation whose artefact is a document and missed the one whose artefact is a running system — and Article `12` is the one that cannot be produced retroactively.**

### 🔴 Trend `2` — the counter-trend, and it is the more important of the two

🔴 **A licence is now a forgeable discovery signal.** `p237` moved supply discovery to the REST channel precisely because the licence filter is server-side and *"inverts the sample"*. One pass later, a single query returned **`13` repositories named for real education products** — `Moodle`, `Google Classroom`, `PRONOTE`, `CmapTools`, `Packet Tracer`, `Stellarium`, `Childsplay`, `Complete Anatomy`, `Adobe Presenter`, `GeoEnZo`, `WordFox`, `CubeTest`, `DiagramPainter` — each with `license: MIT`, a `LICENSE` at `HTTP 200`, `0`★, `0` forks, a distinct throwaway owner, pushed the same day, and every link funnelling to one host.

🔵 **The trend statement: open-source discovery signals are being *constructed*, not merely mislabelled, and the construction targets exactly the checks a careful pipeline runs.** The licence badge in those READMEs is a remote `.svg` served from the funnel host — a *picture* of a licence badge. The authority line is *"Reviewed by the senior review board · Senior Software Analyst · Verified against the official source"*, naming no reviewer, no board and no source.

🟡 **This reframes `p228`–`p237`'s whole grant-ladder programme.** Reading the payload first-hand defeats a repository that is *carelessly* labelled. 🔴 **It does nothing against one that is *deliberately* labelled.** 🟢 **The missing predicate is not about licences at all — it is provenance: stars, age, fork graph, owner history, and whether the README's links concentrate on a single external host.** `P238-1`.

🟡 **And the timing is a trend in itself**: the `Google Classroom` lure was pushed `2026-10-16`, seven weeks after `p236` dated Classroom's shutdown to `2026-08-28`. 🔵 **Platform withdrawal creates a search-demand window, and the window is being farmed.** `p236` called withdrawal *"the year's largest event in education tooling"*; this is its second-order effect.

### 🟢 Trend `3` — supply localisation continues, and its centre moved from APAC to LATAM

`p237` measured localisation at `19` of `35` new rows in non-English languages. 🟢 **This pass: `22` of `43` rows are non-English-language, and for the first time the largest regional intake is LATAM (`13`) rather than APAC (`9`).**

🔵 **And the LATAM rows are institutionally anchored in a way the APAC rows are not yet**: two name a Brazilian **state education secretariat** (`SEDUC-SP`, `SEDU`-ES), two are published by named **foundations**, and one is placed by a **Chilean corporate form in its copyright holder string** (`SpA`) — the first time on these shelves that jurisdiction came out of the licence payload itself. 🟡 **Institutional anchoring beats star count as a procurement signal, and LATAM supply has it while APAC supply has scale.**

🔴 **The measurement that bounds the optimism: `0` of `13` LATAM candidates were already carried.** 🔵 **A `0`% duplication rate is not a compliment to the channel, it is a verdict on the prior reach — these shelves had barely started on Spanish and Portuguese.**

### 🟡 Trend `4` — the duplication rate splits by AXIS, which is a usable planning number

`p236` measured `~45`% duplication on two channels and concluded it was *"a property of this KB's reach rather than of any one query"*. 🟢 **This pass refines that: the rate is a property of the AXIS, not the KB.**

| axis queried | candidates drafted | already carried | rate |
|---|---|---|---|
| education agents *(the usual axis)* | `16` | `5` | 🟡 **`31`%** |
| EU AI Act compliance instruments | `16` | `3` | 🟡 `19`% |
| LATAM Spanish + Portuguese | `13` | **`0`** | 🟢 **`0`%** |
| agent-governance infrastructure | `6` | **`0`** | 🟢 **`0`%** |

🔵 **So "where should the next pass look?" has a numeric answer: the axes this KB has never queried return `0`% duplication, and the axis it queries every pass returns `31`%.** 🟡 **`ACTION VG`: record the axis with every future intake so this table extends instead of being re-derived.**

### 🔴 Trends this pass could NOT establish

- 🔴 **No education-specific APAC regulation or adoption figure.** Eighth-ish consecutive miss; the region word returns enterprise IT press. 🟡 **`ACTION UX`** — query ministries by name (`MEXT`, `MOE` Singapore, `ACARA`, India's `NEP`).
- 🔴 **North America produced `1` of `43` rows.** 🟡 **`ACTION UY`** — the statute words are contaminated; query district systems by name.
- 🔴 **Market sizing still does not converge** — `$6.4` B / `$10.6` B / `$11.4` B on the base year, a `78`% spread, wider than `p236`'s `50`%. 🟢 `ACTION UP`'s rule holds: quote the range and the firm, or quote nothing.
- 🔴 **Not one instrument in trend `1` has been RUN.** 🟡 **`ACTION VB`.** Until then the category is catalogued, not tested — and a trend built on self-descriptions is a reading of marketing copy.

## Pass 237 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND IS THAT EDUCATION AI SUPPLY HAS **LOCALISED**, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS THAT `19` OF THIS PASS'S `35` NEW GRANTED ROWS ARE IN LANGUAGES ENGLISH DOES NOT SHARE

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p237-rest-licence-channel`](../evidence/p237-rest-licence-channel.2026-10-16-p237.tsv) · [`p237-verified-rows`](../evidence/p237-verified-rows.2026-10-16-p237.tsv)

### 🟢 `T237-1` — the supply trend: education agents are being built against NATIONAL systems, not against a market

Every one of this pass's `35` new granted rows binds something **jurisdictional**. Not one is a general-purpose education agent aimed at a global market:

| binding | rows | region |
|---|---|---|
| a **national statute** *(FERPA, COPPA)* | `4` | North America |
| a **national data standard** *(1EdTech OneRoster, Ed-Fi)* | `4` | North America, APAC |
| a **national government API** *(NEIS, 학교알리미, GIGA School)* | `4` | APAC |
| a **national examination** *(Gaokao, ICFES Saber 11)* | `2` | APAC, LATAM |
| a **statutory artefact** *(NRW Zeugnisse)* | `1` | EMEA |
| a **national curriculum or ministry programme** | `6` | APAC, EMEA |

🔵 **This is the opposite of the trend the market reports describe.** The sizing sources in this pass's web channel describe one global market growing from ~`$11B` to `$57–80B`, led by AI tutors and adaptive learning. 🔴 **The code says something different: the buildable layer is integration with a specific country's plumbing, and the "global AI tutor" is a product category, not a repository category.** 🟡 **For a studio that is good news — the thing being built is exactly the thing a services firm sells.**

### 🔴 `T237-2` — the measurement trend, and it is a correction to this file

These shelves have reported regional supply for `236` passes. 🔴 **Every one of those figures was produced by an English-language query, so every regional supply claim this KB has published was a measurement of its own vocabulary.** The size of the distortion, measured this pass:

| channel | education AI agents returned |
|---|---|
| `"top open source AI agents education 2026 github MIT"` *(mandated, English, web, **7 passes**)* | 🔴 **`0`** |
| `教育 agent license:apache-2.0` *(Chinese, REST, licence-filtered, **1 query**)* | 🟢 **`8`** |

🔵 **The trend worth recording is not "APAC is rising". It is that a vertical's open-source supply localises into its own language early, and a KB that reads one language sees a field that is years behind the real one.**

### 🟡 `T237-3` — the maturity trend, stated against this pass's own rows

🔴 **The new APAC and LATAM agent rows are tiny: `0`–`54`★, and `6` of the `12` were created in the last `5` months.** Three were created in the last `14` days; one is **two days old**.

🔵 **That is a real trend and it cuts both ways.** 🟢 It means the field is being written right now and a studio can be early with a fork. 🔴 It also means **none of these is a dependency** — they are fork bases and reference architectures, and a cell that calls any of them production-ready would be wrong. 🟡 **The one structural asset among them is [`3yangyang9/DeepTutor-AILearning`](https://github.com/3yangyang9/DeepTutor-AILearning)'s four-level tenancy** *(platform → institution → campus → teacher/student, with the operator holding model choice and billing)* — the architecture outlives the `44`★ repository that carries it.

### 🟢 `T237-4` — the governance trend reaches the CODE, which it had not before

🔵 **Three of this pass's rows implement a control these shelves have been recommending as prose, and two of them were not aiming at regulation at all:**

- 🟢 [`hanqiaoculture-sudo/HanQiao-GOAI`](https://github.com/hanqiaoculture-sudo/HanQiao-GOAI) *(`Apache-2.0`, Chinese)* ships an explicit **human-confirmation step** in a multi-agent document pipeline — the Annex-III-shaped control, built by a study-abroad team.
- 🟢 [`xsx11223/zhibuzhi`](https://github.com/xsx11223/zhibuzhi) *(`Apache-2.0`)* opens by telling the student **how much it does not know about them**, and formalises that as a POMDP where setting a question is an observation action. 🔵 **Calibrated uncertainty as the product's first screen, not as a disclaimer.**
- 🟢 [`CSOAI-ORG/coppa-ferpa-mcp`](https://github.com/CSOAI-ORG/coppa-ferpa-mcp) *(`MIT`)* makes **children's-privacy compliance callable over MCP**, which is the first row on these shelves where the compliance limb is a server rather than a paragraph.

🟡 **The trend: 2026's regulation is starting to appear as agent-level architecture — approval gates, calibrated abstention, compliance tool-servers — rather than as documentation bolted on afterwards.**

### 🔴 `T237-5` — the counter-trend: the channels themselves are degrading, and that is now two measurements

| channel | p237 state |
|---|---|
| `repo1.maven.org` | 🔴 **rate-limited below useful resolution.** Positive control `429`; the same url returned a `554`-byte real `404` and then a **one-byte `404`** minutes apart |
| `search.maven.org`, `central.sonatype.com` | 🔴 **refused by this session's egress proxy** *(`connect_rejected`)* |
| `api.github.com` direct | 🔴 **`403`** — *"sessions are bound to their configured repositories"* |
| GitHub REST via the MCP server | 🟢 **reachable, and the only path to a server-side licence filter from here** |
| web search | 🟢 reachable; 🔴 **cannot express a licence filter, which inverts its supply sample** |
| `raw.githubusercontent.com` | 🟢 reachable — every grant this pass was read here, first-hand |

🔵 **p232 found a `200` is not a read. p233 found a `404` is not an absence. p237 adds: a channel's REACHABILITY is itself a moving variable, so a supply claim needs the channel named beside it or it will be read later as a claim about the world.** 🟡 **`ACTION UK`'s phrasing rule — publish *"this channel returned `0`"*, never *"there is no X"* — is the only thing that keeps these shelves honest as the channels close one by one.**

## Pass 236 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND IS **WITHDRAWAL**: THE YEAR'S LARGEST EVENT IN EDUCATION TOOLING WAS A PLATFORM BEING TURNED OFF, NOT ONE BEING LAUNCHED

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p236-classroom-decommission`](../evidence/p236-classroom-decommission.2026-10-16-p236.tsv) · [`p236-queries`](../evidence/p236-queries.2026-10-16-p236.tsv)

### 🔴 `T236-1` — platform withdrawal is now a live risk class, and these shelves had no column for it

GitHub Classroom went dark `2026-08-28`. 🔴 **The thing to generalise is not the event, it is that this KB could not have seen it coming and could not represent it afterwards**: every freshness signal on these shelves — last commit, last release, star growth — was *healthy* for the `classroom-resources` repositories right up to the freeze, and several are still healthy now. 🟢 **The only field that distinguishes "quiet" from "finished" is `archived`, and it became readable on these shelves for the first time this pass.**

🟡 **The lesson for every vertical KB, not just this one: a dependency's risk is not its commit recency. It is whether the vendor still intends to run the service the code talks to.** The archived reporter still *runs*; what it synced to no longer exists.

### 🟢 `T236-2` — the 2026 regulatory wave is complete, and education is named in all four regions

| region | instrument | status on `2026-10-16` |
|---|---|---|
| EMEA | EU AI Act — education = high-risk where it gates access or scores exams | 🟢 **enforcement began `2026-08-02`**; Art. 4 AI-literacy duty live since `2025-02-02`; emotion recognition in schools prohibited; 🔴 conformity deadline moved and **disputed** *(Dec 2027 vs Aug 2028)* |
| APAC | Korea AI Basic Act — education is "high-impact" | 🟢 **in force `2026-01-22`** |
| North America | state law, not federal — `134` bills / `31` states | 🟢 live and fragmenting; H.R. 8747 advanced `2026-07-21` |
| LATAM | Estado de México, education law art. 61 | 🟡 **reformed April 2026**, reported as the region's first classroom-specific AI statute |

🔵 **Last pass this file's headline was *"the trend is governance, and the date everyone quotes for it does not reconcile."* 🟢 That holds, and p236 narrows it**: the *existence* of binding rules is no longer in doubt in any region; what remains unreconciled is a single date, the EU high-risk conformity deadline. 🟡 **`ACTION UQ` carries it.**

### 🔴 `T236-3` — the counter-trend nobody is pricing: mandates are outrunning adoption, and one has already broken

🔴 **Korea is the first completed experiment, and it failed.** AI textbooks were mandated, adoption came in **below `30`%**, and the National Assembly removed their official status. 🟢 **Set that against China mandating AI from age six with ≥8 hours a year in Beijing, and India's ten-million-teacher training push**, and the pattern is clear: **the binding constraint is teacher workflow, not content or policy.**

🔵 **This reframes the market numbers.** Reported student AI use is `92`% (2025, up from `66`% in 2024) while institutions are described as still in pilot mode, with procurement moving slower than consumer adoption. 🔴 **The gap is not an adoption lag that time will close — Korea shows a mandate can land on the wrong side of it and be repealed.**

### 🟢 `T236-4` — the supply bottleneck in APAC is licensing, not code

🟢 **First Japanese- and Korean-language supply probe ever run here** *(`ACTION UL`)*: `11` repositories, `9` uncarried, 🔴 **`7` of `9` with no licence whatsoever**, and one — [`GwonDalhyeon/school-workflow-ai-releases`](https://github.com/GwonDalhyeon/school-workflow-ai-releases) — carrying a README that **explicitly forbids sale and modified redistribution** while looking open on any listing page.

🔵 **Combined with `p235`'s LATAM finding and `ACTION UJ`'s three ungranted Brazilian repositories, a cross-regional pattern is now visible on these shelves: outside North America and EMEA, education software is written and published but not *granted*.** 🟢 **A one-line `LICENSE` file is the whole intervention**, and it is cheap enough to be a community contribution rather than a project. 🟡 **`ACTION UJ` extended to the five ungranted APAC repositories found this pass.**

### 🟡 `T236-5` — a channel note that is itself a trend

🔴 **Web search is now the weakest channel these shelves use for supply.** `github trending education AI 2026` returned no live trending page — only aggregators and a LinkedIn post. The mandated agent query has failed **six consecutive times**. 🟢 **The REST channel answered the same questions with server-side filters, exact star counts and the `archived` flag in one call.** 🟡 **`ACTION US`: move supply discovery to REST and keep web search for regulation, market figures and news, where it is still the better instrument.**

## Pass 235 — 2026-10-16 — THE 2026 TREND IS GOVERNANCE, AND THE DATE EVERYONE QUOTES FOR IT **DOES NOT RECONCILE**

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p235-queries`](../evidence/p235-queries.2026-10-16-p235.tsv)

### 🟢 `T1` — from experimentation to governance

🟢 **Converging across independent sources this pass:** `1EdTech` frames 2026 as AI in education shifting *from experimentation to governance*, with institutions building policy and oversight structures. `HolonIQ` reports the clearest gains came from the practical uses — **course-design support and teacher productivity** — and expects attention to move to ethics and demonstrated instructional benefit rather than broad personalisation promises. The **OECD `Digital Education Outlook 2026`** urges **purpose-built educational AI over general-purpose tools**. 🟡 **Several of these are vendor or association blogs; the direction is consistent, the numbers are not independently verified.**

🔴 **The measurable version of this trend is the governance DEFICIT, and it is regional:** LATAM `87%` adoption against `26%` holding a strategy; EMEA `10%` of `450+` institutions with formal AI-use guidelines; North America `60%` of K-12 teachers using AI while `31` states legislate. 🟢 **Every one of those is a demand signal for policy-and-evidence work rather than model work.**

### 🔴 `T2` — the EU AI Act date does not reconcile, and this KB will not pick one

🔴 **Three sources, three timelines, read this pass:**

| source shape | what it says |
|---|---|
| Digital Omnibus commentary | Council gave final approval **`29 Jun 2026`**; still awaiting Official Journal publication. Revised application: **`2 Dec 2027`** stand-alone high-risk, **`2 Aug 2028`** high-risk embedded in regulated products |
| a second commentary | high-risk provisions enter into force **`Aug 2026`** |
| European Commission page | AI Act entry into force **`31 Jul 2026`** |

🔴 **Do not quote any of these to a client from these shelves.** 🟢 **What IS stable and quotable: the Act classifies AI used for admissions, student evaluation and exam scoring as HIGH-RISK** — that classification is consistent across all three readings, and it is what actually scopes an EMEA engagement. 🟡 **`ACTION UN`: resolve the date against the Official Journal text itself, not commentary.**

### 🟢 `T3` — APAC legislates generally, and not for education

🟢 **Binding, dated, and in force:** South Korea's **`AI Basic Act`, effective `22 Jan 2026`** *(it also carries SME and start-up adoption support)*; **Vietnam's national AI law, `1 Mar 2026`**, risk-based with a high-risk list that appears to include education *(source text truncated — not relied on)*; **Taiwan's `AI Basic Act`, passed Dec 2025**; China's framework the most comprehensive in the region, with binding generative-AI and content-labelling rules; Japan, Singapore and Australia voluntary and principles-based.

🔴 **No education-specific AI rule was found in any APAC jurisdiction this pass.** 🟢 **Control on that absence** *(`ACTION UF`)*: the finding rests on English-language search only — **no `学校管理`, no `학교 관리 시스템`, no CJK query has ever been run on these shelves** *(`ACTION UL`)*. 🟡 **So this is a gap in COVERAGE, and saying otherwise would be the error `ACTION UK` was written for.**

### 🟢 `T4` — North America regulates by state, and privacy leads

🟢 **`134` AI-in-education bills across `31` states**, themed on student-data privacy, classroom-use limits and curriculum. Named instruments: **CA `AB 1159`** *(bar student data from training AI models)*, **ID `SB 1227`** *(privacy protections for school AI tools)*, **OK and MD** *(human oversight; no AI in high-stakes student decisions)*, with **MD giving its `24` districts `120` days** from state guidance to adopt their own policies. Federal: **`H.R. 8747`, K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026, advanced in committee `21 Jul 2026`** — 🔴 **committee advance is not passage.** 🟡 **A student-authored national framework *(STUDENTS FIRST Act of 2026, all `50` states)* explicitly rejects both an outright ban and unrestricted adoption** — useful as a stakeholder-consent artefact.

### 🟢 `T5` — the agent-tier trend: tool surfaces for standards, not more tutors

🟢 **The one new agent-tier signal this pass is not a tutor.** It is **`@bncc/mcp` `0.5.0`** — `MIT`, `8` releases, modified **`2026-10-15` 20:15** — an MCP server exposing the `1 721` learning objectives of Brazil's `Base Nacional Comum Curricular`, published by a named Brazilian company, alongside **`bncc-benchmark`**'s open measurement of **`15 300` answers from `17` models** against that same curriculum. 🔵 **The pattern: a national standard published as a mountable tool plus a public accuracy benchmark for it.** 🟡 **It is cheap to replicate for any national curriculum, and nobody has done it for the other markets on these shelves** — which makes it the most reusable thing found this pass.

## Pass 234 — 2026-10-16 — THE REGULATORY TREND CONVERGES ON **ASSESSMENT** IN THREE JURISDICTIONS AT ONCE, AND THE SUPPLY TREND IS THAT PERMISSIVE EDUCATION PLATFORMS COME FROM **MINISTRIES**, NOT STARTUPS

⏱️ **THIRD pass of `2026-10-16`.**
