🔵 **Evidence:** [`p234-queries`](../evidence/p234-queries.2026-10-16-p234.tsv) · [`p234-tu-namespace-census`](../evidence/p234-tu-namespace-census.2026-10-16-p234.tsv) · [`p234-new-rows`](../evidence/p234-new-rows.2026-10-16-p234.tsv)

### 🔴 `TR234-1` — the trend that matters most: **automated assessment is becoming a regulated artefact, in parallel, in unrelated jurisdictions**

🟢 **Three independent legal regimes converged on the same narrow target within about seven months, and none of them cites the others:**

| jurisdiction | instrument | status | what it names |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **EU** | AI Act | 🟡 AI Office + national authorities enforcing from `2 Aug 2026`; standalone high-risk obligations dated `2 Dec 2027` by a second source; *"AI omnibus"* amendments proposed | 🔴 **admissions, student evaluation, EXAM SCORING = high-risk** — and it reaches **non-EU systems whose outputs affect EU students** |
| 🔴 **Vietnam** | national AI law | 🔴 **in force `1 Mar 2026`** | 🔴 **education a high-risk sector, AUTOMATED ASSESSMENT named explicitly** *(compliance 2027; sources split between `1 Mar` and `1 Sep`)* |
| 🟡 **US states** | `134` bills / `31` states | in session | 🔴 **Oklahoma + Maryland bar AI from HIGH-STAKES DECISIONS ABOUT STUDENTS and require human oversight**; California `AB 1159` restricts training on student data |

🟢 **The engineering consequence, and it is one build, not three: provenance on every generated item, human-in-the-loop sign-off before a score becomes a grade, immutable score logging, and an appeal trail.** 🔵 **That is a single assessment-governance layer sold three times. It is also the first time on these shelves that a compliance requirement has been *convergent* rather than *divergent* — previous passes treated regional regulation as fragmentation to be priced; this one is leverage.**

🔴 **The date conflict is published as a conflict.** The `2 Aug 2026` and `2 Dec 2027` dates come from different sources covering the same obligations, and the Commission's proposed omnibus may move both. 🟡 **Quote obligations, not dates; put dates in change control.**

### 🟢 `TR234-2` — the supply trend, newly visible: the permissive platforms in this industry are **public-sector**, not commercial

🔵 **p234 promoted the first two permissive education platforms into this KB's platform tier *(one new, one carried from `p209` and never reconciled)*. Neither is a startup, and the pattern is worth naming because it tells a later pass where to look.**

| row | who holds the copyright | what that predicts |
|---|---|---|
| `svws-nrw/SVWS-Server` *(`BSD-3-Clause`)* | 🟢 **a state ministry** — Ministerium für Schule und Bildung des Landes NRW | 🟢 permissive BY POLICY: a public body publishing publicly funded software has no incentive to copyleft it and a legal reason not to encumber it |
| `openolat/OpenOLAT` *(`Apache-2.0`)* | 🟢 a company serving institutions *(frentix GmbH)* funded by an institutional membership model | 🟢 permissive BY BUSINESS MODEL: revenue is services and membership, so the licence is not the moat |
| Ed-Fi *(`Apache-2.0`)* | 🟢 a US standards alliance | 🟢 the same shape, already on these shelves |
| 🔴 the copyleft tier — Moodle, TAO, Gibbon, Frappe, OpenEduCat | 🔴 vendors and foundations with commercial editions | 🔴 copyleft IS the moat: the licence protects a paid edition |

🟢 **The predictive rule: in education, permissive supply correlates with PUBLIC OR INSTITUTIONAL funding, and copyleft correlates with a commercial edition.** 🔵 **So a pass hunting permissive platforms should search ministries, state education agencies, standards bodies and public universities — not GitHub trending.** 🔴 **And `T234-B` explains why that search is hard: an institutional copyright block is exactly what breaks GitHub's licence detector, so the public-sector rows are the ones a licence-field filter drops.**

### 🟡 `TR234-3` — the industry-level trends, carried with their sourcing flagged

🔵 **From the mandated trends query. Sourcing is mostly vendor blogs and market-research pages; this shelf keeps them because the DIRECTION is consistent across independent sources even where the NUMBERS are not.**

- 🟢 **Experimentation → operations.** AI moving into repeatable workflows *(lesson prep, formative feedback)* with institutional review for accuracy, privacy and integrity. 🔵 Consistent with the governance figures: adoption is ahead of policy everywhere measured.
- 🟢 **Education-specific platforms over generic chatbots.** Described by one outlook as *"the defining trend"*. 🟢 **Corroborated independently by this pass's own supply finding: `HKUDS/DeepTutor` at `41.5k` stars is an education-specific agent workspace, not a chat wrapper, and it is the largest permissive row in the vertical.**
- 🟢 **Agents that plan teaching sequences and draft assessments**, plus adaptive difficulty. 🔴 **Flagged against reality: `AI-Teaching-Agent`'s own README says grading productisation is FROZEN.** The market narrative is ahead of the open-source implementations.
- 🟢 **Governance and institutional AI strategy** as a first-class programme *(1EdTech)*. 🟢 **This is the LATAM arbitrage in `intel/market.md`, independently stated.**
- 🟡 **Digital credentials and skills-based ecosystems** accelerating. 🔵 Ties to the Open Badges rows p233 recovered — 🔴 which are `MPL-2.0` *(weak copyleft, per-file source obligation)*, so credential issuance is NOT in the permissive tier.
- 🔴 **Market size: `$7.52B` (2025) → `$10.6B` (2026) → `$42.48B` (2030) claimed.** One press release, quadrupling in four years. 🟡 **Carried as an order of magnitude and a direction only. Do not put this number in a client deck without a primary source.**
- 🔴 **Refused: *"83% of institutions plan AI teaching assistants."*** A vendor blog with no primary source. Not carried.

### 🟢 `TR234-4` — a measurement trend inside this KB, worth recording as a trend in its own right

🔵 **Three consecutive passes have each found that a CHANNEL, not the field, produced a published absence:**

> p232: **a `200` is not a READ** — PyPI answers `200` with a bot challenge.
> p233: **a `404` is not an ABSENCE** — repo1.maven.org answers `404` when throttling.
> p234: **a NAME is not an ARTEFACT** — a groupId prefix is not the project it resembles; and a platform's LICENCE FIELD is not its grant.

🔴 **The cumulative lesson: this KB's published absences have a worse track record than its published presences.** Every one of the three passes above overturned prior cells, and in each case the error was in the direction of **claiming something was not there**. 🔴 **The nine-pass *"no permissive LMS or SIS"* finding is the largest instance, and the worst, because it is the one case where no new evidence was needed: `p209` had grant-read the counter-example and `p210` had measured the defect that hid its sibling class.** 🟡 **An absence can be wrong because a channel lied *(p232, p233, p234)* — or because the KB did not read itself. The second kind is cheaper to fix and this KB has never had a control for it until `ACTION UG`.**

🟡 **So the standing correction to method, and it costs nothing: a negative finding needs a channel control before it is published, and a licence finding needs the grant BODY. `ACTION UF` registers the forward version of this — every pass must name the control that makes each of its declared absences establishable.**

## Pass 233 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND IS A **CHANNEL** TREND: EVERY ROW THIS PASS CAME FROM A REGISTRY TARBALL, A MAVEN LISTING OR A TASK-SHAPED QUERY, AND **NONE** FROM A TRENDING LIST

⏱️ **SECOND pass of `2026-10-16`.**

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p233-queries`](../evidence/p233-queries.2026-10-16-p233.tsv) · [`p233-tp-tarball-recovery`](../evidence/p233-tp-tarball-recovery.2026-10-16-p233.tsv) · [`p233-maven-channel`](../evidence/p233-maven-channel.2026-10-16-p233.tsv) · [`p233-sh-aiact`](../evidence/p233-sh-aiact.2026-10-16-p233.tsv)

### 🟢 `T233-1` — the standing trend, now with a sixth data point: THE CHANNEL IS THE FINDING

| channel | passes run | granted rows yielded |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 region-shaped query *(×4 regions)* | 6 consecutive | **`0`** |
| 🔴 industry-shaped query *(trending, "top agents")* | p213-p222, p231-p233 | **`0`** |
| 🟢 **task / capability / standard-shaped query** | p231, p232, p233 | **`7` + `9` + `1`** |
| 🆕 🟢 **package-registry search** *(p232)* | 1 | **`9`** |
| 🆕 🟢 **registry TARBALL unpack** *(p233)* | 1 | **`3` recovered + `2` source-only** |
| 🆕 🟢 **package-repository directory listing** *(p233, Maven)* | 1 | `4 798` groupIds classified, `1` lead |

🔵 **p132 learned the substitution once — the SIS tier was invisible until a probe named a CAPABILITY. A hundred passes later it is still the only thing that moves the row count, and the 2026-10 passes have extended it from the QUERY to the CHANNEL.** 🟢 **The generalisation: the supply in this industry is indexed by what it IMPLEMENTS, not by what it is ABOUT, and the places that index implementations are package registries and artefact repositories.**

### 🔴 `T233-2` — the new instrument rule, and it is the exact mirror of p232's

🔵 **p232: a `200` is not a READ** — PyPI answers `200` with a bot-challenge body.
🔵 **p233: a `404` is not an ABSENCE** — `repo1.maven.org` answers `404`, with a one-byte body, when throttling.

🔴 **Measured: `200`, `404` and `429` from the SAME url inside one minute. The throttle-`404` is byte-identical to a real miss, and the negative control exhausts exactly as the positive control does.** 🟢 **The durable consequence: on a rate-limited channel, PRESENCE is provable and ABSENCE is not.** Every Maven verdict here reads `PRESENT` or `NOT-ESTABLISHED`.

🟢 **And the discipline that caught it: a whole paced instrument was KILLED UNPUBLISHED because its own positive control failed.** 🔵 **Seventeen paths would have been tabled as seventeen absences. Believe an instrument's silence only after a known positive — p230's lint lesson, reproduced in a different medium.**

### 🔴 `T233-3` — availability and adoptability came apart in p232, and p233 partly puts them back

🟢 **p232's "`6` live, granted, SOURCELESS packages" is now `1`.** Three have live repositories; two more ship complete `src/` in the tarball. 🔴 **The defect was this KB's probe — the `-v4`/`-v2` suffixes dropped from names that contain them, and the package's own name never tried as the repository name.**

🟢 **The trend that survives, and it is the real one:** the publishing layer and the source layer in this field are **loosely coupled**. A package's registry metadata, its tarball metadata and its repository can all disagree, and the tarball is the most reliable of the three **because it ships with the code**. 🔴 **The `@timeback/*` family — `caliper`, `oneroster`, `qti`, `53`/`58`/`59` versions through `2026-09-25`, no licence, no repository, no homepage — remains the pure form: actively maintained, genuinely useful, unadoptable.**

### 🔴 `T233-4` — the JVM is where this industry's past lives, and it is copyleft

🟢 **Measured from coordinates rather than inferred: `4 798` groupIds under `org.`, and the education estate is exactly the historic Java tier** — Apereo/Jasig, Sakai, DSpace, Opencast, OLAT, FenixEdu, Kuali. **All copyleft or consortium-governed.**

🔴 **The standards body holds ONE JVM artefact: `basiclti-util/`, LTI 1.1-era.** No Caliper, no LTI 1.3, no QTI, no OneRoster, and `org.1edtech` does not exist. 🟢 **Independent corroboration of p230's existence sweep, from a channel that cannot be accused of GitHub bias.**

🔵 **Two rename artefacts, same shape, recorded as a pattern rather than two facts: `org.olat` publishes and `org.openolat` does not; `org.imsglobal` publishes and `org.1edtech` does not. A project renames its identity far faster than its coordinates** — and p230 found the same lag in repository addresses. 🟢 **Practical rule for a build file: use the OLD groupId.**

### 🟡 `T233-5` — the identity-validation trend: three libraries, three different wrongs

🟢 **`lti-tool/lti-tool` rejects a launch with no `sub`** *(schema-required, parsed before the audience and nonce checks)* — **the reference implementation for the identity tier.** 🔴 **`peregrine-lti` and `lti-go` accept one.**

🔴 **The trend worth naming: all three accept `sub: ""`.** peregrine has a length check short-circuited by `!= ""`; `lti-tool`'s `z.string()` has no `.min(1)`; `lti-go` never looks. 🟢 **So the field's convergent defect is not *"nobody validates the subject"* — it is *"everybody conflates ABSENT with EMPTY"*, and in LTI those mean different things because anonymous launch is a legitimate case.** 🔵 **peregrine's doc comment proves the intent is deliberate. The gap is that the caller cannot tell *deliberately anonymous* from *never checked*.**

### 🔴 `T233-6` — the regulatory trend: the education cliff moved, and the transparency floor did not

🟢 **`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744` postponed Annex III point `3` — education — to `2027-12-02`.** 🔴 **Article `50` transparency was NOT postponed and is live since `2026-08-02`; the Article `5` school emotion-recognition ban has been live since `2025-02-02`.**

🔵 **The trend inside the trend: the error keeps reproducing because `2026-08-02` IS a real AI Act date and IS when most of the regulation applied.** A writer who summarises the Act correctly in general gets education wrong in particular — which is why `ACTION SH` stays standing and has now paid out twice. 🔴 **Every date on these shelves is CORROBORATED-SECONDARY. No pass has yet read the Official Journal.**


## Pass 232 — 2026-10-16 — THE TREND IS THAT THIS INDUSTRY'S CODE SHIPS TO **PACKAGE REGISTRIES**, AND A REGISTRY RECORD OUTLIVES THE REPOSITORY IT NAMES

🔵 **The nineteenth consecutive unit-mismatch headline, and this pass's unit is the ARTEFACT versus the REPOSITORY.** 🟢 **`146` artefacts classified across `4` registries; `9` granted rows the repository-shaped harvester had never surfaced; `6` granted packages whose repositories do not exist.**

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p232-queries`](../evidence/p232-queries.2026-10-16-p232.tsv) · [`p232-dead-registry-addresses`](../evidence/p232-dead-registry-addresses.2026-10-16-p232.tsv) · [`p232-ungranted-family`](../evidence/p232-ungranted-family.2026-10-16-p232.tsv) · [`p232-tk-source-read`](../evidence/p232-tk-source-read.2026-10-16-p232.tsv)

### 🟢 `T232-1` — the structural trend: education-standard code is DISTRIBUTED, not starred

🔵 **For nine passes this KB has measured a field that looked thin, using queries that look for repositories.** 🟢 **The field is not thin — it is PACKAGED.** The LTI, QTI, OneRoster, Caliper, xAPI and Open Badges tiers publish as installable artefacts on `npm`, Packagist, `pkg.go.dev` and PyPI, where they are found by the integrators who need them and never appear in a trending list or a listicle.

🟡 **Why the distinction is load-bearing and not a technicality: the registry carries the two facts a proposal actually needs — the RELEASE HISTORY and the LAST PUBLISH DATE — and GitHub stars carry neither.** `npm:caccl` has **`271` versions across eight years**; `npm:@lti-tool/core` has `30` versions and was modified `2026-10-09`; the `oat-sa` assessment estate released through `2026-10-15`. 🔵 **A maintenance signal that survives a quiet quarter is worth more than a star count, and these shelves now have one.**

### 🔴 `T232-2` — the counter-trend, and it is a procurement risk: SUPPLY WITHOUT PROVENANCE

🔴 **The same channel that reveals the supply reveals a failure mode the repository channel cannot see: a package that is live, granted, actively released, and whose source cannot be retrieved.** `6` of `15` registry-sourced addresses probed this pass are `404` — including the `MPL-2.0` Open Badges bakery and validator, which are the published successors to the Mozilla tooling `ACTION TE` found withdrawn in p231.

🔴 **And p231's ungranted one-off is a FAMILY:** `@timeback/caliper`, `@timeback/oneroster` and `@timeback/qti` — `53`, `58` and `59` versions, all released through `2026-09-25`, **all three with `license` NONE, `repository` NONE and `homepage` NONE.** 🟡 **Three 1EdTech standards covered by one publisher, actively maintained, and legally unadoptable.**

🔵 **The trend worth carrying into every engagement: in this industry, AVAILABILITY AND ADOPTABILITY HAVE COME APART.** 🟢 **The billable consequence is that a licence-and-provenance gate over a dependency list is no longer hygiene, it is the deliverable that prevents a build on an address that will not exist at handover.**

### 🔴 `T232-3` — the instrument trend this KB keeps re-learning: a `200` IS NOT A READ

🔴 **PyPI's search endpoint answers `200` with a `3 038`-byte *"Client Challenge"* body and zero results.** A sweep keyed on the status code would have published *"PyPI returns nothing for `oneroster`"* — a false absence that would have been indistinguishable from a measured one. 🟡 **Maven Central is the honest opposite: `000` on both search endpoints, so the JVM education estate is recorded as `UNMEASURED`, not as absent.**

🔵 **Added to the standing list of ways this KB's instruments have lied to it:** a case-sensitive `raw` path *(p231, `3.2%` false absence)*, a name quantifier whose terminator class contained `.` *(p230)*, a TLD alternation matching `.co` inside `.com` *(this pass)*, and now a challenge page served as `200`. 🟢 **Four of the four were caught by a control, and the control is always the same: run a probe whose answer you already know, in the same run.**

### 🟡 `T232-4` — the identity trend: every launch library must be read before it is trusted

🔴 **`ACTION TK` read `marcgabe15/lti-go` in source and found that `sub` is parsed and exposed but NEVER VALIDATED** *(`launch.go:170-206`)*, and that the `ags` write performs correct scope ACQUISITION but no scope CHECK *(`ags/client.go:55-72`)*. 🔵 **p135 found the same shape one layer up: both existing MCP bridges discard the verified identity and trust a caller-supplied subject.**

🟢 **The generalisation, now supported three times over: in this field, the identity guarantee is always assumed by the layer above and never asserted by the layer below.** 🔴 **p136's record is three for three — every tier this KB has read in source has overturned a published cell.** 🟡 **The practice that follows: in a standards integration, the adapter owns the identity guard, and a library's README is never the evidence that it has one.**

## Pass 231 — 2026-10-15 — THE TREND THAT MATTERS THIS PASS IS NOT IN THE FIELD, IT IS IN THE **INSTRUMENT**: EIGHT ZERO PASSES WERE A QUERY ARTEFACT

🔵 **`4` trends, `1` of them a correction to how this KB reads its own saturation.**

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p231-regional-queries`](../evidence/p231-regional-queries.2026-10-15-p231.tsv) · [`te-successor-hunt`](../evidence/te-successor-hunt.2026-10-15-p231.tsv) · [`p231-new-rows`](../evidence/p231-new-rows.2026-10-15-p231.tsv)

### 🔴 `T231-1` — SATURATION WAS A PROPERTY OF THE QUERY. `8` zero passes, then `7` rows from `4` probes

🟢 **p223–p230 reported a measured discovery rate of zero, eight times, and published it as a finding about the field.** 🔴 **It was a finding about the question.** Four capability-shaped probes — two naming a STANDARD *(`QTI 3.0`, `LTI Advantage AGS`)*, two naming a TASK *(`AI text detector academic integrity`, `automated essay scoring rubric`)* — returned `14` + `14` repositories and **`7` granted rows**, against `0` from four region-shaped and two industry-shaped probes.

🔵 **p132 established this exact substitution once before: the SIS tier was invisible to *"student information system"* and visible to *"timetable"*, because the field is named after platforms and tasks rather than after the analyst's category.** 🟢 **The generalisable rule, and it is cheap to apply: when the row count hits zero, change the SHAPE of the query before concluding anything about supply. An industry name is the weakest probe available; a standard's name is the strongest.**

### 🟢 `T231-2` — ASSESSMENT is where education's AI regulation actually bites, and the open-source supply has just caught up

🔵 **Three jurisdictions, one surface.** EU Annex III point `3` names *admissions, assessing learning outcomes, assigning level, monitoring during tests*. Vietnam's Decision `33` names *systems that automatically test, evaluate results and rank learners*. Oklahoma and Maryland bar AI from *high-stakes decisions about a student*. 🟢 **That is the same component described three times, and it is the one every LMS engagement touches.**

🟢 **As of this pass the open-source answer exists and is `MIT` end to end:** a conformance JUDGE for the item bank *([`feyst/qti3-validator`](https://github.com/feyst/qti3-validator))* and a HUMAN-DECISION surface for the score *([`metyatech/qti-scoring-system`](https://github.com/metyatech/qti-scoring-system))*, the second writing its record into the standard's own `COMMENT` outcomes. 🔵 **The trend reading: the compliance artefact these three regimes demand is no longer bespoke, and the human-review carve-out in Vietnam's own text makes the oversight surface the cheapest route out of the high-risk category rather than an overhead.**

### 🔴 `T231-3` — THE REFERENCE-IMPLEMENTATION TIER IS DEPRECIATING, AND THE LIVE SUPPLY IS MOVING TO CHANNELS A REPOSITORY HARVESTER CANNOT SEE

🔴 **Four Caliper sensors and the Open Badges reference server are withdrawn — confirmed on two independent channels, with a live positive control.** 🟡 **The specs and a worked example survive; Open Badges still has a validator.**

🔵 **The sharper half is where the live code went.** `npm:@timeback/caliper` has **`53` versions** and shipped `v0.3.3` on `2026-09-25` — with **no licence, no repository and no readme**. `npm:caliper` is `MIT` and six years stale, on **GitLab**. 🔴 **So the learning-analytics standard reads, on GitHub, as having no supply, and on the registry channel as having maintained-but-unadoptable supply.** 🟢 **The trend: `ACTION PD`'s rule — *every channel that works here is a package or service endpoint* — is now a DISCOVERY rule and not only an access workaround. A KB that harvests repositories will progressively under-read a field that publishes artefacts.**

### 🟡 `T231-4` — THE INTEGRITY MARKET IS MOVING FROM DETECTION TO **PROVENANCE**, and the credible projects are the ones that say so

🔵 **Both integrity rows added this pass disclaim authorship attribution in their own READMEs** — *"never presents an AI score as proof of authorship"*, *"a score cannot settle a misconduct allegation"*, *"no accuracy claims are made"* — and the more developed of the two leads with **C2PA Content Credentials, hidden-Unicode forensics and a SynthID-Text lab**, with the classifier as one check among several.

🟢 **Read that as the market signal it is: the buyer's question is shifting from *"was this AI-written?"* to *"what is the record of how this was written?"* — a question that has an auditable answer.** 🔵 **It also converges with EU Article `50(2)` marking, in force since `2026-08-02`, which asks for machine-readable provenance rather than detection.** 🔴 **The counter-trend to price in: every detector on these shelves is now explicitly non-evidentiary, so a studio that ships one as a verdict is selling a dispute, and an institution that buys one has bought an appeals process.**

## Pass 230 — 2026-10-15 — THE EIGHTEENTH CONSECUTIVE UNIT-MISMATCH HEADLINE, AND THE UNIT IS A **RECORDED PATH**

🔵 **Evidence:** [`ta-prose-path-ladder`](../evidence/ta-prose-path-ladder.2026-10-15-p230.tsv) · [`tb-lazy-quantifier-lint`](../evidence/tb-lazy-quantifier-lint.2026-10-15-p230.tsv) · [`tc-existence-sweep`](../evidence/tc-existence-sweep.2026-10-15-p230.tsv) · [`td-fork-direction`](../evidence/td-fork-direction.2026-10-15-p230.tsv)

### 🔴 `T230-T1` — the unit of a RECORDED PATH is a READING, not a GRANT

🟢 **Seventeen passes running, the headline here has been a unit mismatch: a licence's unit is a FILE, an alias's unit is a REPOSITORY SET, a bound's unit is an ECOSYSTEM, a claim's unit is a `(query, record)` pair. p230 is the eighteenth and the unit is the humblest yet.**

🔴 **`ACTION TA` reasoned that if these shelves record the path at which a grant was read, that path list beats any vocabulary. The harvest was right. The INFERENCE was a unit error:**

> 🔴 **A recorded path is a path at which SOMEBODY READ A LICENCE.
> 🟢 A grant is a path at which THIS REPOSITORY GRANTS.
> These are different objects, and `2` of `2` hits this pass are the first kind.**

🔵 **`MIT-0` under `AmazonBedrock/LICENSE` in an Anthropic repository is Amazon's grant over a subdirectory. `LGPL-2.1` under `phpmailer/LICENSE` in a school management system is PHPMailer's grant over a vendored copy. Both are real, both are first-hand, both are correctly parsed, and NEITHER is the repository's.** 🟢 **Subjects `8` and `9` in the enumeration p228 started at five.**

🟢 **And the discriminator falls out of the same read: COMPARE THE COPYRIGHT HOLDER WITH THE SLUG'S OWNER. `T229-M2` does this for a manifest's `name` field; it works on a licence body's third line, it costs nothing extra, and it would have caught both of this pass's false positives before they were written down.**

### 🔴 `T230-T2` — the lazy quantifier is now a MEASURED class, not an anecdote, and the lint for it was itself truncated

🟢 **p229 found the same defective shape in two instruments in one pass and asked whether it was systemic. `ACTION TB` built the test: expand the quantified character class and the terminator lookahead class, and INTERSECT them. A non-empty intersection means the instrument can stop inside its own subject.**

🔵 **`51` instruments in `compose/code` use a lazy quantifier. `49` are perfectly safe — a grep for `*?` is NOT this lint, which is why eleven passes of eyeballing found nothing.** 🔴 **`2` are defective, both on the character `.`:**

| instrument | status | what the truncation costs |
|---|---|---|
| `p228-shelf-gate/harvest_cells.py:17` | 🟡 already registered as `ACTION TD` | `31` names mangled, `24` never harvested |
| 🆕 🔴 `p129-transcription-audit/bind_tags.py:9` | 🔴 **NEW — not previously known** | it binds every published *"N tags"* FIGURE to an ADDRESS across all ten live shelves, `README` included. A truncated address binds a real figure to a repository that does not exist |

🔴 **And the finding worth carrying beyond this KB: THE FIRST DRAFT OF THE LINT WAS ITSELF TRUNCATED BY THE SAME FAMILY OF DEFECT, ON THE SAME CHARACTER CLASS IT HUNTS.** 🟢 **It captured a lookahead's body with `([^)]*)`. The terminator class of the very defect it is built to find BEGINS with `)`. So the draft read nothing, reported `0` findings across `326` instruments, and reported `0` on its own positive control — the only reason it was caught.** 🔵 **`T230-6`. A lint must be run against a KNOWN POSITIVE before its silence is believed, and this pass is the demonstration.**

### 🟡 `T230-T3` — churn is a measurable property of a knowledge base, and it is `1.4%`

🟢 **The first existence sweep here: `1 904` addresses, `1 862` live, `26` real absences — `1.4%` of `1 888` real slugs.** 🔵 **That is the number a reader needs to interpret every other table on these shelves, and no pass before this one could state it.**

🔴 **The churn is not uniform, which is the trend:**
- 🔴 **`8` of the `26` are single-author MCP servers** — the tier this KB has repeatedly flagged as having no institution behind it. They are the fastest-appearing and the fastest-disappearing rows here.
- 🔴 **`5` are STANDARDS reference implementations** *(Caliper ×4, Open Badges)* — the slowest-moving tier, and the most load-bearing. Standards bodies archive; hobbyists delete; both end as a `404`.
- 🟢 **`1` of the `26` is a MOVE, proven** *(`apereo/opencast` → `opencast/opencast`)*. 🟡 **`MOVED` and `GONE` are indistinguishable in a dead-link table and completely different in a proposal, and separating them costs one extra probe per row.**

🔵 **Standing rule proposed and registered as `ACTION TF`: every trending row carries a LAST-SEEN date, and the sweep runs each pass. One request per address is the cheapest instrument this KB has ever built, and it is the only one that can tell a reader whether a four-pass-old row still exists.**

## Pass 229 — 2026-10-15 — THE SEVENTEENTH CONSECUTIVE UNIT-MISMATCH HEADLINE, AND THIS ONE IS A CHARACTER CLASS

🔵 **Evidence:** [`sw-slug-truncation`](../evidence/sw-slug-truncation.2026-10-15-p229.tsv) · [`sx-withdrawn-gate`](../evidence/sx-withdrawn-gate.2026-10-15-p229.tsv) · [`p229-regional-queries`](../evidence/p229-regional-queries.2026-10-15-p229.tsv)

### 🔴 `T229-T1` — the lazy quantifier is this base's recurring defect SHAPE, and it appeared twice in one pass

🟢 **p228 was the sixteenth consecutive pass whose headline was a unit mismatch. p229 is the seventeenth, and for the first time the mismatch is in a CHARACTER CLASS rather than in a concept:**

| instrument | pattern | what it was supposed to bound | what it actually bounded |
|---|---|---|---|
| `harvest_cells.SLUG_RE` | `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]*?` + a terminator class containing `.` | the repository NAME | 🔴 **the name up to its first dot** |
| `manifest_grant` draft | `.{0,120}?(any later version)?` | the window after the version | 🔴 **the empty string at the first match** |

🟢 **One rule covers both, and it is now written down: a LAZY quantifier against a PERMISSIVE terminator always stops early. If the thing you are matching may CONTAIN a terminator character, the quantifier cannot be lazy and the terminator cannot be optional.**

🔵 **Both were found in one pass because the second was looked for after the first — the shape, once named, is cheap to sweep for** → `ACTION TB`.

### 🟢 `T229-T2` — a correction marker is not a hiding place, and the gate that doubted it was wrong fifteen times

🔴 **`ACTION SX` was registered on the premise that a `<!--corrected-->` marker makes a cell invisible to the gate built to check it. The premise was exactly right about the MECHANISM — `NARRATIVE_RE` matches the word `corrected`, so the marker trips the pattern that suppresses it — and exactly wrong about the RISK.**

🟢 **`54` cells gated, `0` corrected values wrong.** The gate's first draft produced `24` findings, the positional fix cut them to `10`, and reading all ten on their lines left `0`.

🟡 **`AUDIT-ROW-STRUCK-VALUE` enters the subject vocabulary as the SIXTH member of p228's enumeration, and `FOREIGN-MANIFEST` as the seventh:**

| # | subject class | the token is… |
|---|---|---|
| 1–5 | *(p228)* `DIRECTORY-SCOPED` · `BODY-CONTENT` · `CORRECTION-NARRATIVE` · `QUOTED-EXTERNAL-CLAIM` · `UNLINKED-CO-SUBJECT` | |
| 🆕 6 | **`AUDIT-ROW-STRUCK-VALUE`** | the WRONG value a defect table exists to display beside its correction |
| 🆕 7 | **`FOREIGN-MANIFEST`** | a real grant belonging to the UPSTREAM project whose manifest this repo serves |

### 🔴 `T229-T3` — adoption still outruns governance, and the North American number is now the sharpest

🟢 **`86%` of students across `16` countries use AI; `10%` of institutions have a formal AI policy; `71%` of US teachers have had no training.** 🟡 **Every region measured on these shelves inverts the same way except EMEA, where a dated statutory clock runs ahead of any measured adoption figure at all.**

🟢 **So the 2026 deliverable is unchanged and is now three-for-three across passes: an AI-use policy with a monitoring surface and an evaluation loop — except in EMEA, where it is conformity against a date.** 🔵 **What p229 adds is the supply side: the Moodle plugin tier that implements exactly that policy surface is `GPL-3.0-or-later`, read first-hand, and may enter a GPL-3.0 host without the incompatibility that excludes the assessment tier.**

### 🟡 `T229-T4` — a dead slug is a trend signal, not just a broken link

🔴 **`6` of `45` probed slugs do not resolve at all, and `2` of those are neither typos nor truncations** — [`jakerains/scormplayer`](https://github.com/jakerains/scormplayer) and [`joojdev/academic-tracker-mcp`](https://github.com/joojdev/academic-tracker-mcp). 🟡 **Both are small, recent, single-author education MCP/SCORM projects. The churn rate at the bottom of this shelf is real, and a trending table that never re-probes existence accumulates ghosts** → `ACTION TC`.

## Pass 228 — 2026-10-15 — THE TREND IS THAT **GOVERNANCE COVERAGE IS NOW THE MEASURABLE DEFICIT EVERYWHERE**, AND FOUR INDEPENDENT JURISDICTIONS PUT A NUMBER ON IT IN THE SAME QUARTER

🔵 **`4` trends, each with the measurement that makes it a trend rather than a theme.**

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p228-regional-queries`](../evidence/p228-regional-queries.2026-10-15-p228.tsv) · [`sq-europe-queries`](../evidence/sq-europe-queries.2026-10-15-p228.tsv) · [`sr-discovery-channel`](../evidence/sr-discovery-channel.2026-10-15-p228.tsv) · [`sh-inbound-gate-run`](../evidence/sh-inbound-gate-run.2026-10-15-p228.tsv)

### 🔴 `T228-1` — the adoption-to-governance gap is now QUANTIFIED in four jurisdictions, and it is the same gap each time

🔵 **p227 recorded the turn to *"governed adoption"* from the instruments. p228 can measure the deficit those instruments are aimed at, because four unrelated sources published a coverage figure this quarter:**

| jurisdiction | individuals adopting | institutions governing | gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **LATAM** | `92%` of students, `79%` of faculty | 🔴 **`30%` of universities have published AI policies** | **`62` points** |
| 🔴 **North America** | — | 🔴 **`10%` of institutions have formal AI guidelines**; `71%` of teachers untrained | **the widest absolute** |
| 🟡 **UK** | `44%` of teachers using generative AI | 🔴 **~`50%` of schools have no AI policy at all** | ~`-6` points |
| 🟡 **global** | `92%` student use *(secondary)* | 🔴 **maturity rated `35/100`, *"most institutions still in pilot mode"*** | — |

🟢 **The trend is not *"AI adoption is growing"* — that has been true for four years. It is that the DEFICIT has become a published number, from four independent directions, within one quarter.** 🔵 **A number is procurable in a way a theme is not: a buyer who has read that `90%` of peer institutions lack a guideline can justify a line item.**

🟡 **The UK is the instructive outlier and should be sold differently: at `44%` teacher use against `50%` of schools with no policy, its gap is nearly CLOSED by comparison — and it is the only jurisdiction on these shelves with a supplier-facing PRODUCT SAFETY STANDARD *(DfE, `2026-01-19`)* to conform to.** 🟢 **So EMEA's UK play is conformance evidence; LATAM's and North America's is the policy itself.**

### 🔴 `T228-2` — assessment stays the convergent surface, and a FOURTH independent jurisdiction singled it out

🟢 **p227 found assessment flagged by the EU and Vietnam and distrusted by LATAM faculty. p228 adds two more, from queries that had nothing else in common:**

| direction | what it says about assessment |
|---|---|
| 🔴 **EU** | Annex III covers tools that evaluate learners or gate access to qualifications |
| 🔴 **Vietnam** | Decision `33/2026/QD-TTg` classifies *"systems that automatically test, evaluate results and rank learners"* as high-risk |
| 🔴 **LATAM** | faculty's LOWEST uptake is assessment — detecting cheating, generating feedback; `61%` of students fear peer misuse |
| 🆕 🟡 **Netherlands** | a Utrecht seminar on the AI Act raises **AI use in grading tests** as the named risk area |
| 🆕 🟡 **UK** | the DfE route permits AI marking only for **low-stakes** quizzes, **with results checked by a human** |

🔵 **Five jurisdictions, two of them statutory, and all five land on the same boundary: an AI score is acceptable when a human remains answerable for it.** 🟢 **The UK formulation is the most useful because it is operational rather than principled — *low stakes* plus *human check* is a specification, not a value.**

🔴 **And the supply-side gap these shelves have published since p213 is UNCLOSED for a fifteenth pass.** 🟢 **This pass searched for it directly** — `open source AI grading agreement teacher scores rubric github 2026` — 🔴 **and found no open-source agreement study and no agreement template.** 🟡 **What exists is `GradAssist` *(EDULEARN 2026)*, which drafts rubric-aligned feedback for an instructor to finalise, and a vendor claim of *"correlations above `0.90` with human raters"* that carries no methodology and is NOT carried here.**

> 🟢 **The asset that answers all five jurisdictions at once is still the same one, and still nobody's: a grader that reports its own DRIFT against teacher scores.**

### 🟢 `T228-3` — provenance moved from a quality claim to a CONFORMANCE ARTEFACT, and the EU set its date

🔵 **p227's trend was that provenance became a reason a buyer REJECTS work, proved by South Africa withdrawing a national AI policy over fabricated citations.** 🟢 **p228's addition is that the same requirement now has a deadline and a form.**

🔵 **The three-part structure p227 identified as portable — a risk classification, a human-oversight record, a data-provenance statement — maps onto the dates this way:**

| date | what comes into effect | which of the three it needs |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **`2026-12-02`** | EU Omnibus Article `5` prohibitions | risk classification |
| 🔴 **`2027-09-01`** | Vietnam's education compliance deadline | all three |
| 🔴 **`2027-12-02`** | EU AI Act Annex III education high-risk | all three |

🟡 **`7` weeks to the first of them.** 🔴 **The EU date most proposals will quote is `2027-12-02`; the one that binds first is `2026-12-02`, and it is a PROHIBITION rather than an obligation — there is no conformance path, only a stop.**

### 🟡 `T228-4` — the open-source education agent shelf has reached DISCOVERY SATURATION, and that is a trend about the supply side

🔴 **Five consecutive passes in which the mandated `github trending` query returned ZERO education-specific repositories.** 🟢 **p228 tested `ACTION SR`'s remedy — use-case and language-shaped queries instead — and it returned `0` new rows too: `5` of `5` verifiable candidates were already carried, and the only uncarried slug has no grant at all** → [`sr-discovery-channel`](../evidence/sr-discovery-channel.2026-10-15-p228.tsv).

🔵 **So the supply-side reading, stated as a measurement rather than a complaint: the discoverable open-source education-AI surface is SMALL and this KB has most of it.** 🟢 **`520` distinct slugs carried, `468` grants read first-hand, `335` of them permissively licensed. What this shelf lacks is not more repositories — it is depth on the ones it has.**

🟡 **Which reframes where a studio's advantage comes from in this industry. 🔴 In a saturated supply, the differentiator is not knowing WHICH repos exist — any competitor's search finds the same `520` — it is knowing what their grants actually say.** 🟢 **p228 found `3` wrong licence cells in its own shelves after `227` passes of care; a competitor working from search results has not checked at all. 🔵 That is why `ACTION ST` belongs in `make hooks` as a standing step rather than a one-off cleanup.**

### 🔵 What this pass does NOT claim

🔴 **Market sizing does not converge and this pass makes it worse rather than better: a North American series at `USD 951M` (2024) → `USD 2 303.2M` (2029) cannot be reconciled with a `36%` North American share of a `USD 10.6bn` global 2026 market. Both are published, neither is merged** → [`intel/market.md`](market.md) `P228-M2`.

🔴 **`USD 169M` in US federal higher-ed AI funding: single secondary source, no primary — NOT carried.** 🔴 **A UK July-2026 teacher-marking guidance item could not be confirmed against gov.uk and is published as unverified, not as fact.** 🔴 **A vendor claim of `>0.90` grader-human correlation has no methodology and is NOT carried.** 🟡 **Sweden's nationwide *"AI Hub"* and free quality-tested tools come from a third-party summary of SOU 2025:12 and are marked `SNIPPET-ONLY`.**

🟢 **No government or primary host was readable this pass either — `ACTION RO` is structural, not unperformed work.** 🔵 **And the four separate LATAM denominators now on these shelves *(IESALC `200`/`19`; DEC `30 000`+/`29`; the `30%` policy figure; the truncated `19%`)* must not be merged into one.**


## Pass 227 — 2026-10-15 — THE TREND WORTH CARRYING IS THAT **PROVENANCE BECAME A PROCUREMENT REQUIREMENT**, AND THE PROOF IS A NATIONAL AI POLICY WITHDRAWN FOR CITING SOURCES THAT DO NOT EXIST

🔵 **`4` trends, each with the evidence that makes it a trend rather than a theme.**

🔵 **Evidence:** [`sm-za-withdrawal-divergence`](../evidence/sm-za-withdrawal-divergence.2026-10-15-p227.tsv) · [`sl-vietnam-instruments`](../evidence/sl-vietnam-instruments.2026-10-15-p227.tsv) · [`sm-africa-queries`](../evidence/sm-africa-queries.2026-10-15-p227.tsv) · [`p227-regional-queries`](../evidence/p227-regional-queries.2026-10-15-p227.tsv)

### 🔴 `T227-1` — provenance is now a reason a buyer REJECTS work, not a reason they prefer it

🔴 **South Africa withdrew its Draft National AI Policy 2026 because the reference list cited non-existent sources — described by one source class as *"fake, AI-generated citations"*.** 🟡 **The withdrawal date diverges across sources *(`2026-06-12` vs `2026-04-26`)* and is published here as both, with provenance.**

🟢 **This is the first instance on these shelves of a GOVERNMENT-level AI deliverable rejected specifically for unverifiable citations, and it changes the sales argument.** 🔵 **Until now "we read every source first-hand" was a quality claim a studio made about itself. 🔴 It is now a procurement risk a client has watched a national ministry get burned by.** 🟡 **Concretely: in any African or EMEA public-sector education engagement, an auditable citation trail is a deliverable line item, not a methodology slide.**

### 🟢 `T227-2` — the governed-adoption turn is now visible in STATUTE, not just in commentary

🔵 **Every regional source this pass converges on the same shift — 2026 is the year education AI moved from experimentation to governed adoption: governance structures, compliance expectations, evaluation processes, demands for evidence of learning gains.** 🟢 **What makes it a trend rather than a theme is that the instruments now exist and carry dates:**

| jurisdiction | instrument | the date that binds |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **Vietnam** | Law `134/2025/QH15` + Decision `33/2026/QD-TTg` | 🔴 **`2027-09-01`** — education compliance deadline |
| 🔴 **EU** | AI Act, Annex III education high-risk | 🔴 **`2027-12-02`** *(Reg. (EU) 2026/1744)* |
| 🟡 **South Korea** | AI Basic Act | in force January 2026 |
| 🟡 **Taiwan** | AI Basic Act | passed December 2025 |
| 🟡 **US states** | `134` bills / `31` states; `H.R. 8747` federally | rolling |
| 🔴 **South Africa** | — | 🔴 **withdrawn; revision targeted January 2027** |

🔵 **The common structure is worth naming because it is what a product must satisfy in all of them: a RISK CLASSIFICATION, a HUMAN-OVERSIGHT record, and a DATA-PROVENANCE statement.** 🟢 **Those three are the portable core; everything else is jurisdiction-specific configuration.**

### 🟢 `T227-3` — assessment is the lowest-adoption, highest-anxiety surface, and that is where the work is

🔴 **LATAM's survey is the sharpest data on these shelves: `92%` of students and `79%` of faculty engaging with AI, yet `88%` of faculty report only minimal-to-moderate engagement — and the LOWEST uptake is in assessment tasks: detecting cheating and generating feedback. `61%` of students fear misuse by peers.**

🟢 **Vietnam independently classifies *"systems that automatically test, evaluate results and rank learners"* as high-risk, and the EU does the same for tools that evaluate learners or gate access to qualifications.** 🔵 **So assessment is simultaneously the surface faculty trust least, students fear most, and regulators have singled out in two unrelated jurisdictions. 🟡 That convergence, from three independent directions, is the strongest signal in this pass.**

🔴 **And these shelves have published the matching supply-side gap since p213: plenty of LLM graders, nothing that measures itself against teacher scores.** 🟢 **An alignment loop — grade, compare against the human score, report the drift — is the one asset that answers all three parties at once.**

### 🟡 `T227-4` — teacher capacity, not student tutoring, is what national programmes are actually buying

🔵 **The programmes with real budgets and real numbers this pass are all teacher-side:** 🟢 **Nigeria's Naija Teacher AI *(`1.5M` licensed teachers, Aug 2025-Aug 2027)*, Egypt's UNESCO AI competency framework for teachers *(launched `2026-06-03`)*, Kenya's `62 000`+ ICT-trained teachers.** 🔴 **Not one of the four African countries' flagship programmes is a student-facing tutor.**

🔵 **This matches the commentary independently: the most successful AI deployments begin with teachers, not students — and the movement is away from generic AI tools toward platforms purpose-built for education.** 🟡 **It also matches the restrictive side of the regulatory map: student-facing AI is where the moratoria are *(NYC through eighth grade, Katy ISD for K-6)*, while teacher-facing AI is where the procurement is.** 🟢 **For a studio that is a straightforward prioritisation rule, and it is the opposite of where most of the open-source agent tier points.**

### 🔵 What this pass does NOT claim

🔴 **Market sizing does not converge: `USD 10.6bn` for 2026 from one report against `USD 12.3bn` attributed to HolonIQ by another — and a same-named source appearing on both sides of the discrepancy.** 🟡 **Several circulated statistics *(`83%` of institutions, a `42%` improvement, a `62%` test-score gain)* carry no verifiable methodology and are not carried here.** 🟢 **No country-level education AI instrument was found for ANY LATAM state, and that is a declared blank rather than a finding of absence.**


## Pass 226 — 2026-10-15 — THE TREND IS THAT CORRECTIONS DO NOT PROPAGATE AND REFUTATIONS DO NOT BECOME CHECKS: THE SAME CLAIM THIS KB KILLED AT p209 CAME BACK THROUGH THE MANDATED QUERY AND THE GATE BUILT TO STOP IT WAS BLIND TO IT

### 🔴 Trend `1` — a refuted claim's half-life is longer than this KB's memory of refuting it

🟢 **p225 named the hazard: a correction published by the original outlet does not reach the aggregators that repeat it.** 🔴 **p226 shows the second half, and it is about the KB rather than the web: a refutation written into prose does not become a CHECK.**

| claim | refuted here | came back | was there a row to catch it? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Boston *"graduation requirement"* | p218, with the publishers' own corrections | p225, `7` passes later | 🔴 **no** |
| `frappe/lms` is `MIT` | p209, first-hand payload *(`33 893` B)* | 🆕 **p226, `17` passes later** | 🔴 **no** |

🔵 **Both were caught by a human read. Neither was caught by the gate, because neither subject was in the table.** 🟢 **The three subjects that WERE in the table did not recur this pass** → [`sh-inbound-gate-run`](../evidence/sh-inbound-gate-run.2026-10-15-p226.tsv).

🟡 **The generalisable form, and it is not specific to this KB:** an organisation's corrections live in documents, and documents are not executable. 🔴 **Any team doing recurring research accumulates refutations faster than it converts them into checks, and the gap is invisible precisely because the check that would reveal it is the one that was never written.**

### 🔴 Trend `2` — third-party licence claims fail in a CONSISTENT direction, and it is the expensive one

🟢 **Two licence errors surfaced in this pass's results and both were PERMISSIVE-direction:** `frappe/lms` *(`AGPL-3.0`)* called `MIT`, and `Open edX` *(`AGPL-3.0`)* called `MIT`.

🔴 **This is not symmetric noise.** 🟡 **Nobody mistakenly calls an MIT project AGPL — there is no incentive to.** 🟢 **The error runs toward the permissive end because that is the answer a listicle's reader wants, and it is the direction that makes a studio relax at exactly the moment it should not: an `AGPL-3.0` platform is the one where network use is distribution and a hosted customisation triggers the source obligation.**

🔵 **The trend worth carrying into an engagement: treat any licence claim from a comparison page as PERMISSIVE-BIASED and read the repository.** 🔴 **And this pass's own first draft committed the same class of error internally — `GPL` written beside `Gibbon`, which is `AGPL-3.0`** → [`verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) `T226-V0`. 🟡 **The bias is not a property of listicles; it is a property of writing a plausible licence beside a name instead of reading the cell.**

### 🟢 Trend `3` — adoption-outran-governance is now measured in LATAM with an institution-level denominator

🔵 **p224 recorded this as a four-region direction; p225 added numbers in two regions; p226 adds the clearest single measurement yet:**

| region | adoption | governance | gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **LATAM** *(UNESCO IESALC, `200` institutions, `19` countries)* | **`87%`** use AI in `1`+ area; `74%` in teaching and learning | **`26%`** have a formal AI strategy | 🔴 **`61` points** |
| 🟡 **North America** | `60%` of US K-12 teachers used AI in 2024-25; `32%` weekly | `134` bills across `31` states, no federal statute in force | 🔴 regulation ahead of institutional policy |
| 🟡 **APAC** | Singapore `60.9%` diffusion *(cross-sector)* | Korea in force, China binding, JP/SG/AU voluntary | 🔴 no common mode |

🟢 **What makes the LATAM number more useful than the others: it has a denominator and a unit — institutions, not users, not responses.** 🟡 **`87%` vs `26%` is a statement about ORGANISATIONS, which is the unit an engagement is actually sold to.**

### 🔴 Trend `4` — the regulatory horizon for education AI is CONVERGING on late 2027, against a date that keeps circulating as 2026

🟢 **EU Annex III education high-risk: `2027-12-02`. Vietnam high-risk sectors including education: `2027-09`.** 🔵 **Two of the three regulating regions, independently, land roughly two years out** → [`intel/market.md`](./market.md).

🔴 **Meanwhile the number in circulation is `August 2026`.** 🟡 **The practical consequence is a planning error in both directions at once: teams that believe the 2026 date think they have already missed it and buy urgently; teams that then discover it was wrong conclude the whole thing is distant and stop.** 🟢 **The accurate posture is neither — `2026-12-02` is genuinely `7` weeks away for the two Article `5` prohibitions, and the high-risk work is a two-year programme that should be starting now because conformity evidence is accumulated, not produced at the end.**

### 🔵 Trend `5` — the open-source education-agent surface is SATURATED at the tiers this KB searches

🟢 **Four consecutive passes have measured the discovery rate: `89%` of `18`, `75%` of `8`, `100%` of `9`, and now `93.8%` of `32` already carried.** 🔵 **The `32` is the largest candidate population yet and the rate held, which makes saturation a measurement rather than an impression.**

🟡 **Where the surface is NOT saturated is specific and worth naming:** 🔴 **there is still no mature open-source MCP connector for a real student information system** — no Banner, no PeopleSoft, no Ellucian, no Workday Student. 🟢 **What exists is personal-scale: one university's HEMIS instance, a public-data server that avoids student accounts, a portal scraper, a read-only catalogue, and a study assistant with no licence at all.** 🔵 **That gap has survived several passes of searching, which is the best evidence available that it is real rather than unsearched.**

### 🟢 Trend `4` — the egress policy is now a structural fact about what this KB can know

🔴 **`11` government and primary hosts probed this pass — `moe.gov.sg`, `mext.go.jp`, `education.gov.in`, `gob.mx/sep`, `gaceta.diputados.gob.mx`, `canada.ca`, `cmec.ca`, `oecd.ai`, `ai-watch.ec.europa.eu`, `ai.turiba.lv`, plus `unesco.org` and `eur-lex` again — and every one is blocked, on TWO independent channels *(`curl` and `WebFetch`, the latter failing at DNS)*.**

### 🟢 Trend `5` — a blocked channel keeps producing a better method than the one it removed
