## Pass 224 — 2026-10-15 — FOUR REGIONS, FOUR QUERIES, ONE TREND: ADOPTION OUTRAN GOVERNANCE EVERYWHERE, AND GOVERNANCE IS WHAT `2026` ACTUALLY BUYS

🔵 **`1` new cross-regional trend established from numbers measured this pass rather than asserted. `1` methodological trend added to this KB's `9`-pass unit-mismatch series. `2` vendor statistics declined.**

🔵 **Evidence:** [`p224-regional-queries`](../evidence/p224-regional-queries.2026-10-15-p224.tsv) · [`sc-date-convergence`](../evidence/sc-date-convergence.2026-10-15-p224.tsv) · [`ru-grammar-census`](../evidence/ru-grammar-census.2026-10-15-p224.tsv)

### 🆕 🟢 `T224-T1` — the governance gap is the trend, and it is quantified differently in every region

🔴 **Four independent query shapes, four regions, and the same structure comes back each time: the usage is in place and the rules are not.**

| region | adoption | governance | the gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **LATAM** | `87%` of `200` HE institutions use AI somewhere *(IESALC, `19` countries)*; `74%` in teaching and learning | 🔴 **`26%` have a formal AI strategy** | 🔴 **`61` points** |
| 🟢 **LATAM — Brazil, schools** | `53%` of administrators use AI | 🔴 **`22%` of schools have guidelines** | 🔴 **`31` points** |
| 🟢 **North America** | `35`+ states publish AI guidance | 🔴 **`134` bills still in flight across `31` states**; districts given `120` days *(MD)* or a `2027-28` deadline *(OK)* to write a policy | 🔴 **guidance exists, binding policy does not** |
| 🟢 **EMEA** | `—` 🔴 **unpublished: no EMEA-wide education adoption figure exists** | 🟢 three dated statutory clocks *(`2026-08-02` · `2026-12-02` · `2027-12-02`)* | 🔴 **inverted: the rules are dated and the adoption is unmeasured** |
| 🟡 **APAC** | Singapore `60.9%` diffusion; `1` in `10` enterprises *"very mature"* | 🟢 Vietnam, Korea, China binding; Singapore, Japan voluntary | 🟡 **statutes ahead of institutional capability** |

🟢 **1EdTech reaches the same conclusion top-down — the sector is moving *"from experimentation to governance"*.** 🔵 **This KB reaches it bottom-up, from five separately-sourced measurements, which is the stronger form of the same claim.**

🟡 **What it means for a studio, stated as the thing to sell:** 🔴 **the deliverable in `2026` is not a tutor.** 🟢 **It is an AI-use policy with a monitoring surface and an evaluation loop, in the institution's own language, for a client who already has the adoption and none of the governance** — and EMEA is the one region where the order is reversed, so the EMEA deliverable is conformity against a dated clock instead. 🔵 **Recipes: [`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md) `P224-R2` *(governance pack)* and `P224-R3` *(EMEA three-clock)*.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T224-T2` — the methodological trend: a measurement is reproducible only if its ENUMERATOR is published

🔴 **This pass imported p221's own `parse_range()` by path — the literal predicate behind the published `94.3%` — and still could not reproduce p221's population: `4 152` GNU mentions against `2 342`, on a corpus `789` lines larger.**

🟢 **The cause is now named: the unit of enumeration lives in the RUNNER, not in the predicate.** p221 counted table-cell mentions; p224 counted token occurrences including prose. Same predicate, different census.

🔵 **Ninth consecutive unit mismatch in this KB's headline series, and this pass supplies three of them:**

| id | the unit assumed | the unit that decided |
|---|---|---|
| `T218-1` | token | **axis** |
| `T218-3` | heading level | **pass boundary** |
| `T218-4` | line | **sentence** |
| `T219-5` | token | **packaging mode** |
| `T220` | repository | **FILE** |
| `T221` | licence family | **version range** |
| `T222` | token | **ecosystem grammar** |
| `T223` | line | **matched span** |
| 🆕 **`T224-1`** | a repository's **file set** | 🔴 **the owner's REPOSITORY SET** |
| 🆕 **`T224-3`** | the row on the line | 🔴 **the grant's SUBJECT** |
| 🆕 **`T224-4`** | the predicate | 🔴 **the ENUMERATOR** |

🟡 **The series has stopped being a curiosity and is now a checklist item: before publishing a count, name the unit, then check the instrument can see it.** 🔵 **`ACTION QY` stays registered; this pass is three more instances for it.**

### 🆕 🟡 `T224-T3` — two vendor statistics declined, recorded so they are not rediscovered as news

🔴 **The mandated `AI education industry trends 2026` query returned `$12.3`B *(market size)*, *"~`54%` of students use AI daily or weekly"* and *"`5`–`10` hours saved per teacher per week"*.** 🔴 **All from vendor blogs and statistics aggregators; none traceable to a primary instrument.** 🟢 **Declined, and written down here because an undocumented decline looks exactly like an undiscovered fact to the next pass.**

🟢 **What the same query DID yield, and is carried:** agentic lesson-planning and assessment drafting as the year's named development; education-specific platforms displacing generic chatbots; teacher-first adoption as the pattern that survives; digital credentials moving toward the centre of skills-based hiring.

## Pass 223 — 2026-10-15 — A REFUTED CLAIM IS NOT NEUTRALISED BY BEING REFUTED SOMEWHERE ELSE IN THE SAME FILE. THE UNIT OF A CORRECTION IS THE **READING PATH**, NOT THE DOCUMENT

🔵 **The trend this pass is about knowledge bases, and it is measurable in this one: `3` cells asserted a date this KB had already refuted `4` times, and the refutation was in the same file, `~900` lines away.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T223-1` — the correction was never missing; it was unreachable from where the error was read

🟢 **The claim: *"EU AI Act, education high-risk, full effect August 2026."*** 🟢 **The correction: `Reg. (EU) 2026/1744` — Annex III education `2027-12-02`, Art. `50` `2026-08-02`.** 🔴 **Both on `intel/market.md`. The error was at line `48`, in the live regional recommendation; the correction at `~937` and `~2246`, in the archive of earlier passes. A reader who opens the page and reads the top section — which is what the page is *for* — gets the wrong one.**

🔵 **Every previous instrument in this KB has validated **inbound** data: a licence read, a market figure's provenance, a query's verbatim text. 🔴 **None validated the KB's own published prose against the KB's own corrections.** 🟢 **`p223-selfclaim-date-gate` is the first that does, and it found `2` `ASSERTED` + `1` `ATTRIBUTED` cell on its first run.**

🟢 **Eighth consecutive unit-mismatch finding, and the strongest form of it so far:** `T218` line vs sentence · `T220` repository vs file · `T221` family vs version range · `T222` token vs ecosystem grammar · 🆕 **`T223` document vs reading path**. 🔵 **The instrument reproduced the mistake while being built — a quote mark elsewhere on the line exonerated the whole line — which is `T222` again, one level up.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T223-2` — a declared blank is a statement about the instrument, and p222's APAC blank was one

🔴 **p222 declared: no national AI-in-schools policy for Singapore, Australia, India, Japan or South Korea.** 🟢 **One query with a different shape — `ministry guidelines` instead of `players` — returned instruments for China, Indonesia, South Korea, Japan, India, Singapore and Nepal in a single pass.**

🔵 **The generalisation, which this KB should treat as a rule rather than an observation: a blank declared from a *market-shaped* query ("players", "adoption", "vendors") says nothing about *policy-shaped* reality. 🟢 `ACTION RE`'s discipline of publishing the verbatim query is what made this detectable — the blank was honest, and it was still wrong about the world.**

🔴 **What has NOT improved: `0` of the `7` APAC instruments was read at a primary source. `ACTION RO` is open for a fourth pass, and this pass established *why* it keeps failing — see `T223-3`.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T223-3` — this KB's read-the-primary protocol has a structural ceiling, and it is the egress policy

🔴 **Unreachable from this session, all three `CONNECT 403`: `data.consilium.europa.eu`, `www.consilium.europa.eu`, `eur-lex.europa.eu`. 🟢 `unesco.org` has been recorded unreachable since p215.**

🟡 **So: every EU and UN date on these shelves is secondary by construction, and no amount of pass effort will change that from inside this session.** 🟢 **The honest responses are (a) say so in the proposal — "dates are secondary-sourced; confirm against the Official Journal before contracting" is a sentence that builds trust rather than losing it — and (b) stop registering primary-read actions that the egress policy makes impossible, and register instead a *convergence* test: `3`+ independent secondary sources agreeing on a date, with the disagreeing ones published.**

### 🟡 `T223-4` — demand is public and supply is coursework, measured on the LATAM dropout shelf

🟢 **`209` repositories, top ten peaking at `4`★, `2` of `4` probed carrying no licence, `1` `MIT` scaffold with no model.** 🔴 **Meanwhile dropout prediction is the single most-cited AI-in-education use case in LATAM ministry and university discourse on these shelves.**

🔵 **That gap is the trend: in this industry, the *demand* side is written by ministries and the *supply* side by students, and the two have not met in an open-source commons. 🟢 For a studio this is good news — it is a build engagement with a published benchmark to beat *(`AUC-ROC 0.943`)* and no incumbent to displace.**

### 🔵 `T223-5` — policy is converging on restriction-with-a-syllabus, not on adoption

🟢 **China bars independent generative use by primary pupils and bars teachers substituting GenAI for core teaching. Singapore gates P4 use inside the ministry platform. South Korea's mandate was withdrawn after sub-`30%` adoption. India mandates the *subject* from Class `3`. Boston and Ohio State make AI fluency a *graduation requirement*.**

🔵 **Read together: governments are regulating **where AI sits in the curriculum**, not whether schools may use it. 🟢 The product shape that follows is course-scoped, teacher-supervised and auditable — and the product shape that does not follow is the site-wide assistant every vendor is selling.**

## Pass 222 — 2026-10-15 — A LICENCE IDENTIFIER DOES NOT MEAN THE SAME THING IN TWO ECOSYSTEMS, AND EVERY TOOL THAT TREATS IT AS ONE STRING — THIS KB INCLUDED — IS READING A POSITIVE STATEMENT AS A SILENCE

🔵 **Five trends: two about how open-source licence data is actually encoded, one about how the market is being sized, one about where the sector's AI supply is coming from, one about what regional silence means.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T222-A` — the bound is an ecosystem property, not a repository property

🔴 **p221 found that `94.3%` of this KB's GNU licence cells carry no `-only` / `-or-later` bound and registered `ACTION RP` to go and read them. `RP` ran and fired its second limb: `8` of the first `40` rows resolved.** 🟢 **But the sweep's real result is that the question was malformed.**

🔴 **`-only` / `-or-later` is **SPDX's** notation. Four other grammars are in active use on these shelves, and in two of them the bound is carried by **omission**:**

| grammar | where it is read | `or-later` | `only` |
|---|---|---|---|
| **SPDX** | `composer.json`, `package.json`, `pyproject.toml` | `-or-later` suffix | `-only` suffix · 🔵 **bare = nothing said** |
| **CRAN** | `DESCRIPTION` | `GPL (>= 3)` | 🔴 **`GPL-3` — bare IS the only-form** |
| **Odoo** | `*/__manifest__.py` | `'LGPL-3 or any later version'` | 🔴 **`'LGPL-3'` — bare IS the only-form** |
| **PyPI trove** | classifiers | `… v3 or later (AGPLv3+)` | `… v3 (AGPLv3)` |
| **prose** | a GNU file header | *"any later version"* | *"(non-upgradable)"* |

🔴 **The consequence for anyone building licence tooling: a scanner that normalises every value to an SPDX string **before** knowing which file it came from destroys information.** 🟢 **`GPL-3` read out of a CRAN `DESCRIPTION` is `GPL-3.0-only`; the same four characters read out of a `package.json` mean the maintainer said nothing.** 🔵 **Both map to the SPDX id `GPL-3.0` and are then indistinguishable.**

🟡 **Measured corroboration, offline:** CRAN's or-later form `(>= n)` appears **`0` times** in this KB's `120 296` lines of shelf content. 🔴 **Not because the R tier is all `only` — because the KB has only ever recorded the token, never the grammar it was written in.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T222-B` — `GPL-2.0-only` clusters by **publisher**, not by project

🔴 **`6` of `6` repositories from a single organisation — [`oat-sa`](https://github.com/oat-sa), publisher of TAO — declare `GPL-2.0-only`. Read first-hand from `composer.json`, six separate files, one answer.**

🟢 **That is a trend about how licence risk is distributed, and it is good news for diligence economics.** 🔵 **Licence bounds are set by a publisher's legal policy and applied uniformly across its repositories. So the efficient unit of licence diligence is the **organisation**, not the repository: read one `oat-sa` manifest and you have predicted the other five. 🟡 Verify the prediction rather than assuming it — but predict first.**

🔴 **The corollary is the uncomfortable one.** Because `GPL-2.0-only` is incompatible with GPLv3, an organisation-level policy becomes an organisation-level **exclusion**: the entire TAO/QTI assessment supply on these shelves is unavailable for linking into Moodle, Chamilo, ILIAS or Gibbon. 🟢 **Over LTI it is fine. Inside the tree it is not — see [`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md) `P222-R1`.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T222-C` — the sector is being sized by vendors who do not agree within a factor of `10`

🔴 **Four `2026`-vintage estimates of AI-in-education, one query, same day: `2025` base USD `6.4`–`8.3` B; `2026` USD `10.6`–`11.4` B; terminal USD `42.5` B *(2030)* to `79.6` B *(2034)*.** 🟡 **None publishes its market boundary, so the spread is definitional, not empirical.**

🟢 **The usable signal is not a size, it is a pair:** student AI use **`66%` → `92%`** in one year, against a sector *deployment* maturity score of **`35`/`100`**. 🔴 **Near-universal individual use, immature institutional deployment. 🔵 Every engagement thesis that depends on "the market is growing" is weaker than one that depends on that gap, because the gap is what a client can actually see inside their own institution.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T222-D` — the sector's new AI supply is arriving as **LMS plugins from institutional publishers**, not as standalone agents

🟢 **Eight query runs across three passes of the mandated *"trending AI agents for education"* shape have returned **zero** education-sector repositories. The constrained shape — naming Moodle and Open edX — has returned `12` and verified `9`.**

🟢 **And the publishers are institutions, not startups.** 🆕 This pass: **mebis** *(Bavarian state school platform)*, **LMS Cloud**, and at p220 **Saylor Academy** — shipping production Moodle plugins under `GPL-3.0-or-later`. 🔵 **Moodle `4.5`+ having a pluggable core AI subsystem is what made this shape possible: the integration surface is now specified, so a ministry or a state platform can publish a provider rather than a product.**

🔴 **What that means for a studio's sourcing strategy: stop searching for *"AI agents for education"*. 🟢 Search the host platform's plugin surface, and treat the ecosystem's mandated manifest as both the discovery channel and the licence channel — for Moodle, `version.php` answered `100%` of the time this pass, while the optional fields in npm, Composer and PyPI answered `20%`.**

### 🆕 🟡 `T222-E` — a regional blank is a property of the query, and this pass proves it

🔴 **p221 recorded Vietnam's AI law, South Korea's AI Basic Act and Japan's AI Basic Plan from an APAC query. 🔴 The **same query string**, run verbatim this pass, returned none of them — only enterprise-AI governance material.**

🟡 **Neither pass is wrong. The retrieval surface moved.** 🔵 **The methodological consequence is stronger than it looks: a regional finding recorded without its query string and date cannot be re-tested, and a finding that cannot be re-tested is indistinguishable from one that has quietly expired.** 🟢 **`ACTION RE`'s discipline — publish the verbatim query beside every blank *and* every finding — is the only thing that made this detectable. 🔴 This pass marks every p221 APAC date as secondary-sourced rather than silently re-asserting it.**


## Pass 221 — 2026-10-15 — THE SECTOR'S OWN TOOLING ALREADY MODELS A LICENCE AS **FAMILY + VERSION**, AND EVERY KB THAT RECORDS IT AS ONE TOKEN — THIS ONE INCLUDED — IS LOSING THE HALF THAT DECIDES COMPATIBILITY

🔵 **Five trends: two about how the sector is governed, two about how it is measured, one about where the open-source supply actually is.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T221-A` — "high-risk education AI" is no longer an EU-only category, and the sooner deadline is in APAC

🔴 **For many passes this KB treated the EU AI Act as the education-sector compliance driver. Three regimes now put education on a high-risk footing, and the EU's own timing has become conditional:**

| regime | in force | education's status | the date that binds |
|---|---|---|---|
| EU AI Act Annex III | enforcement from **`2026-08-02`** | HIGH-RISK: access, assessment, educational path | 🟡 **conditional** — the proposed *"AI omnibus"* would gate high-risk obligations on harmonised standards being available |
| 🆕 **Vietnam AI law** | **`2026-03-01`**, extraterritorial | HIGH-RISK, explicitly **automated assessment** + **behavioural monitoring** | 🔴 **`2027-03-01`** (+6 months for systems already running) |
| 🆕 **South Korea AI Basic Act** | **`2026-01-22`** | framework-level | — |

🔵 **The trend, stated as a trend: the regulatory *shape* the EU invented is being adopted outside the EU faster than the EU is finishing it.** 🔴 **A studio selling "EU AI Act readiness" is selling against a date that may move; a studio selling Vietnam readiness is selling against one that does not.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T221-B` — the measurement trend, and it is this pass's own correction: a licence family is not a grant

🔴 **This is the sixth consecutive pass whose headline is a unit mismatch, and the unit this time is the one every licence table in the industry collapses.**

🟢 **The sector's own tooling already knows better.** `h5p/h5p-php-library` validates the licence of a *learning object* with **two separate fields**:

```php
'license'        => '/^(CC BY|CC BY-SA|…|GNU GPL|PD|…)$/',
'licenseVersion' => '/^(1\.0|2\.0|2\.5|3\.0|4\.0)$/',
```

🔵 **H5P splits family from version because it knows a family alone cannot answer a reuse question about a piece of content.** 🔴 **And `94.3%` of this KB's `2 342` GNU cell-mentions — `2 208` of them, across `337` rows — record exactly one token and no bound.**

🔴 **What the missing half decides:**

| cell | if the file says | consequence for a studio |
|---|---|---|
| `GPL-2.0` | `-only` | 🔴 **cannot be combined with Moodle, Open edX, or any v3 host** |
| `GPL-2.0` | `-or-later` | 🟢 **can** |
| `GPL-3.0` | `-or-later` | 🟢 may be carried forward to a future licence version |
| `GPL-3.0` | bare at source too | 🟡 **genuinely unknown — ask the maintainer, do not assume** |

🟢 **Same token on the shelf, opposite answers. 🔵 The generalisable rule, which is the sixth in this series: NAME THE UNIT OF THE THING, then check your pattern can see it.** The series: token vs **axis** · heading vs **pass boundary** · line vs **sentence** · token vs **packaging mode** · repository vs **file** · 🆕 **family vs VERSION RANGE**.

### 🆕 🟡 `T221-C` — governance-over-experimentation is now the sector's stated posture, and it matches what the regulators did

🟡 **Across the 2026 outlooks read this pass the same shift is described in different words: from experimentation to **governance** — explicit policies, data boundaries and oversight — and from generic chatbots to **education-specific** platforms.**

| strand | what the surfaces say |
|---|---|
| 🟢 **purpose-built over generic** | generic chatbots *created* work for teachers — prompt-writing and fact-checking — and the move to classroom-built tools is called the defining trend of 2026 |
| 🟢 **evidence demands** | buyers increasingly require proof that a product improves learning, persistence, well-being or job-relevant skills |
| 🟡 **credentials as currency** | digital credentials gaining ground as a hiring signal, pushing skills-based systems |
| 🟡 **agentic, but narrower** | the 2026 framing favours ethics, transparency and *proven instructional benefit* over broad personalisation claims |

🔵 **The LATAM numbers are the cleanest evidence that the governance strand is real demand and not commentary: `87%` of surveyed institutions using AI, `26%` with a formal strategy.** 🔴 **Adoption outran governance, and the sector is now buying the governance.**

### 🆕 🟡 `T221-D` — the open-source education supply is in the PLUGIN layer, and the generic trending query cannot see it

🔴 **Seven consecutive passes in which `github trending education AI 2026` returned no education-sector software** — roundups, course collections, `developer-roadmap`, `nanochat`. 🟢 **Two passes in which a shape constrained to the sector's platforms returned verifiable plugins.**

🔵 **The structural reason, and it is the trend: the AI being built for education in 2026 is not being built as standalone agents. It is being built as `local_*` and `block_*` Moodle plugins, XBlocks, and `edx-platform` extensions — artefacts that live inside a host LMS, are discovered through that LMS's directory rather than GitHub's trending page, and are licensed by the host's rules rather than the author's preference.**

🔴 **Which is why `MIT`/`Apache-2.0` is the wrong filter for this sector.** 🟢 **Moodle *requires* `GPL-3.0-or-later` of directory-listed plugins, and `edx-platform` is `AGPL-3.0`. Every plugin verified across p220–p221 is reciprocal:** `saylordotorg/moodle-local_ai_course_assistant`, `Limekiller/moodle-block_openai_chat`, `yedidiaklein/moodle-local_aiquestions`, `adamjenkins/moodle-local_nomoreai`, `openedx/openedx-ai-extensions` *(AGPL-3.0)*. 🔵 **A studio that filters this sector for permissive licences filters out the sector.**

### 🆕 🟡 `T221-E` — a new commercial shape worth watching: an OSI grant on key-gated software

🟡 **One repository named this pass claimed `GPL-3.0-or-later` **and** a site licence key, verified offline, with a 15-day evaluation period on fresh installation.** 🔴 **It is `404` from this session, so it is recorded as a claim and not as data** — but the shape is one this KB's vocabulary cannot express, and it is not the same as `UNKNOWN-PLUS-TOS` or `PROPRIETARY-API`:

| shape | the grant | the software |
|---|---|---|
| `PROPRIETARY-API` | open | open, but calls a vendor's closed service |
| `UNKNOWN-PLUS-TOS` | unknown | governed by vendor terms |
| 🆕 **`OSI-GRANT-PLUS-KEY-GATE`** | 🟢 **reciprocal and free** | 🔴 **refuses to run without a key the vendor issues** |

🔵 **Worth watching because a reciprocal licence and a key gate are in tension: GPLv3's anti-circumvention and installation-information terms exist precisely for this pattern.** 🟢 **Registered as `ACTION RN` for p222 rather than asserted here.**

## Pass 220 — 2026-10-15 — THE TREND THAT MATTERS IS A MEASUREMENT ONE: A LICENCE IS A PROPERTY OF A **FILE**, AND THE WHOLE SECTOR RECORDS IT PER REPOSITORY

🔵 **Four trends this pass, two of them about the sector and two about how the sector is governed.**

### 🆕 🔴 `1` — Repository-level licence metadata is failing, and education software is where it fails hardest

🔴 **Two of the first two rows this pass read file-by-file declare one licence at the repository and a narrower one in the file that ships. Both are in education software, and both are the kind of code that is redistributed as files rather than as a repository.**

| row | repository | the file a studio redistributes |
|---|---|---|
| [`adlnet/SCORM-to-xAPI-Wrapper`](https://github.com/adlnet/SCORM-to-xAPI-Wrapper) | `Apache-2.0` | 🔴 custom ADL **non-disparagement** grant **+ CC BY-SA 3.0**, in every `APIWrapper.js` dropped into every SCO |
| [`microsoft/moodle-ai-assistant`](https://github.com/microsoft/moodle-ai-assistant) | `MIT` | 🔴 **`@license … GNU GPL v3 or later`** in `local/aichat/version.php` |

🔵 **This is not a coincidence of two repositories; it is structural to this sector.** 🟢 **SCORM packages, Moodle plugins, WordPress plugins, LTI tools and course content are all distributed by dropping files into someone else's tree.** 🔴 **Every automated licence surface in common use — GitHub's own detector, `pkg.go.dev`, SBOM generators, and `220` passes of this KB — reports one licence per repository. For the dominant distribution shape in education software, that unit is wrong.** 🟢 **The shelf-level consequence: `agents/top.md` carried *"Apache-2.0, cleanest of the `11`"* on a row whose delivered bytes are share-alike.**

### 🆕 🟢 `2` — The Moodle AI plugin category became real this quarter, and the provider/quota layer is the valuable part

🟢 **`5` named plugins, `4` verified first-hand, and the release dates cluster in `2026`:** `saylordotorg/moodle-local_ai_course_assistant` `7.8.9` *(`version 2026101500` — stamped today, `MATURITY_STABLE`, in production at Saylor)* · `bycs-lp/moodle-local_ai_manager` `5.1.1` · `bycs-lp/moodle-block_ai_chat` `3.3` · `Limekiller/moodle-block_openai_chat` `4.0.2` · `microsoft/moodle-ai-assistant` `0.1.0` *(alpha)*.

🔵 **The trend inside the trend: the category has split into student-facing chat and an infrastructure layer underneath it.** 🟢 **`local_ai_manager` is not a chatbot — it is API-connection management, **per-role request quotas** and usage accounting.** 🔴 **That is the piece every studio has been rewriting per engagement, and it is `GPL-3.0-or-later` and `MATURITY_STABLE`.** 🔵 **Expect the chat blocks to commoditise and the provider layer to be what institutions actually procure.**

### 🆕 🔴 `3` — The counter-measure trend is now bidirectional: institutions are buying refusal

🔴 **p219 found the first tool built to refuse agents *([`adamjenkins/moodle-local_nomoreai`](https://github.com/adamjenkins/moodle-local_nomoreai), `GPL-3.0-or-later`, `release 0.1.2`, committed `2026-10-14`)*. This pass puts it in context, and the context is policy, not software.**

🟢 **The same quarter produced: a **New York City** one-year moratorium on student-facing AI through `8`th grade; **Katy ISD (TX)** banning generative-AI chatbots K-`6`; **Oklahoma** and **Maryland** statutes barring AI from high-stakes decisions about students; a student-authored national framework from all `50` US states asking for **a right to decline AI use**; and **South Korea's** National Assembly reportedly stripping AI textbooks of official status.**

🔵 **So the demand curve has two sides now, and a studio that only sells adoption is selling into half a market.** 🟢 **`local_nomoreai`'s `Detect only` mode is the commercially interesting shape: measure agent traffic for a term, then decide. 🔴 And its own honest limit is the thing no vendor can sell past — *"an agent operating the student's own computer like a person would cannot be stopped or reliably detected by any plugin."*** 🔵 **Which pushes the real answer toward assessment design, and that is consulting work, not a licence.**

### 🆕 🟡 `4` — Offline-first is re-entering education software, and this time it ships local inference

🟢 **[`Crosstalk-Solutions/Project-NOMAD`](https://github.com/Crosstalk-Solutions/Project-NOMAD) *(`Apache-2.0`, `v1.35.1`)* bundles Kolibri, Kiwix and **Ollama** into one Debian-installable server: Khan Academy courses with progress tracking, offline Wikipedia and medical references, and AI chat over an uploaded document base — with no egress at all.**

🔵 **Offline education servers are not new *(Kolibri and Kiwix are both long-standing)*. 🟢 What is new is that local inference is now cheap enough to put in the same `5` GB / `<1` GB-RAM box, so the AI layer no longer forces connectivity.** 🔴 **Read against [`intel/market.md`](market.md): the region with the world's highest student AI adoption and the thinnest institutional policy is also the one where connectivity is the binding constraint. The two facts have been in tension on these shelves for `200` passes; this row is the first that resolves it.**

### 🆕 🟡 Standing measurement note

🟢 **The `2026` AI-in-education market figure spans `USD 1.94` B to `USD 11` B across `9` research houses — a `5.5`× range for a single year.** 🔴 **A trend statement built on any one of those numbers is a trend statement about a definition.** 🟢 **The segment figure with a stated boundary — AI tutoring and adaptive learning, `USD 9.79` B in `2026`, `20.9`% CAGR — is the only one safe to quote, and even that is a vendor surface not fetched first-hand.**

## Pass 219 — 2026-10-15 — THE COUNTER-MEASURE ARRIVES: THE FIRST TOOL BUILT TO REFUSE THE AGENTS THIS KB RECOMMENDS

### 🆕 🟢 `T219-7` — the trend, and it is the first one on these shelves that points the other way

🔵 **Every trend this KB has recorded for `218` passes has been about agents getting INTO education systems: MCP servers for Moodle, Canvas, Brightspace, Blackboard; web-service tokens; LTI bridges; grade passback.**

🆕 🔴 **[`adamjenkins/moodle-local_nomoreai`](https://github.com/adamjenkins/moodle-local_nomoreai) *(`GPL-3.0-or-later`, `version 2026101400`, `MATURITY_BETA`)* is a Moodle plugin whose purpose is to stop them.** 🟢 **Its own table of agent access routes, and what it does to each:**

| how the agent reaches Moodle | what the plugin does |
|---|---|
| web-service token *(MCP servers, scripts, CLIs)* | 🔴 **stops them** submitting students' work, or blocks student tokens entirely |
| signed cloud agents *(Web Bot Auth)*, telltale user-agent or IP | 🔴 **refuses them** at login and on course pages |
| browser automation *(Playwright, Puppeteer, Selenium)* | 🟡 **flags** them to teachers — never refuses |
| an agent driving the student's own computer | 🟢 **cannot be stopped or reliably detected by any plugin** *(its words)* |

🔴 **Row one is this KB's own Moodle architecture.** 🔵 **`onbirdev/moodle-webservice_mcp` IS a web-service plugin; `loyaniu/moodle-mcp` authenticates at `user/managetoken.php`; `Nasus20202/MoodleMCP` calls the same surface; `Hesham0-0Nasser/tutor-lms-mcp` does the WordPress equivalent over OAuth.**

🟢 **So the trend to record is not "a blocker exists". It is that the LMS-integration layer this KB has spent `218` passes cataloguing now has an institutional opt-out, shipped as a plugin, under a licence that lets any institution fork it.** 🔴 **A studio that ships an LMS→MCP integration in `2027` has to answer a question that did not exist in `2026`: what happens when the site turns `Block submissions` on?**

🟡 **The honest limits, and they are the plugin's own:** 🔴 **`release 0.1.2`, `MATURITY_BETA`, requires Moodle `5.2`/`5.3` — a signal, not a deployment.** 🟢 **And its `Detect only` mode is why it will spread anyway: an institution can measure agent traffic for a term before deciding anything, which is how blocks get adopted.**

🔵 **The adjacent fact, from the same search:** 🟡 **the Moodle plugin directory also now carries an AI-GRADING category — `local_aigrader`, `local_aigrade`, `local_smartgradeai`, `local_assign_ai`, `local_aicheck`, `local_graidy`.** 🟢 **Institutions are adopting AI *inside* the LMS, under the LMS's own `GPL`, through the LMS's own directory — while separately gaining the means to refuse AI that arrives from outside it.** 🔴 **Those two movements are the same institutional instinct: keep it governable.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T219-2` — the methodological trend, and it is this KB's most expensive recurring defect

🔴 **Fourth consecutive pass in which the pass's main finding is an INSTRUMENT whose unit was wrong for the thing it measured:**

| | instrument | unit it used | unit the thing has |
|---|---|---|---|
| `T218-1` | the novelty grep | a **token** | an **axis** |
| `T218-3` | the live/historical test | a heading **level** | a pass **boundary** |
| `T218-4` | `ACTION QE`'s window | a **line** | a **sentence** |
| 🆕 `T219-5` | `ACTION QV`'s upstream grep | a **token** | a **packaging mode** |

🔵 **`T219-5` is the first of the four to be wrong in BOTH directions at once, which is what makes it useful.** 🔴 **A `moodle-` slug prefix called an API client a plugin; the words *"MCP server"* and *"OAuth"* called a WordPress plugin an API client — and that second row was the one with a `GPL-2.0+` grant sitting in its plugin header, unread for `8` passes.**

🟢 **The general rule, now stated in the form the next pass can use:** 🟢 **before grepping, name the unit of the thing being measured, and check that the pattern can see it.** 🔴 **A token cannot see an axis, a line cannot see a sentence, and a slug cannot see how software is installed.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T219-2b` — an upstream is a CHANNEL HINT before it is a risk

🔴 **`ACTION QV` was registered as a risk probe: a reciprocal upstream makes a row *"a dead end even if the author answers."*** 🟢 **It fires — `4` of `47` are derivatives of `OSL-3.0`, `GPL-2.0` or `GPL-3.0` upstreams — and then the conclusion inverts.**

🟢 **`3` of those `4` were never ungranted. The ecosystem that makes a repo reciprocal is the same ecosystem that prescribes WHERE its licence goes:** WordPress in `readme.txt` and the plugin header; Moodle in `version.php` and the file docblocks; Magento in `composer.json`. 🔵 **Read the channel, and the dead end does not arise.**

🔴 **This matters beyond the `4` rows because it reverses an ORDER OF WORK.** 🔴 **`ACTION PH` — *"ask the maintainer to commit a `LICENSE` body"* — has been registered and deliberately untaken for `15` passes. On `3` of these rows it would have asked a maintainer for a file they had already shipped, under a different name, in the place their ecosystem puts it.** 🟢 **Identify the upstream → read its convention → only then consider asking a human.**

### 🆕 🟡 `T219-8` — a regional blank is evidence about a pass, not about a region

🔴 **p216 closed `ACTION QJ` on limb `2` and licensed the next passes to *"report blanks as blanks"*. p218 reported APAC as vendor-only and EMEA as DECLARED BLANK.** 🟢 **This pass the same shape, with `ministry` and `policy` in the query, returned `2` new APAC ministry instruments and education-specific EMEA policy bodies.**

🔵 **Neither p218 nor `QJ` was dishonest — both reported what their pass returned.** 🔴 **The defect is in how the blank was PUBLISHED: without its query, a blank reads as a statement about the region, and the next pass inherits it as coverage.** 🟢 **`ACTION RE`: a declared regional blank must carry the query string that produced it.** 🔵 **That is `ACTION QL`'s rule — *grep before claiming absence* — extended from the shelves to the world.**

## Pass 218 — 2026-10-15 — LANGUAGE WAS THE SYMPTOM, SCOPE IS THE DISEASE: A BILINGUAL GREP ON A PROPER NOUN RETURNS `0` FOREVER, AND THE AXIS STAYS LOUD

### 🆕 🔴 `T218-1` — the general form of `T217-2`, and it supersedes it

🔴 **`T217-2` concluded: every propagation gate this KB owns matches English only, and `1 087` lines of the corpus are Spanish.** 🟢 **True, and the table is now built and wired *([`compose/code/p218-bilingual-gate/`](../compose/code/p218-bilingual-gate/README.md))*.** 🔴 **But it is not the defect that produced `ACTION QS`.**

🟢 **p217's novelty grep for the teacher-side axis ran:** `Profe Gabi` `0`, `Elige Educar` `0`, `Elacqua` `0`, `docentes noveles` `0`, `novice teacher` `0`, `induction`/`inducción` `0`. 🟢 **Six tokens, bilingual, every count correct.** 🔴 **Then it wrote: *"the teacher-side axis … is the one content axis these shelves have `216` passes of silence on."***

🔴 **The axis carries `8` items across `4` shelves in `3` languages — Costa Rica `GuíaDocenteIA` *(ES)*, Spain `INTEF` *Orientaciones … formación docente* *(ES)*, Brazil *IA na prática docente* *(PT)*, Maryland `S.B. 720`, India `NISHTHA`, Google.org `$4.6M`, `H.R. 8747`, Alabama `HB 329`.**

🔴 **`T218-1`: an ENTITY-level grep cannot license an AXIS-level claim of absence. Language was one instance of scope mismatch, not the whole of it — translating `Profe Gabi` into six languages still returns `0`, because the token is a proper noun and the claim is a category.** 🟢 **The rule `ACTION QL` needs is not *"grep in both languages"* but *"compare the SCOPE of what you grepped against the SCOPE of what you are claiming"*.** 🔵 **No script can enforce that. `ACTION QP` ships the mechanical half; the judgement half is a review step, and it is now written down.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T218-2` — the gates are monolingual in DIFFERENT languages, which masks both blind spots

🔴 **`T217-2` said *"`ACTION QL` is a grep. `qg_gate2.py` is a token comparison. Both are English."*** 🔴 **`compose/code/` holds `10` files carrying Spanish tokens, and one of them is a gate: `p415/clase_positiva_v2.py`, whose `RE_RECHAZO` matches `no cede|rechaz|sin licencia|descart|hueco|por que NO` — **Spanish only**.** 🟡 **Its `RE_ENCABEZADO_RECOMIENDA` matches the stem `licenc`, which covers `license` and `licencia` by accident rather than design.**

🔴 **So the corpus has an English gate and a Spanish gate, each blind to the half the other reads. 🔴 That is worse than all-English, because the two blind spots MASK each other: a cell the English gate misses looks swept, because the Spanish gate ran over the same file.** 🟢 **Both halves now sit in one table applied in both directions.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T218-3` — the live/historical instrument conflates a heading LEVEL with a pass BOUNDARY

🔴 **p216 certified `ACTION QO` with *"the enclosing LEVEL-2 (pass-boundary) test calls `0` live. The heading LEVEL was the whole measurement."*** 🔴 **`364` of the `1 349` level-2 headings on these shelves — `27`% — are not pass boundaries:**

| file | shadowing | share | what the shadowing headings are |
|---|---|---|---|
| `compose/patterns.md` | 🔴 **`114`** | 🔴 **`71`%** | recipe titles — the client-facing half of the file |
| `intel/market.md` | 🔴 **`64`** | 🔴 **`46`%** | 🔴 **`53` of them are `## Opportunities by region` — the heading the INGEST SPEC MANDATES** |
| `agents/trending.md` | `71` | `20`% | dated signal blocks |
| `repos/trending.md` | `66` | `19`% | dated signal blocks |

🔴 **The schema itself injects the competing heading, so the test is least reliable exactly in the file whose cells a studio reads first.** 🟢 **Verdict survives *(all `6` stale cells are historical)*; instrument does not. One-line fix committed.** 🔵 **`T218-3` and `T218-4` are the same shape as `T218-1`: in all three the instrument's UNIT was wrong for the thing it measured — a token for an axis, a heading level for a pass boundary, a line for a sentence.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T218-4` — a line-based window cannot read the GPL, because the GPL sentence wraps

🔴 **`ACTION QE` — *"re-read every GNU-family cell from the TITLE WINDOW, not the body"* — has been carried `3` passes unexecuted. p218 executed it on `3` rows and it fails by construction:**

```
line N      "... under the terms of the GNU General Public License as published by"
line N+1    "the Free Software Foundation, either version 3 of the License, or"
```

🔴 **Family on line `N`, version on line `N+1`. A `1`-line window stops one line short of the pin, on every correctly-licensed `GPL-3.0` repository in the corpus — and the `~97` GNU rows `QE` targets are mostly Moodle plugins carrying this exact boilerplate.** 🟢 **`3` of `3` rows pinned this pass: `GPL-3.0-or-later`.** 🟢 **The window must be SENTENCE-based: join to the next `.` before matching.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T218-5` — the trending query has a CATEGORY problem, not a saturation problem

🔴 **`github trending education AI 2026` has returned `0` education-sector repositories on `2` consecutive passes, while returning `7`+ *teaching-material-about-AI* repositories each time — `microsoft/ai-agents-for-beginners`, `pguso/agents-from-scratch`, `rohitg00/ai-engineering-from-scratch`, `avinash201199/free-ai-agents-resources`, `karpathy` *Zero to Hero*, `d2l`, `Made With ML`.** 🔴 **These carry the LARGEST star counts on the list, so the signal is crowded out by the noise.**

🟡 **`T176` named this confusion; `p218` measures its persistence at `4` consecutive passes.** 🟢 **`ACTION QJ` withdrew the analogous proposal for the REGIONAL query after measuring both shapes saturated — but this is the opposite failure: not saturation, a category mismatch.** 🔵 **Registered as `ACTION QW`.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T218-6` — a missing grant on a RECIPROCAL upstream is a different risk, and this KB records neither

🟢 **[`alfredang/ai-mms`](https://github.com/alfredang/ai-mms): no licence prose at all *(`0` hits over `30 558` B)*, built on **`OpenMage LTS v20.12.0`** *(Magento 1 LTS)*, which inherits Magento 1's **`OSL-3.0`** — network-reciprocal.**

🔴 **So an ungranted repository sits on a reciprocal upstream, and if the author granted MIT tomorrow the derivative question would survive it.** 🔴 **The `47` genuine unknowns have never been checked for this shape. A repo with no grant of its own and a permissive upstream is a *lead*; a repo with no grant and a reciprocal upstream is a *dead end even if the author answers*.** 🔵 **Registered as `ACTION QV`.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T218-7` — the falsifier was run before the cell, for the first time

Saved: `2` wrong cells, and a wrong cell on these shelves costs several passes to find and correct, as `P218-F4`'s `6` stale cells show.** 🔵 **The useful generalisation is about ORDER, not about owners: the falsifier is cheap before the cell and expensive after it.**

- 🟢 **The verification cost one search and changed the one word a client contract would turn on: *"require"*.**
- 🟡 **The refutation is MORE useful than the claim would have been. A studio pitching Boston needs to know the district is building a curriculum, not enforcing a diploma rule — those are different buyers with different budgets.**

## Pass 215 — 2026-10-15 — `ABSENT` MEANT *"NOT AT ROOT"*: `27`% OF THIS KB'S UNGRANTED POPULATION WAS GRANTED ALL ALONG, AND THE SAME SHELVES HELD BOTH READINGS

### 🆕 🔴 `T215-1` — the ungranted count was a channel artefact, and now it has a defensible size

🟢 **`25` of `94` rows this KB published as `ABSENT` carry a grant off-root** — `13` in a licence
file one or more directories down, `12` in a manifest. 🔵 **`ACTION QD` limb `1` fires against a
threshold of `15`.**

| this KB's ungranted figure | status |
|---|---|
| `94` *(p211, after `18` root path forms)* | 🔴 **overcount by `25`, and it contains `3` duplicate pairs** |
| `63` *(p215, after the `ls-tree` channel)* | 🟢 **the first defensible ungranted list this KB holds** |

🔴 **Every one of p211's `18` path forms was a ROOT path, and the word `ABSENT` was read as
*"ungranted"* for four passes.** 🟢 **p211 labelled the file correctly — *"an ABSENCE AT ONE
CHANNEL, not a finding of no licence"* — 🔴 **and the derived cells across four shelves dropped
the qualifier.** 🔵 **That is `P211-1`'s propagation defect operating on a METHOD CAVEAT rather
than on a licence value, which is a worse version of it: the caveat is what makes the number
honest.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T215-2` — `2` of the recoveries sit at ROOT, so this was never purely a reach problem

🟢 **`thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem` serves `LICENSE` *(`10 785` B, `Apache-2.0`)* and
`skhdemo/uoft-quercus-mcp` serves `LICENSE` *(`1 086` B, `MIT`)* — at root, under the most
obvious filename there is.** 🔴 **Both were probed with `18` forms and recorded `ABSENT`.**
🔵 **So the `94` decomposes into at least three causes, not one:** 🟡 **off-root placement**,
🟡 **case and extension variance** *(`License`, `License.txt`, `copying.txt` — `3` of `13`)*, and
🔴 **probe loss at root, cause unknown**. 🟢 **`ACTION QH` tests the form list itself instead of
trusting it a nineteenth time.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T215-3` — in EDUCATION, *"licence"* is a credential, and licence locators do not know that

🟢 **New false-positive class, and it is industry-specific.**
[`mn-mde-edfi/MDE-Ed-Fi-ODS-API-SDK`](https://github.com/mn-mde-edfi/MDE-Ed-Fi-ODS-API-SDK)
carries `6` paths matching `/licen[cs]e/i`:

| path | what it actually is |
|---|---|
| `…/LicenseStatusDescriptorsApi.cs` *(`144 764` B)* | 🔴 Ed-Fi API bindings for **teacher certification licence STATUS** |
| `…/LicenseTypeDescriptorsApi.md` *(`41 793` B)* | 🔴 Ed-Fi descriptors for **licence TYPE** |

🔴 **`0` of the `6` are copyright grants.** 🔵 **A teacher's licence, a licensure endorsement, a
licence-status descriptor — all match the same regex a grant locator uses. 🟢 **Any licence sweep
over an education corpus carries a false-positive rate that the identical tool would not have on a
gaming or fintech corpus**, because education data models contain *credentialing* as a first-class
noun.** 🔵 **Two sibling classes, both observed:
[`spdx/license-list-data`](https://github.com/spdx/license-list-data) ships `339 359` B of **other
people's** grants as DATA, and [`alfredang/ai-mms`](https://github.com/alfredang/ai-mms) has `5`
licence paths of which `0` are its own.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T215-4` — a single-axis path filter cannot decide ownership, in either direction

🔵 **`ACTION QD` prescribed rejecting `vendor/`, `lib/`, `node_modules/`, `third_party/`,
`public/javascripts/`. Measured, it fails both ways:**

| direction | instance |
|---|---|
| 🔴 **too narrow** | passes `libraries/htmlpurifier/`, `inc/libraries/`, `js/tiny_mce/`, `app/code/community/Stripe/`, `source/fonts/freefont-…/`, `html/appCore/addons/`, bare `phpmailer/` — all third-party |
| 🔴 **too broad** | rejects `lib/` — and **p214's genuine `formalms` recovery was `html/lib/System/license/license_english.txt`** |

🟢 **The discriminator that worked is not shape but ATTESTATION: a licence file is the project's
own when the manifest or `README` points at it, or when its copyright holder is the project or its
author.** 🔵 **Which is why the manifest channel must run ALONGSIDE the file channel, not as a
fallback — on `foradian/fedena` the manifest-class `NOTICE` is the ONLY channel, and on
`alfredang/ai-mms` the file channel is pure noise.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T215-5` — every byte-count conflict in this KB has one mechanism, and it is `CR`

🟢 **`formalms/formalms`' `html/license.txt`: `18 346` binary bytes, `340` `CR` bytes, `340`
lines.** 🔴 **Read through a text-mode pipe: `18 006` — short by exactly `340`, one byte per
line.** 🟢 **p214 published `18 346` and was right; p215's first read was not.**
🔵 **`T208-8`'s convention *(the blob AS SERVED)* is unchanged — what this adds is that the READ
must be binary, and that `tr -dc '\r' | wc -c` separates the two readings in one call.**
🟡 **Scope, so this is not over-read: `1` of the `25` blobs recovered this pass is `CRLF`, and it
is exactly the `1` that produced a conflict. `24` are `LF` and have one byte count.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T215-6` — the propagation defect, measured a third time, and the composition is the finding

🟢 **`ACTION QG` ran as a gate over `263` grant-claim lines touching the `25` repositories

| | count |
|---|---|
| 🔴 current-state cells contradicting the established grant | **`23`** *(corrected in place)* |
| 🟢 current-state cells that ALREADY held the right answer | **`7`** |
| 🟡 comparator false positives *(cells describing the error, or a naive read, correctly)* | **`4`** |
| 🔵 dated history mentions *(records, not errors — untouched)* | **`93`** |

🔴 **`formalms/formalms` is the cleanest instance this KB has produced: p214 established
`GPL-2.0`, and `4` current-state cells went on saying *"no licence body"* and *"UNRESOLVED"* —

[`qg_gate2.py`](../compose/code/p215-lstree-grant-recovery/qg_gate2.py) is the implementation.**

person's practice requires a channel that can support it, and `ABSENT`-at-one-channel never

### 🆕 🔴 `T214-3` — the GNU family is this industry's standing licence hazard, now total on an independent measure

🟢 **`P211-3` measured `5` of `7` wrong grant cells inside `GPL`/`LGPL`/`AGPL`.** 🟢 **`ACTION
PZ` re-measured the same axis a completely different way — internal self-contradiction rather
than blob disagreement — and got `8` of `8`.**

🔵 **Why education specifically keeps landing here:** 🟢 **this industry's mature platforms are
`2000`s-era civic and university software, and that cohort chose the GNU family.** 🔴 **The one
licence question that decides a hosted education build — *does serving it to students trigger
disclosure?* — is exactly the question the GNU family answers differently across `AGPL` *(yes)*,
`GPL` *(no)* and `LGPL` *(no, and linking is permitted)*. 🔴 **And the three are textually
nested: `GPL-3.0` §13 names `Affero` `3` times; `LGPL-3.0` is published either short *(by
reference)* or embedding the whole `GPL`.** 🟢 **So the axis that matters most commercially is
also the one a text matcher is least able to read.** 🔵 **`T214-3` is a standing instruction:
**never fill a GNU-family cell from a body match — the title window, or a manifest, or nothing.**

🔴 **And the instruction is not new, which is the uncomfortable part.** 🟢 **`P171` wrote the §13
mechanism down in full, and `P205` turned it into the title-window rule.** 🔴 **Yet `4`
`GibbonEdu` cells carried `AGPL-3.0` through every pass since.** 🔵 **A rule written into the
method does not clean the cells authored before it; only a sweep does, and
[`intel/open-gaps.md`](open-gaps.md) `ACTION QE` is that sweep.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T214-4` — the vendored-licence false positive, and why it is an education problem

🟢 **`foradian/fedena` carries `14` licence files and `0` of its own.** 🔴 **A subdirectory sweep
recovers `vendor/plugins/delayed_job/MIT-LICENSE` and publishes a school ERP as `MIT`.**
🟢 **Its real grant is `Apache-2.0`, stated in a root `NOTICE` of `679` B.**

🔵 **This is not a quirk of one repo.** 🔴 **Education's long-lived PHP and Rails platforms
predate dependency managers, so they VENDOR their dependencies into the tree** — `fedena` ships
`fckeditor`, `tiny_mce`, `paperclip`; `formalms` ships `symfony` polyfills, `twig` and a
`jsdoc`/`prettify` **`Apache-License-2.0.txt`** that three comparison articles mistook for the
project's grant. 🟢 **In this industry, the licence files in a repository usually outnumber the
project's own by an order of magnitude**, which makes *"find a LICENSE file"* a measurably
unsafe method here even though it is adequate in most industries.

## Pass 213 — 2026-10-15 — THE INTEGRITY SHELF ANSWERS THE PREVIOUS QUESTION: `385` REPOSITORIES, `0` DETECT AI-WRITTEN TEXT. AND A GRANT IS NOT A SCALAR, IT IS A RESOLVER

### 🆕 🔴 `T213-1` — the whole open-source academic-integrity shelf predates generative AI

🟢 **`topics/plagiarism-detection` — `385` repositories — read first-hand this pass.** 🔴 **Of the
top `20`, `0` detect AI-written text.** 🔵 **Every one is classical similarity:**
[`jplag/JPlag`](https://github.com/jplag/JPlag) *(`2`k★, source-code plagiarism)*,
[`dodona-edu/dolos`](https://github.com/dodona-edu/dolos) *(`351`★)*,
`pycode_similar` *(`191`★)*, `automoss` *(`157`★)*, `text-matcher` *(`140`★)*,
`Writing-Styles-Classification` *(`117`★, stylometry)*, `jieplag` *(`54`★, Rust, inspired by
Stanford Moss)* — n-grams, TF-IDF, cosine similarity, Wagner–Fischer, authorship stylometry.

🔴 **The industry context makes this a gap and not a curiosity.** 🟢 **Student AI use is reported
this pass at `92`% for `2025`, up from `66`% in `2024`** *(secondary roundup — see
[`intel/market.md`](market.md) p213)*. 🔴 **The open-source tools an institution would reach for to
defend assessment integrity were built to answer *"did this student copy from that student"*, and
the live question is *"did a model write this"*.**

🟡 **The one row that even acknowledges the new world is adversarial, not detective:**
[`josemmo/plagpatrol`](https://github.com/josemmo/plagpatrol) *(`42`★, **MIT**, `1 084` B)* —
*"an app for detecting documents tampered to bypass plagiarism detectors"*. 🔵 **It defends the old
instrument; it does not replace it.**

🟢 **Deck line:** *"The integrity shelf is mature, permissively licensed, and aimed at the previous
decade's cheating. That is the largest unbuilt layer in open-source education."*

### 🆕 🔴 `T213-2` — a grant is not a scalar, and not even a path→family map. It is a RESOLVER

🔴 **`P212-A` modelled a repository's grant as `{path-glob → family}` after `pupilfirst` broke a
title-anchored read.** 🟢 **This pass found `4` grants in one population, none of which that model
returns correctly.**

| repo | ★ | 🔴 what the model cannot express |
|---|---|---|
| [`andymatuschak/orbit`](https://github.com/andymatuschak/orbit) | **`1.8`k** | 🔴 **An ELECTIVE DISJUNCTION.** Libraries are `Apache-2.0`; `packages/app` + `packages/backend` are **`AGPL-3.0-or-later` OR `BUSL-1.1`, the licensee's choice**. A glob maps to a SET, not a family — and compiled binaries are carved back out to `Apache-2.0` |
| [`learning-commons-org/knowledge-graph`](https://github.com/learning-commons-org/knowledge-graph) | **159** | 🔴 **AN EXTERNAL AUTHORITY.** The partition key is not a path — it is **dataset identity and access tier**, resolved from a remote catalog, and the file says terms *"can change between releases"* |
| [`codeforlife-education/aimmo`](https://github.com/codeforlife-education/aimmo) | **117** | 🔴 **AN OWNER BOUNDARY.** A `115`-byte `LICENSE.md` naming **no family at all**, pointing at `ocadotechnology/codeforlife-workspace` — which serves **`AGPL-3.0`, `32 920` B** |
| [`chipmates/agoracosmica`](https://github.com/chipmates/agoracosmica) | **149** | 🔴 **A TENSE.** Code is `AGPL-3.0` *(`34 523` B)*; content is **copyright now**, scheduled to `CC-BY-4.0` *"within `6`–`12` months"* of the May `2026` launch |

🟢 **The rule, stated so `P213-A` can implement it:** 🔴 **a licence file is not necessarily a
grant — it is sometimes a ROUTER.** 🟢 **Classification must be followed by RESOLUTION:** follow
in-repo references, follow cross-repo and cross-owner references, emit a set where the text says
*"your choice"*, mark the grant TEMPORAL where it names a conversion or relicensing date, and mark
it EXTERNAL — never cacheable — where authority sits outside the repository.

🔵 **Why this is the same lesson as `T212-1` wearing different clothes:** 🟢 **p210 established
`STATED`-not-`BODIED` for a blob that *names* a family without shipping it.** 🔴 **`aimmo` is the
shape past that: it names nothing, so the `STATED` fallback cannot fire either, and the row lands
in the `ungranted` bucket — the one class p208 proved this KB systematically mis-reads.** 🟢 **An
`ungranted` reading is a question, not an answer. `aimmo`'s real answer is the most restrictive
copyleft in this industry.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T213-3` — the interoperability layer has no AI, and now there is a number for each shelf

🟢 **`T212-2` said the AI layer sits *beside* the platform.** 🟢 **Seven topic populations read
first-hand this pass and last make it a measured disjunction:**

| GitHub topic | population | AI/LLM/agent/tutor in top `20` | shelf |
|---|---|---|---|
| `lti` | `185` | 🔴 **`0`** | interoperability |
| `student-information-system` | `183` | 🔴 **`0`** | system of record |
| `mooc` | `640` | 🔴 **`0`** | courseware |
| `scorm` | `228` | 🔴 **`2`** | packaging |
| `question-generation` | `229` | 🟡 **pre-LLM** *(`T5`/transformer era, `ACL` `2019`–`2021`)* | assessment generation |
| `spaced-repetition` | `2 737` | **`6`** | memory |
| `edtech` | **`4 467`** | 🟢 **`14` of `40`** *(two pages)* | product |

🔵 **`edtech` at `4 467` repositories is the largest education topic this KB has read**, and it had
never been opened before this pass. 🟢 **The gradient is the finding:** the standards and
system-of-record shelves are at or near `0`% AI; the product shelf is roughly `35`%. 🔴 **A studio
proposing *"we will add AI to your LMS"* is proposing to build the adapter, because the standard
that connects tools to the LMS — `LTI`, `185` repositories — contains none.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T213-4` — the unit of distribution in education AI is becoming a PROMPT ARTEFACT

🟢 **`topics/ai-tutor` page `2` *(population `707`)* read this pass.** 🔵 **Of `20` rows, at least
`7` are Claude Code Skills or `MCP` servers rather than deployable services:**
[`ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp`](https://github.com/ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp) *(`49`★, Go — "turns
any LLM into an Intelligent Tutoring System")*, `TovTechOrg/Tov-learn` *(`36`★, "built as a Claude

🟡 **The tail is long and shallow: the star ceiling on page `2` is `64`.** 🟢 **This confirms
p212's `P212-5` at population scale rather than as one anomaly** *(`GarethManning/education-agent-skills`,
`860`★, `CC-BY-SA-4.0`)*. 🔴 **And it carries p212's licence warning with it: a prompt artefact is
content, so the governing grant is a content licence, and `CC-BY-SA` share-alike on a skill pack
reaches the pack, not the tutor that loads it — a distinction no software scanner draws.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T213-5` — `T212-7` measured a QUERY. LATAM's ceiling is not `6` stars

🔴 **p212 published: *"LATAM's open-source ceiling is `6` stars"*, from three GitHub repository
searches in Spanish and Portuguese.** 🟢 **[`menthorlabs/menthor`](https://github.com/menthorlabs/menthor)
— `582`★, description in Portuguese, **`Apache-2.0`** *(`11 337` B, read today)*, `273` commits —
is `97`× that ceiling.**

🔵 **Why three language-keyed queries missed it:** 🟢 **its topic tags and repository name are
English** *(`nuxt`, `vue`)*. 🔴 **It was reachable all along through `topics/edtech`, which no pass
had opened.** 🟢 **The language-keyed query measures how a project DESCRIBES itself, not where it
is from — and in LATAM the prose is Portuguese while the metadata is English.**

🟢 **What survives of `T212-7`, and it is the load-bearing half:** 🔴 **`menthor` has no AI layer
and was **archived by its owner on `2024-12-12`**.** 🔵 **So *"no production-scale education-**AI**
repository in the region's own languages"* still holds; *"the ceiling is `6` stars"* does not.**
🟢 **Stated as the positive it is: LATAM's highest-star education repository is permissive and
abandoned — which makes it a fork, not a competitor.** *(This KB already recorded `menthor` as
archived at p195; what is new is placing it against `T212-7`'s number.)*

🟢 **p212 recorded the **European Reference Framework for the Evaluation of Educational

reference of the Council of Europe's **Committee of Experts on Artificial Intelligence and

🔵 **Two citations this KB was carrying unnumbered, now numbered:** 🟢 **the AI-literacy
recommendation is **`CM/Rec(2026)12`**, adopted by the Committee of Ministers in early September

🟢 **Why the date matters commercially:** 🔴 **an evaluation framework is an evidence requirement,
and `11` weeks is inside a single engagement.** 🔵 **It is also the strongest argument this KB has

🟢 **`77` repositories' grants determined first-hand this pass** *(`73` serving a blob, `4` serving
