### 🆕 🟡 `T213-8` — the classifier's own byte field violates this KB's byte convention, `1` time in `71`

🟢 **`T208-8` fixed the convention: a byte count is the blob AS SERVED by
`raw.githubusercontent.com` at `HEAD`, trailing newline included.** 🔴 **`classify.py`'s `bytes`
field is not that number — it re-encodes the body after `errors='replace'`, so a blob containing
non-`UTF-8` bytes under-reports.**

🔵 **Measured across all `73` blobs served this pass: `1` divergence —
[`adlnet/xAPI-Spec`](https://github.com/adlnet/xAPI-Spec), **served `11 525` B**, classifier
`11 325` B, **gap `200` B**.** 🟢 **The KB's published cell says `11 525` and is correct.**

🔴 **Why this is worth a paragraph at a `1`/`71` rate:** 🟢 **`T208-8` carved out `1`-byte
differences as a convention artefact that must not consume a `<!--corrected-->`.** 🔴 **A `200`-byte
gap is outside that carve-out and looks exactly like a real correction.** 🟢 **The rule: publish
the fetcher's `wc -c`, never the classifier's `bytes`.**

### 🟢 Carried forward

## Pass 212 — 2026-10-15 — THE SATURATION WAS THE INSTRUMENT'S; THE AI LAYER IS BESIDE THE PLATFORM, NOT IN IT; AND EUROPE HAS TURNED FROM PERMISSION TO EVIDENCE

### 🆕 🔴 `T212-1` — "the field is saturated" is a claim about a reader, and this KB made it eight times

🔴 **Eight consecutive passes ran the task's discovery queries, found nothing new, and escalated
the conclusion: p204 "the query shape is wrong", p211 "the evidence is now overwhelming".** 🟢 **The
vocabulary observation was right — an education agent calls itself a tutor or a grader, not an
"agent".** 🔴 **The saturation conclusion was false, and one `WebFetch` to a GitHub topic page
falsified it.**

🟢 **`6` reads of GitHub's own HTML surface returned `105` rows, `54` verified first-hand, `22`
new agent rows, `17` rows of a shelf this KB never had, a new licence family, and `3` permissive
platforms that nine passes had reported as non-existent.**

🔵 **This is the fourth time in `15` passes that a published claim about the world turned out to be
a measurement of the instrument** *(p197's three numbers, p198's byte count, p211's "curl reaches
nothing", and now this)*. 🟢 **The pattern is specific enough to be a rule:** 🔴 **when a negative
result repeats, instrument the channel before publishing the generalisation.** 🔵 **A repeated
`0` is evidence about the probe at least as often as it is evidence about the population — and the
more confidently a corpus restates it, the less anyone re-tests it.**

🟢 **Deck line, and it generalises past this KB to any client building an intelligence function:**
*"The most expensive error in a knowledge base is not a wrong fact. It is a confident absence —
because nobody audits a gap."*

### 🆕 🔴 `T212-2` — the AI layer in education is not in the platforms, and will not be there in time

🔴 **`18` of the top `20` repositories in GitHub's `lms` topic — a `2 468`-repository
population — make no mention of AI or an LLM at all.** 🟢 **The whole AI surface in this industry
is adjacent: Moodle plugins, `MCP` servers, `LTI 1.3` tools, `xAPI` emitters.**

🔵 **Set that against the dates: EU AI Act high-risk classification of education AI reported
effective **August `2026`**; Vietnam's education-named obligations due **`2027-03-01`**; India's
compulsory AI curriculum from the **`2026`–`27`** session.** 🔴 **The installed platform base will
not have shipped an agent layer by any of them.**

🟢 **Consequence, and it is a positioning statement rather than an observation:** **the integration
seam is the product.** 🔵 **A studio that sells a platform replacement is selling against the
client's sunk cost and a multi-year migration. A studio that sells the seam — a plugin, a
connector, an emitter — ships in weeks against the system the client already runs, and lands on the
right side of a deadline.** 🟢 **The one counter-example found, `Selleo/mentingo` *(`MIT`, `95`★,
AI-native LMS)*, proves the shape is possible and its star count proves it is not yet the market.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T212-3` — Europe has moved from *may we use it* to *prove it works*, and that is a different sale

🟢 **The Council of Europe's `EDU IA` committee lists two deliverables that change the shape of an
EMEA engagement: a **European Reference Framework for the Evaluation of Educational Technologies**
*(in drafting)* and **a proposal for a legal instrument to regulate AI systems in education**
*(work programme `2026`–`27`)*. Its adopted **Recommendation on AI Literacy** carries `22`
principles in `4` themes and implements **Article `20` of the Framework Convention on AI**.**

🔵 **Read the sequence: `2024` was permission *(may AI be used in a classroom)*. `2026` is
**evaluation** *(does this specific system do what it claims, and can you show it)*.** 🔴 **Those
demand different artefacts.** 🟢 **A permission regime is satisfied by a policy document; an
evaluation regime is satisfied only by **learner-activity evidence in a standard schema** — which
is why `P212-4`'s discovery of the `xAPI`/LRS shelf in the same pass is not a coincidence but the
other half of the same finding.

🟡 **Honest caveat, carried because it decides whether this goes in a deck:** the adoption dates
are reported inconsistently across Council of Europe pages and press coverage, and neither
deliverable's text was reachable from this environment. 🟢 **The *direction* is safe to sell; the
*dates* must be re-verified at coe.int first.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T212-4` — the licence risk has moved from code to content, and no scanner follows it

🔴 **p211 established that this industry's licence danger is `GPL`-vs-`AGPL`, inside the GNU
family. That remains true for platforms.** 🆕 🔴 **For *agents* it is no longer the risk at all.**

🟢 **`9` of the `22` new agent rows this pass ship as **skills** — YAML headers plus prompt
scaffolding, installed into a coding agent or served over `MCP`.** 🔴 **The largest of them,
[`GarethManning/education-agent-skills`](https://github.com/GarethManning/education-agent-skills)
at `860`★ with `165` pedagogical skills, is **`CC-BY-SA-4.0`**: copyleft *content*.** 🟢 **A second,
`gen-mentor`, is **`CC0-1.0`** — more permissive than `MIT`.**

🔵 **Why this defeats tooling rather than merely complicating it:** 🔴 **licence scanners classify
software dependencies. A skill pack is neither a dependency nor software — it is prose in a
repository — so it is either skipped or mislabelled.** 🔴 **And the obligation it carries is the one
clients care about most: a client-specific fork of the pedagogy must itself be published
share-alike.** 🟢 **For a client who regards its teaching method as proprietary IP, `CC-BY-SA` is a
harder blocker than `AGPL`, and nothing in the stack will warn them.**

🔵 **Deck line:** *"In `2026` the licence that stops an education engagement is not on your code.
It is on your prompts."*

### 🆕 🔴 `T212-5` — a compulsory AI curriculum against a connectivity gap is the largest concrete opportunity in this KB

🟢 **India's Ministry of Education has made **AI and Computational Thinking mandatory for Classes
`3`–`8` from the `2026`–`27` session**, extending to Classes `9`–`10` in `2027`–`28`.** 🔴 **A
third-party `2026` report measures the system it lands in: **`64.7`%** of schools with computers,
**`63.5`%** with internet.**

🟢 **A mandate is a budget with a date; a `35`-point infrastructure gap is a specification.** 🔵
**Together they specify **offline-first, low-footprint, local-model delivery** — not a cloud tutor.
🟢 **The stack already exists and is permissive:**
[`learningequality/kolibri`](https://github.com/learningequality/kolibri) *(`MIT`, built for
offline)* plus a local model plus
[`pupilfirst/pupilfirst`](https://github.com/pupilfirst/pupilfirst) *(`MIT`, Indian-built)* or
[`frappe/lms`](https://github.com/frappe/lms) *(`AGPL-3.0`, Indian-built)*. 🔵 **See
[`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md) `P212-D`.**

🔵 **The wider pattern across all four regions, now stated as a rule:** 🟢 **in the Americas the
buying trigger is a **governance vacuum**; in EMEA it is an **evaluation obligation**; in APAC it is
a **curriculum or compliance date**.** 🔴 **One offer sold one way lands in exactly one of them.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T212-6` — a licence file can hold several grants partitioned by PATH, and both of this KB's orderings fail on it

🔴 **[`pupilfirst/pupilfirst`](https://github.com/pupilfirst/pupilfirst) *(`978`★)* names
`CC-BY-SA-4.0` in its first grant clause and `MIT` in its third and fourth. The code is `MIT`; only
`docs/` is `CC-BY-SA`.** 🔴 **This pass's own probe returned `CC-family` and would have blocked an
`MIT` platform.**

🟢 **Put beside p211's `odoo` trap, the pair is instructive:**

| repo | whole-body matcher | title-anchored matcher | 🟢 correct |
|---|---|---|---|
| `odoo/odoo` | 🔴 `GPL-3.0` | 🟢 `LGPL-3.0` | **`LGPL-3.0`** |
| 🆕 `pupilfirst/pupilfirst` | 🟡 `MIT` *(by volume)* | 🔴 `CC-BY-SA-4.0` | **`MIT` code / `CC-BY-SA-4.0` `docs/`** |

🔴 **Neither ordering is sufficient, and the two failures run in opposite directions — so no fixed
precedence rule saves you.** 🟢 **The only correct instrument reads the **partition**: a licence
file may bind different grants to different paths, and a repository's grant is therefore a
*function of the file path*, not a scalar.** 🔵 **`P212-A` in [`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md)
carries the fix, and it is three lines of logic, not a rewrite.

### 🆕 🔴 `T212-7` — LATAM's open-source ceiling is `6` stars, and that is information

🟢 **Three GitHub repository searches in Spanish and Portuguese, sorted by stars: `308` results,
top star counts of `6`, `4` and `2`.** 🔴 **There is no production-scale education-AI repository in
the region's own languages.**

🔵 **Stated as a positive, because it is one:** 🟢 **a LATAM engagement is differentiated by the
`87`%/`26`% governance gap, by `chamilo` *(the LMS actually installed in Spanish-speaking
institutions)*, and by Spanish/Portuguese language capability — **not** by regional code, which
does not exist above hobby scale. 🔵 **The code comes from Munich, Berkeley and Hong Kong. Saying
so is more credible than implying a regional ecosystem that three measured queries say is absent.**

### 🟢 Carried forward

🟢 **`T211-1`** — in education the dangerous licence mistake is reading `AGPL` as `GPL` and
discovering network copyleft after shipping a hosted deployment. 🟢 **Still true for platforms;
`T212-4` extends it to content.** 🟢 **`T211-2`** — a mature corpus's dominant failure mode is
propagation, not error: `6` of `7` wrong cells named a repository whose correct grant this KB had
already found. 🆕 🔵 **`T212-1` adds its twin: the second failure mode is a confident absence.**
🟢 **`T211-3`** — adoption outruns governance in all four regions. 🟢 **`T211-5`** — the market
number is a range; a point figure with a decimal place is a tell.

## Pass 211 — 2026-10-15 — THE GNU FAMILY IS THIS INDUSTRY'S REAL LICENCE RISK, AND ADOPTION IS OUTRUNNING GOVERNANCE IN ALL FOUR REGIONS

### 🆕 🔴 `T211-1` — the licence risk in education is `GPL`-vs-`AGPL`, not open-vs-proprietary

🔴 **`5` of the `7` wrong grant cells found in this pass's full-column audit are confusions
*inside* the GNU family.** 🟢 **`0` were permissive-vs-copyleft mixups, and the permissive half
has now measured clean four passes running.**

🔵 **The mechanism is textual: `GPL-3.0`, `LGPL-3.0` and `AGPL-3.0` open with near-identical
boilerplate and differ in a clause most scanners never reach.** 🔴 **The consequence is
operational: the three licences differ on exactly the question every education deployment turns
on — *does serving students over a network count as distribution?*** 🟢 **For an industry whose
default delivery model is a hosted, student-facing platform, that is the only licence question
that changes an architecture.**

🔵 **Deck line:** *"In education the dangerous licence mistake is not using closed software by
accident — it is reading `AGPL` as `GPL` and discovering network copyleft after you have
shipped a hosted deployment."*

### 🆕 🔴 `T211-2` — a knowledge base's dominant failure mode is propagation, not error

🔴 **`6` of this pass's `7` wrong cells name a repository whose correct grant this KB had
**already found and published elsewhere**.** 🟢 **The research was right; the shelf was stale.**

🔵 **This generalises past this KB and is worth saying to a client building their own
intelligence layer:** 🟢 **once a corpus is append-only and large, the marginal value of
*another sweep* falls below the marginal value of *reconciling what you already know*.** 🔴 **An
audit that only looks for new errors will keep finding the same old ones re-served from stale
rows.**

### 🟢 `T211-3` — adoption is outrunning governance in all four regions, and the gap is the market

| region | adoption | governance | gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| **LATAM** | `87`% of institutions use AI *(UNESCO IESALC, `200` institutions, `19` countries)* | 🔴 **`26`%** have any formal framework | 🔴 **`61` pts** |
| **North America** | near-universal student use | 🔴 **`10`%** of institutions with formal guidelines; `71`% of US teachers untrained | 🔴 **largest reported** |
| **Global** | student usage reported `66`% → `92`% *(`2024`→`2025`)* | analyst maturity read **`35`/`100`** — most institutions still piloting | 🔴 consistent |
| **EMEA · APAC** | 🟡 not measured by the queries that reached here | 🟢 **but both have DATED obligations** *(EU AI Act high-risk, Aug `2026`; Singapore teacher module end-`2026`; Vietnam `2027-03-01`)* | 🔵 **inverted** |

🔵 **The inversion in EMEA/APAC is the useful part.** 🟢 **In the Americas the buying trigger is
the governance vacuum; in EMEA and APAC it is a compliance date.** 🔴 **The same offer sold the
same way in both will land in only one.**

### 🆕 🟡 `T211-4` — a national AI-education programme has been *reduced*, and it is the first on these shelves

🟢 **South Korea's AI digital textbook rollout has been rolled back:** Korean and home economics
dropped, social studies and science delayed to `2027`, adjustments applied from `2026`, after
education governors asked for a slower pace. 🔴 **Sources are `2024`–`2025` and the `2026` status
could not be verified from here.**

🔵 **Carried because every other national datapoint in this KB is an expansion, and a deck built
only on expansions is selling a trend rather than describing one.** 🟢 **The honest framing:
national programmes are being *re-phased* as they meet classrooms, and a studio that plans for
re-phasing wins the second contract.**

### 🟢 `T211-5` — the market number is a range, and quoting a point figure is a tell

🔴 **Three "2026" global figures from three houses: `$6.4`B, `$10.6`B, `$11.4`B.** 🟢 **All agree
on `25`%+ growth.** 🔵 **Use the range and the direction. A single figure with a decimal place
signals the deck was built from one vendor's press release.**

### Carried forward, unchanged

🟢 **Vietnam remains the only jurisdiction on these shelves with a compliance date that *names*
education** *(in force `2026-03-01`; automated assessment and behavioural monitoring high-risk;
due `2027-03-01`)*. 🟢 **The EU AI Act's high-risk classification of education AI, reported
effective August `2026`, is the largest single dated obligation in this KB.** 🟢 **p210's
commons-at-the-seams finding stands: the platforms are healthy and the connective tissue is
`1`-star.**

## Pass 210 — 2026-10-15 — A COMPLIANCE DEADLINE WITH EDUCATION NAMED IN IT, AND THE COMMONS IS ROTTING AT THE SEAMS

### 🆕 🔴 `T210-1` — Vietnam is the first jurisdiction to put a COMPLIANCE date on education AI, and its grandfather clause has already expired

🆕 🔴 **Vietnam's AI law has been in force since `2026-03-01`; its high-risk list NAMES education,
including automated assessment and behavioural monitoring; compliance is due `2027-03-01`; and
systems already operating in education before `2026-08-15` received an extra `6` months**
*(search-snippet, unverified)*.

🔵 **Every dated instrument on these shelves until now was a rollout date, a guidance date or an
enforcement-machinery date. This is the first DEADLINE — a day on which a non-conformant education
system becomes unlawful.** 🟢 **And the grandfather cut-off is in the past, so the population is
closed: whatever was running in Vietnamese education on `2026-08-15` is the set that gets the grace
period, and nothing deployed after it does.** 🔴 **`~16` months to conformity for an installed base
that cannot grow.**

🟢 **Trend statement: regulatory pressure on education AI has moved from DECLARATION to DEADLINE,
and it happened in APAC first, not the EU.** 🔵 **The EU named education high-risk earlier and is
still arguing about which date applies to it (`2026-08-02` vs `2027-12-02`); Vietnam shipped a date
and a grace window.** 🔴 **A KB that tracked only the EU would have called this trend wrong.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T210-2` — LATAM's defining shape is a `61`-point gap between adoption and governance

🆕 🟢 **`87`% of `200` higher-education institutions across `19` LATAM countries use AI somewhere;
`26`% have a formal AI strategy** *(UNESCO IESALC)*. 🔵 **Corroborated downward through the system:
Brazil `53`% of administrators using AI against `22`% of schools with guidelines; teachers in
Brazil, Chile, Colombia and Costa Rica at `52`–`56`% against an OECD average of `36`% (TALIS
`2024`); `~9` in `10` `15`-year-olds across `13` countries using AI chatbots for schoolwork (PISA
`2025`).**

🟢 **Trend statement: in LATAM the adoption question is CLOSED and the governance question has not
been opened.** 🔵 **This inverts the sales motion every other region needs. There is no case to
make for AI — teachers, students and administrators are already using it, ahead of the OECD. The
deliverable is retrofit: policy, evidence, equity and risk controls over a deployed base.**
🔴 **And the equity dimension is measured, not rhetorical — Argentina `75`% vs `42`% by
socio-economic class — so an equity audit is a priced component, not a slide.**

🔵 **Why this is a durable trend and not a snapshot: it is sourced from UNESCO, TALIS and PISA
rather than vendor aggregators, which makes it the best-evidenced regional statement on these
shelves.** 🟢 **It also now has an institutional venue — UNESCO's Observatory on AI in Education for
LATAM, launched `2026-04-14` in Santiago.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T210-3` — open-source education has robust PLATFORMS and abandoned CONNECTIVE TISSUE

🟢 **`ACTION PV`'s first measurement, across the `20` rows this KB has read first-hand, found the
risk is not where it was expected** *(full table in [`repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md) p210)*:

- 🟢 **Every PLATFORM is fine:** `edx-platform` `8.2k`★, `moodle` `7.5k`★, `sakai` `1.2k`★, `chamilo` `1.0k`★, `kolibri` `1.1k`★, `openeducat_erp` `889`★, `Artemis` `818`★, `frappe/education` `666`★, `opencast` `508`★.
- 🔴 **Every fragile row is a SEAM:** `opensalt/OB3DefinitionWidget` **`1`★ carrying `19` recipes**, `ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp` **`48`★ carrying `23`**, `Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/edfi-oneroster` **`6`★ carrying `13`**, `openfun/ralph` **`51`★ carrying `20`**.

🔵 **Trend statement: the education commons sustains large institutional platforms and does not
sustain the glue between them — the badge widget, the MCP tutor server, the roster adapter, the
learning-record store.** 🟢 **The reason is structural: a university funds Moodle because it runs
on Moodle; nobody's budget line is "the `xAPI` bridge", so the glue is written once by one person
and left.**

🟢 **The commercial inversion, and it is the most useful thing in this pass:** 🔵 **the seams are
exactly what a studio builds and owns anyway.** 🔴 **No client pays Globant to maintain Moodle.**
🟢 **It pays for integration — and the measurement says the integration layer of this entire
vertical has NO INCUMBENT. A hardened, permissively-licensed `xAPI`→knowledge-tracing bridge, or a
maintained `OneRoster`/`Ed-Fi` adapter, is simultaneously the fix for this KB's own fragility and a
reusable asset across every engagement in the industry.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T210-4` — grant VISIBILITY is now a measured defect, and `STATED` is not `BODIED`

🔴 **`3` of the `20` rows read first-hand — `15`% — have NO GitHub-classified licence:
`OpenOLAT` (`Apache-2.0`, `10.7` KB body), `openeducat/openeducat_erp` (`LGPL-3.0`, `8.05` KB
body), `frappe/education` (a `19`-byte statement).** 🟢 **p209 found the first and called it a trap;
two passes make it a rate.**

🔵 **Consequence for the industry, not just for this KB: a client's SBOM or licence-scan tool keyed
on GitHub's detection — which is what most are — reports NO LICENCE for roughly one in seven of the
major open-source education platforms, and will then block two of them.** 🟢 **Shipping the blob
URL, byte count and read date beside every grant is now standard practice on these shelves.**

🆕 🟡 **And a new axis the `frappe/education` reading forced into existence:**
🟢 **`BODIED`** — the repository ships the verbatim licence text; the grant is self-contained.
🟡 **`STATED`** — the repository names a licence and ships none of it. `frappe/education`'s entire
`license.txt` is `19` bytes: `License: GNU GPL V3`. 🔴 **No body, no holder, no year, no terms.**
🔵 **Legible intent, incorporated by reference to a document the repository does not contain — and
GitHub's classifier declines to call it a licence at all.** 🟢 **Fine for evaluation; a diligence
flag in any deliverable a client's counsel reviews.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T210-5` — "North America" is three procurement systems, and the pitch does not transfer

🟢 **`ACTION PW` closed this pass** *(detail in [`intel/market.md`](market.md) p210)*. 🔵 **The
three systems:**

| country | unit of procurement | the lever |
|---|---|---|
| **USA** | the STATE, restricted by AGE BAND | `134` bills / `31` states in `2026`; Maryland's `24` districts by fall `2026` |
| **Mexico** | the FEDERATION — one buyer, one language | SEP consultation open since `2026-07`; UNESCO LoI `2026-09-07`; 🔴 **DOF device ban in force `2026-11-03`** |
| **Canada** | the PROVINCE — and in Ontario, the BOARD | 🟢 **Ontario's MANDATORY educator PD topic for `2026-27` with no ministry memorandum to implement it** |

🔵 **Trend statement: regional labels are a liability in this industry.** 🔴 **A single "North
America" education deliverable cannot exist — the per-state, per-grade policy switch that a US
engagement requires is the wrong abstraction for a federal Mexican buyer and meaningless to a
Canadian province.** 🟢 **Build the policy layer as a pluggable jurisdiction profile, not a US
feature with exceptions.**

🔴 **And two informed gaps, stated rather than papered over: Alberta has no locatable provincial
AI-in-education guidance, and Mexico has no final basic-education AI guidelines — only proposed
ones.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T210-6` — compliance-led EMEA, strategy-led Gulf, and APAC's public opinion runs backwards

🔵 **Three regional pitch shapes, now distinguishable:**
🟢 **EU — sell CONFORMITY.** The AI Office and Member-State authorities have been enforcing since
`2026-08-02`, with education's admissions/assessment/grading uses high-risk and conformity
assessment required pre-deployment.
🟢 **Gulf — sell ALIGNMENT.** UAE's National AI Strategy `2031` and Saudi SDAIA's governance
framework are state-led and capital-rich, with 🔴 **no education-specific rule found — an informed
gap.** 🔵 **Same deliverable as the EU, opposite framing: national-strategy contribution, not
Annex III defence.**
🆕 🟢 **South Africa — sell READINESS.** A Draft National AI Policy out for comment since
`2026-04-10`, with an explicit education-and-skills limb (STEAM curricula, community AI centres).
🔴 **A draft, so influence work rather than compliance work — and the first actionable African
instrument these shelves have carried.**

🆕 🟡 **The APAC inversion, which a studio will get wrong by default:** 🔴 **Ipsos's Education
Monitor `2026` finds Australia and New Zealand show HIGHER public support for banning AI in schools
than the Asian markets surveyed.** 🔵 **The two APAC markets easiest to sell into on language and
contracting have the most hostile publics; the harder markets are the receptive ones.** 🟢 **In ANZ
lead with governance, evidence and opt-out design. In Southeast Asia lead with capability.**

🟢 **Seventh consecutive pass returning `0`–`2` new candidate education agents and `0` accepted;

🔴 **A separate and sharper channel finding: `github trending {industry} AI {year}` has returned no
education-specific trending list in seven attempts, and this pass explains why.** 🟢 **Education
repositories do not trend. `Moodle` at `7.5k`★ after two decades is this vertical's ceiling, while
a coding agent clears `100k`★ in a quarter.** 🔵 **The trending channel is a popularity instrument
and this industry is not popular — the query shape is wrong for the vertical, not unlucky.**
🟢 **Retire it; spend the budget on verification, where `20` rows have now been read by hand and
`20` of `20` were correct.**

## Pass 209 — 2026-10-14 — THE DISCOVERY CHANNEL IS SATURATED SIX TIMES OVER, AND THE LOBBY HAS NAMED ITS CLAUSE

### 🆕 🟢 `T209-1` — Italy has put a DATE on classroom AI, and its own universities are `87`% short of it

🆕 🔴 **Italy's practical rollout in schools begins `2026-08-03`**, under ministry guidelines framed
as regulating rather than banning classroom AI. 🟢 **Set that against p158's measurement that
`13`% of Italian universities hold operational AI governance.** 🔵 **A dated national obligation
meeting a measured `87`-point readiness gap, in a single language and a single procurement
perimeter, is the most actionable EMEA shape on these shelves** — more actionable than the AI Act
itself, because the Act's education deadline is still contested *(`2026-08-02` vs `2027-12-02`)*
and this date is not.

### 🆕 🟢 `T209-2` — for the first time this KB can name the LOBBY and the CLAUSE, not just the law

🆕 🔴 **The European EdTech Alliance (`200`+ reported member companies) participated in AI Act
consultations and pushed back on ANNUAL EXTERNAL AUDITS specifically.** 🔵 **Nine passes of EMEA
regulatory intelligence produced dates, annexes and prohibitions; this is the first CONTESTED TERM
with an identified interest behind it.** 🟢 **Audit frequency is the variable that decides whether
Annex III education compliance is a one-off project or an annuity** — and an evidence pipeline
built for annual re-audit is the same artefact either way, sold as obligation if the clause
survives and as differentiator if it does not. 🔴 **Track the clause, not only the deadline.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T209-3` — K-12 is a different BUSINESS from higher education, and now there is a number for it

🆕 **AI in K-12 priced apart from the whole for the first time on this shelf: `$0.7`B in `2026` →
`$18.5`B by `2036`, `38.1`% CAGR** *(Future Market Insights, search-snippet, unverified)*.
🔵 **K-12 is `~6`% of today's AI-in-education market and compounds `12` points faster than it.**
🟢 **Trend statement: the money is in higher education and the growth is in K-12, so they are two
commercial motions — present budget versus future budget — and the common error is one price book
for both.** 🔴 **Compounding is also why the US age-band restrictions matter commercially: a K-6
chatbot ban *(Katy ISD, effective `2026-27`)* bites the fastest-growing slice.

### 🟢 `T209-4` — the discovery channel is saturated on a SIXTH reading, and the budget has moved

🟢 **Sixth consecutive pass returning `0`–`1` new qualifying education agents, and `0` new
foundational repos.** 🔵 **Six independent readings make this a property of the field, not a run of
quiet weeks: the permissively-licensed education agent population this KB can reach is
ENUMERATED.** 🔴 **Which inverts the standing instruction to search first — the marginal value of
another discovery sweep is near zero, while `142` of `179` shelf rows had never been read against
their own repositories.**

🟢 **p209 moved the budget accordingly and it paid: `10`/`10` recipe-critical masked rows confirmed
first-hand** *(`agents/top.md` p209)*, plus a secondary source caught publishing `frappe/lms` as
`MIT` when it is `AGPL-3.0`. 🔵 **Trend for this KB's own method: in a saturated field, VERIFICATION
produces more defensible value per pass than DISCOVERY.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T209-5` — the tooling trend: machine-readable licence metadata cannot be trusted alone

🔴 **`OpenOLAT` ships a verbatim `Apache License 2.0` blob (`197` lines, `10.7` KB) and GitHub's
classifier names NO licence for it** — the sidebar shows an unnamed `License` link, and the API's
`license` field is the same detection. 🔵 **So the industry-standard cheap signal produces a FALSE
UNGRANTED on one of the most-depended-on platforms in European higher education.** 🟢 **Generalised
rule, and it applies to client due-diligence as much as to this KB: an SBOM or licence-scan that
reports "no licence" for a major platform is reporting its own blind spot.** 🔴 **Four of ten
GitHub pages read this pass also returned an EMPTY `Languages` section, so the unreliability is not
confined to one field.**

## Pass 208 — 2026-10-14 — THE ORGAN RULE GENERALISES AND ITS LIST WAS INCOMPLETE; AND A LINE IS NOT A RECORD

### 🆕 🔴 `T208-1` — `ACTION PQ` fires on limb `1`, and corrects p207's own list

🟢 **p207 retired the pan-regional query and replaced it with: *run the query once per organ that
could issue the instrument — ministry, council, legislature, state.* `ACTION PQ` asked whether
that holds on the two regions that had never filled from a regional sweep. Limb `1` wanted `3`+
dated instruments EMEA and APAC do not carry.** 🔴 **It returned `9`, and `5` of them come from
organ types p207's list cannot name:**

| organ type | the instrument it issued | why p207's list misses it |
|---|---|---|
| 🆕 🔴 **supranational executive** | the **European Commission**'s revised *Ethical guidelines on the use of AI and data in teaching and learning for educators* *(first ed. `2022`, revision `2026`, written by the **European Digital Education Hub**'s working group)* | 🔴 **It sits ABOVE every ministry in EMEA, and in the EU it is also the only layer that issues BINDING law** |
| 🆕 🔴 **standing conference of subnational ministers** | Germany's **KMK** *Handlungsempfehlung … zum Umgang mit KI in schulischen Bildungsprozessen* *(Oct `2024`, all `16` Länder via the `Ad hoc-AG KI` under NRW's lead)* | 🔴 **A federation with no competent national ministry has no ministry to name.** 🔵 **Same shape as Canada's `CMEC`, which p207 recorded as *"no federal instrument"*** |
| 🆕 🟡 **agency or institute under a ministry** | Spain's **INTEF** *Guía … v2.0* *(`1` Sept `2026`)* and *Orientaciones … formación docente, Actualización `2026`* | 🟡 The ministry (`MEFP`) is in the credits; **the issuer is the institute** |
| 🆕 🟡 **examination board + curriculum institute** | India's **CBSE** circular and **NCERT**'s Grades `11`–`12` textbook team, under the MoE's `AI & CT` mandate | 🟡 The mandate is the ministry's; **the documents a school actually follows are the board's** |
| 🆕 🟡 **the organ that ANSWERS rather than issues** | Singapore **MOE**'s written parliamentary reply *(`25` Feb `2026`)* and forum-letter replies *(`13` Apr, `18` Sep `2026`)* | 🔴 **There is no titled document to find.** The ministry's position exists only as replies |

🔵 **So the axis is not the language *(p206, refuted by p207)*, not the country *(p205,
subsumed)*, and not quite the organ either — it is the ORGAN TYPE, and which types exist is set
by how a country allocates authority over education.** 🟢 **Federal systems put it in a
conference of states; EU members have a supranational layer above the ministry; ministries with
technical institutes put it in the institute; common-law systems answer in parliament.** 🔴
**Enumerate the organ types for the jurisdiction BEFORE searching, or the sweep will find the
country's weakest instrument and miss its binding one.**
[`evidence/organ-type-sweep.2026-10-14-p208.tsv`](../evidence/organ-type-sweep.2026-10-14-p208.tsv).

### 🆕 🔴 `T208-2` — in this KB, a markdown LINE is not a record. Three instruments inherited the same defect this pass

🔴 **The same error appeared three times in one pass, in three unrelated instruments, and it is
the reason `Gap 467` was mis-read for three passes:**

| instrument | what it treated as a record | what that cost |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 the licence audit *(p205–p207)* | the repository, as the **union** of every line naming it | 🔴 **`152` of `179` rows unauditable**, and `2` wrong cells survived three audits |
| 🔴 its `24` historical false positives | the **line** | 🔴 a token belonging to the previous item in a list, read as this repository's grant |
| 🆕 🔴 p208's own `ungranted` population *(`ACTION PS`)* | the **line** | 🔴 **`26` of `26` "recoveries" were artefacts** — every one is a repository this KB already publishes a grant for |

🟢 **The fix is identical in all three cases and it is not a better regex: the unit of comparison
must be the CELL — a repository plus the licence column of its OWN table row.** 🔵 **Measured:
per-cell flags `12` candidates of which `6` are true (`50`%); per-line flags `61` of which `7`
are true (`11`%); per-repo flagged `24` across three passes of which `0` were true.** 🔴 **And
neither new comparator dominates — `openfun/ralph`'s wrong cell is a PROSE sentence inside a
recipe, invisible to the per-cell reader.**
[`compose/code/p208-seeded-error-audit/`](../compose/code/p208-seeded-error-audit/README.md).

### 🆕 🔴 `T208-3` — the first true positives in four passes, and both are corrections that were never propagated

🔴 [`towardsai/ai-tutor-app`](https://github.com/towardsai/ai-tutor-app) was published **`MIT` in
`6` cells across `4` files**; its own blob is **`Apache-2.0`, `11 386` B**. 🟢 **Its `LICENSE`
was added ONCE — commit `b151505`, `28` June `2026` — and never replaced, and the sidebar reads
`Apache-2.0`.** 🔴 **So the cell was never right; this is not a relicensing the KB missed.**

🔴 [`openfun/ralph`](https://github.com/openfun/ralph) was published **`Apache-2.0`** in a p193
recipe step; its blob is **`MIT`, `LICENSE.md`, `1 094` B**.

🔵 **In BOTH cases this KB already holds the right value somewhere else** — `Apache-2.0` for
`ai-tutor-app` on the agent shelf, complete with an `md5`; `MIT` for `ralph` at some fifteen
other sites. 🔴 **The wrong value survived three dedicated audits precisely BECAUSE the right
value existed: it was in the union those audits compared against.** 🟢 **The class, named: a
correction in this KB is PUBLISHED, not PROPAGATED — and the composition recipes, which are what
a client engagement actually reads, are the last place a correction arrives.** `7` cells
corrected in place behind `<!--corrected-->` markers:
[`evidence/licence-cell-corrections.2026-10-14-p208.tsv`](../evidence/licence-cell-corrections.2026-10-14-p208.tsv).

🔵 **Consequence that is not about this KB:** `MIT` → `Apache-2.0` keeps the deliverable
permissive, so *"usable"* survives. 🔴 **What does not survive is the obligation list — `Apache-2.0`
carries `NOTICE` preservation, a change-statement duty and an express patent grant with a
termination clause, and a client told *"`MIT`, do what you like"* has been told the wrong thing.**

### 🆕 🟡 `T208-4` — `ACTION PT`'s falsifier landed between its own limbs, and that was the informative outcome

🟢 **`5` deliberate errors seeded into a scratch copy; limb `1` was *"catches `4`+"*, limb `2`
*"catches `≤2`"*.** 🔴 **It caught `3`.** 🟢 **And the `3`/`2` split is not noise: the `3` caught
are the three seeded on rows whose true family is printed at exactly one site, and the `2` missed
are the two seeded on rows where it is printed at many.**

🔵 **This is `T268` again — a falsifier that names a threshold must name the population it counts
over.** 🔴 **`5` seeds drawn from a population that is `85`% unauditable cannot land on either
limb**, and the fix is to state the limb as a RATE over a declared stratum: *"catches `≥80`% of
seeds placed on single-site rows"* would have fired cleanly, as would *"catches `≥1` seed on a
multi-site row"*. 🟢 **Both are now how p208 writes its own actions.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T208-5` — Singapore's ministry says, in its own words, the thing this KB inferred from Korea

🟢 **p207's APAC cell carried a rule inferred from outcomes: mandates aimed at TEACHER capability
survive, mandates aimed at CLASSROOM TOOLS do not.** 🆕 **MOE now states the tool half directly.**
🔴 **In a written reply to Parliament on `25` February `2026`, the Education Minister said the
ministry has NO local data on whether student AI use is linked to weaker cognitive ability and is
studying the impact.** 🟢 **Its forum replies of `13` April and `18` September `2026` say learning
stays **print-first** at younger ages, AI is introduced **progressively**, and teachers may
**deliberately withhold** AI where it does not serve the learning goal.**

🔵 **Read as a procurement signal this is unambiguous: the most AI-forward education ministry in
APAC is slowing classroom tools while continuing to fund teacher capability.** 🟢 **Sell the
teacher layer, instrument the student layer for evidence, and expect to be asked for the evidence
— because the ministry has said in Parliament that it does not have it yet.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T208-6` — India's mandate is dated, national, and lands on a base where one school in three has no internet

🟢 **`AI & Computational Thinking` is MANDATORY for Classes `3`–`8` from the `2026-27` session and
Classes `9`–`10` from `2027-28`** *(announced `29` October `2025` by the MoE's Department of School
Education and Literacy)*, with **NCERT** writing Grades `11`–`12` textbooks and **SOAR** carrying
educator AI literacy. 🔴 **And the base it lands on, from `UDISE+` `2024-25`: `64.7`% of schools
have computers, `58`% functional for teaching, `63.5`% have internet connectivity.**

🔵 **That single pair of numbers settles an architecture argument before it starts.** 🔴 **A
cloud-first tutor cannot serve a national Class-`3` mandate when a third of the schools cannot
reach it.** 🟢 **Offline-first — `learningequality/kolibri` (`MIT`), content packaged and synced
rather than streamed — stops being a sustainability preference and becomes the only shape that
satisfies the instrument.**

### 🆕 🟡 `T208-7` — EMEA's instrument stack is three deep and only one layer is law

🟡 **A client asking *"what must we comply with in Europe"* now has three answers at three
altitudes: the EU **AI Act** *(law — Annex III high-risk for assessment, admissions and
learning-outcome evaluation, `2` December `2027` under Reg. (EU) `2026/1744`, with Article `50`
marking already live and the education emotion-recognition ban in force since `2` February
`2025`)*; the **Commission's ethical guidelines** *(not law, but the text an inspectorate and a
works council will quote)*; and the **national frameworks** — France's `MEN` cadre, Germany's
`KMK` recommendation, Spain's `INTEF` guides.* 🔵 **Only the first is enforceable, and it is the
only one that is not teacher-facing.** 🟢 **Practical consequence: the compliance work is sold
against the Act and the ADOPTION work is sold against the national framework, and conflating them
is how an EMEA proposal loses both audiences.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T208-8` — `ACTION PR` discharged: the byte convention, stated once

🟢 **This KB's convention, now declared rather than implied: a byte count is the licence blob
AS SERVED by `raw.githubusercontent.com` at `HEAD`, trailing newline INCLUDED.** 🔵 **`11` of the
`15` conflicts p207 adjudicated were one trailing `LF`** — the higher cell is the blob as served,
the lower is the blob minus its final newline. 🔴 **Two cells differing by exactly `1` byte are
therefore the same measurement under two conventions, not a correction**, and `<!--corrected-->`
must not be spent on them.

### 🔴 What this pass measured and did NOT find

🔴 **The agent discovery channel is saturated for a FIFTH consecutive measurement.** `top open
source AI agents education 2026 github MIT` and `github trending education AI 2026` returned
`microsoft/ai-agents-for-beginners`, `pguso/agents-from-scratch`,
`rohitg00/ai-engineering-from-scratch` and `avinash201199/free-ai-agents-resources` — **all four
already on this shelf, all four on the teach-about-AI axis this KB rejects as an industry asset.**
🟢 **One genuinely new verified repository, and it is rejected under that same rule:**
[`naorpeled/aitutor`](https://github.com/naorpeled/aitutor) — **`MIT`, `1 067` B, `217`★, Go,
*"vimtutor like guide for AI"*** — a terminal tutorial about context windows, MCP and subagents.
🔵 **Real, permissive, well made, and not an education-industry asset.**

🔴 **No permissive education ERP, for the sixth pass running.** The vertical sweep returned
**OpenEduCat** *(`LGPL-3.0`)* and **Chamilo** *(GPL)* — both already shelved, both copyleft.

🔴 **Informed gap, written down rather than left silent:** the **eLeDia Moodle MCP connector** is
presented as open source but is **not on GitHub** *(it is distributed from `eledia.de`)*, so every
instrument this KB owns — the locator, the census, the per-cell comparator — cannot reach it. 🔵
**A grant this KB cannot read is not a grant it can recommend building on.**

## Pass 207 — 2026-10-14 — IT WAS NEVER THE LANGUAGE. IT IS WHICH ORGAN YOU NAME

### 🆕 🔴 `T207-1` — p206 changed two variables at once and credited the wrong one

🔴 **p206's headline finding — `Gap 468`, *"language is a cross-channel axis"* — does not
survive a control.** 🟢 **p205 ran *"LATAM"* in English and got nothing; p206 ran *"MEC
inteligência artificial educação diretrizes"* in Portuguese and got four instruments; p206
concluded the variable was LANGUAGE.** 🔴 **It had changed two things: the language, and the fact
that it named a COUNTRY and a MINISTRY.**

🟢 **p207 ran the missing cells of that design. Three query shapes, one language — English:**

| shape | query | instruments | what came back |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **pan-regional** | `AI education LATAM 2026 adoption regulation players` | **`0`** | adoption surveys (UNESCO IESALC, Digital Education Council). No national instrument |
| 🟡 **country, no organ** | `Brazil artificial intelligence in schools policy 2026` | **`3`** | 🆕 `PL 2397/2026` in the **Senado** · 🆕 **Paraná's** state technical AI course · `PBIA 2024-2028` |
| 🟢 **country + organ** | `Brazil MEC artificial intelligence education national guidelines 2026` | **`5`** | 🆕 **`Parecer CNE/CP nº 15/2026`** · the April basic-education document · the March sandbox · the early-childhood recommendation |

🔵 **The three result sets are very nearly DISJOINT.** 🔴 **Naming the ministry returns the
ministry's instruments; naming only the country returns the legislature's and the states';
naming the region returns neither, only researchers writing about the region.** 🟢 **The English
country+organ query returned MORE than p206's Portuguese one, including the instrument p206
missed.**

🟢 **Confirmed on a second country in both directions:** Mexico returns the same four SEP items
in English *and* in Spanish; Canada returns its provincial guidance from a plain English query.
🟢 **Language did nothing in four of four cells.**
[`evidence/query-shape-control.2026-10-14-p207.tsv`](../evidence/query-shape-control.2026-10-14-p207.tsv).

🔵 **So the axis is not language and it is not even country — it is the ORGAN.** 🟢 **A search
index is organised around named institutions, because that is how the documents are titled and
reported.** 🔴 **This retires `Gap 468` as stated and replaces it: run the query once per organ
that could plausibly issue the instrument — ministry, council, legislature, state.** 🟢 **Brazil
has all four, and three passes of this KB saw one of them.**

🟢 **p205's *"per-country beats pan-regional"* was right and is now subsumed: per-ORGAN beats
per-country beats pan-regional.** 🔴 **Language stays worth testing where a human wrote the
artefact in one language and no index translated it — which is still true of the LICENCE channel,
where p206's two Portuguese `AGPL` bodies were real.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T207-2` — three passes, three populations, three methods, `0` true positives

🟢 **p207 pointed the audit at the half of the licence column nobody had checked.** p205 audited
the `60` highest-★ rows; p206 audited `281` **copyleft** rows; both found `0` wrong cells. The
standing hypothesis was that the **permissive** half would be dirtier, because a row published
as `AGPL-3.0` has no upside to being wrong and a row published as `MIT` does.

| pass | population | method | reported | 🟢 **true** | 🔴 false |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| p205 | `60` highest-★ rows | regex over the licence column | `3` | **`0`** | `3` |
| p206 | `281` copyleft rows | table-row extraction, blobs at `HEAD` | `3` | **`0`** | `3` |
| 🆕 **p207** | **`199` permissive rows**, `185` bodies read | blob-first, fixed instrument | **`18`** | **`0`** | **`18`** |

🔴 **The hypothesis is refuted. The permissive half is as clean as the copyleft half.** 🟢 **`24`
reported mismatches across three passes; `0` true.** 🔵 **`Gap 467` stops being a suspicion about
one method and becomes a measured property of this column.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T207-3` — the false positives got MORE seductive, and that is the finding

🟢 **p206's false positives were incidental — a university named `MIT` in prose, a vendored
third-party file.** 🔴 **p207's two best candidates were neither. Both were rows whose PROSE IS
ABOUT A LICENCE DIVERGENCE, which is the only reason the word `MIT` is in them.**

🔴 [`ShuyunYuan/talentcoach`](https://github.com/ShuyunYuan/talentcoach) — the audit saw a row
mentioning `MIT` over a body reserving all rights. 🟢 **The KB publishes it as `VIEW-ONLY /
DO-NOT-USE` and had already written up *"its view-only negation of `MIT`'s verb list"* and the
verbatim-copied MIT warranty paragraph.**

🔴 [`cccareers/open-source-curriculum`](https://github.com/cccareers/open-source-curriculum) —
the audit saw `MIT` over a `CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0` deed. 🟢 **The KB publishes `CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0` and
records the `package.json`-vs-`LICENSE` split as `P204-1`: *"the divergence IS the row"*.**

🔴 A third, [`learning-commons-org/knowledge-graph`](https://github.com/learning-commons-org/knowledge-graph),
is this KB's own `P965`: **a file at a licence filename can be a FRAMEWORK, not a grant.**

🔵 **The rows that look most like findings are the rows where the curation already happened.**
🔴 **Had p207 shipped its first draft, it would have replaced three correct, hard-won cells with
two wrong ones and a redundant one.** 🟢 **The rule, sharpened: when a flagged row's prose is
about licensing, assume the ROW is the finding and the audit is the error.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T207-4` — there is a worse failure mode than `UNRECOGNISED`, and p205 and p206 built it

🔴 **`T206-4` called a silent `UNRECOGNISED` on a non-OSI family *"the single worst failure mode
a licence reader has"*.** 🟢 **It is not. Feed p206's classifier an `MIT` body carrying a
`Commons Clause` rider and it returns `MIT`** — no suffix, no flag, nothing to notice. 🔴 **A
confident clean permissive answer is worse than `UNRECOGNISED`, because `UNRECOGNISED` at least
asks to be looked at.**

🔵 **And it is structural, not incidental.** 🟢 **p205's repair was to anchor on the title line;
p206's was to undress it. A rider does not REPLACE a licence, it AMENDS one — so the title line
it sits above belongs to the licence being restricted.** 🔴 **The better the anchoring gets, the
more reliably it finds the inner licence and misses the rider.** 🟢 **The rider test must run
BEFORE the title walk, and now does.**

🟢 **Three more defects, all in the LOOKUP TABLE rather than the anchoring logic — which nobody
had re-read in two passes of fixing how the title line is found:** 🔴 `D11` the dot in
`cc0 1\\.0 universal` was **double-escaped**, so no CC0 body ever matched its own title *(three
published rows affected, all three of this KB's cells correct)*; 🔴 `D12` `BSD` was in the title
regex with **no arm in the case block**, so a titled `BSD-3-Clause` body came back
`BSD-2-Clause`; 🔴 `D13` `attribution-noncommercial` was tested **before** any ShareAlike check
and the deed branch never tested `/by-nc-sa/`, so the most restrictive CC combination was the one
that fell through.

🟢 **`12`/`12` offline:**
[`compose/code/p207-permissive-half-audit/`](../compose/code/p207-permissive-half-audit/).

### 🆕 🔴 `T207-5` — `L1`: the LOCATOR has the same disease, one level up

🔴 **`raw.githubusercontent.com` is case-sensitive, and every path list this KB has shipped is a
fixed-case enumeration.** 🟢 [`openedx/XBlock`](https://github.com/openedx/XBlock) — `Apache-2.0`,
`11 358` B, **in this KB's own foundations table** — spells its file **`LICENSE.TXT`** and
returns `404` on all `22` path forms p206 used. 🔴 **p207's first probe recorded it as
ungranted.**

🔵 **p206 fixed everything the TITLE LINE can be wearing. The FILENAME wears the same costume.**
🟢 **`90` path forms later — `10` bases × `9` extensions — `15` apparent absences resolve to `3`
recovered (`LICENSE.TXT`, `license.txt`, `License`), `2` `NOTICE`-only (p206's `D7`), and `12`
true absences.** 🔴 **An absence is only reportable after the case variants have been tried**,
which is `M2`/`M4` applied to a filename.
[`evidence/licence-locator-case.2026-10-14-p207.tsv`](../evidence/licence-locator-case.2026-10-14-p207.tsv).

### 🆕 🟢 `T207-6` — `ACTION PM` answered, and its premise was wrong

🟢 **The sweep it asked for: `95` repositories are printed with two or more different byte
counts. `15` adjudicated against their own blobs.** 🔴 **`0` need a `<!--corrected-->` marker.**

| what the conflict actually was | n |
|---|---|
| 🟢 **the trailing `LF` counted in one cell and not the other** — higher value = blob as served, lower = blob minus final newline | **`11`** |
| 🔴 numbers that were never byte claims (my extractor) | `2` |
| 🟡 a **trending row pinned to a sha** — a dated observation, correctly recorded | `1` |
| 🟡 a row inside a **dated pass section** — likewise history, not a current-state cell | `1` |

🔵 **In a KB whose files are append-only dated sections, two cells disagreeing is usually two
dated OBSERVATIONS, not one error.** 🔴 **Backfilling `<!--corrected-->` across them would mark
history as corrections and make the archive unreadable.** 🟢 **The real remedy is one sentence,
not a sweep: declare the convention — bytes are the blob AS SERVED, trailing newline included —
and all `11` resolve at once.**
[`evidence/byte-cell-conflicts.2026-10-14-p207.tsv`](../evidence/byte-cell-conflicts.2026-10-14-p207.tsv).

### 🆕 🟢 `T207-7` — `ACTION PK` closed on limb `1`: novelty measures tail depth, confirmed

🟢 **`topic:open-educational-resources` page `10`: `20` of `20` rows new to this KB — `100`%
novelty — at a median of `1`★, a maximum of `1`★, and `6` rows at `0`★.** 🔴 **p205's falsifier
fires exactly as written, and `T205-1` is now confirmed rather than argued: on a star-sorted
channel, novelty rises monotonically with depth and cannot falsify anything without a VALUE
floor.**

🟢 **Two bounds worth keeping:** the topic holds **`380`** repositories in total, so the channel
is finite and cheap to price; and 🔴 **`2` of the `20` rows are not OER at all** — a used-price
index and a parts reference for DJI drones. 🔵 **At tail depth the topic TAG itself degrades, so
a star floor is also a relevance floor.** 🟢 **Fund this channel to ~`10`★ and stop.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T207-8` — `ACTION PN` closed on limb `2`: the agent channel is language-agnostic

🟢 **Three discovery queries in Chinese, Portuguese and Spanish. Every verified candidate was
already on this shelf** — `Zenglian990/AI_Tutor_Release`, `plastic-labs/tutor-gpt`,
`datawhalechina/hello-agents`, `Priyamakeshwari/TeachGPT`, `microsoft/ai-agents-for-beginners`,
`pguso/agents-from-scratch`. 🟢 **One new row:**
[`guedesindev/ai_tutor_forge`](https://github.com/guedesindev/ai_tutor_forge) — 🔴 **`GPL-3.0`,
`35 149` B**, read from its own blob.

🔵 **`1` is inside limb `2`'s `0`–`2` band, so saturation holds for a fourth measurement.** 🟢
**Together with `T207-1` this resolves `T206-5`'s asymmetry, and the explanation is not the one
p206 proposed:** 🔴 **GitHub's index is organised by REPOSITORY, which has no issuing organ and
no official language, so there is nothing for an organ-shaped or language-shaped query to
unlock.** 🟢 **Policy channels are organ-gated; code channels are not gated at all, they are
simply exhausted.**

### 🟢 Trends that are about the industry rather than this KB

🆕 🔴 **`T207-9` — Brazil's binding instrument exists, is dated, and is one homologation away.**
**`Parecer CNE/CP nº 15/2026`**, approved by the **Conselho Nacional de Educação on `1` September
`2026`**, summary published in the **`Diário Oficial da União` on `6` October `2026`**, now with
**MEC for homologation — at which point it binds.** 🟢 **It sets parameters for AI across every
level and modality, subordinates AI to educational purposes and student rights, and states it
must not replace education professionals.** 🔵 **p206 reported the binding instrument as *"in
committee"*; it has been approved for six weeks.** 🟡 Secondary sourcing — `gov.br` is `000` from
this environment.

🆕 🟡 **`T207-10` — the region's policy is arriving on three tracks at once, and they are not
coordinated.** Brazil now has a **council** instrument (CNE/CP 15/2026), a **ministry** document
plus a regulatory sandbox, a **Senate** bill (`PL 2397/2026`, introduced `14` May `2026`, national
ethical parameters for public and private institutions), and a **state** programme (Paraná, first
in Brazil, technical AI course in `32` public schools, ~`2 000` students). 🔵 **A client asking
*"what must we comply with in Brazil"* has four answers with four timelines**, and only the first
is close to binding.

🆕 🟢 **`T207-11` — Mexico has quantified the gap every other market only asserts.** The **SEP**
presented **`10` recommendations for ethical and critical use of generative AI** on **`15` April
`2026`** at the `19ª` ordinary session of **CONAES**, on a survey of **`1.5`M+ students and
`166 000`+ teachers**: 🔴 **~`70`% of students use AI for academic work, ~`80`% do not know their
institution's policy, and fewer than `30`% have had any training.** 🟢 **The SEP has said
explicitly it will not ban, but regulate.** 🔵 **`80`% unaware of a policy is a procurement
brief: the product is not the tutor, it is the policy surface around it.** 🟢 A technical AI track
enters the national `bachillerato` in **`2026-27`**.

🆕 🟡 **`T207-12` — North America's "regulatory vacuum" is a reporting artefact of pan-regional
queries.** 🔴 Secondary roundups describe US education AI as operating with *minimal external
oversight* and only **`10`% of institutions** holding formal AI guidelines against **`71`% of US
teachers** untrained. 🟢 **But Canada has provincial instruments** — British Columbia's K-12
*Considerations for Using AI Tools*, Québec's ministry guide updated for **2026** and its **Cadre
de référence de la compétence numérique** refreshed **`21` April `2026`** with AI folded into the
existing `12` dimensions — and **Mexico has the SEP work above.** 🔵 **Education is provincial in
Canada: CMEC is holding to provincial jurisdiction and there is no federal instrument to find.**
🟡 Sources disagree on the count (three ministries vs four); 🔴 **flagged rather than resolved.**

🆕 🟡 **`T207-13` — a second national AI-literacy mandate, and it is dated `2027`.** France will
make **one hour per week of AI compulsory for all `seconde` students from the `2027` rentrée**,
attached to the existing `SNT` strand, announced June `2026`; the stated content is model
mechanics, uses, ethics, digital sovereignty and critical thinking. 🟡 Press-sourced; no ministry
text reachable. 🔵 **Set beside Singapore's compulsory teacher module (p205) and South Korea's
withdrawn textbook mandate (`T206-9`), the pattern holds: mandates aimed at TEACHER capability
survive, mandates aimed at CLASSROOM TOOLS do not.** 🟢 **France is aiming at the curriculum, with
an `18`-month runway — a dated window, and the deliverable is teacher-facing.**

## Pass 206 — 2026-10-14 — LANGUAGE IS AN AXIS, NOT A GAP; AND AN AUDIT'S FALSE-POSITIVE RATE IS A PROPERTY OF THE INDUSTRY

### 🆕 🟢 `T206-1` — the same blind spot was found in two unrelated channels in one pass

🔴 **p205 left `ACTION PJ` open on a suspicion: that Brazil's missing national AI-education policy
was a query-LANGUAGE problem rather than an absence.** 🟢 **It fires.** A Portuguese query returns
**four** dated 2026 instruments from MEC — see [`intel/market.md`](market.md) LATAM.

🟢 **Independently, and without looking for it, `ACTION PI`'s licence sweep found the SAME
property in the licence channel:** two Brazilian municipal school systems ship the AGPL **in
Portuguese**, and every classifier this KB has shipped returned 🔴 `UNRECOGNISED` on them.

🔵 **Two instruments that share nothing — a search channel and a `grep` over licence bodies —
failed on the same axis in the same pass.** 🟢 **So language is not a coverage gap to be patched
where it is noticed. It is a dimension every channel has to be tested along**, and the test is
cheap: run the query, or the matcher, in the language the artefact is actually written in.

🔴 **Operationally this retires a phrasing this KB has used for `200`+ passes.** 🟢 *"Nothing
found for X"* is only honest when the probe was run in X's language. 🔵 **p205 established
`per-country beats pan-regional` for APAC. p206 establishes `per-language beats English-only`,
and the second subsumes the first.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T206-2` — an automated licence audit in THIS industry has a ~`100`% false-positive rate

🟢 **Measured twice, on two methods:**

| pass | method | mismatches reported | true positives | false positives |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| p205 | regex over the licence column | `3` | 🔴 **`0`** | 🟢 `3` |
| p206 | table-row extraction over `281` repos, blobs re-read at `HEAD` | `3` | 🔴 **`0`** | 🟢 `3` |

🔵 **The false positives are not noise — each is a SHAPE this industry produces and others do
not:** 🔴 a **university named MIT** in a row's prose *(`sdv-dev/SDV`, p205)*; 🔴 a **relicensed
corpus** whose old family is correctly still printed with its version range
*(`UD_Spanish-AnCora`)*; 🔴 a **vendored third-party file** whose grant differs from the repo's
*(`foradian/fedena`'s `fckeditor`)*; 🔴 a **row the KB had already corrected** behind a
`<!--corrected-->` marker *(`untis4j`)*.

🟢 **The rule, and it is now evidence-backed rather than cautionary: an automated licence audit
must adjudicate against the published CELL and its prose before it reports a single mismatch.**
🔴 **An audit that reports blob-vs-token disagreement is measuring its own extraction.**

### 🆕 🟢 `T206-3` — the licence column was right and the READER had eight defects

🔴 **`ACTION PI` was written to find a wrong column. It found a wrong instrument.** `0` of `281`
published cells change family; `25` of `281` came back with no determinate family at all.

🔵 **The defects cluster into two families that have nothing to do with licensing:**

| family | defects | what it actually is |
|---|---|---|
| **the title line is DRESSED** | `D1` markdown heading · `D2` underline pushing the version out of the window · `D8` UTF-8 BOM · `D10` wrapped prose at column `0` | 🔴 **a text-parsing problem**, and it cost `16` rows their GPL **version** |
| **the title line is ABSENT or FOREIGN** | `D3` Portuguese · `D5` narrative grant · `D6` `19`-byte statement · `D4` a family not in the table | 🔴 **a corpus-coverage problem** |

🔴 **The `16` version losses are the commercially load-bearing half.** `GPL-2.0` has no patent
grant, no inbound `Apache-2.0` compatibility and no anti-tivoization clause; `GPL-3.0` has all
three. 🟢 **A studio reading `GPL-unversioned` and assuming the friendlier one has assumed a
patent grant that does not exist.**

🟢 **`20`/`20` offline, on real bodies:**
[`compose/code/p206-classifier-language-shape/`](../compose/code/p206-classifier-language-shape/).

### 🆕 🔴 `T206-4` — `Elastic-2.0` returning `UNRECOGNISED` is the worst failure mode a licence reader has

🟢 **[`canyongbs/advisingapp`](https://github.com/canyongbs/advisingapp) is a higher-education
student-success CRM under the `Elastic License 2.0`** — 🔴 **source-available, NOT open source.**
🔴 **The instrument returned `UNRECOGNISED`**, which reads to a hurried reader as *"unknown,
probably fine"*.

🔵 **Rank the failure modes by what they cost:** 🟢 a **wrong permissive family** is caught by
counsel; 🟡 a **lost version** costs a patent grant; 🔴 **a silent non-OSI family is the one that
ships proprietary terms into a client deliverable under the word "open source".** 🟢 **p206 adds
`NON-OSI-Elastic-2.0` beside the existing `NON-OSI-BSL`, `NON-OSI-SSPL` and `NON-OSI-faircode`,
and the lesson is that this list must be extended on sight, not on need.**

### 🟢 `T206-5` — the discovery channel is saturated for a third measurement, now in three languages

🟢 **Eight queries, four of them non-English or non-English-targeted. Every verified candidate was
already on the shelf. `0` new agent rows, `0` corrections.** 🔵 **p203 found saturation, p204
re-measured it, and p206 fails to break it with the one instrument — language — that this same
pass proved works everywhere else.** 🔴 **That asymmetry is the finding:** language unlocked the
POLICY channel and the LICENCE channel, and did **not** unlock the agent-discovery channel.

🟢 **The honest reading: GitHub's own discovery surface is already language-agnostic** — the
Chinese-language [`Zenglian990/AI_Tutor_Release`](https://github.com/Zenglian990/AI_Tutor_Release)
*(`MIT`, `64`★, 人教版-aligned, APAC)* and
[`HugeCatLab/ChatTutor`](https://github.com/HugeCatLab/ChatTutor) *(`AGPL-3.0`, `1.3`k★)* were
both already here, found by English queries. 🔴 **It is PROSE channels — government sites,
policy reporting, licence bodies — that are English-gated**, because a human wrote them in one
language and no index translated them.

### 🔴 `T206-6` — the search channel asserted two licences this pass, and was wrong about one of them

🟢 **Measured, because it is the channel a studio is most tempted to trust:** a search summary
called [`nirholas/ai-tutor-mcp`](https://github.com/nirholas/ai-tutor-mcp) *"proprietary"*; its
`LICENSE` is 🟢 **`Apache-2.0`, `11 338` B**. The same channel presented
[`kvnloo/tutor-mcp`](https://github.com/kvnloo/tutor-mcp) as an independent project; it is 🔴 **a
`0`★ fork** of [`ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp`](https://github.com/ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp) with a
byte-identical `1 073` B `LICENSE`. 🔵 **Both were caught by one blob read each. Neither would
have been caught by reading more summaries.**

### 🔴 `T206-7` — `ACTION PD` / `Gap 241` / `Gap 242`: re-tested in one call, unchanged

🟢 **`curl example.com` → `000`.** 🔴 **No general web egress. The EU AI Act's text, `mext.go.jp`
and `unu.edu` remain unreachable, and all three falsifiers remain correct and undischargeable from
this network profile.** 🔵 **Re-priced honestly for the sixth time: these need a session with
general egress, not another pass.** 🟢 **One call, not three probes — the re-test shape p205
prescribed, and it worked.**
[`evidence/channel-egress.2026-10-14-p206.tsv`](../evidence/channel-egress.2026-10-14-p206.tsv).

### 🟢 Trends that are about the industry rather than this KB

🔴 **`T206-8` — the binding deadline moved and the product window is `14` months.** The EU
`Digital Omnibus` pushed Annex III high-risk systems to **`2 December 2027`**, while **Article 50
transparency and Article 4 AI literacy are not delayed** and the **education emotion-recognition
ban has been in force since `2 February 2025`**. 🔵 **Build switchable, not retrofittable.**

🆕 🔴 **`T206-9` — a mandate without teacher readiness fails, and there is now a national-scale
data point.** South Korea's **AI Basic Act entered force `22 January 2026`** and names
**education** as *high-impact*; its mandatory AI textbooks reached **under `30`% adoption by
March** and were **stripped of official status by the National Assembly in August**. 🟡
Single-sourced and flagged as such. 🟢 **Against it: Singapore made teacher AI-literacy module `1`
compulsory BEFORE pushing tools at classrooms.** 🔵 **Same region, opposite sequencing, opposite
outcome.**

🟢 **`T206-10` — the policy layer has started naming the product shape, and in three regions it
names the SAME shape.** EMEA Annex III names assessment, admissions and proctoring. Brazil's MEC
referencial names human supervision, transparency and **student authorship**. North America's NYC
guidance is a permitted / cautioned / prohibited taxonomy. 🔵 **All three describe a policy-aware
layer over existing teaching tools, and none of them describes a tutor.** 🔴 **The open-source
shelf this KB tracks is overwhelmingly tutors.**

## Pass 205 — 2026-10-14 — THE "BLOCKED SITE" GAPS ARE ONE CHANNEL PROPERTY, AND A NOVELTY RATE CAN LIE

### 🔴 `T205-1` — novelty is not value in a star-sorted channel

🟢 **Measured: `98.8`% novelty across `80` OER rows, with a median of `5`★ and a range of `27`★
down to `2`★.** 🔴 **A GitHub topic page is star-sorted, so novelty RISES monotonically as
pagination descends — in any channel, whether or not it is a frontier.** 🔵 **A pre-registered
test of the form *"novelty stayed above `X`% → fund the channel"* is therefore unfalsifiable on a
sorted channel: it fires at page `N` for every large `N`.** 🟢 **The repair is cheap: pair every
novelty threshold with a VALUE floor** *(here, stars)*, and state the floor in the action. 🔴 **This
is a defect in how this KB writes falsifiers, not in any one pass's work**, and it applies to
`ACTION PE`, `OZ` and `PA` alike.

### 🔴 `T205-2` — the OER layer is a content supply, and nearly half of it is unusable as-is

🟢 **`79` new OER rows, grants read from their own blobs: `15` ungranted, `14` `CC-BY-SA-4.0`,
`6` `CC-BY-NC`, `9` shipping only a licence DEED.** 🔴 **`23` of `79` (`29`%) refuse commercial use
or force derivatives open; with the ungranted, `38` of `79` cannot be dropped into a client
deliverable.** 🔵 **The code shelf's failure mode is "no grant"; the content shelf's is "a grant
that says no".** 🟢 **`CC-BY-NC` is the one to brief account teams on** — it is not a copyleft
inconvenience, it is a flat refusal of the commercial use a studio engagement IS.

### 🆕 🔴 `T205-3` — three "blocked site" gaps are one fact, and it has a one-call test

🔴 **p204 recorded `eur-lex.europa.eu`, `ec.europa.eu`, `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu`,
`artificialintelligenceact.eu` and `unu.edu` as individually `ENOTFOUND`/`000`, and passes keep
re-probing them one at a time.** 🟢 **p205 ran the control that settles it:**

| host | class | inline `curl` | `WebFetch` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🔵 **`example.com`** | **neutral control** | 🔴 **`000`** | — |
| `www.mext.go.jp` | primary source | 🔴 `000` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` |
| `unu.edu` | primary source | 🔴 `000` | — |
| `raw.githubusercontent.com` | code host | 🟢 `200` | — |
| `api.github.com` | code host | 🟢 `200` *(repo-scoped only)* | — |
| `pypi.org` | package registry | 🟢 `200` | — |
| `github.com` *(repo + topic pages)* | code host | 🔴 `403` | 🟢 **`200`** |

🔴 **`example.com` returns `000`. Nobody blocks `example.com`.** 🟢 **So this is not site-specific
blocking: this environment has NO general web egress on `curl` or `WebFetch` — only GitHub hosts
and the package registries in the proxy's `noProxy` allowlist, plus `WebSearch`, which is a search
service and returns secondary summaries by construction.**

🟢 **Consequences, which retire recurring cost rather than adding any:**
🔵 **(a)** `ACTION PD`, `Gap 241` and `Gap 242` are not three blocked sites — they are **one
structural property**, and no future session with this network profile will discharge any of them.
🔵 **(b)** The remedy is not *"a later pass"* but **a session with general web egress**; priced
honestly, that is a change of environment, not of effort.
🔵 **(c)** Any pass can re-test the whole class in ONE call — `curl example.com`. 🔴 **Stop
re-probing the primary sources individually.**
[`evidence/channel-egress.2026-10-14-p205.tsv`](../evidence/channel-egress.2026-10-14-p205.tsv).

🟢 **The same census found a capability this KB had not been using: `github.com` repository pages
render through `WebFetch`**, so **star counts, licences, default branches and release tags ARE
first-hand verifiable here** — only `curl` and the search API are closed. 🔵 **`HKUDS/DeepTutor`
was re-verified this way** *(`41.4`k★, `Apache-2.0`, `main`, `v1.6.14` of `2026-10-08`)*, against
three mutually inconsistent secondary figures.

### 🔴 `T205-4` — the instrument this KB measures grants with was wrong about three licence families

🟢 **`GPL-3.0` and `MPL-2.0` both NAME the AGPL inside their own text**, so a body-wide classifier
that tests the AGPL first labels them `AGPL-3.0`. 🔴 **This KB's classifier did exactly that from
p203.** 🟢 **Published exposure was `0` rows — but only by luck of sampling, and `4` of p205's own
rows were wrong before re-adjudication.** 🔵 **The general lesson is the one worth keeping: a
licence body is adversarial input to a substring matcher, because licences quote each other.**
🟢 **Anchor on the title LINE.** `Gap 466`.

---

## Pass 204 — 2026-10-14 — EVERY SOFTWARE CHANNEL IS SATURATED; THE UNSATURATED ONE IS CONTENT

### 🔴 `T204-1` — saturation is not a vocabulary property, it is a CODE-vs-CONTENT property

🟢 **Three independent discovery channels were measured to exhaustion across two passes:**

| channel | measured | NEW to this KB | kind |
|---|---|---|---|
| `topic:ai-tutor` | p203 | 🔴 **`0` of `20`** | code |
| `topic:scorm` | **p204** | 🔴 **`0` of `20`** | code |
| `topic:xapi` | **p204** | 🔴 **`0` of `20`** | code |
| `topic:lti` | **p204** | 🔴 **`0` of `20`** | code |
| **EMEA regulatory sweep** | **p204** | 🔴 **`0` new facts** | regulation |
| `topic:open-educational-resources` | p203 | 🟢 **`12` of `20`** | 🟢 **content** |
| `topic:open-educational-resources` **page `2`** | **p204** | 🟢 **yields** *(see `ACTION PA`)* | 🟢 **content** |

🔴 **`80` of `80` software rows across four code vocabularies were already held.** 🟢 **Controls
ran on the novelty measurement** — invented slugs classify `NEW`, known rows classify `KNOWN` —
so the `0` is a measurement, not a broken matcher.
[`evidence/oz-vocabulary-novelty.2026-10-14-p204.tsv`](../evidence/oz-vocabulary-novelty.2026-10-14-p204.tsv).

🟢 **So the trend this KB should act on is about itself: `204` passes have exhausted the code
supply of this industry, and the content supply is barely touched.** 🔴 **This arrives
independently of `P203-1`, which found the same seam inside the licence column** — content
grants (`CC-*`) behaving differently from software grants. 🟢 **Two unrelated instruments, one
boundary.** `Gap 465`.

🔵 **Consequence, and it is a budget instruction rather than an observation:** 🔴 **stop
spending passes on code-vocabulary discovery.** 🟢 **The yield is in (a) OER pagination — `358`
rows still unread, now REACHABLE (below), (b) non-GitHub channels, and (c) the regional
primary sources this environment cannot reach.**

### 🟢 `T204-2` — the channel map, measured on both channels this pass

🔴 **`WebFetch`'s DNS is a per-domain ALLOWLIST, not a general resolver — and inline `curl`
fails on exactly the same domains.** 🟢 **Measured, not inferred:**

| domain | `WebFetch` | inline `curl` | consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `github.com` *(topic pages)* | 🟢 renders | — | 🟢 **discovery works, and PAGINATES** |
| `raw.githubusercontent.com` | — | 🟢 **`200`** | 🟢 **grant bodies readable** |
| `api.github.com` | — | 🔴 `403` | 🔴 no exact star counts |
| `eur-lex.europa.eu` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` | 🔴 `000` | 🔴 `Gap 241` cannot close |
| `ec.europa.eu`, `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` | 🔴 `000` | 🔴 the Commission's OWN page is unreachable |
| `artificialintelligenceact.eu` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` | 🔴 `000` | 🔴 **`ACTION PD` NOT DISCHARGEABLE here** |
| `unu.edu` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` | 🔴 `000` | 🔴 **`Gap 242` cannot close** |
| `marketsandmarkets.com` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` | 🔴 `000` | 🟡 regional figures stay snippet-sourced |

🔴 **This retires a hope three passes have budgeted on.** Pass 44 named the UNU paper as the
cheap remedy for `Gap 242`; pass 43 named the Act's own text as the way to settle `ACTION PD`.
🟢 **Both are free for anyone with a browser and IMPOSSIBLE from this session.** 🔵 **The
correct record is not "unread" — it is "unreadable here", and the two must not be confused in
a cost estimate.**

🟢 **What this environment CAN do, and should therefore be pointed at:** read grant bodies at
scale from `raw.githubusercontent.com`, resolve default branches and dead addresses with
`git ls-remote --symref`, and paginate GitHub topic vocabularies. 🔴 **What it cannot do: cite
any primary legal or market source.**

### 🟢 `T204-3` — a grant has a CHANNEL before it has a path, and the channels can disagree

🔴 **`P203-1` established that a licence needs a `scope` (which path it covers). `P204-1`
establishes that it needs a `channel` first.** 🟢 **The ladder, strongest to weakest:**

**licence BODY at a path** → **manifest field, no body** → **README prose** → **badge**

🔴 **And the rungs can CONTRADICT each other in one tree.**
[LearnPress/learnpress](https://github.com/LearnPress/learnpress) `develop` @ `f9f2d6e2`:
`package.json` says 🟢 `ISC`, `readme.txt` says 🔴 `GPLv3`, and the WordPress `readme.txt` is
the manifest that governs the DISTRIBUTED plugin. 🔴 **The permissive channel is the
non-authoritative one.**

🟢 **Measured frequency: `1` of `8` manifest-declared rows** — `7` have no second channel at
all. 🔵 **So this is a rare mechanism tied to the WordPress convention, NOT a general defect**,
and it is declared at that strength.

🔴 **The method rule it forces is general even though the defect is rare:** 🟢 **when a row's
grant comes from a manifest rather than a body, read a SECOND channel before publishing it as
permissive.** 🔵 **Cost: one `curl`.**

### 🟢 `T204-4` — `ECL-2.0` is in this industry's water supply, and it is not Apache-2.0

🟢 **Two rows measured this pass carry the `Educational Community License 2.0`:**
[`opencast/opencast`](https://github.com/opencast/opencast) *(`develop` @ `ae86c6aa`)* and
[`Apereo-Learning-Analytics-Initiative/OpenLRW`](https://github.com/Apereo-Learning-Analytics-Initiative/OpenLRW).

🔴 **The licence body states its own deviation in its first paragraph:** it is *"the Apache 2.0
license, modified to change the scope of the patent grant in section `3` to be specific to the
needs of the education communities using this license."*

🟢 **So `ECL-2.0` is permissive for copying, modification and redistribution — treat it as
Apache-class for those purposes.** 🔴 **Its PATENT grant is narrower, and it is narrowed
specifically to education use.** 🔵 **The practical trigger: an engagement that builds a
patentable pipeline on an `ECL-2.0` component, or that intends to serve a NON-education market
from it, needs counsel on section `3`.** 🟢 **For an education deployment, which is what this
KB is for, the narrowing is aligned with the use.**

🔵 **`Apereo` is a recurring publisher in this industry** *(`Sakai`, `OpenLRW`, `Opencast`,
the Learning Analytics Initiative)*, 🔴 **so this is a family to expect, not a one-off row.**

## Pass 203 — 2026-10-14 — THE CHANNEL SEAM INVERTED, AND FOUR FALSIFIERS DISCHARGED

### 🔴 `ACTION OT` CLOSED: the seam is a CONJUNCTION — network AND from-file

🟢 **Measured this pass in one session, all four cells:**

| | inline command | committed script |
|---|---|---|
| **no network** | — | 🟢 **RUNS** — `test_classify.sh`, **`19`/`19`** |
| **network** | 🟢 **`200`** — `~300` reads of `raw.githubusercontent.com` | 🔴 **REFUSED** |

🔴 **Neither axis alone predicts the verdict.** Network is not the seam: inline network
calls ran freely all pass. Being a committed file is not the seam either: an offline
committed script executed. 🟢 **The predicate is the CONJUNCTION — network-bearing code read
from a file.**

🔵 **So p202's restatement of the pass-74 note — *"el seam real no es librería vs script, es
RED vs NO-RED"* — is CORRECT ABOUT SCRIPTS and incomplete as stated**, because it implies no
network call can run, and `~300` did. 🔴 **This pass's own first draft made the opposite
error**, concluding from the single denial that *"the seam is where the code lives, not
whether it touches the network"* — falsified ten minutes later by the offline script
running. 🟢 **Both one-axis readings fit the earlier evidence; only the 2×2 distinguishes
them, and no prior pass had filled all four cells.** `Gap 462`.

🟢 **Consequence, and it is actionable rather than merely recorded:** an instrument should be
**split at the network boundary**, so the decidable half is always runnable by the pass that
writes it. 🟢 **p203 did this:**
[`compose/code/p203-grant-blob-reader/`](../compose/code/p203-grant-blob-reader/) ships
`classify.sh` *(pure, no network, `19`/`19` offline suite including `8` regressions —
`CC-BY-SA` must not collapse to `CC-BY`, `AGPL` not to `GPL`, an empty body not to MIT)* and
`read_grant.sh` *(the fetch, refused here, runs in CI)*. 🔵 **The pass performed its `~300`
reads inline and says so; the script is the record of method, never presented as the
measurement.**

### 🟢 `ACTION OV` CLOSED — limb `2`. `4` of `16` foundation rows are not permissive

🟢 All `13` rows p202 published as `(not blob-read)` are read, plus `3` more.
**`12` permissive · `3` copyleft · `1` content-copyleft over software.** 🔴 **`g3i/lxHive`
(GPL-2.0), `oat-sa/lib-lti1p3-core` (GPL-2.0), `cerpus/Edlib` (GPL-3.0),
`cybercussion/SCOBot` (`CC-BY-SA-4.0`).** 🟢 `P202-1` survives and now names a `10`-row
permissive spine. See [`repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md).

🔴 **The trend worth naming: this layer's copyleft is OLD copyleft.** Two GPL-**2.0** grants
on actively used interop libraries — **a 1991 instrument governing the LTI plumbing of 2026
AI deployments.** 🟢 Permissive alternatives exist for every one of the four.

### 🟢 `ACTION OX` CLOSED — limb `1` fires. The licence column needs a `scope` field

🟢 **`16` rows measured; `10` carry an EXPLICIT two-scope grant** — `## Instructional
Material` → `CC-BY-4.0`, `## Software` → **MIT**, in one `LICENSE.md`. Plus `5` rows whose
grant is content-only **over executable notebooks**, and `1` `CC0` dedication over an index.
🔴 **With p202's `8`, that is `24` rows where a single licence cell is wrong about part of
the repository.** Limb `1`'s threshold was `10`+.

🟢 **So the restructure is earned:** a row publishes `licence` **per path**, and `MIT` stops
being publishable without saying *of what*. 🔵 **The strongest form of the finding: the
Carpentries instrument is carried by `carpentries/lesson-example`, the lesson TEMPLATE —
so the two-scope grant PROPAGATES to every lesson generated from it.** `P203-1`.

`PlaypowerLabs/EdOptimize` — *"`LICENSE NOTE: CURRENTLY, THIS PLATFORM IS LICENSED GPLv3.
ALL MODIFICATIONS/COPIES OF THIS PLATFORM MUST HAVE GPLv3 LICENSE AND MUST DISCLOSE ALL OF
THEIR SO[URCE]`"*. 🔴 **Limb `1` needed `3`+ more and found none.** 🟢 **No `permanence`
field. Record `EdOptimize` and stop asking.**

France Université Numérique`)* — p202 published a badge; p203 publishes the blob.

🔴 **And the sweep found the historical under-read p202 asked about.** `260` shelf rows
