### 🆕 🔴 `Gap 461` — discovery vocabulary determined the content shelf for `202` passes

🔴 **`topic:ai-tutor` returned `20` rows and this KB held `20` of them: the first saturated
channel.** 🟢 **The same hour, `topic:open-educational-resources` (`378` repositories) yielded
`12` rows new to this KB — the entire Carpentries family**, the largest instructor-led open
curriculum organisation in the world. 🔴 **Cause: Carpentries repos carry no vocabulary that
`ingest/config.json` searches.** 🟢 **The KB's content shelf was a function of its query list,
and nobody had measured that.** `ACTION OZ`.

### 🟢 Trends that are about the industry rather than this KB

1. 🔴 **Regulation has stopped asking for principles and started naming duties on PEOPLE.**
   EU Article 4 (AI literacy, in force), Maryland's district `AI coordinator`, Oklahoma and
   Maryland's human-oversight requirements, India's Class-`3` mandate against a `15`%
   teacher-fluency base. 🟢 **The 2026 deliverable is trained staff and documentation; the
   model is assumed.**
2. 🔴 **"Agentic" has become the incumbent's word.** Instructure shipped `IgniteAI Agent` in
   March 2026; PUC Chile runs `194` pedagogical agents across `~100` courses for `4 600`+
   students. 🟢 **Agent-shaped tutoring is no longer a differentiator — the differentiator is
   evidence of learning outcomes, which PUC Chile itself says it is still building.**
3. 🟢 **Pedagogy is moving into the developer's harness.** `nykooi1/vibe-wise` (MIT, `~3.4k★`,
   `~+3.3k` this window) teaches the user *while the model writes the code*. 🟢 **A plugin,
   not an app** — and this KB had no row of that shape.
4. 🔴 **The interop layer is pre-AI, standardised, institutionally adopted, and mostly
   permissive — and every tutor in this KB still invents its own progress model.** `xAPI` +
   `SCORM` + `LTI` + analytics is `~1 088` repositories *(p202's `ACTION OP`)*; p203 blob-read
   its spine and `10` rows are permissive.
5. 🔴 **Content licensing is the industry's real legal surface, and it is not the software
   surface.** `24` measured rows where one licence cell misdescribes part of the repository;
   a SCORM run-time library under `CC-BY-SA`; the second-largest agent-skill repo with **no
   software grant at all.**
6. 🔴 **A government AI policy was withdrawn because its own citations were AI-hallucinated**
   *(South Africa, published `2026-04-10`, withdrawn `2026-04-26`)*. 🟢 **Citation provenance
   is now a procurement risk with a precedent, and this KB's blob-read discipline is the
   direct answer to it.**

## Pass 202 — 2026-10-14

### 🔴 T1 — The agent payload is content, and content licensing has different reach than software licensing

**The structural trend of this pass.** A 2026 education agent ships as `SKILL.md` markdown,
not as a service. 🔴 **Markdown is content, and the content world's licences are Creative
Commons — which behave nothing like MIT.** Measured on `8` repositories this pass
*(committed as [`evidence/content-code-grant-split.2026-10-14-p202.tsv`](../evidence/content-code-grant-split.2026-10-14-p202.tsv))*:
`education-agent-skills` is **`CC-BY-SA-4.0`** across `858★` of skills *and* its
`mcp-server/` code; `pupilfirst` is MIT for code and **`CC-BY-SA-4.0` for `docs/`**;
`human-skill-tree` is AGPL with `skills/` **dual MIT**; `bncc-dev` splits
`LICENSE-CODIGO`/`LICENSE-DADOS`.

🟢 **Consequence for a studio: `ShareAlike` on a prompt payload is a stronger constraint
than AGPL on a server**, because the skill *is* the deliverable — there is no "we only call
it over the network" escape. **Ask "what grant covers the PATH I am shipping", never "what
licence is the repo".**

### 🟢 T2 — Regulation has started specifying a product SHAPE, and the OSS shelf already built it

🆕 **China** *(MoE "AI + Education" plan, April 2026; 2025 classroom guidelines)* prohibits
primary-school students from independently using open-ended content generation and bars
teachers from substituting genAI for core teaching. 🆕 **Indonesia** *(joint ministerial
decree, March 2026)* forbids school students from using instant AI applications that
directly generate answers, while permitting **purpose-built educational AI tools**.

🟢 **The two largest education systems in APAC have outlawed the chatbot tutor and licensed
the instrumented one** — which is the architecture p201 measured arriving on its own:
pedagogy in auditable tools *(RL, FSRS, Bayesian Knowledge Tracing behind MCP calls)*, the
LLM confined to rendering. 🟢 **Better pedagogy and easier compliance now point the same
way, which is rare and worth building on.**

### 🔴 T3 — Article 50 is live; Annex III is 14 months out

**Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** *(OJ `2026-07-24`, in force `2026-07-27`)*: Article 50
transparency has applied **since `2026-08-02`**; Annex III high-risk obligations — which
name **education and exam grading** — move to **`2027-12-02`**. 🟢 **So the compliance work
that is due TODAY is synthetic-content marking, and this KB built those instruments `12`
passes ago.** ⚠️ Secondhand: `eur-lex.europa.eu` does not resolve from this session.

### 🟢 T4 — The standards layer is the untouched foundation

`xAPI` + `SCORM` + `LTI` + `learning-analytics` = **`1 088` matching repositories**, mature,
institutionally adopted, mostly **permissive**, and almost entirely pre-AI. 🔴 **Every tutor
in [`agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md) invents its own progress model while `xAPI`
standardised one a decade ago and an LRS already stores it.** 🟢 The composable gap of the
year: agent ↔ LRS.

### 🔴 T5 — "Adaptive learning" is now a majority-ML phrase

`topic:adaptive-learning` is **`70`% not-education** in its top `20`, and its two largest
rows are a maths library (`1.2k★`) and a screen-brightness daemon (`948★`). 🟢 **For a KB,
a studio deck or a search: `learning` alone no longer denotes education.** Use `e-learning`,
`learning record`, `learning analytics`, or a standard's name.

### 🟢 T6 — Governance lag is the most reproducible finding in this industry

Three regions, independent surveys, two passes: **LATAM `87`% use / `26`% strategy / `8`%
student awareness** *(UNESCO IESALC `200` institutions; Digital Education Council `30 000`+
respondents)*; **Brazil `53`% administrator use / `22`% with guidelines**; **APAC `87`%
encourage / `47`% govern**; **Germany `94`% student use** *(OECD 2026, secondhand)*;
**`10`% of institutions globally with formal guidelines, `71`% of US teachers untrained**.
🟢 **A `40`–`80`-point gap that reproduces everywhere money figures contradict each other.
It IS the engagement.**

### 🟢 T7 — North America's buyer arrived by statute

**`8` states now legally require districts to adopt written AI policies** *(`ID` `IL` `MD`
`OH` `OK` `TN` `UT` `VA`)*; **Maryland requires a designated `AI coordinator` per district**.
🔴 **Thousands of small buyers with a legal duty and no evaluation capacity** — a
policy-to-implementation market, not a product market.

### 🟡 T8 — Grants are declared as provisional

🆕 `PlaypowerLabs/EdOptimize`: **`"CURRENTLY, THIS PLATFORM IS LICENSED GPLv3."`** The code
taken today stays GPL-3.0, but the project's trajectory is not plannable. 🔵 **A grant has a
tense, and this KB has never recorded it.** `ACTION OU`.

### 🔴 T9 — The GPL version policy is unstated more often than it is stated

Of `4` GPL rows read for version policy this pass, `2` say **or-later** *(Chamilo,
`moodle-ai-skill-navigator`)* and `2` name **"Version 3"** with no policy at all
*(`adapt_framework`, `EdOptimize`)*. 🟢 **Read from the GPL-3.0 § 14 fixture committed in
this repo: naming a numbered version without "or any later version" means that version
governs; specifying NO version means the licensee may choose ANY.** 🔴 **So a flat
`GPL-3.0` label is wrong in both directions, and the policy lives in the project's NOTICE,
never in the licence text — which is byte-identical either way.**

### 🔴 T10 — Education technology has a live adversarial shelf

`CloakBox` (`403★`, hides a VM from proctoring software) is the **second-largest row** on
`topic:proctoring`; `MoodleGPT` (`87★`) hides ChatGPT inside a Moodle quiz. 🟢 **Proctoring
is an arms race with a public open-source opposition, and a proctoring recommendation that
does not acknowledge it is not advice.** 🔵 **Relevance is not suitability** — the KB's
shelf data now carries an `adversarial` column because no topic-keyed instrument can make
that call for a client.


## Pass 201 — 2026-10-14 — CURRENT TRENDS

### 1. 🆕 🔴 The grading shelf was never missing — the search term was

**This is the window's correction, and it reverses a build recommendation.** p200 published
*"Open source has no grading shelf. If AI Studios sells agentic grading, AI Studios builds it."*
🟢 **`topic:autograding stars:>20` returns `16` repositories**, `3 439★` across the top eight,
**five permissive and university-maintained**: [`Artemis`](https://github.com/ls1intum/Artemis)
(**MIT**, `818★`, TU Munich), [`Autolab`](https://github.com/autolab/Autolab) (**Apache-2.0**,
`808★`, CMU), [`Submitty`](https://github.com/Submitty/Submitty) (**BSD-3**, `803★`, RPI),
[`okpy/ok`](https://github.com/okpy/ok) (**Apache-2.0**, `370★`, Berkeley),
[`otter-grader`](https://github.com/ucbds-infra/otter-grader) (**BSD-3**, `161★`, Berkeley).
🔵 **The trend to read off it: assessment infrastructure in education is UNIVERSITY-MAINTAINED
and PERMISSIVE, while the LMS layer around it is corporate and copyleft.** 🟢 **The remaining
white space is narrow and real — LLM free-text grading with an audit trail — and `Artemis` is
its insertion point, not a blank page.**

### 2. 🟢 The agent-skill tutor layer consolidates, and it is an APAC phenomenon

🔵 p200 identified the shift *(the tutor ships as a `SKILL.md`, not a web app)* and it holds.
🟢 **Measured wider this pass: `33` repositories under `topic:ai-tutor`, `23` with licences read
from bytes, `15` of them MIT.** 🔴 **Regional origin: `15` APAC, `4` EMEA, `3` Global, `0` North
America, `0` LATAM.** 🟢 **And the shelf has acquired a second idiom beside the skill — the MCP
server:** [`anki-mcp-server`](https://github.com/ankimcp/anki-mcp-server) (**MIT**, `515★`,
spaced repetition as tool calls) and [`tutor-mcp`](https://github.com/ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp)
(**MIT**, Bayesian Knowledge Tracing in Go). 🔵 **The skill carries the pedagogy; the MCP server
carries the state.** That split is the architecture this layer is settling on.

### 3. 🆕 🟢 Pedagogy is being pulled OUT of the model

**The most interesting engineering trend on the shelf, and it is a reversal.** 2025's tutors
asked an LLM to be a teacher. 🟢 **2026's best-designed ones give the LLM the smallest possible
job:**
[`HumphreySun98/Smart-Study-Agent`](https://github.com/HumphreySun98/Smart-Study-Agent) (**MIT**)
states it as a design rule — *"RL picks the action, FSRS picks the timing, **the LLM only writes
the quizzes**"*; [`tutor-mcp`](https://github.com/ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp) puts **BKT** behind
a tool call; [`universal-examprep-skill`](https://github.com/ZeKaiNie/universal-examprep-skill)
grounds every claim in **a cited slide page**; [`Bloom`](https://github.com/Li-Evan/Bloom)
implements **Bloom's 2-sigma** explicitly. 🟢 **Cognitive science as deterministic code, the
model as a renderer** — this is both better pedagogy and a far easier thing to audit under a
high-risk regime. 🔵 It also explains the small star counts: these are `50`–`500★` projects doing
the work the `41 386★` platform does implicitly.

### 4. 🔴 The newer the platform, the stronger the copyleft — and `71`% of deployment is cloud

🟢 Measured from blobs this pass: the LMS layer built since ~2020 is **AGPL-3.0** —
[`frappe/lms`](https://github.com/frappe/lms) (`3 312★`),
[`learnhouse`](https://github.com/learnhouse/learnhouse) (`2 377★`),
[`classroomio`](https://github.com/classroomio/classroomio) (`1 717★`),
[`courselit`](https://github.com/codelitdev/courselit) (`1 274★`),
[`tutor`](https://github.com/overhangio/tutor) (`1 127★`),
[`Claroline`](https://github.com/claroline/Claroline) (`350★`) — while Moodle, Chamilo, ILIAS and
H5P remain plain **GPL-3.0**. 🔴 **AGPL plus the `71.22`% cloud deployment share is the one
combination that can compel disclosure to a client's end users.** 🟢 **The counter-trend is real
and newly measurable: a permissive option now exists at every layer** — `mentingo`/`ulearn`/
`academico` (MIT), `richie` (MIT), `scorm-again` (MIT), `flat` (MIT), `OpenVidu` (Apache-2.0),
`OpenOLAT` (Apache-2.0), `Kolibri` (MIT), `Artemis` (MIT). **Nine exits, where this KB listed two
an hour ago.**

### 5. 🆕 🔴 "Open source" in education is acquiring a non-OSI fringe

🟢 [`leemonade/leemons`](https://github.com/leemonade/leemons) (`291★`) ships a **"Fair Code
License" v1.0** — bespoke, non-OSI — on an LMS that presents as open source. 🔵 The `n8n` pattern
arriving in this vertical. 🔴 **And `3` rows of the `topic:lms` shelf carrying `1 068★` grant
nothing at all on any channel** — `LMS-Laravel` (`505★`), `OLD-LMS-Laravel` (`288★`),
`LearnPress` (`275★`, **not even a `License:` line in its WordPress plugin header**). 🟢 **The
practical trend: the licence question in education can no longer be answered by a scanner's
label.** `9`% of a real shelf has no grant, and the loudest non-OSI row is the one a buyer would
shortlist first.

### 6. 🆕 🟡 The grant is moving OUT of the licence file

🟢 Three shapes measured this pass, all on repos with adoption:
**(a) split scope inside one notice** — `human-skill-tree` (`skills/` **AGPL OR MIT**, `app/`
AGPL-only), `pupilfirst` (`docs/` **CC-BY-SA-4.0**, the rest **MIT**), `leemons` (the rest
**Fair Code**);
**(b) version policy only in the project's own notice** — 🆕 **Chamilo declares
`GPL-3.0-**or-later`** in its `license.txt`, a distinction the full GPL text cannot express, and
this KB had published plain `GPL-3.0` since cycle 3**;
**(c) the grant at a path no scanner looks at** — lowercase `license.txt` (`erpnext`, `paella`),
non-root `docs/License.txt` with a **UTF-8 BOM** (`openSIS`). 🔴 **`pupilfirst` and `leemons`
open with the SAME boilerplate sentence and end in opposite licences.** 🟢 **So *"MIT"* is
becoming an answer that needs a path attached to be true.**

### 7. 🟢 Discovery is a capability, and `topic:` is not a taxonomy

🔵 p200 opened ranked discovery and it is transformative — two whole shelves found in two passes.
🔴 **The same channel also shows its own failure mode: `12.5`% of `topic:lms stars:>200` is not
education** *(Lyrion Music Server ×2, the LMS colour space, a mis-tagged coding agent)*, **and by
stars the noise outweighs the signal — `28 473★` of it above the largest real education row.**
🟢 **The trend for anyone building an industry KB: ranked discovery without a relevance gate
publishes a music server as a top-3 LMS.** Every row on these pages carries a sentence saying
what it is, for precisely this reason.

### 8. 🔴 Adoption is settled; governance is not — and that gap is now measured twice

🆕 **Global: student AI use `66`% (2024) → `92`% (2025), against `10`% of institutions with
formal AI guidelines and `71`% of US teachers untrained.** 🔵 **LATAM, properly sampled
(`200` institutions, `19` countries): `87`% use, `74`% in teaching and learning, `26`% with a
formal strategy.** 🟢 **Two independent measurements of the same `60`–`82`-point gap, in two
regions.** 🔴 **APAC supplies a third from the agent side: `87`% of organisations encourage AI
agents and `47`% govern them.** 🟢 **This is the most reliably reproduced finding in the whole
file, and it is the engagement: the client has already adopted, and cannot evidence it.**


## Pass 200 — 2026-10-14 — CURRENT TRENDS

### 1. 🆕 🟢 The tutor stopped being an app and became an agent skill

**This is the window's structural change, and it is about distribution, not models.** `11`
tutoring projects verified alive today, **`3 655` stars combined, `9` of them MIT** — and none
appears in any prior cycle of this KB. They ship a `SKILL.md` loaded by Claude Code, Cursor,
Codex or Antigravity rather than a hosted product: [`Study-Mate`](https://github.com/Miaotofu01/Study-Mate)
(`950★`), [`human-skill-tree`](https://github.com/24kchengYe/human-skill-tree) (`572★`),
[`anki-mcp-server`](https://github.com/ankimcp/anki-mcp-server) (`515★`),
[`universal-examprep-skill`](https://github.com/ZeKaiNie/universal-examprep-skill) (`307★`),
[`algo-sensei`](https://github.com/karanb192/algo-sensei) (`287★`),
[`Bloom`](https://github.com/Li-Evan/Bloom) (`284★`).

🔵 **Why it matters more than the star counts suggest:** the distribution problem that killed a
decade of edtech — getting a tool in front of a learner who already has a workflow — is solved
by the learner installing a skill into a tool they already use. **There is no app to adopt, no
LMS integration to negotiate, no procurement.** 🟢 **And the pedagogy is getting better, not
worse, in this layer:** `universal-examprep-skill` teaches **from the course's own slides with
page citations** and crops the figures, i.e. grounding by provenance; `Bloom` runs Socratic
questioning on 2-sigma framing; `universal-diagnostic-tutor-skill` is diagnosis-first with no
command menu. 🔴 **The whole layer is APAC-origin** (`9` APAC, `1` EMEA, `0` North America,
`0` LATAM) **and mostly Chinese-language.** MIT licences mean it ports; nobody has ported it.

### 2. 🟢 Moodle's integration layer caught up with Canvas's — and a third of it is unshippable

p199 recorded the Canvas MCP cluster (`6` servers) as the quarter's finding and found only `2`
Moodle servers. 🟢 **With discovery available there are `8`**, led by
[`peancor/moodle-mcp-server`](https://github.com/peancor/moodle-mcp-server) (**MIT**, `43★`),
and one — [`G36maid/moodle-mcp`](https://github.com/G36maid/moodle-mcp), **Rust, MIT** — was
**created `2026-10-12`, two days ago.** The layer is being written this week.
🔴 **But `loyaniu/moodle-mcp` (`42★`, second-most-starred) grants nothing on any path or
manifest, `haanhtuandev/vgu-mcp` likewise, and `ink-waffle/moodle-mcp` declares MIT in
`package.json` with no grant.** 🟢 **`5`/`8` carry a real MIT grant — so in this layer the pick
is never the top of the star ranking**, and p199's generalisation holds in its stronger form:
*the LMS integration layer is no longer a custom build, for Moodle as well as Canvas.*

### 3. 🔴 Agentic grading is pre-adoption, and now there is a number for it

The three grading agents this KB tables as canonical hold **`4` stars between them** —
`grading_assistant` `4`, `autograder` `0`, `auto-assessment-agent` `0` *(created seven days
before the pass that listed it)*. 🔵 All three were `not read` in p199 and are now read.
🟢 **This is the most decision-relevant trend in the pass precisely because it is negative:
grading is the highest-demand, most-regulated AI task in education and open source has not
built it.** Read `auto-assessment-agent` for its criterion-level, evidence-backed audit trail —
then build. **There is no shelf.**

### 4. 🆕 🔴 Education has its own OSI licence, and this KB had been reporting it as Apache-2.0

**ECL-2.0** — the Educational Community License — *"consists of the Apache 2.0 license, modified
to change the scope of the patent grant in section 3 to be specific to the needs of the
education communities,"* quoted from Sakai's own blob. **`license:ecl-2.0 stars:>20` returns
`18` repositories GitHub-wide**, topped by [`sakai`](https://github.com/sakaiproject/sakai)
(`1 238★`) and [`opencast`](https://github.com/opencast/opencast) (`508★`).
🟢 **Permission behaves like Apache-2.0; the patent peace is scoped to education** — which is a
contract question for any deliverable serving a non-education licensee. 🔵 Cycle 3 published
`Sakai(Apache2)`; p199 flagged the clause correctly; **this pass read the text and sized the
family.**

### 5. 🔴 The root `LICENSE` file is becoming the exception — four fresh surfaces in one pass

| surface | instance | what a scanner reports |
|---|---|---|
| **split scope inside one notice** | `24kchengYe/human-skill-tree` (`572★`) — `skills/` **AGPL-3.0 OR MIT**, `app/` AGPL-only, in `1 134` B | 🔴 "AGPL" → the MIT-available part is discarded |
| **lowercase `license.txt`** | `frappe/erpnext` — `GPL-3.0`, `35 149` B | 🔴 "no licence" |
| **a `19`-byte grant REFERENCE** | `frappe/education` (`665★`) — `License: GNU GPL V3`, and nothing else | 🔴 "GPL-3.0" with no grant behind it |
| **a non-root grant, with a BOM** | `OS4ED/openSIS-Classic` (`345★`) — `GPL-2.0`, `17 286` B at `docs/License.txt` | 🔴 "no licence" |
| **manifest-only declaration** | `polimediaupv/paella` — `ECL-2.0` in `package.json`, no blob at `15` path/branch combinations | 🟡 "ECL-2.0" from a manifest, not a grant |

🟢 **The generalisation to carry forward: *"no `LICENSE` file"* is a statement about a path,
never about a grant** — and a licence column produced by a single-file scan is unreliable on
repos with real adoption, not just on long-tail ones.

### 6. 🔴 And the classifier trap is in the licences themselves

**The GPL-3.0 text contains the phrase *"GNU Affero General Public License"* in its own § 13.**
A detector that decides the family by searching for that phrase **labels every GPL-3.0 repo as
AGPL** — which this pass's own first read did, for Moodle, Chamilo, ILIAS and H5P, before being
caught. 🟢 **p199's `GPL-3.0` rows were right.** The clean discriminator, measured on `8` blobs:
**count of `affero` = `3` (incidental, § 13) for GPL-3.0 versus `15` for AGPL-3.0**, with the
title line agreeing and no overlap. 🔵 **Why this is a trend and not just a bug: GPL vs AGPL is
the difference between "you may host this for a client" and "hosting it may compel disclosure."**
A one-character-class mistake in a scanner becomes a wrong architecture.

### 7. 🟢 Regulation is converging on ONE requirement across three continents

**A human must remain accountable for high-stakes decisions about a student.**
**Oklahoma and Maryland** require human oversight and bar AI from high-stakes student decisions;
the **EU AI Act** imposes human-oversight duties on admissions/assessment/placement/proctoring
(Annex III.3); **Vietnam's Law No. 134/2025/QH15**, in force **1 March 2026** and applying to
foreign companies, puts automated assessment and behavioural monitoring on its **high-risk
list**; **Australia** proposes mandatory guardrails explicitly including education.
🟢 **Four jurisdictions, one control.** The requirement is converging faster than the market is
building for it — and per trend `3`, the open-source shelf for it is empty.

### 8. 🔵 "Evidence, not chat" is now the stated direction of travel

Independent sources this pass push the same way: **OECD's 2026 outlook** recommends moving
**beyond general-purpose AI tools toward purpose-built educational AI** designed for durable
learning gains; **HolonIQ** expects *selective* acceleration weighing ethics, transparency and
proven instructional benefit over personalisation claims; **1EdTech** describes institutions
building governance structures and evaluation processes; and systems increasingly **demand
evidence that products improve learning, persistence or job-relevant skills.** 🟢 **Evaluation
capability is becoming a procurement gate**, which favours a studio that can measure outcomes
over a vendor that can only ship features. 🔵 **This is the fourth consecutive cycle in which no
dominant open-source education-AI evaluation harness was found** — the gap p199's predecessors
also declared, still open.

### 9. 🟡 Adoption is wide, shallow and ungoverned — and the numbers finally agree on that

**LATAM: `87%` of `200` institutions use AI somewhere, `26%` have a strategy** (UNESCO IESALC).
**EMEA: `10%` of `450+` institutions have formal AI guidelines** (aggregator).
**North America: `60%` of US K-12 teachers used AI in 2024–25, `32%` weekly** — against a
**quarter** of HiEd institutions having deployed it formally (p199's ACE figure).
🟢 **Three regions, three instruments, one shape: usage has outrun governance everywhere.**
🔵 The sourcing quality differs sharply — UNESCO's is an institutional sample, the EMEA figure
is a roundup — **so the shape is reportable and the individual percentages are not equally
trustworthy.**

## Pass 199 — 2026-10-14 — CURRENT TRENDS

### 1. 🟢 The LMS integration layer commoditised — this is the quarter's real change

`8` MCP servers for the two dominant LMSs were verified alive and licensed today: `6` for Canvas
(`vishalsachdev` at `v1.14.0`, **October 2026**, `102` tools; `JohannsenLum` gatewaying all
`1,116` Canvas endpoints) and `2` for Moodle. **In cycle 3 (1 August) this layer did not appear
in this KB at all.** Consequence: *"integrate an agent with the LMS"* has stopped being the hard
part of an engagement. 🔴 **But none is official** — no Instructure or Moodle HQ server exists —
so every one is a community project a client would inherit.

### 2. 🟢 Agentic, not generative — and the administrative work is the beachhead

Market analysts describe the shift from experimental generative-AI apps to **autonomous agentic
workflows** handling scheduling, grading and administration; Microsoft added agent features to
its **Education AI Toolkit in April 2026**. 🔵 Note *where* the agency is landing: **back-office
and assessment, not the tutoring conversation.** The verified repos agree — this pass found `3`
new MIT rubric-grading agents and `0` new credible tutoring agents besides DeepTutor.

### 3. 🔴 Framework churn just invalidated a standing recommendation

**`microsoft/autogen` now states it is in maintenance mode and directs new users to
`microsoft/agent-framework`** (MIT, `14.1k`★, Python + .NET, with published migration guides
from both AutoGen and Semantic Kernel). Cycles 2 and 3 of this KB listed AutoGen as a top
education framework. **Any education pilot standing on AutoGen is now on a maintenance-mode
base** — in a 10-week engagement that is a migration you pay for, not a risk you note.

### 4. 🟢 One education agent reached real scale; the rest did not

**`DeepTutor` went from ~`24k`★ (cycle 3, 1 August) to `41.4k`★ today** — `+17k` in ~10 weeks —
shipping `v1.6.14` on **2026.10.8** with lifelong-memory tutoring. 🔴 **Everything else in the
education-specific category is small**: the next-largest genuinely education-specific tool
verified this pass is a `4.4k`★ *curriculum*, then `388`★, `292`★, `266`★.
🔵 **Read this carefully: there is no broad open-source education-agent ecosystem.** There is one
breakout project and a long tail of prototypes. Plan to compose general frameworks over LMS MCP
servers rather than to adopt a vertical agent stack that does not exist.

### 5. 🔴 Published star counts in this KB were systematically inflated

Independent re-measurement found `Educhain` at **`388`**★ against a published `~12k` (`~30×`) and
`OATutor` at **`266`**★ against `~1.5k` (`~5.6×`). The same defect was already recorded for the
technology KB in `rotation.json`. 🔵 **Treat every unverified star figure in this KB's history as
suspect**, and note that `smolagents` (`29.8k` vs published `~30k`) and `learn-agentic-ai`
(`4.4k` vs `~4.5k`) were accurate — so the inflation is not uniform and cannot be corrected by a
blanket discount. It has to be re-measured per row.

### 6. 🟢 Compliance moved from theme to deadline (EMEA), and the deadline moved

High-risk duties for assessment/admissions/proctoring AI slid to **2 December 2027** via the
Digital Omnibus (**2 August 2028** for embedded systems), while **generative-AI transparency
obligations were not delayed**. **Emotion recognition in schools has been banned since
2 February 2025** at up to **€35M / 7% of turnover**. 🔵 The practical read: *transparency work
is due now; conformity assessment has ~14 months of runway*, which is exactly long enough for
institutions to start procuring it this fiscal year.

### 7. 🟢 National curriculum mandates are now the largest funded demand in the sector

China (compulsory from age six, **September 2025**; AI-literacy infrastructure by **2030**) and
India (compulsory from **Class 3 in 2026–27**) have committed at national scale. 🔵 Mandates
create a **teacher-capability gap** before they create a software market — which is why
`learn-agentic-ai`-style curriculum assets and train-the-trainer models (Google.org/Raspberry
Pi's `$4.6M` for `1.25M` LATAM students by 2028) are the instrument that matches the demand.

### 8. 🔴 Public sentiment is not uniformly permissive

Ipsos Education Monitor 2026: **Japanese support for banning AI in schools rose `21%` → `29%`**,
even though Asian markets are generally *less* pro-ban than the global average. Meanwhile in US
higher education ~`22%` of institutions **formally prohibit** generative AI on assignments while
only ~`14%` formally allow it. 🔵 **Assessment integrity, not capability, is the live objection**
in a buying conversation — which is why the deterministic-plus-agentic grading split in
`compose/patterns.md` is the pattern that survives procurement.

### 9. 🟢 Sovereignty became a procurement criterion, unevenly

**Latam-GPT** (Chile, **February 2026**, CENIA, 60+ institutions / 15 countries, Spanish and
Portuguese first, Colombian textbooks in training data) gives LATAM public buyers a sovereignty
story; EMEA has the AI Act plus permissively-licensed local platforms (`OpenOLAT`, Apache-2.0).
🔴 **Neither has an education-specific open-source implementation** — Latam-GPT's education use
exists in press coverage, not in a repo. **That gap is a build opportunity, not a product to
resell.**
## Pass 198 — 2026-10-14

### 🆕 🔴 First: this pass ran CONCURRENTLY with p197, and p197 refutes three of its claims

🔵 **p197 was published by another session while this pass was mid-flight** *(commit `9cc3e97`,
`12:51`Z)*. 🟢 **It is the better outcome on three of the limbs, and the corrections are made
here rather than left for a reader to notice:**

| what this pass recorded | 🟢 what p197 actually established | correction |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 *"the execution lane is CLOSED; all `290` recipes are unrunnable"* | 🟢 **p197 RAN `lib/test_license_family.sh`: `207`/`207` from SIX invocation forms, `0` skips** | 🔴 **The closure is SESSION-SCOPED, not environmental.** `bash <repo script>` is denied by *this* session's permission classifier. It is a property of the reader, not of the KB |
| 🔴 the suite fixture fix is *"half-answered, blocked"* | 🟢 **DONE and better than pre-registered.** p196's diagnosis was wrong: the pre-fix suite yields **three** denominators *(`207`/`183`/`163`)*, and the cause is not the directory but whether a relative `dirname` survives the suite's own `cd`. 🔴 Through a symlink the classifier never loaded and the suite **still printed a total** *(`19`/`163`)* | ✅ **closed by p197** |
| 🔴 *"the `26` population rows no census has reached are still unreached"* | 🔴 **p197: that number is FALSE AS STATED.** `30` folded rows probed; `16` were already published with the **same byte figure** | ✅ **the `26` was this KB's own instrument reach, not a property of the shelf** |

🟢 **And p197's headline generalises this pass's own finding:** *three published numbers
measured an instrument's reach rather than a property of the world.* 🔴 **This pass supplies a
fourth, below — a byte count of its own that measured its reader.**

### 🆕 🟢 Two independent instruments, same rows, same hour — and the cross-check paid

🔵 Both passes read the same two limbs. 🟢 **`OWN-GRANT-AT-ROOT`: `3`/`3` EXACT — same family
and the same byte count on every row**, from two separately written probes:

| slug | p197 | 🟢 this pass | agree? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `kstateome/lti-launch` | `LGPL-3.0` · `7 651` | `LGPL-3.0` · `7 651` | 🟢 **exact** |
| `open-spaced-repetition/fsrs4anki-helper` | `MIT` · `1 079` | `MIT` · `1 079` | 🟢 **exact** |
| `openedx/XBlock` | `Apache-2.0` · `11 358` | `Apache-2.0` · `11 358` | 🟢 **exact** |

🔴 **`Lumi`'s population DIVERGED, and each instrument missed a row the other found:**

| row | p197 | this pass | 🟢 who was right |
|---|---|---|---|
| `H5P.BranchingScenario-1.8/src/fonts/LICENSE.txt` | `11 560` | 🔴 `11 358` | 🔴 **p197.** See the CRLF defect below |
| `assets/h5p/core/fonts/open-sans/LICENSE-2.0.txt` | 🔴 not returned | 🟢 `Apache-2.0` · `11 358` | 🟢 **this pass** |
| `client/assets/legal/eula.html` *(`259 095` B)* | 🟢 returned | 🔴 missed | 🔴 **p197** — a `259` KB **End-User Licence**, and this pass's name filter only matched `licen[cs]e`/`copying` |

🟢 **The union population is `16`, not `15`** — and 🟢 **both passes reach the same conclusion
from different populations: `Lumi` grants nothing of its own; every text is vendored.** 🔵
[`evidence/cross-pass-agreement.2026-10-14-p198.tsv`](../evidence/cross-pass-agreement.2026-10-14-p198.tsv).

### 🆕 🔴 The fourth number that measured its reader: this pass's own byte counts

🔴 **Python text mode applies UNIVERSAL NEWLINES.** Reading a licence as text and reporting
`len(text.encode())` loses **one byte per CRLF line**, so the figure is the LF-normalised
length, not the file's size:

```
  BranchingScenario font LICENSE.txt   wc -c = 11 560   text-mode = 11 358   CRLF = 202
```

🟢 **`11 560 − 11 358 = 202`, exactly the CRLF count.** 🟢 **Blast radius, measured rather than
assumed: ONE row.** Every other text read this pass — all `30` root-licence rows, all `3` root
payloads, the other `13` `Lumi` texts — is LF, `delta 0`, so 🟢 **every byte figure in the
tables below stands**, and the `3` root payloads are independently confirmed byte-for-byte by
p197.

🔴 **But the band table was computed from these counts**, so a CRLF row would have shifted a
ratio silently. 🟢 **The fix is in `p198-title-line-family/`: read in BINARY, report `len(raw)`,
and report the CRLF count alongside it** so a reflowed text is visible as reflowed rather than
showing up as a band failure. 🔵 [`evidence/crlf-byte-undercount.2026-10-14-p198.tsv`](../evidence/crlf-byte-undercount.2026-10-14-p198.tsv).

🔵 **The lesson, and it is the same one three times over now:** `GPL-2.0`'s preamble made a
*window* report the wrong family; a filename list made a *first match* report the wrong file;
text mode made a *byte count* report the wrong size. 🟢 **Every one is a reader's property
published as the world's.**

🆕 🔴 **The trend this pass can state for the first time is that a silent channel is more
dangerous than a wrong one — and the silence is BIASED.** 🟢 Across `15` licence rows the
GitHub MCP channel has never once diverged from the bytes. 🔴 **But where it goes silent
(`NOASSERTION`), half the rows turn out to be non-commercial or share-alike** — the exact
licences that cannot ship in a client engagement.

### 🆕 🔴 T1 — `NOASSERTION` is not "no licence"; it is "unread", and it hides the restrictive ones

| channel verdict | n *(p196+p197)* | 🟢 agreement with bytes |
|---|---|---|
| a declared `spdx_id` | `15` | 🟢 **`15` agree, `0` diverge** |
| 🔴 `NOASSERTION` | `6` | 🔴 **all `6` resolvable from bytes — the channel simply could not parse them** |

🔴 **And of this pass's `4` `NOASSERTION` rows, `2` are undeliverable:**
[`krahets/hello-algo`](https://github.com/krahets/hello-algo) at **`130 684`★** is
🔴 **`CC-BY-NC`**, and `Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills` is 🔴 **`CC-BY-SA`** in a `620`-byte
pointer. 🟢 **The rule: `NOASSERTION` must raise the flag, never lower it.** A reputation-led
pick with a non-commercial licence is the most expensive mistake available on this page.

### 🆕 🔴 T2 — Education's top-of-rank is licensed as CONTENT, not as code

🟢 Of the `23` highest-ranked education repositories active in the last `30` days,
🔴 **`6` carry a Creative Commons licence** *(`CC-BY-4.0`×`2`, `CC-BY-SA`×`2`, `CC-BY-NC`,
`CC0`)*. 🔴 **CC licences were not drafted for software**: no patent grant, and `-NC`/`-SA`
variants constrain exactly what an engagement does.

🟢 **This is a structural property of the education industry, not a sampling artefact** — the
sector's biggest repositories are curricula, book lists and algorithm courses, i.e. *content*.
🔴 A studio that filters education repos by *"permissive"* and accepts `CC-BY` has mistaken a
content licence for a code grant.

### 🆕 🔴 T3 — The licence is drifting AWAY from the file a reader opens

🔵 Four distinct shapes, all found this pass, all on real education rows:

| shape | row | what a naive read returns |
|---|---|---|
| **dual-file**: content licence at `LICENSE`, code grant elsewhere | [`microsoft/autogen`](https://github.com/microsoft/autogen) | 🔴 `CC-BY-4.0` instead of `MIT` |
| **licence as a translated UI asset**, nothing at root | [`formalms/formalms`](https://github.com/formalms/formalms) | 🔴 *"no licence"* |
| **`NOTICE`-only**: family declared, 🔴 **no licence text shipped** | [`foradian/fedena`](https://github.com/foradian/fedena) | 🔴 *"Apache-2.0"* with nothing behind it |
| **`19`-byte pointer** | [`frappe/education`](https://github.com/frappe/education) | 🔴 *"GPL-3.0"* with no grant text |

🟢 **The trend to carry forward: `LICENSE` at the root is becoming an unreliable convention**,
and tree enumeration plus per-file classification is becoming the only sound read. 🔴 Every
licence column in this KB that was built from a first-match filename list is suspect to the
degree this shape is common — and **counting how common is pre-registered for p198.**

### 🆕 🟢 T4 — Every copyleft family has a sibling-naming sentence; classify on the TITLE LINE

🟢 **The `GPL-3.0` §13 trap is not special. `GPL-2.0`'s PREAMBLE does the same thing**, and it
sits inside the first `40` lines of the canonical text, so no window size avoids it:

| family | the sentence | a window classifier returns |
|---|---|---|
| `GPL-3.0` | *"13. Use with the GNU Affero General Public License."* | 🔴 `AGPL-3.0` |
| 🆕 `GPL-2.0` | *"(Some other FSF software is covered by the GNU **Lesser** GPL instead.)"* | 🔴 `LGPL?` |

🟢 **One rule defeats both: take the first line in DOCUMENT order that matches a licence title.**
The real title always precedes the sentence that names a sibling. 🔴 **This KB's stored data
had the defect: `4` rows in `p441`'s `families` column read `LGPL?` and all `4` are `GPL-2.0`;
zero are LGPL.**

🔵 **The generalisation worth keeping:** *widening a filename list cannot fix a list* `(P441)`;
🆕 **resizing a window cannot fix a window.** Both are the same error — a positional heuristic
standing in for a structural read.

### 🟡 T5 — Licence byte bands are family-dependent, and two families have no usable anchor

🟢 Bands hold tightly where the text is distributed verbatim, and fail where it is not:

| family | ratio to SPDX pristine | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `GPL-3.0` *(n=`5`)* | `1.013`–`1.014`, 🟢 **`28` B spread** | 🟢 **HOLDS** |
| `ECL-2.0` | `1.001` | 🟢 holds *(n=`1`)* |
| `AGPL-3.0` *(n=`4`)* | `1.015`–`1.033` | 🟢 **HOLDS** |
| `Apache-2.0` *(n=`4`)* | `1.068`–`1.110` | 🟢 **HOLDS** — the appendix is filled in |
| `MIT` *(n=`15`)* | `0.982`–`1.058` | 🟡 **holds after excluding `1` row** that carries more than one notice |
| 🔴 `GPL-2.0` *(n=`4`)* | 🔴 `0.874`–`0.997` | 🔴 **FAILS** — reflowed variants run `13`% **under** pristine |
| 🔴 `LGPL-3.0` *(n=`3`)* | 🔴 **`0.182`–`0.196`** | 🔴 **FAILS — wrong anchor.** The shipped form is a `~7.6` KB addendum; SPDX publishes a `42 098` B standalone |

🟢 **So a byte band is a legitimate instrument with a stated domain**, not a general one. 🔴 **A
freshness or integrity gate that centres `LGPL-3.0` on SPDX's anchor flags every real
repository** — that is a false-positive generator, not a control.

### 🟢 T6 — The industry's own trend line: purpose-built is beating general-purpose

🟢 **Independent convergence, and one source is institutional:** the **OECD `2026` Digital
Education Outlook** recommends moving beyond general-purpose tools toward purpose-built
educational AI designed for durable learning gains; vendor and practitioner sources describe
the same shift away from generic chatbots because general tools **added** prompt-writing and
fact-checking work for teachers.

🟡 Adoption figures travel with it but are weaker: `92`% of university students using AI *(up
from `66`%)* and `54`% of K-12 students *(RAND `2025`)* — 🔴 single-source, carried as
indicative.

🔴 **Market size remains irreconcilable:** $`10.6`B `2026`→$`42.48`B `2030` *(Research &
Markets)* vs ≈$`6`B `2024`→$`32`B `2030` *(K-12 Academics)* vs $`12.3`B *(HolonIQ, via a vendor
blog)*. 🟡 **Fifth consecutive pass carrying this as a conflict rather than a number.**

### 🆕 🟢 T7 — Regulation is converging on ASSESSMENT as the regulated surface

🟢 **Four jurisdictions, independently drafted, land on the same component:**

| jurisdiction | what it names |
|---|---|
| EU *(Annex III)* | determining access to education, **assessing learning outcomes**, influencing an educational path |
| 🆕 Vietnam *(law `2026-03-01`)* | 🔴 **automated assessment** and behavioural monitoring, high-risk |
| South Korea *(AI Basic Act)* | education as **high-impact** |
| North America *(Charleston County; Idaho)* | 🔴 AI not the **sole basis** for high-stakes determinations; no replacing teachers |

🟢 **The engineering consequence is a single architectural rule that satisfies all four: the
assessment path is the regulated path** — human-in-the-loop, per-action receipts, and
learner-facing disclosure of which spans are AI-authored. 🟢 One build, four markets.

### 🆕 🔴 T8 — The adoption/governance gap is the global pattern, and it is now measured on two continents

| region | adopting | governed |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **LATAM higher ed** *(UNESCO IESALC, `200` institutions, `19` countries)* | 🟢 **`87`%** | 🔴 **`26`%** |
| 🟡 **EMEA** *(`450`+ institutions)* | — | 🔴 **`10`% have formal guidelines** |
| North America K-12 | — | 🟢 `33`–`35` states have **guidance**; few mandate policy |

🔴 **The scarce artefact worldwide is not a model — it is a governance instrument**, and in
LATAM the `61`-point gap makes the policy artefact itself the sellable deliverable. 🟢 This is
the first pass where that claim rests on a survey rather than on inference.

### 🆕 🔴 T9 — A method trend this KB must record about itself: the register outlived the instruments

🔴 **`bash <repo script>` is denied this pass.** All `290` recipes are readable and none is
runnable. 🟢 **`P0a` — read the register before building — was worth MORE in this state, not
less:** it answered `6` of `7` pre-registered actions from prose and stored results alone.

🔴 **But `3` pre-registered actions were blocked outright**, and the honest lesson is about
sequencing: 🟢 **run the lane census BEFORE planning the pass.** p196 pre-registered seven
actions against instruments that turned out to be unrunnable; a census first would have
pre-registered seven that were.

## Pass 197 — 2026-10-14

🔵 **Trend ids continue the shelf's sequence** — the highest id this KB had published was
`T316`, so this pass opens at `T317`. 🔵 A new trend reusing a live id is the collision class
`p150-identifier-collision/` exists to catch, and it is cheaper to check than to unpick.

🆕 🔴 **`T317` — the trend this pass can prove is that the AI in education is moving INTO the LMS
as plugins, and the plugin layer is `GPL-3.0` almost throughout.**

| | evidence |
|---|---|
| population of the layer | 🟢 **`198`** repositories *(`moodle AI language:php pushed:>2026-06-01`, ranked channel)* |
| licences read this pass | `GPL-3.0` ×`2` *(`35 149`, `35 178` B)*, `GPL` in source headers ×`3` *(`40`/`50`, `255`/`278`, `64`/`91` files)*, 🟢 `MIT` ×`1` *(`microsoft/moodle-ai-assistant`, `1 084` B)* |
| what the layer contains | a **governance** plugin *(providers, quotas, per-tenant policy)*, a **chat** block on top of it, an **LLM-graded question type**, an **oral-exam** tool *(created `2026-10-05`)*, a **generative question bank**, and an **MCP server over the LMS web-service layer** |

🔵 **Why this is a trend and not a list.** Through `2026` this KB recorded agent frameworks
arriving **beside** the LMS — a tutor you integrate. The rows above arrive **inside** it, as
plugins installed by an administrator, governed by the platform's own permission model and
bound by the platform's licence. 🟢 **That changes who buys** *(the LMS administrator, not the
innovation office)* 🔴 **and it changes what you may keep** *(a derivative plugin is `GPL`)*.

🆕 🔴 **`T318` — a shelf-wide «no licence» rate cannot survive contact with a layer that licenses
per file.**

| probe | what it says about `3` Moodle plugins |
|---|---|
| root filename | 🔴 *«no licence»* |
| complete tree *(`P441`'s channel)* | 🔴 *«absence enumerated»* |
| 🟢 source headers *(p139's instrument)* | 🟢 **`GRANT_GPL` in `40`/`50`, `255`/`278`, `64`/`91`** |

🔴 **So `16.2`% overstates absence in this layer**, and p139's *«`0` of `9`»* — measured over
**npm** packages — does not generalise to it. 🟢 **The durable form of the figure is per layer,
with the layer named**, which is pre-registered rather than asserted.

🆕 🔴 **`T319` — `NOASSERTION` is now a reliable signal of something, and it is not «unknown».**
Third instance in two passes: `TrenTorch` *(PolyForm Noncommercial)*, `frappe/education`
*(`19` bytes of declaration)*, and this pass 🔴
[`nakafaai/nakafa.com`](https://github.com/nakafaai/nakafa.com) *(`7 705` B,
**`NONCOMMERCIAL-NOT-OSI`**, `79`★, an AI-native K-12→university platform)*. 🟢 **Read the file,
not the field** — and the cost of not doing so is a deliverable built on a licence that bars it.

🆕 🟡 **`T320` — the regulatory centre of gravity moved from «AI acts» to instruments that were
already in force.** Three measured this pass, none of them an AI statute:

| region | the instrument that binds NOW | not |
|---|---|---|
| EMEA | 🆕 Article `4` **AI-literacy duty, since `2025-02-02`**; Article `5` **emotion-inference ban**; Article `50` marking since `2026-08-02` | 🔴 Annex III high-risk, deferred to `2027-12-02` |
| LATAM | 🔴 Chile's **Ley `21.719`** data-protection/profiling regime, **in force `2026-12-01`** | 🔴 Chile's AI bill, second constitutional stage |
| North America | 🔴 **Maryland `S.B. 720`** *(AI not the primary basis for grading, discipline or placement, since `2026-06-01`)*; Ohio's district-policy mandate *(`2026-07-01`)* | 🟡 a federal framework that may preempt them |

🔵 **The pattern is the same in all three: a general-purpose obligation reaches the education
deliverable before the education-specific rule does.** 🟢 For a studio this is good news — the
artefacts are the ones engineering can produce *(a marking layer, a profiling record, a
human-in-the-loop grading trail)*, not a conformity programme waiting on `2027`.

🆕 🟡 **`T321` — the appetite curve may have turned in one region, and only one source
differences it.** The Digital Education Council's `2026` higher-education survey has faculty
intent to use AI in **US & Canada down `76`% → `67`%** year over year, other regions steady.
🔴 **One source, secondary, and it measures intent rather than deployment** — recorded because
it is the only year-over-year regional difference any channel returned, and because it points
the opposite way to every vendor trend piece in the same sweep.

### 🔴 Carried, and sharpened: what this KB still cannot measure

🔴 **Flow.** `github.com/trending` `403`, **fourth consecutive pass**, code recorded.
🔴 **Agentic market size.** No figure, **fifth pass**.
🔴 **The `2026` market estimates reached this pass disagree by `28`%** *(`$9.58`bn vs `$12.3`bn)*
and a third projects `$32.27`bn by `2030` on a different basis. 🟢 This page's standing rule
holds: **publish the spread, never the midpoint.**

## Pass 196 — 2026-10-14

🆕 🟢 **The trend this pass can state for the first time is about this KB's own method, and it
is measurable: the register is now worth more than the instrument.** 🟢 p195 pre-registered four
actions; a grep of `compose/code/` *(288 recipes)* and the four pages answered **three of them
before any code was written**, and one of the three turned out to have been **pre-registered as
an instrument this KB proved does not work four passes ago.**

🔴 **The second trend is that the measurement lane keeps moving, and *"the lane is gone"* has
twice meant *"this door is shut."*** p195 declared the GitHub API lane dead; it answers through
MCP. 🟢 A lane census must name the **door**, not the destination.

### 🆕 🟢 T1 — Education's copyleft estate carries NO narrowed grants, and the negative is the finding

🟢 **`83` copyleft rows, every one banded against two external anchors, `79` `IN-BAND`, `4`
flagged, and all `4` flags are false positives for narrowing.** 🟢 **`0` of `83`.**

🔵 The `4` resolve into two previously unseparated classes:

| class | n | what it is |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **GNU short-notice form** | `3` | the FSF's prescribed standard notice + a `gnu.org` pointer. **A valid grant by incorporation by reference** |
| 🟢 **missing non-operative appendix** | `1` | `rosariosis` ends at `END OF TERMS AND CONDITIONS`; sections `0`–`12` all present |

🔴 **The short-notice rows look exactly like `Gap 421` and are its legal opposite.** `Gap 421`'s
registered shapes are sub-`1`kB payloads that **grant nothing**; these grant **everything the
full text grants**. 🟢 **Same size class, opposite content** — which is why a byte band is a
trigger to read and can never be a verdict. 🔵 The trend: **as copyleft adoption spreads to
small plugin repositories, the notice form becomes more common, and every size-based control
will see more of it.** `3` of `83` today on this shelf.

### 🆕 🟢 T2 — Licence documents are BIMODAL by publisher convention, and single-interval controls get the big platforms backwards

🟢 **`LGPL-3.0` ships in two legitimate forms** — short *(`7 651` B, `GPL-3.0` by reference)*
and full *(`42 098` B, `GPL-3.0` by value)* — and this shelf carries platforms on both:
`bigbluebutton` `7 652`, `openeducat_erp` `8 241`, and 🟢 **`odoo/odoo` `43 529`.**

🔴 **A one-interval band calls one of the two forms "narrowed," and on this shelf it would have
flagged `odoo/odoo` — the largest row in the family.** 🟢 **The structural lesson generalises
past licences: a control that assumes one canonical form per category will misread the
publisher convention, not the content.** The fix was a band **SET**, with p195's
single-interval families as the one-element case.

### 🆕 🔴 T3 — A filename list has now failed FIVE times on the same repository, and the fifth failure was written into a pre-registration

| pass | the widening | result on [`openedx/XBlock`](https://github.com/openedx/XBlock) |
|---|---|---|
| p436 | `14` names | 🔴 missed `LICENSE.TXT` |
| p440 | `41` names | 🔴 missed, **third** time |
| p195 | `19`-name ladder | 🔴 missed, **fourth** time — then published inside its `88` |
| 🔴 **p196's pre-registration** | `docs/` + `legal/` + `.github/` | 🔴 **would have missed it a FIFTH time — the file is at the ROOT** |

🟢 **`P441`'s channel answers it in one call**: `git clone --filter=blob:none --no-checkout
--depth 1` then `git ls-tree -r`. `88`/`88` clones, `0` failures, **`12` of `88` rows carry
their own grant and `3` of those are at the root** *(`LICENSE.TXT`, `License.txt`, `License` —
each two or three characters from a name the ladder tried)*.

🔴 **And the trend is not about one script.** This pass's own throwaway `12`-name probe **missed
`frappe/education`** because its path is lowercase `license.txt`, and this pass's population
extractor shipped **two successive list-widenings of its own** *(`repos/..`, then
`repos/...`)*. 🟢 **Naming a defect does not confer immunity to it.** The durable form is a
**rule** — *"a name with no non-dot character is not a repository"* — not a longer list.

### 🆕 🔴 T4 — `NOASSERTION` is a blind spot, not a soft "unknown", and it is blind where delivery is decided

🟢 `13` rows cross-checked, MCP's `license.spdx_id` against this KB's byte reads:
🟢 **`11` AGREE, `0` DIVERGE** — 🔴 **`2` API-BLIND.**

| row | the channel | 🔴 the bytes |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 `TrenTorch/TrenTorch` `385`★ | `NOASSERTION` | 🔴 **PolyForm Noncommercial `1.0.0` — `commercial_use_ok` PROHIBITED** |
| 🔴 `frappe/education` `665`★ | `NOASSERTION` | 🔴 **`19` bytes, in full: `License: GNU GPL V3`** |

🟢 **`0` DIVERGE licenses the ranked channel for DISCOVERY.** 🔴 **`2` blind forbids it for
DELIVERY.** 🔵 The trend to watch: **both blind rows are small, recent repositories** — the

### 🆕 🟡 T5 — Declared licence rigour varies WITHIN a publisher, and it decides a hosted deliverable

🔵 Two Frappe repositories, same organisation:

| repo | licence artefact | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 [`frappe/lms`](https://github.com/frappe/lms) | `AGPL-3.0`, **`33 893` B** of real text | 🟢 a grant — this KB's costliest finding, on three channels |
| 🔴 [`frappe/education`](https://github.com/frappe/education) | 🔴 **`19` B**: `License: GNU GPL V3` | 🔴 a **declaration** |

🔴 **One publisher, two rigours, and the difference decides whether a hosted deliverable can
ship.** 🟢 The practical rule: **organisation-level licence reputation does not transfer across
repositories.** Read the row, not the owner.

### 🟢 T6 — Regulation's centre of gravity moved from national statute to LOCAL POLICY MANDATE

🟢 **North America is the clearest case, and it is dense**: `77` AI-in-classroom bills across
`27` states in the `2026` session, ≈`33`–`35` states with official K-12 guidance, and at least
`5` states obliging **districts** to adopt AI policies — Maryland *(AI coordinators + funded
PD)*, Ohio *(`2026-07-01`, model policy published)*, Oklahoma *(`2027-28`, four named clauses)*,
Tennessee *(maintain **and report**)*, Idaho *(and AI may not replace teachers)*.

🔴 **The vendor-side counterpart is Vermont H.B. `650`: edtech providers must register and
certify privacy compliance ANNUALLY.** 🟢 **The trend: the regulated artefact is shifting from
the model to the deployment's governance paperwork** — approved/prohibited-use registers, data
minimisation, family transparency, periodic review. 🟢 That is buildable, and this KB already
has receipt and audit primitives for it.

### 🆕 🟢 T7 — The EU deferred the half that was in the future and left the half that already binds

🟢 **Regulation (EU) `2026/1744`** *(OJ `2026-07-24`, in force `2026-07-27`)* moved **Annex III
high-risk — education included — from `2026-08-02` to `2027-12-02`**, a `16`-month deferral,
and Annex I to `2028-08-02`.

🔴 **Article `50` transparency was NOT delayed and has applied since `2026-08-02`.** New
Article `5` prohibitions land `2026-12-02`.

🔴 **So the widely-reported *"education AI relief"* inverts the operative picture.** 🟢 The duty
that touches a shipped deliverable **today** is marking and disclosure — and this KB's
Article `50(2)` instrument family is therefore the **live** half of its regulatory work, not
the anticipatory half. 🔴 **Caveat carried, not buried:** `eur-lex` does not resolve from this
session, so the OJ number is secondary convergence, not a primary read.

🟢 **And the convergence across regions is itself the trend: China's binding AI-content
labelling is the same instrument class as EU Article `50`.** 🟢 **One marking layer, two
jurisdictions** — see [`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md) this pass.

### 🟡 T8 — Market sizing has stopped being usable; adoption has not

🔴 **Four firms priced `2026` between `$6.4` bn and `$11.4` bn — a `78`% spread on the CURRENT
year** — and one firm publishes **two different CAGRs** *(`34.52`% and `36.02`%)* in its own
pages. 🔴 *"AI tutors `$2.7` bn"* and *"AI tutoring platforms `$4.12` bn"* in the same year are
two definitions, not two measurements.

🟢 **The adoption figures held up and are the ones to quote**: student AI use **`88`%**, faculty
**`77`%** *(Digital Education Council, July `2026`)*; Khanmigo `45` → **`380`+** district
partners. 🔴 **And the gap, declared: no channel reached this pass sizes AGENTIC tutoring at
all** — the segment this KB's agent shelf actually serves.

### 🟡 T9 — The product direction visible in the leading agent is practice and PROGRESS STATE

🔵 [`HKUDS/DeepTutor`](https://github.com/HKUDS/DeepTutor) shipped twice this week:
**`v1.6.14` (`2026-10-08`)** adds visual practice, a **learning journal** and managed plugins;
`v1.6.13` (`2026-10-04`) added question-bank practice and configurable speech.

🟢 **Read across the two releases, the move is from *explanation* to *assessable sequence with
retained state*** — question banks, practice, a journal. 🔴 **Which is exactly the surface
Brazil's PL `2338/2023` would classify as high-risk *(educational evaluation)* and the EU's
Annex III already does.** 🟢 **The trend and the regulation are converging on the same
feature**, and a studio building here should assume the assessment path is the regulated path.

| 🔴 `AGPL-3.0` | **`41`** | 🔴 **`11`%** |

| 🔴 source-available *(`PolyForm` `4`, `Elastic` `1`)* + non-commercial `2` | **`7`** | 🔴 `2`% |

🔴 **The permissive share is high and it is NOT distributed evenly — it is a function of what
the repository IS.** 🟢 The agent, tutor, curriculum and MCP-server layers are overwhelmingly

Frappe LMS, `classroomio`, `learnhouse`, `tutor`. 🟡 **So *"education OSS is permissive"* is
true of the layer you build and false of the layer you host**, and a proposal that averages the
two is wrong in the direction that costs money.

🔴 **The standards are open; their reference implementations are not a licensed starting
point.** 🟢 **The trend statement, falsifiable: as education software gets more interoperable,
the licence risk moves from the PLATFORM to the GLUE** — the platforms all ship a licence, and
the adapters increasingly do not.

a counter-example — [`OS4ED/openSIS-Classic`](https://github.com/OS4ED/openSIS-Classic) carries

### 🆕 🔴 T3 — A hardened classifier reads three restrictive licences as `MIT`, and the mechanism is COMPOSITION

🔵 `osi_family_of` returns `MIT` and `commercial_use_ok` returns ALLOWED for all three:

| repo | bytes | real title | what the body does |
|---|---|---|---|
| [`CaviraOSS/PageLM`](https://github.com/CaviraOSS/PageLM) | `8 563` | *"PageLM Community License"* | 🔴 non-commercial + revenue-share |
| [`GautamVhavle/learner-verse`](https://github.com/GautamVhavle/learner-verse) | `2 594` | **BUSL 1.1** | 🔴 bars *"direct or indirect commercial activity"* |
| [`willwearing/graspful`](https://github.com/willwearing/graspful) | `1 439` | *"O'Saasy License Agreement"* | 🔴 anti-SaaS non-compete |

🔴 **`P171` widened the title window to `40` lines so a REFLOWED `AGPL` would be caught, and
that window swallows MIT's canonical *"Permission is hereby granted, free of charge"* sentence
out of licences that are not MIT. `P250`'s gate then trusts the family and never reads the
body.** 🟢 **Two hardened controls, each correct alone, composing into the one wrong answer
that costs a delivery.** 🔵 `learner-verse` is the proof: `commercial_use_ok` **has** a `BUSL`
branch returning PROHIBITED, and it never fired because the family answered `MIT` first.

🟡 **This is a trend about the LICENCES, not only about the tool: authors are writing MIT's
grant sentence at the top of restrictive licences.** 🔴 **Four distinct instances now
*(`PageLM`, `learner-verse`, `graspful`, and `talentcoach`'s verbatim MIT warranty paragraph
over an all-rights-reserved grant)*, which makes it a drafting pattern rather than an accident.**

🟢 Resolved this pass against p194's CONTESTED cell: an **August `2025`** law reclassified

uptake fell *(`37`% → `19`%; second-semester use `1 686` schools, `−58.8`%)*, and ≈`10`

🟡 **The trend, stated so it transfers:** the reported failure causes are **classroom technical
failure, data-privacy objection, and teacher and parent opposition — not model quality.**
🟢 **So the reversal risk in a ministry-anchored engagement is an OPERATIONS and CONSENT risk**,
and the deliverable that survives it is standards-based content plus an exportable record,
not a programme-specific platform.

🟢 **LATAM remains the only region where adoption measurably outruns governance** — `92`% of

🆕 🟡 **APAC's mandates are ahead of both, and Korea has now shown they are reversible** —
which is a different risk from the one this KB has been describing.
🔴 **APAC still has no measured adoption RATE here, second pass, and the searches are named in

inside `±10`, net `+22` across ≈`120 000`★ — `0.018`%.** 🔵 Combined with the loss of the

## Pass 194 — 2026-10-14

🆕 🔴 **The trend this pass measures is that education's open-source output has moved to a
publication format no education taxonomy indexes, and arrived there carrying licences that bar
the use Globant would make of it.** 🟢 Both halves measured today, both falsifiable.

### 🆕 🔴 T1 — Education trends in exactly one lane, and thirteen passes probed the wrong one

| lane | rows | education rows |
|---|---|---|
| default weekly + daily + `trending/python` + `spoken_language_code=pt` | `32` | 🔴 **`0`** |
| 🟢 `trending/jupyter-notebook` + `spoken_language_code=es` | `18` | 🟢 **`10`** |

🟢 **The mechanism, and it is not a GitHub quirk: the notebook IS the pedagogical artefact.**
Lesson text, runnable cell and output live in one file, so a filter on `.ipynb` is accidentally
a filter on *teaching*. 🔴 **`trending/python` returned `0` education rows in the same hour that
`trending/jupyter-notebook` returned `9`** — same ecosystem, same week, same language family.

🔵 **Falsifier for p195:** probe `zh`, `fr`, `de`, `ja`, `hi` spoken-language lanes. 🟢 If
education concentrates in narrow lanes generally, they should return non-zero education rates
on small denominators. 🔴 If they return `0`, then the notebook lane is doing the work and
language filtering is not, and this trend narrows to **one** lane rather than a class of them.

### 🆕 🔴 T2 — The non-commercial tutorial economy: half the stars, none of the rights

🔵 `10` education rows, `229 058`★, licence read from bytes on every one:

| | stars | share |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **no commercial grant** | `113 904` | 🔴 **`49.7`%** |
| 🟢 deliverable | `109 418` | `47.8`% |
| 🟡 conditions attach *(`LGPL-3.0`)* | `5 736` | `2.5`% |

🔴 **And `91 466` of the `109 418` deliverable stars are a single repository**,
`microsoft/ML-For-Beginners`. 🔴 **Remove one corporate publisher and education's trending shelf
is `84`% unusable for client work.**

🟡 **The licence class is not new to this KB — `lib/license_family.sh` has modelled
`NONCOMMERCIAL-NOT-OSI` for passes, and it classified all three of these payloads correctly
without prior knowledge of them. 🟢 What is new is which repositories populate it, and their
share.** `NirDiamant`'s
three repositories share one byte-identical `Custom License Agreement`: *"expressly prohibited
from using the Licensed Material, in whole or in part, for any commercial purpose"*, with **all
commercial rights exclusively reserved.** 🔵 AGPL permits commercial use and attaches disclosure;
**this permits none.** 🟡 Ordering: `permissive → weak copyleft → Affero → **no commercial
grant**`.

🔵 **Why it is a trend and not a catalogue entry:** the barred repositories are precisely the
*production-oriented* ones — `agents-towards-production` describes itself as tutorials for
*"enterprise deployment."* 🔴 **The corpus for taking agents into an enterprise is licensed so
an enterprise cannot use it**, and it gained `163` stars this week.

### 🆕 🔴 T3 — Curriculum ships under CONTENT licences; the code inside it does not notice

| repo | licence | 🔴 the mismatch |
|---|---|---|
| [`datawhalechina/every-embodied`](https://github.com/datawhalechina/every-embodied) | `CC-BY-4.0` *(`858` B)* | 🔴 a robotics/VLA **code** curriculum under a licence written for prose |
| [`datawhalechina/llm-algo-leetcode`](https://github.com/datawhalechina/llm-algo-leetcode) | `CC-BY-4.0` *(`792` B)* | 🔴 solutions and test cases — **code** — under the same |
| [`xiaolai/the-craft-of-selfteaching`](https://github.com/xiaolai/the-craft-of-selfteaching) | `CC-BY-NC-ND-3.0` *(README only, **no file**)* | 🔴 `NC` **and** `ND`: may not be sold **and** may not be adapted |
| [`enkidevs/curriculum`](https://github.com/enkidevs/curriculum) | `CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0` *(`189` B)* | 🔴 carried since p192; `NC` |

🟡 **Creative Commons explicitly advises against using CC licences for software**, because they
address neither source availability nor patent grants. 🟢 **`CC-BY-4.0` is still workable** for
an engagement — commercial use permitted, attribution required — 🔴 **but it tells a client
nothing about the code's warranty or patent position**, and three of the four rows above are
`NC` on top. 🔵 **The trend: as curriculum becomes code, its authors keep reaching for content
licences.** 🟢 Falsifier: if p195's wider byte-read over the shelf finds `CC-*` concentrated in
pre-`2024` repositories, this is legacy rather than a trend.

### 🆕 🔴 T4 — The `2026` cohort still ships no licence, now with large repositories

🔵 p193 measured five `2026`-created education repositories at `333`–`515`★, **all five with no
licence file.** 🆕 **p194 shows the pattern is not confined to small new repositories.**

| repo | ★ | probe | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🟡 [`anthropics/prompt-eng-interactive-tutorial`](https://github.com/anthropics/prompt-eng-interactive-tutorial) | `38 564` | `19` filenames + README | 🟢 **`MIT` at `AmazonBedrock/LICENSE`** *(`947` B, p215)* — 🔴 the `19` filenames were all ROOT <!--corrected--> |
| 🔴 [`JushBJJ/Mr.-Ranedeer-AI-Tutor`](https://github.com/JushBJJ/Mr.-Ranedeer-AI-Tutor) | `29 553` | same | 🔴 **no licence, none named** |
| 🔴 [`midudev/libros-programacion-gratis`](https://github.com/midudev/libros-programacion-gratis) | `6 072` | same | 🔴 **no licence, none named** |

🔴 **`29 553`★ is the `#3` row on this KB's own shelf.** 🟢 **No licence is not permissive —
it is the absence of a grant**, and default copyright applies. 🟡 **Direction across two passes:
p193 found it in the new-and-small cohort, p194 finds it in the large-and-established one, so
the honest statement is not *"new projects skip licences"* — it is *"education repositories skip
licences at every size."***

### 🆕 🟢 T5 — Platforms and curriculum are now visibly two markets at two speeds

🔵 Not one of the `15` platform rows in [`verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md)
appeared in any of six trending lanes. `openedx/openedx-platform` moved `+3` stars in `4` days.
🟢 Meanwhile `9` curriculum corpora gained `1 266` stars in a week.

🟢 **The actionable form: platform choice is an installed-base and licence decision; curriculum
choice is an attention and licence decision.** 🔴 **An engagement that reads only trending builds
a course with nothing to run it in; one that reads only the platform shelf ships a system with
`2019` content.** 🔵 This KB should stop expecting one shelf to serve both, and p195's shelf
re-pricing should report platforms and curriculum **separately**.

### 🆕 🟢 T6 — The study layer keeps being published as runnable artefacts, now on a fourth surface

🔵 p192: the second-largest `edtech`-topicked repo on GitHub is a **PDF→skill compiler**
*(`virgiliojr94/book-to-skill`, `34 806`★, `MIT`)*. p193: `mattpocock/skills` *(`290 184`★)* and
`vercel-labs/skills` *(`34 736`★)* trending, neither tagged education. 🆕 **p194: the week's
education momentum is `9` notebook corpora and `0` platforms.**

🟢 **Four independent surfaces agree** — topic, shelf, trending-default, trending-by-language.
🟢 **The study layer is shipping as skills and notebooks, outside every education taxonomy**,
which is the mechanism behind thirteen passes of empty topic queries.

### 🆕 🟡 T7 — Adoption and governance are sequenced differently by region, and it is measured

| region | adoption | governance | 🔴 gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| **LATAM** | 🟢 `87`% institutional, `79`% faculty | 🔴 `26`% strategy, `45`% guidance | 🔴 **`61` points** |
| **EMEA** | `63`% teachers | `≈70`% guidance | 🟢 governance ahead |
| **North America** | `60`% teachers | `≈70`% guidance; `>28` states | 🟢 governance ahead |
| **APAC** | 🔴 **not measured** | 🟢 ministry mandates *(China, Japan)* | 🟡 regulation ahead of measurement |

🔴 **LATAM is the only region where adoption measurably outruns governance** — and it has the
KB's highest measured adoption. 🟢 **Not a weakness story, a sequencing story**: it changes the
first deliverable per region, as [`intel/market.md`](market.md) sets out. 🔴 **APAC's blank
adoption cell is this pass's named gap**, not an implied zero.

### 🆕 🟡 T8 — Consolidation reaches the tooling an education engagement would have built on

🔴 [`continuedev/continue`](https://github.com/continuedev/continue) — **`Apache-2.0`
*(`11 348` B, read from bytes this pass)*, archived and read-only after acquisition by Cursor,
June `2026`** *(secondary)*. 🟢 Permissive licence means existing copies remain usable;
🔴 **upstream has stopped.** 🟡 **The pattern to watch: a permissive licence protects the code
you already have and does not protect the roadmap.** 🟢 Recorded so this KB does not recommend a
frozen base to a coding-education engagement.

### 🟢 What did NOT change, stated so the trend lines stay honest

🟢 The `15`-row platform licence matrix: **re-read from bytes through an independent channel,
`15` / `15` unchanged.** 🟢 p193's `frappe/lms` Affero correction: **confirmed byte-exact by a
second lane.** 🔴 The EMEA vendor-ranking gap: **open for a third pass.** 🔴 The Korea
AI-textbook rollout: **contested, no position asserted.** 🔴 Government primary sources:
**structurally unreachable, not retested, no calls spent.**

## Pass 193 — 2026-10-14

🆕 🔴 **The trend this pass measures is a PUBLICATION practice, and it decides whether any of
`2026`'s education software can be delivered to a client at all.**

### 🔴 T1 — The licence cliff: the `2026` cohort ships stars and no licence

🔵 Five education repositories created in `2026`, `333`–`515`★, every one discovered this
pass, every one's **root tree read from bytes**:

| repo | ★ | created | paths | region signal | licence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [`buynao/aipath`](https://github.com/buynao/aipath) | `515` | `2026-06-12` | `78` | 🔵 bilingual `README.zh-CN.md` — **APAC** | 🔴 **none** |
| [`aaryansamanta/ai-ethos`](https://github.com/aaryansamanta/ai-ethos) | `501` | `2025-12-01` | `150` | 🔵 US `501(c)(3)`, `7` languages — **North America** | 🔴 **none** |
| [`mabdulre9/electrical-engineering`](https://github.com/mabdulre9/electrical-engineering) | `487` | `2026-09-20` | 🔴 **`1`** | 🟡 unplaced | 🔴 **none** |
| [`jude-miller-dev/Milky-institute-online`](https://github.com/jude-miller-dev/Milky-institute-online) | `367` | `2026-01-30` | `786` | 🔵 Chinese/English, Spring Cloud — **APAC** | 🔴 **none** |
| [`bedriyan/medkit-app`](https://github.com/bedriyan/medkit-app) | `333` | `2026-04-26` | `110` | 🟡 unplaced | 🔴 **none** |

🔴 **Five of five.** Add [`JushBJJ/Mr.-Ranedeer-AI-Tutor`](https://github.com/JushBJJ/Mr.-Ranedeer-AI-Tutor)
at **`29 553`★ with no licence object at all** and the pattern spans two orders of magnitude
of reach. 🔵 **No licence is not a gap in the metadata — it is all rights reserved.**

🟢 **Why this is a TREND and not five anecdotes:** the shelf — `30` rows, almost all created
before `2026` — carries a licence on `29` of `30`. 🔴 **The cohort created in `2026` carries
one on `0` of `5`.** 🟡 The two populations differ on exactly the attribute that decides
adoptability, and they differ completely.

🔵 **Mechanism, stated as a hypothesis rather than a measurement:** the `2026` cohort is
published as *content* — a curriculum, a course, a skill, a simulator — by authors who are
teachers rather than maintainers, into harnesses that never asked them for a `LICENSE`.
🔴 **Commercial consequence: the more education-native the project, the less likely it can be
used.**

### 🔴 T2 — The unit keeps shrinking, and it has reached one file

🟢 p192: *"the unit of education software is shrinking from a deployable platform to a few
files of pedagogy."* 🔴 **p193's limit case: `mabdulre9/electrical-engineering`, `487`★,
created `2026-09-20`, `1` path — a single `README.md` — the fastest-accumulating education
row this KB has ever measured.**

🔵 Alongside it, the global trending surface *(measured today, first time in this KB's
history)* carries `mattpocock/skills` at `290 184`★ and `vercel-labs/skills` at `34 736`★.
🟡 Neither is tagged education and both are study/reference artefacts.
🔴 **Three independent surfaces — topic, shelf, trending — now point the same way, and none
of them classifies this material as education.** 🟢 Which is why thirteen passes of
education-topic queries could not see where the field went.

### 🟢 T3 — The field has a denominator now, and it is `7`

🔵 `topic:edtech stars:>400 pushed:>2026-08-01` → **`7` repositories worldwide.** `5` already
on this shelf; of the other two, one is a job-board list and one is unlicensed. 🟢 **Net
adoptable discoveries: `0`.**

🔴 **This is the strongest form p192's *"the frontier has moved inside existing projects"*
claim has taken.** 🟡 A percentage *(`84`% already-known)* says the sampling is saturated. 🟢 A
denominator of `7` says something harder: **the maintained, visible, `edtech`-topicked
open-source population is small enough to recite from memory, and this KB has read all of
it.**

### 🔴 T4 — Licence metadata is not a signal, in either direction

🔵 Measured across `30` shelf rows this pass: **`9` return `NOASSERTION`, `1` returns
`null`.** 🔴 Of those `10`, **`4` hide a licence that bars or encumbers delivery**
*(`CC-BY-NC-SA`, the PageLM community licence, `CC-BY-SA`, `LGPL`)*, **`2` hide a permissive
one** *(`Apache-2.0` ×2)*, `1` is genuinely unlicensed at `29 553`★, and `3` are unread.
🟢 **So `NOASSERTION` predicts nothing** — it is not even conservatively wrong.

🟡 **Trend, not incident: the ecosystem's licence metadata degraded exactly as licence
diversity increased.** Content licences *(`CC-*`)*, bespoke community licences and no licence
at all are all invisible to a field designed for OSI code licences. 🔴 **Every tool that
filters a dependency list by that field — procurement portals, SBOM scanners, approved-tool
registers — is blind in the same place.**

### 🔴 T5 — Governance dates are splitting into cohorts, and the briefings are quoting one

🔵 EMEA, measured this pass: Art. `50` marking applies from **`2026-08-02`** to systems placed
on the market after that date, and from **`2026-12-02`** to generative systems already on the
market. 🔴 **This KB published the December date alone one pass ago, and so does most of the
public commentary.** 🟢 Annex III high-risk — education — moved to **`2027-12-02`** by
Regulation (EU) `2026/1744` *(OJ `2026-07-24`, in force `2026-07-27`)*, with **no substantive
requirement changed.**

🟡 **The trend: deferral is making compliance dates COHORT-dependent rather than
calendar-dependent.** 🔴 Two systems in the same school, doing the same thing, now have
obligations `16` months apart based on when they were placed on the market. 🟢 **An inventory
with placement dates is becoming a compliance artefact in its own right** — and almost nobody
has one.

### 🟡 T6 — The instrument is part of the finding

🔵 This KB spent thirteen passes recording *"GitHub Trending: NOT MEASURED"* and one pass
recording *"the API is gone"*. 🔴 **Both were artefacts of a gated lane inside the measuring
environment, and the `249`-byte body of the `403` said so the whole time.** 🟢 Diagnosis in
[`repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md).

🟡 **Trend for anyone building agentic research pipelines, which is most of this KB's
audience:** as agents acquire more lanes to the same resource — direct `HTTP`, a CLI, an MCP
server, a page fetcher — **a failure in one lane stops looking like a failure and starts
looking like a fact about the world.** 🔴 The `0` that gets published is indistinguishable
from the `0` that is true. 🟢 **The defence is cheap and this pass proves it: read the error
BODY, and ask the gate a question it will answer *(`rate_limit` returned a full live quota)*
before declaring a source dead.**

a recovered discovery channel returns `84`% already-known.** `31` candidate repositories
surfaced, `7` with zero prior mention here, `5` of those actually education. 🔵 **Open-source
education AI has stopped producing new top-level projects faster than this KB can read
them** — the frontier has moved inside the projects that already exist.
